# 3DCraft design: Booleans

Union, difference and intersection of solids (`kernel::boolean`, one file per step in `crates/kernel/src/boolean/`). Method 2 of the map ([`../architecture.md`](../architecture.md) §5); what is still owed is the boolean checklist of [`../roadmap.md`](../roadmap.md).

## Method

`boolean(a, b, op, tolerance)` returns the body and a source for every face, edge and vertex of it (`FaceSource`, `EdgeSource`, `VertexSource`; `merged` lists every operand face a result face has a piece of). Operands are any solids on planes, quadrics, tori, Bézier and NURBS surfaces (rational ones, with poles, closing on themselves), with edges on lines, circles, ellipses and splines.

1. **Section** (`section.rs`, `hits.rs`). For each pair of faces that can meet (boxes, sides and covers: *Faces that lie near one another* below), the curves in which their surfaces meet come from `geom::meet_surfaces`, asked inside the box the two faces share (grown by 16 tolerances and 1.3 % of the larger face's box; when what lies in that box cannot be traced, asked again with the room of the smaller face, *A plane beside a rippled surface* below), so what else either body holds does not change them. A curve is cut where an edge of either face meets the other's surface (closed forms: lines, circles and ellipses against planes and cylinders, and since 2026-10-22 lines and circles against balls and lines against cones, whose distance is monotone between turning points in closed form; else roots of the signed distance isolated by a bound on how fast it can change, never by sampling) and where it ends; a stretch is kept when its middle is in or on both faces, and left out without that when its middle or an end lies clear of either face's box (between its cuts a stretch crosses the boundary of neither face); every edge of the result on a part of a spline curve gets that part as its curve (knot insertion, after merging), so an edge's curve reaches no further than the edge. A vertex is always where an edge's *own* curve meets a surface, except that the hit of a spline edge (one that stands for where two surfaces meet) is taken to where its two faces' surfaces and the other surface meet when that is more than a quarter of the tolerance away and within the tolerance of the edge (`corner.rs`, since 2026-10-22: *A spline edge that meets a surface at a shallow angle* below), and a spline edge's touch of a surface (its distance from it least within the tolerance, no crossing) to where the curve it stands for comes nearest that surface, where it reaches it, on the boundary of the face it touches (since 2026-11-24: *A tangent edge across a spline border* below) and inside that face where one of the edge's faces is tangent to it there and the result keeps both (since 2026-11-25: *A pinch along a curve* below). Where on its edge such a place lies is asked of the edge's nearest point (`outline::nearest`; a spline's by branch and bound on its one speed bound and, where that runs out of stretches, on the boxes of its Bézier pieces, since 2026-11-21), and a place of a spline edge that is not found on it again refuses the boolean: it is never a cut left out (*A plane cut the boolean's own check refused as an invalid body* below).
2. **Imprint.** Edges are split at the vertices found; edge pieces and stretches become arcs, two arcs between the same vertices along the same curve are one, and an edge that lies on a face or edge of the other body is shared from then on. Where two faces' surfaces only touch (points and no curve: a contact along a curve with no closed form reads as touches), an edge of either that lies on the other's surface is a stretch of its own, its own curve clipped like any meeting curve (`section::lying_edges`, since 2026-10-18: the strip of a fillet's tool on the face it rounds, *Tangent contact along an edge* below). Nothing is matched by position afterwards.
3. **Regions** (`regions.rs`). Around each vertex the arcs are sorted by direction in the tangent plane, told apart further along when they leave together (`untie`), except that an imprinted arc that leaves along a piece of the face's boundary is put on the face's side of it without measuring (`order_run`, *Tangent contact of higher order at the boundary* below); loops are followed keeping the region on the left and grouped in a chart of the surface: a point just left of a loop (`outline::left_of`), stepped in from the middle of one of its pieces with nothing nearer to it than that piece, says whether the loop is an outline or a hole and in which outline, and where another loop touches the piece at that very place (a wall that thins to nothing, cut across) the point is stepped in from another place of the piece (since 2026-11-21, the same finding); where the chart cuts its surface open along a circle (a torus, a freeform surface closed both ways), the point is taken on the loop's side of that circle (since 2026-11-22, *A torus cut beside its axis and then square to it* below).
4. **Classify** (`classify.rs`, `chart.rs`). One point well inside a region decides it: on a coincident face of the other body, or inside or outside it by ray casting. A ray counts a crossing or is not used: one that meets an edge, a vertex or a face tangentially is abandoned for the next of twelve, and so is one whose meeting with a freeform face could not be told (`hits::MISSED`: the distance at a place no bound could clear is as near as the search for the nearest point got, and is held against the bound below the distance before a hit is left out on its word); a point no ray can place is not placed (`Place::Unknown`, and for a region the next of its points), never guessed (*A crossing left out* below). A region that holds no point four tolerances from its boundary (a sliver: the corner a curve cuts off a face just past its vertex) is read at its boundary instead, from what the other body is along an arc of it (`classify::at_boundary`, *Slivers read at their boundary* below); a sliver the other body does not pass through (no arc of it is on that body: a face that is itself a sliver, left by an earlier boolean) lies on one side of it as a whole and is told by a point as close as a quarter of the margin to its boundary (`classify::samples`, *A face that is itself a sliver* below). Inside or outside a face is a winding number in a chart of its surface computed from points in space, so seams and poles are ordinary points: conformal maps for the analytic kinds (a torus cut open along a circle around its tube or its axis that the loops provably do not cross, and held to that along every stretch of the winding sum: *The fuzzer's wrong results* below), the parameter rectangle (drawn as a wedge, a lens, a disc or an annulus where a border is a pole or the surface closes) for freeform faces, whose nearest points and bounds come from a `PatchTree` of Bézier pieces (`freeform.rs`).
5. **Select** by the table below (coincident faces with the same or opposite normals included).
6. **Sew** (`sew.rs`). Where more than two faces meet along an arc or a vertex, each fan gets its own copy, so every result is a manifold.
7. **Merge** (`merge.rs`). Pieces of one operand face, or coincident faces of both, that share an edge and a surface become one face again, but never one that would lie on both sides of an edge: faces that use two of the copies sewing made of one arc (where parts of the result touch along it) stay apart, and the touch is the zero-thickness refusal's to find (since 2026-11-22, *A union whose level tops meet along a line where the bodies part* below); the result passes `topo::check` or the operation fails.

**Tolerances.** Points within half the tolerance are one vertex, apart at twice it, refused in between; the same for faces over each other, and a lens thinner than twice the tolerance between faces of different kinds, or beside a curve geometry calls a touch (since 2026-10-22), is measured: up to half the tolerance they touch, above it the operation refuses. No decision is taken on the sign of a number that could be rounding. A place where an edge meets a surface within twice the tolerance of an end of the edge that lies on the surface is that end; where a curve runs through the place more than the tolerance from the end and the curve's piece is then missing from an imprint, the two are refused as two points too near (since 2026-11-22, *A plane that passes a vertex by less than a tolerance* below). Bodies that only touch have nothing in common: the union is both bodies, the intersection empty, the difference the first body. Inside an edge or a face, where none of these places is, the result itself is looked at (`near.rs`, since 2026-11-24, *Near approaches inside an edge or a face* below): two of its parts that come within twice the tolerance of each other are refused as zero thickness, two walls of one shell between half and twice the tolerance apart as too near to tell apart, unless the wedge readings join them at the place (a wedge, a wall that thins to a knife edge). These distance rules, and the snapping of hits onto the ends of edges, are structural debt (the map's §9): they read one tolerance for every entity.

**Tolerant operands** (since 2026-10-21, `boolean/loose.rs`; the decision of 2026-10-18, option (4)). An operand with loose entities (a vertex or an edge with a tolerance of its own, [`topology.md`](topology.md) › *Tolerant entities*) is worked on at the largest tolerance of both operands and the document's, the *working tolerance*: every rule above runs unchanged with that number in place of the document's (no rule reads an entity's own tolerance yet; that is for later, one rule at a time, where a measurement shows the coarseness costs something). A working tolerance above the cap (`BooleanOptions::loosest`, the document's `tolerance.loosest`, by default 1000 times the linear tolerance) is refused (`KernelError::ToleranceAboveCap`). Each operand is first checked at the document's tolerance with its own table and the cap: a gap no entity records is the operand's defect and refused (`InvalidBody`), never hidden by the working tolerance. The result's entities: a vertex or edge kept from an operand in which it was loose keeps that tolerance; every other one starts exact. The result is checked at the document's tolerance with its table, and each entity not within its own is *measured* (`topo::measure_edge`, `measure_vertex`) and given what it needs (`RECORD_MARGIN` above the measure, never more than the working tolerance it was built at), but only an entity the boolean could have moved: one it made, one loose in its operand, or an exact one next to those (an end of a new or loose edge, an edge ending at a new or loose vertex, an edge of a face merged from both operands or bounded by the other operand's edges). Every tolerance given is reported in `BooleanResult::raised` (entity, tolerance, origin: new, loose or exact), and `document.check` lists every body with loose entities (`loose`). What still fails is refused (`InvalidBody`), never returned. So new entities that lie within the document's tolerance stay exact, and a result whose loose entities were all cut away is exact again. Two exact operands never take this path: their boolean is bit for bit what it was. The operations on one body (fillet, chamfer, offset, move, draft and delete face, shell, thicken) do the same since 2026-10-22 (`kernel/src/loose_op.rs`, *Tolerant modelling, the second slice* below), the boolean between such a body and a tool made from it at four times the working tolerance (`loose_op::for_tool`, *the third slice*); a sweep, a thread or an extrusion meets a loose body only through this boolean.

**Speed.** The faces of B a face of A can meet, the faces a ray or a point near the boundary can reach, and the edges of a face near the other face of a pair come from trees of boxes (`boolean/facetree.rs`, *Many lumps* below), in the order testing every one would take them, so the work grows with the faces, not with their product. A face whose box is loose (a curved piece turned off the axes) is covered by boxes of patches of its surface, a tree made as it is asked (`boolean/cover.rs`, since 2026-10-22): two faces whose covers are further apart than 16 tolerances are not intersected, and a point further than four tolerances from a face's cover is outside it without its outline being asked. Outlines are made when a pair of faces first needs them, kept with a box per boundary piece; pure functions are remembered by the exact bits of what was asked (nearest points per tree, the trees themselves, checked bodies, projectors, numeric intersections), so nothing changes but the time. A freeform face's tree takes its Bézier spans in groups of four neighbours, level by level up to the whole surface (`freeform.rs` › `Group`, since 2026-11-17): a search for the nearest point, a bound below a distance or the least singular value near a point passes over a group by its box, so a question costs the spans near it and the levels above them, not every span of the face (*A face of 2¹⁸ spans* below). Each change meant only for speed is held bit for bit to the corpus digest and the random runs.

**Bounded work** (`freeform::holding`, `OPERATION_WORK`, `craft3d_geom::allowance::asked`; since 2026-11-16). Everything that enters through the budget of held trees, the boolean, interference (`interference`, `common_volume`, `contact`), the boolean's own check and the classification of points (`contains_points`, `classify_points`), is held to a bound on its work, as the face operations are: past it the operation is refused, *"it needs more work than one operation is allowed"*, named as the operation it was, with the faces the step that reached the bound was on; it never runs on. The bound is on the **ledger**, not on the count. Beside the count each thread keeps what its operations asked for: every unit charged; what a tree's memos answer from memory (each answer keeps what finding it asked, measured through the regions where the count or the ledger is held back, `allowance::costs_of`, so the same whoever searched it and why; and an operation pays for a question at its first asking, and again only where a memo of its own of the same size, 16 384 answers let go all at once when full, would have forgotten it, whether the shared memo still holds it or not: `allowance::asked_first_time_in`, `unasked`); a tree once an operation; a fine piece at every look into it, at what making it asked, so a piece a store let go of and made again is in it; and the normal net of a rational patch, which the count leaves out (in the ledger alone, priced as the polynomial net is counted: some 136 units a bicubic piece). The count of an operation outside a bound is not a function of the operation alone, since the section's helper threads fill the memos whose hits it does not charge; the ledger is, the same with the caches cold or warm, after other operations on the same surfaces and on one, two or all threads (`boolean_ledger.rs`). The bound opens no scope (`allowance::asked_within`): the helpers, the shared memos and every count stay as they are, and nothing below it is decided differently. The helpers meeting the section's pairs ahead are held to it pair by pair and keep nothing found past it, and are called off once the section ends, refused or built (`allowance::stopping_when`). A try that reached the bound is not believed, whatever it gave. Its value, ten billion units, is a hundred and eighty times the dearest operation of the corpus, the hunts, the benchmark, the examples and the side repositories' documents (*A boolean that runs for most of an hour* below).

**Lumps and cavities** (`kernel::lumps`, `topo::check` per shell; since 2026-10-17). A solid is a set of closed shells, one outer shell per lump and any cavities in it. The boolean needs nothing special for them: a region is classified by the parity of a ray against the whole other operand, every shell of it, so a shell that does not meet the other body is kept or dropped by the same rule as the faces that do, and sewing makes the shells as the connected components of what is kept, so a result falls into lumps and cavities by itself. Which shell is which is geometry, told by `lumps(body, tolerance)` for every consumer (STEP export, `document.check` deep): a few points of a shell classified against each other shell alone (each other shell prepared once, for the points of every shell its box holds) say which shells it lies in: a point on the other shell is refused as shells that meet, points that disagree or none that can tell are unsupported, since that shells do not cross is the self-intersection check's to find; an outside lies in an even number of shells, a cavity in an odd number, the shell it lies in most deeply is of the other kind one level up, and a cavity belongs to that lump: that is what lumps and cavities are, and it decides (since 2026-10-20; before, the sign of the volume decided and the nesting had to agree). The volume a shell encloses (`topo::shell_volumes`: the faces integrated once, summed per shell, with the integrator's bound and the shell's area) is held to it where it has a sign to read, beyond the integrator's bound plus what the tolerance allows (twice the tolerance times the area, and the flux through the gaps of up to twice the tolerance along the shell's edges, bounded by two thirds of the tolerance times the diagonal of the box the point the integrals are about lies in times the edges' length: `lumps::uncertainty`; a shell whose sign the body's diagonal leaves unread is measured again alone, about a point in its own box, and held to its own diagonal); a shell whose readable volume says the other kind is turned inside out. Anything else is `Defect::ShellNesting`. `topo::check` holds each shell to hanging together through edges, sharing no edge with another shell and, in a solid of several, to Euler–Poincaré on its own (`V − E + F − R − 2H = 2(1 − g)`, `topo::shell_genera`). Volume is the signed sum over the shells. `document.check` deep reports a body that is not lumps and cavities as a problem and one whose shells cannot be sorted under `marginal`; the STEP export sorts nothing when exactly one shell faces outward. A feature whose result has several lumps gives one body (decided 2026-10-18; separate bodies through `body.split`, owed). Held to: `crates/kernel/tests/lumps.rs` (cuts into two lumps, cavities of a block, a ball, a ring and a spline cone, a ball in a ball, operands with a cavity or two lumps opened, kept, filled, drilled and joined, a hollow in a hollow, seeded mistakes of nesting; closed-form volumes, lumps and cavities, the genus of each shell, watertight meshes, the full check and the identities of sets, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6) and `crates/engine/tests/lumps.rs` (the same through the commands, with fillets, offsets, shells, delete face, STEP and the round trip); FreeCAD reads six such documents at three scales (`step_freecad.rs`). Sheet bodies are refused as operands ("booleans need solids"): owed.

**Sheets** (`boolean/cut.rs`, `classify::sheet`; since 2026-10-18). A sheet (an open shell, [`topology.md`](topology.md)) is an operand of a subtract or an intersect with a solid, read as point sets, and the result is of the target's kind. *A sheet trimmed by a solid* (the sheet the target): its regions are classified by the solid's rays as any region is; intersect keeps those inside, subtract those outside, nothing of the solid is kept, and the result is a sheet, so `area(S ∩ A) + area(S ∖ A) = area(S)`. *A solid cut by a sheet* (the sheet the tool): the sheet is a wall whose material lies behind it, as behind a face of a solid; the sheet's regions inside the solid are the cap, kept as they are for intersect and turned over for subtract (subtract keeps the side the sheet's normal points to: decided 2026-10-18); the solid's regions have no ray to tell them (a sheet has no inside), so each is given its side where it meets the sheet: along an arc where the sheet meets the solid's boundary (the cap's boundary, where the sheet passes through the boundary; and the boundary of a region of the solid that lies on the sheet, where the sheet's contact with a face ends: since 2026-10-20) the direction from the arc into the region (`n × t`, the face to the left of its loop) points along the sheet's normal (in front: subtract keeps it) or against it (behind: intersect keeps it), and regions that share an arc that is no such meeting are on one side (a flood over the solid's arcs). So `vol(front) + vol(behind) = vol(A)`. The sheet must reach across the solid: every arc of the cap's boundary lies on an edge or across a face of the solid (checked before the solid's faces are divided), divides the face it crosses (a ring cut once is one piece of material on both sides of the cut), every region is reached from a meeting, and none from both sides; otherwise, and for a cut that is tangent along a curve, it is refused with the reason. *A piece of the sheet on a face of the solid* counts as inside the solid, a closed set (decided 2026-10-18, built 2026-10-20), whichever way the face looks: a trim keeps it for intersect and takes it away for subtract; in a cut it is a face the solid and the material behind the sheet share, so the solid's region there stands for it, kept as between two solids (`classify::solids`: intersect keeps it when it looks the sheet's way, subtract when it looks the other way), told by its class and not by its arcs, and the sheet's piece is never a cap (a sheet on a face of a block, facing out, leaves nothing in front and the whole block behind). A boundary edge of the sheet lying inside such a face is imprinted on it from the edge itself (`section::lying_edges`, `Lying::Boundary`: an edge of a solid is imprinted there by its second face, a boundary edge has none), so the face is divided where the sheet on it ends, from where the rim enters the face to where it leaves it (`section::edge_crossings`: the rim and the face's edges lie on one surface, so where they cross is found between the curves, since 2026-10-21); the region beyond continues the sheet's surface and is told nothing by that arc (reached from another meeting, or refused: a disc on a plate around a boss does not reach across the plate). A sheet whose shell is closed is a solid's shell mislabelled and is refused by `topo::check` (`SheetClosed`): edges and none on the boundary, or a face without loops on a surface that closes all around (a sphere, a torus, a freeform surface closed both ways; since 2026-10-21). A sheet result whose boundary comes back to itself in a point (a hole tangent to what a trim takes away, the figure of eight a plane leaves of a ring it touches inside) is refused as zero thickness: a vertex within twice the tolerance of another vertex or an edge of its shell (`craft3d_topo::touches`, the rule regeneration holds every body to); the sewing gives each side its own vertex there, so `topo::check` alone does not see it. *Two sheets* (`two_sheets.rs`, decided 2026-10-22, built the same day): the target sheet is cut by the tool sheet as a solid is, by the same flood: its faces are divided where the tool passes through them, a region beside such an arc is in front of the tool when the direction into it within the target's surface points along the tool's normal, regions joined by an arc the tool does not meet are on one side; subtract keeps the part in front, intersect the part behind, and the result is a sheet of pieces of the target's faces, facing as they did. A union sews two sheets along boundary edges they share (their edge pieces are one arc where they coincide, and coincident faces that share one merge), and is refused where they meet otherwise. Refused as well (work owed): a union of a sheet with a solid, and a shell of the solid the sheet does not cross (which side of an open sheet a lump or cavity lies on is not told yet). The check of the result (`verify.rs`) holds a trim to every rule of a boolean (the volume of a sheet is zero; no point is compared, a sheet has no inside), and a cut to the volume and reach of a difference, to its seeded points one way (inside the result is inside the solid) and to the side kept: a cut face (on the surface of a face of the sheet and of no face of the solid) faces against the sheet's normal for a subtract and along it for an intersect; the far-face rule does not hold for a cut, which keeps of a face far from the sheet the side it is on. Ray classification of points against a sheet (`contains_points`, `classify_points`) and `interference` with one are refused.

**Interference** (`contact.rs`, `interference`; the command `document.interference`; `common_volume` is its first step alone, for the `overlap` measure, since 2026-10-26). Whether two solids are clear, touch or overlap, read-only. Boxes grown by twice the tolerance that do not meet are clear at no further cost. Otherwise the intersection (parts that touch allowed) is the overlap; when it is empty, the section says whether and how they meet: a stretch of curve of positive length is contact along an edge, vertices where both meet, points where faces only touch and a corner of one on a face of the other are points; where faces of both lie on one surface with their outward normals opposite (a face's orientation counts: a pin in its hole, a body in a cavity), their union decides between a shared area (the union's boundary is smaller than both boundaries by twice that area, which is reported) and only an edge of it. Where none of those places is a contact, an edge or a face of one within twice the tolerance of an edge or a face of the other is a touch in a point (`near.rs`, since 2026-11-24). Contact points and the faces they are on come with it; a refusal of the boolean is an error, never "clear". Held to closed forms at three scales (boxes on a face, an edge and a corner, a pin in its hole, a block against an inner wall, a body filling a cavity, a cylinder through, lying on and a ball on a plate, a tilted block on an edge and a corner) and to the laser box of the showcase, whose overlaps at kerf 0.15 mm agree with an independent cell grid to 4 digits (`crates/engine/tests/interference.rs`). The command checks at most 2000 pairs whose boxes meet per question.

**A proof of apart before the boolean** (`crates/engine/src/apart.rs`, 2026-11-05). Between the box test and the exact query, `document.interference`, the `overlap` measure and a motion's collisions (`view.motion {collisions}`, `motion.bake`) try to prove a pair apart from meshes; a pair proven apart is answered clear (no common volume) without the boolean, every other pair goes to the kernel as before, so every touch, overlap, volume, contact area and refusal reported is the kernel's own. *What it rests on, honestly:* each body is meshed at a chordal tolerance `ε` of its own with the angle criterion of the clearance measure's meshes (12 degrees, `verify::MESH_ANGLE`). The tessellator holds every triangle to `ε` **at samples** (the middles of its sides and its centroid; only the first grid of a spline face is sized by a proven bound), so "every point of a face within `ε` of its triangles" is the promise the clearance measure's lower bound already rests on (`verify::Part::err`), not a proof. Where a face is near quadratic over a triangle the error inside it is at most 4/3 of what the samples show; the test takes four times `ε` (`PROMISE_FACTOR`), a margin of three over that. The meshes are compared on trees of boxes (median splits, eight triangles a leaf; triangle pairs by the distances of their corners and sides and whether a side pierces the other): meshes further apart than `D = 4 (εa + εb) + 2 w + s` (`w` the working tolerance, the largest of the document's and every entity tolerance of either body, within which the boolean takes faces as touching and by which the box test grows the boxes; `s` a rounding slack of 1e-8 of the bodies' extent and distance from the origin) are taken to bound surfaces further apart than `2 w`. *Why then one corner per piece decides containment:* a body's boundary is one or more closed surfaces (a lump each, a cavity each, several lumps in one body since 2026-10-18). The mesh is cut into its connected pieces (corners joined by the triangles they share and where their positions are the same bits; each face has corners of its own, meeting its neighbours on the same points), so every shell is one piece or more. If the two boundaries are further apart than `D`, each piece of one lies wholly inside or wholly outside the other solid: its winding number against the other mesh is constant along it, because it never meets that mesh. And if the solids share a point while their boundaries do not meet, the boundary of their common part, which is not empty, lies on one of the two boundaries inside the other solid: some piece is then inside. So a corner of **every** piece of each mesh is tested against the other by its winding number (a sum of solid angles, Van Oosterom and Strackee; the corner lies on its own surface, further than `D` from the other mesh, so the sum is well conditioned), and anything but 0 within a tenth for any of them is not proven. Before 2026-11-06 only the first corner of each mesh was tested: a body of two lumps, one far off and one inside the other body, was answered clear when the far lump's face came first (a review found it; `bodies_of_several_shells_answer_as_the_kernel_alone`). A mesh that cannot be made, a winding number that is not near an integer, a search past its bound: not proven, never apart. *What it does not check:* the validity of the operands, which the boolean checks first: like the box test, the proof answers before the boolean would, so an invalid body far enough from another is clear rather than refused (an invalid body never comes out of regeneration). *Static data once:* a placed body (an assembly's instance, `meshes::note_placed`) is tested through the body it was placed from, moved by the relative pose of the pair, so its mesh and tree are made once per body and tolerance and kept (512 MB at most, the least recently used let go; 64 MB in a browser; a shape being made holds what a mesh of the triangle cap may take against that budget before it is made, and callers wait for room while others are made): a moment of a motion brings only the poses. The tolerances stand on a ladder of powers of two, `1/8192` of the body's size first (at least the document's tolerance); when the meshes come within `D` but further apart than touching surfaces would leave them (`2 w` and the two chords), the coarser mesh is made finer once, so that the new `D` is at most half the room between them (at most to `1/131072` of the size), so the meshes made for one moment serve the next. A proof is a function of the two bodies, the pose and the tolerance alone, so the answers are the same on any number of threads; `craft3d_engine::set_mesh_proof(false)` turns it off for tests that compare. *Held to* `crates/engine/tests/collisions_fast.rs`: blocks touching, apart by half, twice and ten times the tolerance and further, deep overlap, a block nested inside another, a ball in a closed cavity, a pin in a hole with room and one without, at 0.01, 1 and 1000; bodies of two lumps with one inside the other body, in both orders, hollow balls nested in each other's cavity and too large for it, a body of two lumps one of which lies in a closed box's cavity: `document.interference` and the overlap assertion answer bit for bit as without the proof, the ball and the loose pin proven apart; the same on one thread and on many; the planetary gearbox's interference, the collisions of two moments and `motion.check` of its overlap assertion as without it, every pair past its boxes proven apart. *Measured:* see [`motion.md`](motion.md) › *Findings* (2026-11-05).

**Its own check** (`verify.rs`, since 2026-10-17). Before a result is returned it is held to what every exact boolean of its operands keeps, and refused as `KernelError::WrongResult` (a bug, named) when it does not: every vertex and the middle of every edge within the tolerance of where the result can be (the box of A or of B for a union, of A for a difference, of both for an intersection), and every face of an operand whose box is further than the tolerance from the other operand's box in the result as it was (union: of A and of B; difference: of A): the same surface, side, loops and edges. `boolean::verify` with `VerifyOptions::default()` adds the volume bounds and seeded points (below, *The boolean's own check*); the tests and `document.check` deep run that, and `CRAFT3D_BOOLEAN_CHECK=full` makes every boolean run it.

**Held to**: closed forms, the identities of sets, analytic twins of rational faces, 400 to 4000 seeded points against exact membership, the mesh self-intersection check, and a corpus of 3996 pairs against FreeCAD (11,988 outcomes: 11,573 agree, 249 refused, none wrong or invalid; [`../testing.md`](../testing.md)).

## Findings and history

> Moved verbatim from `docs/architecture.md` on 2026-10-15. Section numbers (§4.1, §5.3, …) in the text are those of that file before the split; [README.md](README.md) says where each one is now.

### Booleans as built (M7, generalised 2026-10-13)

`kernel::boolean::boolean(a, b, op, tolerance)` returns a `BooleanResult`: the body and, per face, edge and vertex of it, a source (`FaceSource`, `EdgeSource`, `VertexSource`). `boolean_with` takes `BooleanOptions` (`merge`, on by default). `boolean::contains_points` classifies points against a solid with the same ray casting the booleans use; it does not go through `mass_properties`, so a count of sampled points measures a body independently of it.

**Method** (imprint, classify, select, sew, merge; one file per step in `crates/kernel/src/boolean/`):

1. *Section* (`section.rs`). For each face of A and face of B that can meet (their boxes meet and neither lies wholly to one side of the other's surface), the curves in which their surfaces meet are asked of `meet_surfaces`, with the box where both faces are, grown by 16 tolerances and 1.3 % of the diagonal of the larger face's box (until 2026-10-15 the box where both *bodies* are, so that what else a body held decided where two surfaces were looked at: *The box of the two faces* below). A curve is cut where an edge of either face meets the other face's surface (`hits.rs`: the closed forms of `geom::intersect`, and where there is none the roots of the signed distance along the curve, isolated by the fact that the distance changes no faster than the curve moves), and where the curve itself ends. A stretch between two cuts is kept when its middle is in or on both faces. The boolean does not know which pair of surfaces a curve came from, nor whether it is exact: a vertex is always where an *edge's own curve* meets a surface, and is then found on the stretch within the tolerance.
2. *Imprint.* Edges are split at the vertices found on them. Edge pieces and stretches are registered as arcs; two arcs between the same vertices along the same curve are one arc. An edge of B that lies on a face of A, or along an edge of A, is shared from then on, and nothing is matched by position afterwards.
3. *Regions* (`regions.rs`). A face with arcs across it falls into regions: around each vertex the arcs are sorted by direction in the tangent plane (at the apex of a cone: seen along the axis), arcs that leave in one direction are told apart a little further along, and loops are followed keeping the region on the left. An arc with the same region on both sides divides nothing and is dropped. Loops are grouped in a chart of the surface: a loop that goes around a point just to its left is an outside, any other a hole of the innermost outside around it.
4. *Classify* (`classify.rs`, `chart.rs`). One point well inside a region decides for the region: on a face of the other solid with a coincident surface, or inside or outside the other solid by ray casting (a ray that meets an edge or grazes a face is abandoned for another, twelve at most; a point within twice the tolerance of the other body is no use, and the next of up to four is tried). **Inside or outside a face is a winding number in a chart of its surface**, computed from points in space, so no seam or pole is a special place: a plane is its own chart; a cylinder is mapped conformally (angle and `exp(height / radius)` as polar coordinates, evaluated without the exponential); a cone is dropped along its axis onto a plane, which makes the apex an ordinary point; a sphere is projected stereographically from a point chosen clear of the boundary; a torus is cut open along a circle that the boundary does not cross; a freeform face has its own parameters, drawn as a wedge or a lens where a border of its patch is one point. A face on a sphere or a torus may contain the place its chart leaves out; the winding of its loops about a point just inside it says which. A face without loops is its whole surface.
5. *Select.* See the table below.
6. *Sew* (`sew.rs`). Faces connected through edges form a shell. Where more than two faces meet along an arc, faces and material alternate around it: each face that runs against the arc is paired with the next face counter-clockwise (faces tangent there are put in order a little way off the arc) and every pair gets its own copy of the edge. Where several fans of faces meet at a vertex, every fan gets its own copy of the vertex. The copies are in one place and share nothing, so every result is a manifold. A bounded face on a sphere gets a frame whose poles are clear of its boundary (a sphere is the same surface whichever way its poles point; what reads a face through its parameters is at its best that way).
7. *Merge* (`merge.rs`). Faces that share an edge and lie on one surface facing one way are one face when they are pieces of one face of an operand, or pieces of a face of A and a face of B that are coincident; every edge inside such a group goes and the loops are spliced where it was. A vertex left with two edges that continue each other along one curve goes too, unless its operand had it like that. The face that remains has the source of the lowest-numbered face of A in the group, else of B; a joined edge the lesser of its sources (an edge of A before an edge of B before an intersection). The result passes `topo::check` or the operation fails.

| Region of | Outside the other | Inside the other | Coincident, same normal | Coincident, opposite normals |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A, union | kept | | kept (the source is A's face) | |
| A, intersection | | kept | kept (the source is A's face) | |
| A, difference | kept | | | kept |
| B, union | kept | | | |
| B, intersection | | kept | | |
| B, difference | | kept, turned inside out | | |

**Contact without overlap.** Bodies that only touch (face against face along a line, a circle or at a point; an edge against a face or an edge; a vertex against anything) have nothing in common: the intersection is `EmptyResult`, the difference is the first body with the entities it had. A curve along which two faces touch is imprinted like any other; where the result does not need it (it ends inside a face, or has one face on both sides) it leaves no trace, and where it does (a cylinder that touches all four sides of its block cuts it into four parts) it is an edge.

**Zero thickness (the developer's decision of 2026-10-15).** A result whose parts (shells) touch each other, along an edge, a line or in a point, is refused, as other modellers refuse it ("Cannot create zero-thickness geometry"): `KernelError::ZeroThickness`, with the place and a face of each operand there; regeneration names both faces by their persistent names and suggests moving the parts apart by more than the tolerance or into each other. That is the union of bodies that only touch, and any result whose parts hang together only by an edge or a vertex (two opposite quarters cut out of a plate, the corners a cylinder leaves of the block it touches on all four sides, the two halves of a rod that goes on in a ring). Separate bodies may touch; a cavity apart from the outside is two shells and fine. Parts can only touch where the operands meet, or where an operand already touches itself (a shell meeting itself, or two of its shells touching: such an operand cut in two may leave parts touching where the operands never met), so only those places are looked at (`zero_thickness` in `boolean/mod.rs`): the vertices both operands have, the vertices the section made, the points where two faces only touch (`Sections::touches`), a point inside every stretch of intersection curve, the vertices of either operand that lie on a face of the other (`on_the_other`, only those in the other's box, against the faces whose boxes hold them, through a grid of face boxes), and the places where an operand's vertex lies on a vertex or an edge of that operand not its own (`craft3d_topo::touches`). Regeneration's `body_touches_itself` looks at every result for those vertex and edge forms as well. A place is a touch when it is within twice the tolerance of a face of one part and of a face of another, not outside either face's boundary, and the nearest points of the two faces are within twice the tolerance of each other: the parts are no further apart than the tolerance can tell, the rule `craft3d_topo::touches` applies to vertices and edges. With the refusal off (`BooleanOptions::refuse_zero_thickness`, used by blend's tool steps and the cut of an extrusion up to a face, whose results regeneration checks) the result is both parts in one body, shells that share no entity, every edge shared by exactly two faces; the result is the same, bit for bit, as before the refusal (the corpus digest `19af10963b6f12c2` and both random digests unchanged).

*What it changed (expectations):* the corpus counts 773 such refusals (tangent 295, contact 281, near 197), each held to FreeCAD's answer by the body made without the refusal and to a point on two of its parts (`contains_points` of each part alone); the score reads 10800 agreeing + 773 refused by design. The random runs refuse 45 (flat) and 30 (every shape) results, all on the grid; `self_intersection.rs` 18. **Documents that relied on it no longer regenerate:** a union of bodies that touch, an extrusion of profiles that touch at a corner, a loft, sweep, pattern or mirror whose shells touch (a pattern's instances are united, and regeneration's check, `body_touches_itself`, now looks between the shells of a solid too). Owed: tangent contact of two faces where the section reports no stretch and no touch point; faces of one operand that touch each other with no vertex there (an operand that is itself zero thick that way); a result whose faces cannot be outlined is not looked at for this.

**Where an edge meets the other body's surface** (2026-10-18, the fuzzer's seed 19604). A ball that touches the rim of a cone's base from outside, in one point in the plane of the base, was united into two shells that touch: no vertex of either lies there, the section's curves (the base's plane cuts the ball in its equator) leave no stretch inside both faces, and `Sections::touches` holds only points where two surfaces touch, which the plane and the ball do not. The only sign is that the rim meets the ball's surface there, which `edge_cuts` finds and `clip` drops with the stretch it bounds. Every such point (`Sections::edge_meets`: where an edge of one face meets the other face's surface, for every pair whose surfaces meet in a curve) is now a place `zero_thickness` looks at. *What it changed (expectations):* 158 unions of the corpus that were scored `match` (157 of two shells, FreeCAD's answer too, and `contact/cone-spline/005` of one) are refused as zero thickness now, all in `contact` (100) and `near` (58), where an edge of one body touches a face of the other; each is held by the corpus's own witness, a point on two parts of the body made without the refusal (`on_parts`, rays on each part alone) or, for the one shell, `touches_itself_at`. The agreement sum stays at 11582 and the digest at `3f00bc8ced79a654` (the bodies made without the refusal are the bodies made before); the outright agreement goes from 10717 to 10559 and the zero-thickness count from 865 to 1023, so `MATCHED_FLOOR` and `ZERO_THICK_CEILING` move with them (an expectation change, logged in the dev log). Pinned by `boolean_contact.rs` › `a_ball_that_touches_the_rim_of_a_cone_from_outside` (refused at three scales, held to the rules of bodies that only touch; three tolerances apart, two parts).

*What it costs (2026-10-15, release, a machine loaded to about 40 by other agents):* a row of 250 disjoint cubes united one by one 5.1 s with the refusal, 4.6 s without; of 1000, 50.7 s and 52.9 s (noise: a row of disjoint parts has no place where the operands meet, so nothing is looked at and this measures nothing but that; the drilled rows below are the real measure); 100 and 200 holes drilled one by one into a block with a ball cavity (two shells every step) 0.22 and 0.69 s with, 0.19 and 0.67 s without. `craft3d_topo::touches` on the 8000 vertices of the 1000 cubes (all at one x, which degraded the former sort along x) 11 ms with a grid of about one vertex per cell (`crates/kernel/tests/zero_thickness_cost.rs`).

**Supported and not** (tests: `crates/kernel/tests/boolean.rs`, `boolean_surfaces.rs`, `boolean_contact.rs`, `boolean_random.rs`, `boolean_corpus.rs`):

| Configuration | Result |
|---|---|
| faces on planes, cylinders, cones, spheres and tori; whole spheres and tori; edges on lines, circles, ellipses and splines | operands like any other |
| coincident faces (same or opposite normals; overlapping, touching or sharing edges), on any of those surfaces | handled by the table above, and merged |
| faces that touch along a curve or at a point; bodies that touch along an edge or at a vertex | handled: see *Contact without overlap* |
| curves that leave a vertex in one direction (a circle tangent to an edge it meets) | handled |
| a boundary through the apex of a cone or a pole of a sphere | handled |
| intersection curves that are approximations (splines within a deviation of both surfaces) | handled: tolerant edges |
| a pair of surfaces whose intersection `meet_surfaces` does not give | `Unsupported`, with its reason |
| a Bézier, B-spline or rational NURBS face in either body (a domed block against a block, a cylinder, a cone, a ball, a torus or another dome; a rational patch that is a piece of a cylinder, a cone, a sphere or a torus, or none of those, against the same) | operands like any other: see *Freeform faces* below; the curves in which its surface meets another are splines within the tolerance |
| a freeform face with a pole (a border of its patch that is one point: a dome or a drop swept by a spline that ends on the axis, an octant of a ball as a rational patch), cut away from the pole, around it or through it | an operand like any other since 2026-10-14: see *Freeform faces with a pole* below |
| a freeform face on a surface that closes on itself (in u, in v, both ways, with a pole at one end or at each; with or without a seam edge), cut across its seam, along it, through its poles or around it | an operand like any other since 2026-10-14: see *Freeform faces that close on themselves* below |
| a freeform face that closes into one point on two adjacent borders (a pole at a corner of its parameters: a lune of a ball by stereographic projection, a cone's apex at a corner), cut away from the pole, around it, through it and at the far corner | an operand like any other since 2026-10-15: see *Poles at a corner of the parameters* below |
| a freeform face whose parametrisation folds, or stalls anywhere but at a border that is one point; one that closes into a point on more than two borders, or on two adjacent ones without leaving it in a fan; one that folds back over its point, or comes to it as a cusp at either end of a surface with a pole at each (a cusp with one is built, §5.3 › *Cusps*); a rational one whose weights differ by more than a factor of two million within one span (a thousand apart and more is built and measured since 2026-10-17: see *Rational faces*) | `Unsupported`, with its reason |
| a freeform face that lies on the surface of a face of the other body, where that face is analytic (a rational quarter cylinder against the cylinder it is a piece of, a rational zone of a ball against the ball) | handled since 2026-10-14: `meet_surfaces` finds that the patch lies on the surface; a point of the analytic face beyond the patch is off the freeform face (not placed in the patch's chart), and the two faces are not merged (a face on a patch ends where the patch does; pieces of one patch still are) |
| a freeform face on a face of the other body that is not wholly under it on both sides (the rational quarter rod against its analytic twin moved along the axis), and two freeform faces on one surface (against itself moved, against a wider patch of itself, upright and tilted) | handled since 2026-10-15: see *Coincident freeform faces* below |
| two faces between half and twice the tolerance apart where one is over the other; two faces of different kinds that go through each other by that much; two points likewise; a piece of a face thinner than about eight tolerances | `Unsupported` ("at the edge of tolerance") |
| two faces of different kinds that go through each other by less than half the tolerance | touching: nothing is imprinted |
| a curve in which two faces cross, strictly inside both, that ends up dividing neither | `Unsupported` ("imprint lost"): a consistency check, zero on the corpus and the random runs |
| more than two faces along an edge where two of them are tangent to second order | `Unsupported` |
| a cylinder that goes on in a torus across a border the two faces share (the fillet of a line and of the arc it continues in): tangent along a circle that is not about one axis of both, with a second curve that crosses it; cones tangent along a ruling up to an apex | handled since 2026-10-14 (the closed forms of §5.1); before that `Unsupported` ("surfaces that touch along a curve … without a closed form") |
| a torus that goes on in another torus (tubes of one radius, middle circles tangent in one plane, from outside or from inside) | handled since 2026-10-14 (the closed form of §5.1) |
| a pair that touches along a curve without a closed form and keeps to one side of it (a fillet's tool against the faces it rounds: a spline or rational face tangent to a plane, a cone, a spline wall along a line, a circle or a spline) | handled since 2026-10-18: the contact reads as touches, the edge of either face along it is imprinted from its own curve; see *Tangent contact along an edge* below |
| any other pair that touches along a curve or to more than second order and crosses through it without a closed form, other than at a tacnode or a cusp (where the Hessian has rank one; §5.1 › *Tacnodes and cusps*); two imprinted curves, or two boundary pieces, that leave a vertex together and stay within a few tolerances of each other further than the shortest of them reaches (a curve that leaves along the face's boundary is built since 2026-10-21: *Tangent contact of higher order at the boundary*) | `Unsupported` ("surfaces that touch along a curve … without a closed form", "marching … stalled", or "two curves leave a point in the same direction and stay together") |
| a cylinder whose axis is square to a torus' axis, inside the tube and touching its top (fourth-order contact, the random runs' seed 2001169; built 2026-10-15) | the point of contact is a vertex where the two loops of the meeting begin and end, all four ends tangent there (§5.1 › *Tacnodes and cusps*). Held in `boolean_contact.rs` › `a_cylinder_touching_the_top_of_a_torus_tube_from_inside` to the common volume integrated from its closed form (`∫ min(L/2, sqrt((R + w)² − x²)) dx` across in closed form, Gauss–Legendre up), within the tolerance times the result's area, for union, intersection and difference both ways, at three scales, square and tilted, for cylinders of a half, three tenths and a tenth of the tube and lifted out or sunk in by three tenths of the tolerance; the regions sort the four tangent arms where they are four tolerances apart (`untie`). |
| a curve that runs a few tolerances from a cone without a closed form for it (a circle about the cone's axis; a line along a ruling through the apex, on the cone up to the apex) | handled since 2026-10-14 in `hits.rs`: a circle about the axis of a cone, sphere or torus is at one distance all round and is decided by one point (when its tilt and offset from the axis leave no doubt); a line along a ruling through the apex is on the cone on the cone's side and meets it at the apex. Before, the root isolation (which has no bound on a cone's bend) cut such a curve into pieces the size of that distance and refused "a curve that runs along a surface" |
| a touch where a curve dips through a surface by less than half the tolerance between two crossings (a cone's rim a tenth of a tolerance over a face whose edge it touches in the plane: the cone's side, carried on, crosses the edge twice, half a millimetre apart) | handled since 2026-10-14: the touch is put where the curve goes deepest, between the crossings, not on whichever crossing the search for the nearest point happened on (which split the edge where the rim, touching it between them, did not, and was refused as "an imprinted curve that does not fit"); a deeper dip keeps a crossing |

The curves come from `geom::intersect::meet_surfaces` (since 2026-10-13; the stand-in of the first version is gone): exact where there is a closed form, splines within the tolerance otherwise. What it cannot give (surfaces tangent or nearly coincident over an area, a touch along a curve without closed form) is refused with its reason. Curve/surface hits use geometry's closed forms for lines, circles and ellipses against planes and cylinders, and for every other analytic pair the bounded root isolation in `hits.rs`, which takes a piece as given (a hit at its end is a touch) and never samples. `BooleanResult::merged` lists, per face, every face of the operands it has a piece of; `BooleanOptions::trust_operands` skips the validity check of operands the kernel itself returned.

**Tolerances.** Points within half the linear tolerance are one vertex; between that and twice the tolerance the operation refuses. The same holds for faces: two faces on one surface, or on surfaces that never cross, are the same surface within half the tolerance of each other where one is over the other, apart at twice the tolerance or more, and refused in between. Joined faces are not moved onto each other: the volume of such a result is right to the area in contact times the gap. The counterpart for faces of different kinds (a ball that dips into a plate, a torus lying a hair too deep in a block): beside every curve in which two faces cross, `section::passage` walks one surface away from the curve both ways until it is twice the tolerance from the other. Where that happens on opposite sides of the other surface the faces cross, exactly as given. Where it happens on the same side, the surface went through the other and came back: a lens thinner than twice the tolerance, whose thickness is measured; up to half the tolerance the faces touch and nothing is imprinted, above it the operation refuses. A body ten tolerances thick is a body. No decision is taken on the sign of a number that could be rounding: membership is a winding number, a root is isolated by a bound and not by sampling, and what cannot be told is refused.

**Speed (2026-10-13).** Measured by stage on a cylinder through a plate with 1024 holes (1030 faces): classification 73 %, section 24 %, both from asking every boundary piece of a face with a thousand loops about every point. Now each outline keeps a box per boundary piece, sorted along x (`outline::Boxes`), and "is anything nearer than r" looks at the few pieces near the point; in a plane a loop is not followed around a point outside its box; edges are split only near a vertex; a region is classified by one point, the others being looked for only when it does not decide; a whole face away from the other solid's box is outside it. Bit-identical results (a digest of every result of the corpus and the random runs is printed by the tests). 100 holes one by one 371 → 134 ms, 1024 holes 82 → 9.9 s, the cylinder through the 1030-face plate 269 → 47 ms, two plates of 1030 faces each 0.64 to 0.85 s per operation.

**Speed (2026-10-14): what is asked again is answered from memory.** Measured by phase on an aircraft built by commands (not in the repository: a revolved spline fuselage, lofted cubic wings mirrored, a tailplane and a fin, five unions, every face a rational or polynomial NURBS face but the end caps): building the `PatchTree`s of the operands' freeform faces about a fifth of each union (a fuselage half 7848 nodes, about 10 µs a node for its speed bound), classification and the clipping of the curves nearly half, nearly all of it nearest-point searches (a winding number about a point of a freeform face projects every end and every bisection point of the face's boundary into its chart, and the next winding number about another point does it again), `meet_surfaces` a quarter to a third, the operand check and the final check a few percent. Five memos, each a pure function remembered, so that nothing changes but the time:
- *the nearest point per tree* (`PatchTree::nearest`), by the bits of the point asked about: nearly three quarters of the searches of a union were for a point asked about before (up to 16 384 points per tree, then forgotten all at once);
- *the trees*, shared between booleans (`PatchTree::shared`, least recently used out; since 2026-10-15 also let go when no open document's body has their surface, `craft3d_kernel::release_unused`, which the engine calls after a command once a document was closed or regenerated, and on WebAssembly at most 128 trees and 128 MiB): in a row of unions each operand's faces are on the surfaces the last one had, and the fuselage is indexed once instead of five times. *Since 2026-10-15* up to 1024 trees and 768 MiB (`PatchTree::bytes`: the nodes and the pieces the leaves keep, about 520 bytes a node); it was 32 trees and 131 072 nodes, about ten trees of a lofted blade (12 000 to 16 000 nodes each). The impeller of the showcase (a hub with six blades and six splitters, lofted, filleted and chamfered: 62 freeform faces, some 860 000 nodes) then took its trees round the shelf in the order its booleans asked for them, each pushing out the one asked for next, and every boolean on the finished body built all of them again: 124 trees for a chamfer of the two bore circles (12 s of its 12, CPU), 62 for the bore's subtraction (8 s), 68 for the union with the splitters. A boolean holds the trees of every freeform face of both operands while it works, so the shelf keeps no more than such a body takes anyway (the peak memory of the impeller's run is the same, 0.7 GB). Measured (release, CPU time, a machine other agents loaded; `craft3d-cli run` of `impeller.steps.json`): the chamfer 8 to 17 s before, 1.1 to 1.7 s after; the bore 3.7 to 8 s, 0.9 to 1.1 s; the union with the splitters 18 to 31 s, 12 to 16 s; the fan blades of the turbofan 12 to 16 s, 5 to 9 s. Every output line of both runs is the same text. `the_trees_of_a_body_of_many_freeform_faces_are_built_once` counts the trees built (`trees_built`), not time;
- *the checked bodies* (`boolean::checked`, up to 8 bodies and 32 MB written out): a union's result is checked as a result and again as the first operand of the next; written out, a body is compared in about a twentieth of what checking it costs. Since 2026-10-15 written out as its bits rather than as text (`craft3d_math::Bits`, implemented in `math`, `geom` and `topo` by destructuring every field, so a field added later does not compile until it is written out too; a list starts with its length, an enum with its variant): formatting every number of a plate with a thousand holes was a quarter of a long row of cuts;
- *the projectors* of `geom::intersect` (`Projector::shared`, 32), the sampled grid a freeform field starts its nearest-point search from, made once per surface instead of once or twice per pair of faces;
- *the numeric intersections* (`meet_surfaces`, after the closed forms: 64 of them, 2¹⁸ curve points at most): the union, difference and intersection of the same bodies ask the same questions (the three booleans of a corpus case: its slowest case, a cylinder lying on a spline roof, spent 1.1 s of each of its three booleans marching the same 700 000 cells), and so does a boolean done again on operands an edit did not touch.

A key is the thing written out in full (`{:?}`, which writes every `f64` so that it reads back to the same bits, -0.0 included, or for the checked bodies the bits themselves), never a hash, so two things are one entry only when every number of them is the same. Each memo is a `Mutex`, poisoning ignored, and nothing is computed under a lock. Besides the memos, two scans that looked at every node of a projector's 25 × 25 grid per sample (the nearest node, and the curvature near a point) look only at the nodes in a window along x, from a list of the nodes in order of x: a node whose x alone is further than the best so far is further whatever rounding does, so the answer is the same node, ties to the first in the grid's order included (held to the full scan at three scales, at a pole where nodes coincide, and halfway between nodes); and the leaf of a tree gives its own box to a bound query instead of one made again. On the aircraft the five unions went from 3.8 to 1.2 s (CPU time per union, the least of three runs each, on a loaded machine; the two ran in turn); what is left is `meet_surfaces` (about half: the quadtree that finds every branch, some 27 000 samples of the fuselage's field for the two curves of a wing root), classification (a fifth) and the checks. Bit for bit the same: the verbose corpus output of every one of its 11 988 outcomes (3996 cases, three operations each), its digest, the long random runs, and `craft3d-cli info` and the STL and OBJ meshes of every showcase document and of the aircraft. Not taken, because they would give other (equally valid) curves: asking `meet_surfaces` about the box where the two faces are rather than where the two bodies are; tighter derivative bounds or a cell budget from curvature in the marcher's quadtree; a step from curvature where it marches; a box tree of the field surface that settles empty cells without a nearest-point search (a third of the samples are empty cells, but nearly all within four times the cell's reach of the surface, where a box bound proves little).

**Many lumps (2026-10-17, the welding table's finding W2, `boolean/facetree.rs`, `boxtree.rs`).** An n × n grid of small prisms minus an n × n grid of posts, one into each, cost more per lump the more lumps there were (2500 lumps: 6.6 ms per lump against 0.4 at 100). Measured by stage at 2500 lumps (15 000 faces a side): classification 80 % and the section 7 %, both from looking at every face. The section tested the boxes of every face of A against every face of B (36 n⁴ box tests, 225 million at n = 50, for 10 000 pairs that meet), and the ray casting of `classify::inside` tested every face of the other solid twice per point, once for the points near its boundary and once per ray, for every region of every face (also quadratic). A third, in the welding table's weld subtractions: a rib's side face is one face with an edge for every tab along it, and `edge_cuts` asked every edge of it where it meets the surface of every slot's face it meets (all edges for each of n pairs).

*Method.* The crate's `BoxTree` (made for profile detection, now for boxes in the plane or in space, `TreeBox`) over each operand's face boxes, made the first time it is asked, and per face over its edges' boxes (`outline::piece_box`: the ends, a whole circle or ellipse for a piece of one, a spline's control points). A search hands each node's hull to the same test the loop applied to each face, goes below only where it passes, and returns the faces (edges) that pass **in ascending order**; the loop then runs exactly as before over them. Every test asked is monotone in the box (a box against a box, a point or a line, each grown by a margin; floating point's rounding is monotone, and a hull is minima and maxima, exact), so the faces found are those testing every face finds, in the same order, and nothing after it can tell. Faces whose box is not finite (empty, infinite or NaN corners) are in no node and always handed on; fewer than 16 boxes get no tree. For `edge_cuts` the test is new: an edge is asked only when its box comes within 16 tolerances (`EDGE_REACH`) of the overlap of the two faces' boxes. A cut farther away bounds only stretches that `clip` leaves out without looking at them (a kept stretch has its middle and both ends within twice the tolerance of both boxes, and between two such cuts the curve cannot leave both faces without crossing an edge of one where it is still within the other, which is a cut near the overlap), so the stretches, vertices and splits are the same; four tolerances would do, the rest is room. What changes there is only what was asked of edges far from the other face: an error such an edge would have raised (an ambiguous edge, a failed hit) no longer stops a boolean it had no part in. A face with fewer than 16 edges gets no tree and asks every edge, as before, so there such an error from a far edge still stops the boolean while on a larger face it no longer does: deterministic either way, but a difference by edge count, owed (open: the same reach on every face, or none).

*Work counts* (`craft3d_kernel::work`: `face_candidates`, `edge_candidates`, in the gate's benchmark from now on): per lump 18 faces handed on and 32 edges asked at 100, 400, 900 and 2500 lumps alike; a plate with 50, 200, 800 holes and a block beside each hole through its top: 28 edges asked per block at every size (all of the top's edges for every block before). `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_lumps.rs` holds both to the same work per lump at 16 times (8 times) the lumps, at three scales.

| lumps | before (s, ms per lump) | after (s, ms per lump) |
|---|---|---|
| 100 | 0.039 (0.39) | 0.036 (0.36) |
| 400 | 0.224 (0.56) | 0.115 (0.29) |
| 900 | 0.848 (0.94) | 0.312 (0.35) |
| 2500 | 5.43 (2.17) | 0.89 (0.36) |

(`feature.boolean` in `craft3d-cli serve`, release, the two binaries in turn on the same machine; the kernel alone at 2500: 16.5 s before, 1.2 to 2.0 s after, under load.) The welding table's weld subtractions (WeldsX, Welds) at 30 × 30, 48 × 36, 96 × 48 and 120 × 60 (80 to 740 slots): 0.24/0.39, 0.46/0.51, 1.43/1.24, 2.14/2.38 s before, 0.14/0.13, 0.29/0.26, 0.88/0.76, 1.53/1.68 s after, now about 2 ms per slot at every size. What is left there is per pair, not per lump: the pairs of a slot's round end with a tab's rounding, cylinders at right angles of different radii, have no closed form and are marched (about 1 ms each, 60 % of the section), and each slot's pair is a new question to the memo of numeric intersections, being somewhere else.

*Bit for bit:* the result of every grid and plate above (the `{:?}` of the whole `BooleanResult`), every non-ignored test of the boolean test files but the corpus (whose digest the gate holds), both random digests and both long random runs (`a34c9fd949b32318`, `12c52ecf020fa04c`), and `document.inspect` and the STEP export of the four benchmark models, the W2 probes and both welding tables; the benchmark's counts unchanged.

**Freeform faces (2026-10-13, `boolean/freeform.rs`).** A Bézier or B-spline face is indexed by a `PatchTree`: the surface as Bézier pieces (`geom`: `NurbsSurface::bezier_spans` by knot insertion, `RationalBezierPatch` with de Casteljau splitting), each quartered five times (since 2026-10-23 halved only across the way it is long where it is short the other way: *Patch trees of thin strips, and a budget for the trees of one operation* below), with the box of every piece's control net (the piece lies in it). The tree answers what the booleans ask of a surface: a bound below the distance from a point to the surface that never overestimates (the distance to the nearest box, split further on the spot to a quarter of the tolerance or of the distance), which is what calls a stretch of a curve provably clear of the surface in the root isolation of `hits.rs`; the nearest point, by Newton's iteration (second derivatives from differences of the first, steps held to the domain's sides) from the middle of the nearest piece and from every piece whose box is nearer than the best point found; the signed distance; and a bound on how fast the parameters change per length travelled on the surface (the smallest singular value of the Jacobian, from the Bézier nets of the derivatives and of the normal field), which the chart of the face needs: a freeform face's chart is its own parameter rectangle, reached through the nearest point. A `Sheet` (a surface with its index) is what the helpers of `outline`, `hits`, `section`, `classify`, `regions` and `chart` take; an `Outline` builds the tree for a freeform face once and shares it with the pieces of the face. The hull of a freeform face includes its control net. Where a freeform surface meets another, the surfaces count as crossing (no lens is looked for). A NURBS surface or curve evaluated beyond its domain continues the end span's polynomial, as a Bézier does, because the marcher of the surface intersections needs something to correct onto past a border. Since the evening of 2026-10-13 the root isolation (`hits.rs`, `numeric`) judges a whole stretch of a curve against a freeform face at once wherever the curve's bend is bounded (lines, circles, ellipses and splines, rational ones included, whose second derivative is bounded through the nets of their Bézier pieces: `NurbsCurve::derivative_bounds`): a stretch on the surface is proven to stay within the tolerance from the distances at its ends and a bound on the surface's second derivatives over its domain (from the Bézier nets: the difference between curve and surface along the parameter path between the feet stays within an eighth of the bend, `PatchTree::stretch_within`); a stretch beside it is proven clear by a lower bound on the distance from its chord to the surface (`segment_lower_bound`; the distance from a segment to a box is exact, one convex quadratic per side crossing) less how far the curve can leave the chord. The tree bounds its pieces by boxes in the piece's own frame (as thick as the piece bends, not as it tilts: a tilted piece's axis-aligned box is as thick as the piece is wide, which left a curve at 1.06 tolerances from a face unprovable), takes a threshold above which nothing matters, and the point bound is only asked where it can prove something. The slowest corpus case (a contact of two spline-roofed blocks, 200 s per boolean) takes 0.19 s.

**Rational faces (2026-10-13, late).** A face on a rational Bézier or NURBS surface (weights that are not all one) is an operand like a polynomial one. A rational patch lies in the hull of its control points just the same, so the boxes of the tree, the bound below the distance, the nearest point and the signed distance needed nothing. What was polynomial-only were the three bounds that came from derivative nets, and each now has a proven counterpart in `geom` (`nurbs.rs`; the arguments are in the doc comments):

- *First and second derivatives* (`RationalBezierPatch::derivative_bounds`, `NurbsCurve::derivative_bounds`). With `S = A / w` (weighted points over weight, both in Bernstein form), `S_u = (A_u − S w_u) / w` and `S_uu = (A_uu − 2 w_u S_u − w_uu S) / w`. `A_u − S w_u` is a Bernstein form whose coefficients are `m (w₁ (P₁ − P₀) + (w₁ − w₀) (P₀ − S))` over neighbours, and `S` lies in the hull, so `|P₀ − S|` is at most the reach of `P₀` across the box of the net; `w` is at least the lightest weight; the second derivatives likewise from second differences of `w (P − S)`. For weights that are all one these are the hull bounds of a polynomial patch, to the last bit. They loosen as the weights of a piece differ and tighten as the piece shrinks, so they are taken from small pieces: the leaves of the tree for the bound that `stretch_within` uses, the pieces of a span quartered twice for the marcher's field (which used estimates from samples for a rational surface before), a span halved three times for a curve.
- *The normal* (`RationalBezierPatch::weighted_normal_net`). `w³ (S_u × S_v) = w (A_u × A_v) + w_v (A × A_u) + w_u (A_v × A)` is a polynomial where the normal is not; its Bézier net (degrees `3m − 1` by `3n − 1`, products of Bernstein forms) holds it in its hull, so the normal is at least as long as the distance from the origin to the box of that net over the cube of the heaviest weight. With the bound on the first derivatives that gives the smallest singular value of the Jacobian, and so the parameter speed the chart needs.
- *The bend of a rational edge* (`hits.rs`, `turn_bound`) comes from the curve bound above, so a rational spline is judged a whole stretch at once like any other curve.

Measured on a NURBS cylinder the parameter speed bound is within 20 % of the true value and the second-derivative bound within 50 %; on a bicubic roof with the weights 1, 2, 0.5 and 3 within 36 % and 24 %. The parameter speed is now taken in the surface's own parameters, with the width of the piece: before, it was the piece's own, which is right only for a span one wide, and a B-spline face over a wider knot span got a bound that was too small by that width (found here, fixed for polynomial faces too; `the_parameter_speed_is_in_the_parameters_of_the_surface`).

The oracle (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_rational.rs`): a rational surface can *be* a piece of a quadric or a torus, so each test solid exists twice, with its curved faces on the analytic surfaces and on the rational patches that equal them (a quarter rod, a quarter ball zone, a quarter frustum, a quarter slice of a ring with an outer and an inner face; patches that end on the trims and patches a fifth larger; circular edges as circles and as rational arcs). The twins pass `check`, measure as the closed forms to 1e-9 and mesh watertight; their three booleans against a block, a cylinder, a ball, a cone and a torus through the rational face agree with the same booleans of the analytic solid within 5.1e-8 in volume and area (360 booleans; held to 1e-6) at the scales 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, in the world's frame and tilted; tangent contact with a plane and a ball and an edge of a block on the face come out as for the analytic twin. A roof that is no quadric (the weights above) is held to a quadrature of its own and to the identities of sets, against a block, a ball, a block over a corner (which cuts the rational roof edges) and a cylinder.

*Weights far apart (2026-10-15).* A patch whose weights are a hundred apart moves a hundred times faster with its parameters near its light corners than near its heavy ones, and the winding numbers, which halved the loops by one bound on the chart's speed over the whole face, gave up ("too close to a face boundary"). On a surface drawn in its own parameters a stretch that the bound for the whole face would split is now judged by the bound near it, on every such surface (2026-10-15; the first version did so only where the pieces' bounds were more than sixteen apart): `PatchTree::sigma_near`, the least bound of the pieces that come within twice the stretch's length plus the distance of its start from the surface, which is where the feet of its points are; a piece wholly within reach gives its own bound, one partly within reach is looked at closer. It is never looser than the bound for the whole face. Held in `boolean.rs` › `what_is_not_supported_says_so`: the plate with its top as a bilinear patch with the weights 1, 100, 100, 1 against a ball at a corner of its bottom and at a corner of its top (which cuts the weighted face), union, difference and intersection, each the same body as the same boolean of the plain block (400 seeded points classified by ray casting, `boolean::contains_points`, against the block's result and against the exact membership of block and ball; the same face counts). *Measured since 2026-10-17* (`topo::mass_properties`; the method is in [`topology.md`](topology.md) › *Rational weights far apart*): the plate with weights 1, h, h, 1 to 1e-12 for h up to a thousand, and the analytic twins with weights up to ten thousand apart per span to 1e-9, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6; the booleans of the plate with weights a hundred apart above are now held to the volume, area and centroid of the plain block's too. Two causes, two general methods. (1) Where weights have a product form (`w_ij = a_i b_j`: every surface of revolution, and the twins skewed by a Möbius reparametrisation of their spans), a reparametrisation takes the unevenness out: each inner integral is summed in the parameter of the Möbius map that makes the weights at its stretch's ends equal (exact; a change of variable). Without it a cylinder skewed a thousandfold along its axis *settled 7e-6 short*, because the inner panels stopped doubling at 64 a stretch while the boundary's went on to 128, so the last two sums had the same inner error (fixed: they double to the last). (2) Where no reparametrisation helps (the plate: `w₀₀ w₁₁ / (w₀₁ w₁₀) = 1 / h²` in every parametrisation), the boundary integrand gathers whole strips of the face on a stretch `1 / h²` of an edge long next to a heavy corner, and doubling every panel ran out of doublings (the earlier diagnosis is right, the corner is the heavy one); the panels are now halved only where their own halves disagree, twenty times there for h = 1000 and not at all elsewhere. The plate at h = 1e4 (a stretch 1e-8 long) is refused: its sums scatter at the rounding of the boundary's parameters; weights further apart than 1e4 after evening (what no reparametrisation removes) are refused before anything is summed, since from 1e7 the sums settled wrong. *Refused until 2026-10-17* (see the next paragraph): weights a thousand apart in the booleans, by the winding numbers ("too close", where the face's boundary passes near a heavy corner) and by the surface intersection ("tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area": a ball through the weighted face at a light corner, at a heavy corner already with the weights a hundred apart, and the twins skewed a thousandfold), and more than a factor of a thousand apart within a span at the tree ("rational … weights"). Both came from bounds taken over too much of the surface at once: the tree's bound on the Jacobian's least singular value pairs the shortest normal of a piece with its longest derivative, and next to a heavy corner the Jacobian changes by a factor of 150 within one leaf (h = 1000: bound 9.4e-6 against a true 3e-3), and the marcher's cells were classified with the derivative bounds of the whole surface.
