- *Walks keep their blend factors* (`DerivativeCache`, three values of each parameter): the walks that evaluate a spline around each step, at `(u ± h, v)` and `(u, v ± h)` (`Surface::descend`, `PatchTree::descend`), work them out for three values of each parameter, not five; the walk's last derivatives are given back with its answer (`project_from_with_derivatives`) instead of being asked for again.
- *The point of a spline alone* (`Surface::eval` no longer evaluates the derivatives of a spline only to drop them), and the sine and cosine of the angle only for the kinds that turn.
- *Projections remembered by their surface* (the bits of the point; 32,768 per surface and about a million in all, counted globally, each surface letting go of its own when full; kept with the projection grid, so clones of a surface share them).
- *Meshes of faces remembered* (`tess::remembered`, [`tessellator.md`](tessellator.md)): a face's mesh is a function of its surface and sense, of the direction, curve and points of every coedge around it, whether its loops close, the parameters and the round; written out in full that is the key, 64 MB of meshes kept (8 MB in a browser), the oldest let go first.
- *Every operation's check remembered* (`check_remembered` in every kernel operation, blends included), so regeneration does not check a result again.

*Proof of no change:* the corpus digest `3f00bc8ced79a654` with 10,815 + 767; the benchmark's work counts all the same (no count fell: the counters count faces measured and meshed, not how they were made); the STEP files and `document.measure`/`inspect` of the impeller, the turbofan, the helmet, the lamp and the bike the same bytes; tests that compare every new path with the old one bit for bit (the line evaluators, the cache of blend factors, the walk's derivatives, the remembered projections past their cap, and the meshes and self-checks of six bodies sharing faces at three scales with and without what was kept).

*Before and after* (release, e49f295 against this round, pairs at the same time; seconds wall / CPU): the lamp 679 / 1387 → 506 / 949 (load 30–44; 456 / 1336 → 366 / 1028 with projections remembered but not meshes, load 14–19); the turbofan's measure alone 47–57 / 188–213 → 46–54 / 166–171 CPU (its regeneration 150–183 → 167–170 CPU: the same within the load); the impeller 69 / 89 → 59 / 78 (load 9); the helmet 106 / 110 → 103 / 106; the turbofan whole 150 / 374 → 154 / 343 (load 16–27); the bike within the noise (two runs each, 139–152 against 137–170 CPU at loads 31–54); the cusp's first boolean 34 → 26 s; the corpus 844 → 806 s CPU; the bench models' CPU impeller 21.8 → 13.8, engine block 9.4 → 7.5, rotor 1.7 → 1.1, the turbofan and the holes the same. The lamp's peak memory 2.32 → 2.67 GB (the projections and meshes kept).

*Open:* the face's boundary is still worked out before its mesh is found (its projections now found again; the regions and `seed_cells`' derivative bounds are not); the inner integrals across v collapse every row at every node; the self-check's scope still meshes the new faces whole.

### A shell that touches itself (fuzzer seed 5279, 2026-10-17)

**Found** (the random-design fuzzer, `wrong-self-intersection/5279`): the union of a disc (the rectangle x 0..10, y 5..10 turned once about y) and a quarter turn about z of an L on the yz plane was accepted; `verify` and the volume identities passed, and `document.check` deep found the body touching itself at (0, 5, 7.5), at every scale. **Which side was wrong: the boolean.** In closed form: the L's step is the cylinder x² + y² = 25 for z in 5..7.5, which reaches y = 5 only along its edge with the L's start face, the line x = 0, y = 5, and lies below it elsewhere (y = 5 − x²/10 to second order); that edge lies inside the disc's bottom face y = 5, the L's material below it and the disc's above. The L's lower part runs into the disc, so the union is one shell, and that shell touches itself along the line: zero thickness under the decision of 2026-10-15. `zero_thickness` only looked for touches between two shells.

**Fixed** (`within_a_shell` and `walls_apart` in `boolean/mod.rs`), with three rules:

1. *Two faces of one shell at a place where the operands meet (within twice the tolerance, not outside either boundary, their nearest points within twice the tolerance of each other) touch, unless they share a vertex or an edge within four times the tolerance of the place.* General because in a manifold every point of a shell lies inside one face, on one edge (two faces) or at one vertex (the faces round it): two faces at one place with nothing in common there are two walls of the shell. The margin for "joined" is twice that for "at"; that covers faces meeting at an edge at 29° or more (their gap at a distance d from the edge is 2d·sin(θ/2)), and for a sharper wedge or groove the margin of a shared edge grows to the tolerance over sin(θ/2), θ read from the outward normals at the edge's nearest point (`wedged`; review, 2026-10-18), capped at a tenth of the smaller face. The places looked at are the ones `zero_thickness` already had (vertices both operands have, section vertices, touch points, a point inside every stretch, vertices of one operand on a face of the other, an operand's own touches); a pair already joined there costs no outline.
2. *Two such walls touch as parts of the solid only when each stays on the outer side of the other a little way off the place* (eight directions in the tangent plane at a tenth of the smaller face's box, onto the surface, inside the face; a point more than twice the tolerance inside the other's material says no, counted only where its foot on the other surface lies inside the other face: past its boundary a surface says nothing about material, and a bar turned 45° lying on a plate, its side faces under the plate's plane beyond their shared edge, was taken for a thinning wall; review, 2026-10-18). General because two tangent walls with opposite outward normals are one of two configurations that agree to first order at the place: materials on their far sides, the gap between them closing up (two parts touch: refused), or material between them thinning to nothing (a hole whose wall touches the plate's side along a line: a wall of no thickness). The second has always been accepted and is pinned (`a_hole_that_touches_the_side_and_a_cylinder_that_fills_its_block`, `cones_that_share_a_ruling`); whether it is refused too is an open decision for the developer.
3. *Where no point of a wall can be looked at, the walls count as apart*: the refusal errs on the side of no zero-thickness result.

The refusal now runs for every result, not only one of several shells (`refuse_zero_thickness` alone decides). **Saved documents:** a `.3dcraft` whose history holds such a boolean (one shell touching itself along a line or in a point that regeneration's self check did not see, as the finding) now fails that feature on regeneration with the zero-thickness error, by the decision of 2026-10-15, where it regenerated before; those whose result had two vertices in one place failed already, in the self check.

**Expectation changed** (`boolean_contact.rs` › `a_result_whose_parts_hang_together_by_an_edge`): the two blocks joined by an edge, united again with the cutter (two blocks sharing that edge, z −1..2), was accepted as one body; above and below the plate (z 0..1) the cutter's blocks still touch along the edge, so the union touches itself there: now refused at a point of that edge outside z 0..1, its volume (26) still pinned through `solve_parts`. In `boolean_freeform.rs`, the union of the dome and a block whose underside lies in the roof's tangent plane at a saddle (`a_block_on_the_tangent_plane_of_a_saddle_of_the_roof`) and where the roof curves little (`…_where_the_roof_curves_little`, the corpus case tangent/block-spline/005) was accepted: the gaps between plane and roof on the sides where the roof dips under it meet in the touch, and sew gives that vertex two fans, two vertices in one place, which `craft3d_topo::touches` (the check regeneration and `document.check` hold every body to) reports as a shell touching itself. Now refused at the touch, the volume still pinned without the refusal, and the self-touch asserted by `topo::touches` as a witness. The bar ∪ torus tool of `fillet.rs` (`a_cylinder_that_goes_on_in_a_torus_touches_it_along_their_border`: they touch along the arc of the round as two diagonal quarters) is refused the same way. **The regression net** (reviews, 2026-10-18): a refusal of one shell is held, in `solve_parts`, the random pairs and the corpus, to `boolean_common::touches_itself_at` on the body made without it: three looks at the body that ask nothing of the boolean, each a sample and not a proof (hence a net, not an oracle). `craft3d_topo::touches` near the place; two pieces of material about it on a sphere of a thousandth of the body's box and on one of 75 tolerances both (`material_pieces_at`, by `sectors_at`: 3000 points by `contains_points`, a point counting only when all its neighbours within 3.3° are of its kind, so that two pieces meeting in a point of the sphere are not joined through it and a band wider than about 7° is kept; two radii, so that a rib or a thin plate nearby that one sphere reaches is not taken for a touch; pieces of space are not counted, since they cannot tell a wall that thins out from a touch); or two faces through the place (`walls_meet_at`: each surface within twice the tolerance of it, the foot inside the face by its boundary edges and a fine mesh, the two feet within twice the tolerance of each other) that share no vertex there (within four tolerances) and no edge (within four tolerances over sin(θ/2), so that a sharp wedge is one), with material just past each (twenty tolerances out and to the sides), which is what tells two parts that touch from a wall that thins to nothing. It says no on a plain boundary (`the_witness_of_a_self_touch_says_no_on_a_plain_boundary`) and by what fooled the first version (`the_witness_says_no_by_a_slot_a_rib_a_thin_plate_and_a_thinning_wall`: slots 2.5 to 1000 tolerances wide, a rib 1e-3 thick 2e-3 away, plates 1e-3 and 3e-3 thick, the hole tangent to a side), at three scales. Several shells: the place on two of them, as before. Where both faces of a touch are pieces of one face of an operand, the refusal names another face at the place.

**The corpus** (`boolean_corpus.rs`, whose rule for a refusal is now: the place lies on two parts of the body made without the refusal, or, for one shell, on it and caught by the net above): 108 unions of one shell are refused now (tangent, contact and near pairs with a dome or a spline), 98 of them scored as refused by design (zero-thick 767 → 865), the rest among the cases set aside; the agreement sum stays at its floor of 11582, the outright agreement (match + match~ + empty) goes from 10815 to 10717, 0 mismatch, 0 invalid, and the digest is unchanged (`3f00bc8ced79a654`: the bodies made without the refusal are the bodies made before). So that a refusal cannot hide in the sum, the corpus now also holds a floor on the outright agreement (`MATCHED_FLOOR` 10717) and a ceiling on zero thickness (`ZERO_THICK_CEILING` 865). Every one of the 108 is caught by the net (second review's tightened version too): 100 by `craft3d_topo::touches` (two vertices in one place or a vertex on an edge of the same shell (`craft3d_topo::touches`, which regeneration and `document.check` hold every body to, so those bodies never reached a document), 13 by two pieces of material at both radii, 99 by `walls_meet_at`; `tangent/cone-dome/008` by `walls_meet_at` alone. The 8 added by counting only feet inside the other face (all `contact/*/…` with a curved edge of a domed block against the other body, whose common is two or three pieces) have two pieces of material and two of space about the place. The others are an edge of one operand touching a curved face of the other in a point between two places where they overlap (`contact/dome-spline/002`, `contact/block-dome/003` looked at closely). The random pairs (`boolean_random.rs`) refuse 54 and 42 results of zero thickness (42 was 36 before only feet inside the other face counted), every one-shell refusal caught by the net; their digests are unchanged.

**Tests:** `boolean_contact.rs` › `a_union_that_overlaps_and_also_touches_along_a_line` (the finding at three scales, either way round: refused on the line x = 0, y = 5, z 5..7.5; made without the refusal one shell of volume 500π + 265.625π − (500π/3 − 125√3), the intersection 500π/3 − 125√3 in closed form), `a_bar_on_its_edge_along_a_plate_and_into_a_pillar` (the review's case: a square bar turned 45° on its edge along a plate's top and into the pillar of an L, refused along the edge, volume 19.25, two pieces of material there) and `the_witness_of_a_self_touch_says_no_on_a_plain_boundary`; `crates/engine/tests/fuzz_findings.rs` replays the reproducers.

### Several lumps and cavities (2026-10-17, checklist item 5)

**Surveyed first**: cuts that fall apart, cavities of planar, spherical, toroidal and freeform shape, operands with a cavity or several lumps (opened, kept, filled exactly, cut open, used as the tool), lumps nested in cavities and hollows in hollows, and fillets, chamfers, shells, offsets, delete face, patterns, mirrors, mass, meshes, STEP, the `.3dcraft` round trip and `document.check` deep on such bodies: every one was built and right; delete face refuses a lump's face its neighbours cannot close, with the reason. What was missing was the proof and the checks: `topo::check` held the counts of the whole body only (a shell of two pieces, an edge in two shells, two shells whose counts make up for each other passed), nothing but the mesh-level self-check saw shells nested the wrong way (a cavity turned inside out passes `check` and measures 1064 instead of 936), and the STEP export had a sorting of its own.

**Built:** `topo` (`shells.rs`: `shell_alone`, `face_groups`, `shell_genera`; three defects per shell in `check`), `kernel::lumps` (the method above; the STEP export now uses it, and no longer writes a lump that lies in another's material as two solids), `document.inspect` lists `shells` per body, `document.check` deep checks how they nest.

**Names.** The faces of every lump are copies of operand faces as before. The pieces of one face that end up in different lumps share its name, as pieces in one lump do: a reference to it means every piece (an offset moves all of them; a fillet between two faces takes the edge in every lump that has it). Since 2026-10-20 `body.split` makes one body per lump, and the piece of a face in one lump is then a copy with that split's prefix (`F4:copy(0)/F1:face(2)`; [`names.md`](names.md) › *Splits*); shells and lumps themselves have no names, a split is said by a face of its lump.

**`body.split` (2026-10-20, checklist item 5).** One split feature per lump (`doc::Split`, `regen/src/split.rs`, `engine/src/split_cmds.rs`), the lumps as `lumps` sorts them, a cavity with the lump it lies in (`topo::shells_alone`: the outer shell and its cavities as a body of their own, with which entity of the whole each entity is). The source's body goes into the splits together, and every lump is some split's: a lump none names fails the last split, and `body.split` on the body adds the splits it lacks ([`names.md`](names.md) › *Splits*); the sorting of a body and the lumps of each of its names are remembered by the body (`split::sorted_remembered`, `by_name_remembered`), so a body of many lumps is sorted once, not once per split. A sheet is refused (a sheet has no lumps; its pieces are not split yet), as are a body of one lump and a body whose shells are not lumps and cavities (with the kernel's reason). Held to by `crates/engine/tests/body_split.rs`: closed-form volumes and areas per piece summing to the whole (a block cut in two, a block with a cavity cut above it, the two caps of a ball of height 4, `π h² (3R − h) / 3` each, turned about a slanted line), the crescents of a ring cut by a tube (half the whole each within 1e-7, their sum the whole), a pattern's instances, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6; STEP export one solid per body. Found on the way, not fixed: a ring (R 10, r 2) less a tube of radius 1 along y through its centre (two holes through the tube) fails with "face 0 cannot be tessellated: its loops do not bound a region" in the self-check; the ring less a tube of radius 3 (which swallows the tube) and less a short tube that crosses it once both go through.

*Review (2026-10-18).* The first version prepared the other shell for every pair (a plate with 400 cube cavities, 2406 faces: 676 ms; now 83 ms, one preparation per shell and one integration of all faces), measured each shell again alone (the rotor benchmark's deep check +34 % mass work; now the shells' volumes are kept with the body's measure in the engine, from the same integration), took a volume below the cube of the tolerance for none (now the integrator's bound per shell), and let other points outvote one on the other shell (now refused as shells that meet).

### Tangent contact along an edge (2026-10-18, checklist item 2: the contact a fillet's boolean refused)

A fillet made as a boolean with its tool (the solid the fillet takes away, whose round face is tangent to the two faces it rounds along curves) was refused wherever that contact has no closed form. The cases, and what they were (`crates/kernel/tests/fillet_contact.rs`): a block's edge less the corner section extruded, with its cylinder on the rational patch that is that quarter of it and a fifth larger, so that the lines of contact run through the middle of the patch; a quarter of a rod's rim tool with its torus and side strip on such patches; the blade root's edge between two spline walls less its exact tool (the two Bézier pieces and the ball's arc, extruded flush and past both ends, the arc also on a patch); and the tools a rolling-ball fillet takes away (the body less the fillet) along a straight edge between a plane and a cone and along the rim of a lofted blade's tip. All were "boolean produced an invalid body" (edges used once); the blade root at 1e-3 also "tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area".

*Why they fell outside.* An edge of one body on a face of the other is imprinted only through a curve in which that face meets one of the edge's two faces. Here one of them is the tool's strip, which lies on the face it rounds (coincident: no curve), and the other is the round face, tangent to it along the edge: the numeric intersection settles such a contact as a string of one-sided touches (it keeps to one side of the face: nothing is divided there), not as a curve. So the edge was in neither, the face was not divided where the tool's strip ends, and the regions came out wrong. The closed forms (a cylinder on a plane: the line) and a contact along a border of the walked patch (a lying border, reported as a curve) had worked; a contact in the middle of the walked surface, or along a border of the other one, had not. At 1e-3 the contact was not even settled as touches: geometry's *A contact along a curve judged on its valley*.

*Method* (`section::lying_edges`). After every pair of faces has been met, each pair whose surfaces only touch (geometry gave points and no curve) looks for edges of either face that come within the tolerance of the other face's surface at one of nine places. Where there are any, the edges of both faces are cut where they meet the other surface (`edge_cuts`, exactly as for a pair that meets in a curve), each such edge is cut at those places, and every piece that lies on the other surface is a stretch of contact along that edge: proven, not sampled (`geom::certify`'s bound on how far a curve strays from a surface, pieces halved where it is over the tolerance, at most 4096; five places first only to turn away cheaply the edges that are not; a proof not reached is no imprint. Five samples alone took a rod's top circle for lying on a lid's underside that touches the rod's top along two lines through its centre: at 45° the circle is 0.05 off, and it was imprinted; the review's probe, `fillet_contact.rs` › `an_edge_that_touches_a_surface_at_places_does_not_lie_on_it`): its own curve, clipped against both faces by `clip` like any meeting curve (tangent, so it divides nothing it runs over the boundary of without a cut there). A piece is cut where a stretch already on the face it lies on ends on it, and each part whose middle is on such a stretch is left out; the rest is imprinted. The pass adds no stretch where an edge of neither face is in the contact: on both sides of a one-sided touch the face is on the same side of the other body, so such a contact needs no imprint for classification (zero thickness there is looked for at the touch points, as before). Only the pairs that only touch and have an edge on the other surface do any new work, so the corpus (no such pair) has the same digest `3f00bc8ced79a654` and counts (10717 + 865 = 11582, 0 mismatch, 0 invalid), and the benchmark's counts are unchanged.

*Held to* (`fillet_contact.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6): the blend's fillet and the boolean with the tool are the same body (volume within the tolerance times the area, area within a band of the tolerance along every edge, the same faces, shells, edges and vertices, the checker and the boolean's own check in full); closed forms (the block 1 − (1 − π/4) r², the rod by Pappus, a whole ring and a quarter); the rational tools against the analytic ones (tilted too for the block); the identities (the common part and the cut make up the body; the fillet and its tool share nothing; put back, the body). The blade root's rolling fillet (a spline face within a quarter of the tolerance of the exact one) and the boolean with the exact tool agree to 1e-2 of the tolerance times the area. B less the tool a rolling fillet takes away gives the fillet; put back, the body.

*Still owed.* Where an edge touches a face tangentially (the body's edge across the end of a fillet, against the round face of its tool) the touch is placed within the length over which the edge stays within the tolerance of the face, `sqrt(2 tol r)` (the reach the test allows), not at the vertex of the other operand that is there: B less the tool a rolling fillet takes away has one short extra edge per such end (27 tolerances at scale 1 on the frustum, 48 on the lofted tip), and the test allows exactly those vertices within that reach. Snapping a touch to the other operand's vertex is tolerant entities' work (structural debt). A contact along a curve inside both faces still reads as touch points.

### Sheet bodies (2026-10-18, checklist item 5)

What the survey found: `BodyKind::Sheet` existed, the tessellator and the measure took any face, the STEP export wrote open shells, both renderers draw both sides of every triangle; nothing made a sheet, `topo::check` held a sheet to almost nothing (no rule on edge uses or Euler), and `section::prepare` refused a sheet ("booleans need solids").

Built: the check of a sheet ([`topology.md`](topology.md)), `feature.fill` (`kernel::extrude::fill`, a fill's face named as an extrusion's start cap), and the two booleans of the Method above, without a new operation: subtract and intersect read as point sets give the cut and the trim, and a result has the target's kind. Of the three ways to split a solid by a sheet (one body of two lumps that touch over the cut, refused by the zero-thickness rule of 2026-10-15; one body per piece, which needs `body.split` (D-open-13 decided 2026-10-18: one body of several lumps); one side per feature, by subtract or intersect) the last changes the least and is built; the developer chose it (2026-10-18). The cut face is a piece of the sheet's face and is named after it as a copy (`F7:copy(1)/F5:startCap(e1,e2,e3,e4)`), with nothing new in names.

How the solid's sides are told is topology after one local look: a region's side is read where it meets the cap, from the outward normal of its face, the direction of its loop along the arc and the sheet's normal at the arc's middle; everything else is a flood over shared arcs. A cavity the sheet crosses opens into the cap (a block of 10 with a cavity of 4 cut through it: one shell each side, the cap with a hole); one it does not cross is refused, as is a second lump the sheet misses (owed: placing a shell the cut does not reach by the lump it lies in, `kernel::lumps`).

Held to (`crates/kernel/tests/sheets.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6): fills of a square with a hole and of a parabolic arch bounded by a quadratic spline (closed-form areas to 1e-12, Euler with boundary loops, meshes open only along the boundary and as long there as the boundary); the seeded defects of a sheet (an edge used twice the same way, three times, by no face, a pinch); trims by a block, a rod, a ball, the spline cone of the test kit and of the spline-bounded arch by a block, each to the closed-form area inside and the sum of the two; cuts of a block (also by a tilted sheet), a rod, a ball, a ring (an annulus for a cap), the spline cone and a hollow block, each to the closed-form volume in front and the sum of the two, with closed meshes; every refusal by its reason. Through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/sheets.rs`): measure, deep check, STEP open shells, save and reopen, the names of the cut face, the refusals as failed features, bad parameters as errors. FreeCAD reads the open shells and the cut solid to the closed forms (`crates/io/tests/step_freecad.rs`, `cargo xtask freecad`).

Review round (2026-10-18): a trim that pinches the sheet in a point passed `topo::check` (BoundaryPinch sees one shared vertex; the sewing makes two) and was refused only by regeneration's self-check, in words meant for solids; now the boolean refuses it as zero thickness (`touching_itself`, sheets only: for a solid's shell a wall that thins to nothing is accepted, decided 2026-10-18), and regeneration says "the trimmed sheet would touch itself". The check of a cut now sees the wrong side. A ring cut once is refused as a sheet that does not divide the solid (it was "imprint lost": a curve that divides nothing is not held to dividing a face in a cut). Shell and thicken of a sheet say that they are not built yet.

Not changed: the corpus (only solids; digest and floors as they were), the bench.

### A piece of the sheet on a face of the solid (2026-10-20, checklist item 5)

Refused on 2026-10-18 until the developer chose closed sets (D-open-14 (b2)): such a piece counts as inside the solid. Built in the classification as it stands, with no new class: `classify::sheet` reads `OnSame` and `OnOpposite` as `In` for a trim (both ways the face can look: a sheet has no material, so its orientation says nothing about where it is), and as nothing for the sheet in a cut, where the solid's own region on the sheet stands for the piece and is kept by the table of two solids (`classify::solids`, operand A), the way a face both solids share is kept. What the cut needed beyond that was one generalisation: a region's side was read where it meets the cap; now it is read wherever the sheet meets the solid's boundary, which is the cap's boundary and the boundary of the regions on the sheet alike (`cut.rs`: one loop over the *meetings*, the sheet's normal taken from the sheet's faces along the arc), and a region on the sheet is left out of the flood and of the side reading (it has none). Where the direction into a region is square to the sheet's normal the arc tells nothing: at a cap arc that is the tangent cut refused before; at the rim of a piece on a face it is a face that continues the sheet's surface (the plate's top beyond a disc on it), skipped, so that the region is told by another meeting or refused as a part the sheet does not reach across (`NOT_CROSSED`). Two more things the rule needed to be exact rather than lucky: a boundary edge of the sheet lying inside a coincident face divides it, from the edge itself, since no second face of the sheet can (`section::lying_edges` with `Lying::Boundary`, run for every coincident pair after all pairs are met; a solid's edge there is imprinted by its other face, or by `lying_edges` for the touching pair when that face is tangent, so solids get no new stretch); before it the plate's top was one region, and one sample point said for the whole of it whether it was on the sheet. And a sheet whose shell is closed is refused by `topo::check` (`Defect::SheetClosed`): it has an inside, so it is a solid, and the trim would otherwise keep a closed plate marked as a sheet whole as "on the face" (the guard `boolean.rs` › the plate marked as a sheet relied on the refusal that is gone).

Held to (`crates/kernel/tests/sheets.rs` › `a_piece_of_the_sheet_on_a_face_of_the_solid_is_inside`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, as built and turned about an off-axis direction): a plate of 4 × 3 × 1 with a step, a round boss, a hemisphere and the spline cone of the test kit standing on its top, and a sheet of 20 × 20 in the top's plane facing up and facing down: trimmed, 12 inside both ways (the top plus the section, one face after merging) and 388 outside, Euler with the boundary, the mesh open only there; cut, the part above (6, π/4, 2π/3, 2π/3) in front of the sheet facing up and behind it facing down, the plate (12) on the other side, closed meshes, one cap at most on each piece, `vol(front) + vol(behind) = vol(A)`; the cone's side (a NURBS face with its apex) as a sheet of its own, trimmed by the cone's solid with the half towards one seam swallowed by a block: all of it inside (half of π r √(r² + h²)), nothing outside; a disc on the plate around the boss: trimmed, all inside, and the cut refused as a part the sheet does not cross; a closed box marked as a sheet (`SheetClosed`, the boolean refuses it); and the sheet on the block's top of the refusal test now trims to 12 and 388 and cuts to nothing in front and the block behind, an expectation changed under the decision (logged). Through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/sheets.rs` › `a_fill_on_a_face_of_the_part_cuts_and_is_trimmed_there`): a plate with a block on it and a fill in the top's plane, cut into the block (its new bottom named after the fill) and the plate (its own top whole, no cap), and the fill trimmed to the top plus the footprint (12, one face) and the rest (388), checked deep. Every behaviour test fails on the code before (refused "lies on a face"). Still owed: a face tangent to the sheet along the rim of the piece on the face (a filleted plate: the fillet is told nothing by that arc and the plate's sides are reached by no other meeting, refused), two sheets, a lump or cavity the sheet does not cross.

### A sheet's rim that enters a face and leaves it (2026-10-21, review of the above)

**Finding.** The imprint of a boundary edge on a coincident face (`Lying::Boundary`) covered only rims wholly inside the face (a disc) or wholly outside it (a wide sheet), and rims whose ends land on the face's edges. A rim that enters the face from outside and leaves it again was not cut where it crosses the face's edges: `edge_cuts` cuts an edge where it meets the other face's surface, and the rim lies on that surface everywhere (its only cuts were its own ends), while the face's edges lie on the sheet's surface too, so no surface crossing sees the place. `clip` then found the whole rim partly in and partly out of the face and, the curve being a touch, dropped it; the face stayed whole and one sample point (`classify::on_coincident`) decided for all of it. Probes (block 4 × 3 × 1, sheets in its top's plane): a sheet over x ≤ 2 cut by luck to the right answer, its mirror image (x ≥ 2) was refused as passing through nothing, a strip from inside as nearly coincident, a block with a boss broke (`edge used 1 times`) or was refused as a tangent cut. Trims were right throughout: the sheet is divided by the solid's other faces, which cross its surface. Second, `topo::check` let a closed shell with no edges pass as a sheet (`Some(0) if edges > 0`): a whole sphere marked as a sheet checked clean, and a sphere sheet trimmed by a ball came back whole.

**Method.** `section::edge_crossings`, for every boundary edge of either face of a coincident pair against every edge of the other face near it: where on the boundary edge it comes within the tolerance of the other edge, by branch and bound on the distance from a point of the edge to the other edge's piece (`outline::nearest`), which moves along the edge by at most its speed bound (`hits::speed_bound`) times the change of parameter. A piece whose middle is further than the tolerance plus that bound times its half width is dropped, the rest halved to a quarter of the tolerance; a run of pieces that follow one another is one place: a vertex of either edge within twice the tolerance of both (as `edge_cuts` takes a hit near an end to the end), else the point of the run nearest the other edge. The places are cuts like any other (`Cut` with both edges), so `clip` divides the rim there and judges each stretch by its middle, and the face's edges are split at the new vertices as at any crossing. It works on curves alone (lines, circles, ellipses, NURBS) and on any surface the two lie on: nothing in it reads the surface's kind. New conditions, each general: *dropped* when `d > tol + L·h` (a proof for exact curves, an estimate for NURBS through their speed bound, as in the curve–surface hits); *pieces of a quarter tolerance* (the resolution of the curve–surface hits); *a shared stretch* (`ALONG` = 64 tolerances): a piece at least that long within the tolerance of the other edge at nine places lies along it, is not halved and gives no place (the two share a stretch, which their vertices bound; without it two edges along each other would be halved down to the tolerance over their whole length); crossings flatter than about two degrees (within the tolerance of each other over 64 tolerances) read as such a stretch, and their stretches are refused by `clip` as before if they straddle the face; *at most `CROSSING_PIECES` pieces* per pair of edges (2^16 since the second review below), refused beyond with the reason. And `topo::check` marks a sheet's shell `SheetClosed` when it has no boundary loop and either has edges or holds a face without loops on a surface that closes all around (`mass::closed_all_around`, the test the measure takes: sphere, torus, a freeform surface closed or ending in poles both ways); a face without loops on a plane or a cylinder is unbounded, not closed, and stays the exporters' and the measure's to refuse.

**Review nits.** The boundary edges of both bodies are found once per boolean, and not looked at when both bodies have none (two solids). `cut.rs` reads the sheet's normal along the boundary of a region on the sheet from the face of the sheet that region lies on (`classify::on_coincident` now says which), not from every sheet face on that surface. And a region beside a cap arc square to the sheet's face there is a tangent cut only when its face is not on that face's surface: a sheet folded along a face of the solid (one face on the top, one through the block) has the top beyond the fold continuing the sheet's top, told nothing there and given its side by the face through the block, where before it was refused as a tangent cut.

**Held to** (`crates/kernel/tests/sheets.rs` › `a_sheet_partly_on_a_face_of_the_solid_divides_it_where_its_rim_crosses`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, as built and turned off-axis): the block's top with sheets facing up and down over x ≤ 2, x ≥ 2, x ≥ 0.5 and a strip from inside, a slanted rim (7.5 inside) and a spline arch crossing an edge twice (10a − 56a³/3, a = √(5/28)), the block with a boss beside and under the sheet: trimmed to closed forms with `area(S ∩ A) + area(S ∖ A) = area(S)`; cut, nothing in front of a sheet facing up and the whole solid behind (12, 12 + π/4), the other way round facing down, the boss in front over the boss (π/4); the folded sheet trimmed to 9 of 30 and cutting the block into halves of 6; a half tube on a rod's side (rim lines crossing the bottom circle) trimmed to π/4 and cutting nothing off; a piece of the spline cone's side beyond y = 0.3 on the cone's frustum (a freeform face, its rim crossing the frustum's top): inside √5 times the plan's segment area, to the tolerance times the length of the fitted sections, and cutting nothing off. On a18b06b every cut fails (refused as passing through nothing, as tangent, or a broken body) except the plain block's left half, right by luck; the trims passed. `crates/topo/tests/mass_surfaces.rs` › `a_whole_closed_surface_marked_as_a_sheet_is_closed` (sphere, torus and their spline forms `SheetClosed`; plane and cylinder not) and `sheets.rs` › `what_a_sheet_must_be` (the sphere sheet refused by the boolean both ways) fail on a18b06b.

### Rims beside an edge (2026-10-21, second review)

**Finding.** `edge_crossings` dropped a piece of the rim only once its half width was under (d − tol) / speed, d its distance from the other edge: a rim running beside an edge of the face at a distance d visits about L / d pieces, and the cap (then 2^14) was reached on ordinary inputs: a rim 0.1 inside the long edge of a block 4000 long, 0.01 inside one 400 long, 300 tolerances inside the 4 × 3 block's edge, a spline arch rising 10 or 20 tolerances beyond an edge; all refused, with a reason ("within the tolerance … in more places than can be told apart") that was not true there.

**Method.** A second lower bound, from the chords: a piece of a curve lies within `turn · h² / 2` of its chord (`hits::turn_bound` bounds the second derivative; h is half the parameter range), so the distance between the chords of a piece of the rim and a piece of the other edge (exact, `section::segments_apart`: the clamped closest points of the two segments and the four end-to-segment distances), less both strays, bounds the distance between the pieces from below. The search now runs over pairs of pieces, halving the longer; two lines side by side are told apart at the first pair, two arcs as soon as their chords hug them. The middle-distance bound stays (to the other edge's piece), and is all there is where a curve has no bend bound. Cap 2^16 pairs, refused with a reason that says what happened: the crossing could not be told within the work allowed. The bounds are proofs for lines, circles and ellipses; for a rational spline the speed and bend bounds come from its Bézier nets and are estimates, so a crossing of a heavily weighted spline edge (as a STEP file may bring) could in principle be missed.

**Noted.** A disc on the top of a block whose rim lies 0.5 tolerance inside the top's edge counts as reaching that edge (one place) and cuts; 2 tolerances or more inside, the top beyond the rim is a part the sheet does not cross and the cut is refused (`NOT_CROSSED`). In between is the tolerance's own band.

**Held to** (`sheets.rs` › `a_rim_beside_an_edge_of_the_face_is_told_apart_from_it`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6): the three rectangles and spline arches 10 and 20 tolerances below and beyond the edge, trimmed to closed forms and cut (all of the block behind); on e3d0fbe the rectangles and the arches beyond the edge fail at every scale (the arches below passed).

### Slivers read at their boundary (2026-10-18, the Garabit pier's joint)

**Finding.** Three L 70 × 70 × 8 angle bars turned 0°, 44° and 77° about one point (the joint of a riveted truss; `target/work/garabit/findings/`, data) could not be united: `(T0 ∪ T1) ∪ T2` refused with "a curve that is partly on and partly off a face boundary", `(T1 ∪ T2) ∪ T0` with "a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it"; any two of the bars united fine, and so did `(T2 ∪ T0) ∪ T1`. The cause is a near-coincidence of the model, not of any one pair: in the plane `y = −0.164` (the top of every bar's horizontal leg, which the turn about y keeps) the inner edge of the first bar's vertical leg (`x = −0.192`), the line of the second's outer face and the end of the third's horizontal leg nearly meet in one point, their three crossings 3.4, 4.3 and 6 tolerances apart at every scale, so the imprint cuts a triangle of inradius about one tolerance off a corner of the union's top face. Plain blocks in the same placement have no edge through that point. Two things then failed, in order, both by sampling: `section::clip` read the chord's side on the face at its middle (1.2 tolerances in: `In`) and at a quarter point (0.6 from the edge: `On`) and refused the disagreement; and after that, `regions::build` found no witness point four tolerances left of the triangle's loop, and `classify` no point inside it, both as `SLIVER`. The smallest case is two bodies: a plane that cuts the corner off a block three tolerances from its vertex, which failed the same two ways (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_truss_joint.rs` › `corner_cut_a_few_tolerances_from_the_vertex`).

**Method.** Three rules, each general by construction. (1) Between its cuts a stretch crosses no edge of either face (a crossing is a cut), so it is either along the boundary, within the tolerance of it everywhere, or inside the face: a sample within the tolerance of the boundary says nothing by itself, one clear of it says inside. But a cut can be lost in that band: an edge fitted within the tolerance of its curve meets the surface at a shallow angle more than a tolerance from the meeting curve, or a hit is taken to the edge's end (`hugs` in `edge_cuts`). So `clip` reads a stretch whose three samples are `In` and `On` as inside only when, at seven places along it, every place in the band is inside by the sign of its offset from the nearest edge (`Outline::inside_by_sign`: the sign `left_of` reads a witness by, with the foot away from the piece's ends and no other piece as near), and refuses it otherwise; a stretch with a sample `Out` and another not is refused as before, one `On` at all three is along the boundary as before, and a touching curve with mixed samples is dropped as before. (2) Which side of its own loop a point is on is a question of the loop's pieces as they are, so a loop with no room at the margin gets its witness as close as a sixteenth of the margin (a quarter tolerance at the four the regions use: `outline::left_of` continues with the same passes at `margin / 16`); the candidate still steps in from a piece's stations, so with vertices two tolerances apart it is a tolerance from the piece's ends at the middle station (half that at the others), nearer to its piece than to anything else, and a winding about a point within rounding of another loop is refused (`TOO_CLOSE`); nothing else reads that point. (3) A region with no point at the margin from its boundary is classified at its boundary, which is exact where a sample is not (`classify::at_boundary`, the neighbourhood classification of the textbooks): at the middle `p` of each arc of the region along which the other body passes through the face, the direction into the region is `n × t` (the region lies to the left of its loop seen from outside); the faces of the other body that hold the arc (within the tolerance of `p` and of the arc's quarter points; a face that holds `p` and not the arc would make `p` a vertex, and is refused) are either one face through `p`, or the two along an edge of the other body that runs along the arc, with any face on the region's own surface among them. A face on the region's surface that reaches to the inward side (the direction into it from its boundary piece along the arc, `n_i × t_i`, has a positive dot with the inward direction) has the region on it, `OnSame` or `OnOpposite` by the normals as for a sampled point; otherwise the region is inside the other body when the inward direction points behind the one face, or into the wedge of the two (behind both when the edge is convex, which it is when each face lies behind the other's surface; behind either when it is concave; behind the one when they continue each other smoothly). A dot within `ANGLE_GUARD` (1e-6, the guard `regions` puts in order by) of zero is faces tangent there, not read; a wall with `p` on its boundary whose other face was not found (a fitted edge whose second face is further than the tolerance at the arc's middle) is not read either, since a half-space would read a convex edge as inside; the arcs of a region must agree (at most 64 are read), and a region with no such arc keeps the sliver refusal. This reading's "on" is a direction (a dot within `ANGLE_GUARD` of zero), where the sampled point's is a distance (within twice the tolerance of a face: `ON_A_FACE`): the reading is the limit at the arc, exact to first order, so a face at a small angle is read by its sign where a sample next to it could not be used; what it decides is a region whose every point is within the margin of its boundary, so a result read differently from what a sample would have said, had there been room for one, differs from the exact result by at most that region, inside the band the boundary already has. The sampled point stays the first method, so every result that was decided is decided as it was: of the corpus only outcomes that were refused move (below).

**Why the rules are general.** None names a configuration: (1) follows from what a cut is, with the band checked by sign because a cut in the band may be lost; (2) from what a winding number is; (3) is the exact classification of a point of the boundary, valid for any surface with a normal and any region, which the sampled point approximates. The only numbers are the ones the kernel already had (the tolerance for "holds", `ANGLE_GUARD` for "tangent", the margin), the seven places of (1), and the arc-holding test, which keeps the reading local. What a sliver *is* in the result is unchanged: the sliver of a union is merged back into the face it was cut from, the sliver of a difference stays as the tiny face the exact geometry has (the corner cut at three tolerances leaves a triangle whose vertices are 4.2 tolerances apart, which `craft3d_topo::touches` accepts).

**Held to** (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_truss_joint.rs`, at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6): the corner cut at 3 and 6 tolerances, the cube's edge beside a convex edge (a turned block) and a concave edge (a notched prism) of the other body at 3 and 5 tolerances (the review's probe: two walls read, both ways round, closed-form volumes), union in both orders (12 faces), difference (7 faces: the block's six and the tiny triangle) and intersection (the corner tetrahedron, 4 vertices and faces, volume δ³/6 within the integrator's bound); every pair of bars in both orders and the three bars in all six orders to the union's volume by inclusion–exclusion of the legs' strips (convex polygon clipping, closed form), the third bar cut from and kept in common with the union of the other two; every result through `topo::check` and the boolean's own check. The four reproducers of the pier build through `craft3d-cli run`, `document.check` clean (the eight-bar chain in 10.8 s on a debug build).

### A face that is itself a sliver (2026-10-20, the Garabit pier's 107th union)

**Finding.** Uniting the pier's 307 bodies one by one (the union-memory run of 2026-10-19, `target/work/garabit/model/pier.3dcraft`, data) stopped at the 107th union: the horizontal bar `HB3b_g` into the union so far, refused as "a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it" on face 13123 of that union. Measured (a print of the region's loops at the refusal): the region is a *whole face* of the union, a triangle on the plane of a diagonal's side face (`PD3a_pm`, `F350:side(e19)`) with sides of 11.8, 9.0 and 14.9 tolerances (1e-6 m) and an inradius of 3.0, bounded by a long edge of a bar, an end edge of the diagonal's face and an edge an earlier union had imprinted: a legitimate tiny face three earlier unions left between three bars (`craft3d_topo::touches` accepts it: its vertices are nine tolerances apart). No pair of the pier's bodies refuses (every pair whose boxes meet, by `document.interference`), nor any triple with the diagonal; the 25 bodies whose boxes meet the bar's, united in order, then the bar, does. The bar's box holds the triangle but the bar does not touch it, so no arc of the face is on the bar; `interior_points` asks for a point four tolerances from the boundary, more than the inradius, and `at_boundary` had nothing to read and gave the sliver refusal. The smallest case is two bodies: the unit cube less its corner at 3, 6 or 9 tolerances (the triangle face of `corner_cut_a_few_tolerances_from_the_vertex`, inradius 1.2 to 3.7 tolerances) united with a plate through the cube that leaves the corner alone but whose box holds it (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_sliver_face.rs`): refused the same way before the fix, in both orders.

**Method** (`classify::samples`). The margin keeps a sample away from the arcs along which the other body passes through the face, which lie on that body: a point nearer to such an arc than the margin is within reach of the other body's faces and tells nothing. A region none of whose arcs is such an arc (`crossed` false) is not divided by the other body, so it lies on one side of it as a whole, or on a coincident face, and any point of it that the classification accepts decides. So when no point at the margin is found, one is looked for as close to the boundary as a sixteenth of the margin, the distance `outline::left_of` reads a loop's witness at (rule 2 of *Slivers read at their boundary*: which side of its own loop a point is on is a question of the loop's pieces as they are), and `Outline::side` takes no point within the tolerance of the boundary, so the floor is one tolerance. The point is then classified as every sample is: on a coincident face of the other body by that face's outline (`On` refused), otherwise by rays, which refuse a point within two tolerances of a face of the other body (`ON_A_FACE`); none of the safeguards against the other body is relaxed. The point at the margin comes first, so every region that one decided is decided as it was; a crossed region without one is read at its boundary as before; only regions that were refused change. No configuration is named: the rule follows from what the margin is for, and the only number is the sixteenth `left_of` already had. (`MARGIN`, the four tolerances, is now a named constant of `classify`.)

**Held to** (`boolean_sliver_face.rs`, at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6, the triangle at 3, 6 and 9 tolerances): union in both orders (one shell, 17 faces, the triangle kept, volume `1 − δ³/6 + 1.8 − 0.2/√2` in closed form: the plate's band `|z − y + ½| ≤ h` has the area `h` in the unit square), the cube less the plate (two lumps), the plate less the cube, their common part (6 faces), every result through `topo::check` and the boolean's own check; the truss-joint tests unchanged. The corpus is untouched (11,988 outcomes, 0 mismatch, 0 invalid, 11,591 agreeing, 231 refused by reason, digest `895441d7261f1afe`: no case takes the new path). The pier's 107th union, rebuilt from the 25 bodies, goes through; whether the chain of 307 reaches the end is open until it runs on the Strix (`target/work/garabit/scratch/pier-union-all-307.json`).

### The fuzzer's wrong results of 2026-10-20 (two causes, four seeds)

The one-hour run on the Strix (94,697 seeds, 710,052 groups, 90,015 booleans, no crash) left four wrong results, the most serious kind of finding, reduced to two causes. Neither was a wrong boolean: the bodies were the exact results. What was wrong was what the kernel *said about* them afterwards, by two methods that any later boolean with such a body as an operand would have used too.

**A torus face charted where its boundary ran** (seeds 20796666, a union of a ball with a hollowed ring less another ball; 20815546, a ring in common with a filleted rod after an edit of the fillet; 20820477, a hollowed pattern of balls less a ring; at scales 1e3, 1e-3 and 1). The boolean's own check reported points outside both operands as inside the result (37, 11 and 148 of a 9³ grid through the box: every one a point the mesh of the same body placed right, and the volumes were the exact ones). Measured, the ray classification took a hit on a torus face of the result for outside the face where it was inside: the face's chart. A face on a torus is charted by cutting the torus open along a circle around the tube (or around the axis) that its loops do not cross, told by the stretch of the angle each boundary piece covers, followed from sample to sample and widened by what the piece can stray between two (`Chart::new`), and the widest stretch of the circle none of them covers (`widest_gap`). That walk started where the first stretch (sorted by start) started and reached forward; a stretch that started later and ran on past a full turn covered the start of the walk as much as its end, and was not counted there. In seed 20796666 the inner torus face of the result has one loop of three pieces, two from the balls and one from the other ball running almost all the way round (the angle about the axis from 4.81 on past 2π to 4.61: 6.08 of 6.28), and the walk took the stretch from 3.80 to 4.81 for a gap, cut the chart at 4.31, through the long piece. In a chart that is no chart of the face the winding of the loops is whatever the pieces add up to, and `Outline::side` read a point of the face as outside it.

*Method.* (1) `widest_gap` takes every stretch as pieces of the one turn `[0, 2π)`: a stretch that passes `2π` is two pieces, one to `2π` and one from `0`; the walk is then over pieces that never leave the turn, and the gap across `2π` is the one from the last piece's end to the first's start as before. General because it is what covering a circle means; no configuration is named. (2) The winding sum holds every stretch of a cut-open torus chart to the chart itself: the distance of a point's image from the chart's origin is `1 + (angle from the cut) / 2π`, which moves by at most the curve's speed over `2π` times the least distance from the axis (a `Tube` chart) or the tube's radius (a `Ring` chart) per unit of parameter, exactly on the torus; a stretch whose image moves further is a jump between the annulus's two ends, i.e. the boundary runs through the cut, and the face is refused (`CUT_CROSSED`, named a bug) rather than classified by a winding of no meaning. The one number is `CUT_SLACK` (1e-2): a boundary curve fitted within the tolerance of the torus moves faster than the bound by at most the tolerance over the radius the chart's angle is taken at (the least distance from the axis, major − minor, for a `Tube` chart; the tube's radius for a `Ring` chart), which is below a hundredth where that radius is over a hundred tolerances. A fat torus whose major − minor is below about a hundred tolerances can therefore get a false `CUT_CROSSED` (a refusal, never a wrong result: the chart's bound itself is exact). (1) is the fix; (2) is the net that turns the same mistake, should another way to it exist, into a refusal. Both sit in `chart.rs`.

*Held to* (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_torus_chart.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3): a ring with a dimple (a small ball taken out of the top of its tube, one loop over a tenth of a turn) less a ball that reaches every part of the tube but a short stretch across from its centre, so that the second loop covers all but that stretch and the only circle the two loops leave is in it; every point of a 13³ grid through the box, clear of the surfaces by a thousandth, is inside the result exactly when the closed forms say so (17 of 2195 were placed wrong before the fix), the boolean's own check in full, and a block cut from the result whose cut and common part make it up again. In `chart.rs`'s own tests: the walk with the finding's three stretches (the one gap is from 4.61 to 4.81), a stretch alone that passes `2π`, and a loop around the axis in a chart cut along a circle around the tube, refused as through the cut, and charted around the axis instead winding once about the inner end. `crates/engine/tests/fuzz_wrong.rs` replays the three designs.

**Slivers a boolean leaves as lumps of their own** (seed 20756595, a cone less a cylinder at scale 1e-3, tolerance 1e-9). `document.check` deep found "shell 0 is a cavity that lies in no part of the body". The cylinder runs through the cone's base, its axis 7,300 tolerances under the base's centre and tilted against the base plane by 0.01, so the base circle (radius 2.5 mm, as the cylinder's) leaves the cylinder along two arcs of 15,000 tolerances by 11 tolerances at most, and the cone's side just above them by 66, before the cone re-enters the cylinder and leaves it for good higher up. The exact difference is three lumps: the cone above the cylinder's arch, and two slivers at the sides of the base, 15 µm long and 66 nm thick, each three faces (a piece of the base, of the side and of the cylinder); the boolean built them right (the checker, its own check and the identity of sets pass). Their volume is about 1e-21 mm³, and the faces of a shell are integrated each to its own boundary: where two meet along an edge within the tolerance the gap between them is up to twice it, whose flux about the point the integrals are taken about (the body's middle, 0.0125 away) is up to 1e-16: the slivers measured −2.3e-18 and −3.6e-18 with the integrator's own bounds of 5e-19 and 8e-19 (which cover the quadrature, not the model), and `lumps` read the sign as a cavity, outside every lump, and refused the body.

*Method.* Where a shell lies says what it is: inside an even number of other shells the outside of a lump, inside an odd number a cavity, the shell it lies in most deeply of the other kind, a cavity's lump that one. That is the definition of lumps and cavities, and it now decides (`lumps_measured`); the volume is held to it as a check where it has a sign to read, beyond what it can be off by: the integrator's bound, plus twice the tolerance times the shell's area (the band around the shell), plus the flux through the gaps along its edges (two thirds of the tolerance times the reach of the point the integrals are about, times the edges' length by their curves' speed bound: `lumps::uncertainty`, with `ShellVolume` now carrying the area from the same integration). The reach is the diagonal of a box that holds both that point and the shell: the body's, for the volumes of one integration of the whole body, about its middle; where that leaves a shell's sign unread and the shell's own box is smaller, the shell is measured again alone (`lumps::measured_alone`, about a point in its own box) and held to its own diagonal, so the bound scales with the shell and not with what is around it. The review of 2026-10-21 found the body's diagonal alone too wide: a cube of 0.05 or 0.01 in a block of 1000, facing out in the block's material (a cavity turned inside out), measured 1.25e-4 against a bound of about 7e-4, nearly all of it the diagonal term, and was accepted; with its own diagonal the bound is below 1e-7 and it is refused again (`crates/kernel/tests/lumps.rs` › `a_small_shell_in_a_large_body_is_held_to_its_sign`: that case, the right cavity and a small cavity apart, in the middle of the block and near two corners, at three scales; failed on the first version of the fix). General because the gap flux of a closed shell is what it is about any point and its bound is taken about the point actually used; measuring alone is only done where it can only tighten. A shell whose readable volume says the other kind is turned inside out, `Defect::ShellNesting` as before (the seeded cases of `crates/kernel/tests/lumps.rs` are unchanged: a cavity turned inside out, the outside turned inside out, a cavity apart from everything, a block inside a block both facing out). General because it is derived, not tuned: every term is a bound on what the tolerance lets the integral be off by, and the decision by nesting holds for any shells that do not cross, which the self-intersection check holds every body to. The STEP export's shortcut (one shell facing outward, the rest its cavities, without a ray) now applies only when every shell's volume has a sign to read.

*Held to* (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_sliver_lumps.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3): the finding's cone and cylinder, whose difference passes the checker and the boolean's own check, has three shells of 2, 3 and 3 faces, is three lumps without cavities (refused before the fix), only the cone's shell having a volume with a sign to read, and with its common part makes up the cone within the tolerance band of the operands' areas. `crates/engine/tests/fuzz_wrong.rs` replays the design.

### Tangent contact of higher order at the boundary (2026-10-21, checklist item 2: the Garabit pier's union 127, the Perseverance's drill tip)

Two models were refused "two curves leave a point in the same direction and stay together (tangent contact of higher order)" the same day. The Perseverance: a cone (r 30) whose base circle lies on the wall of a drill (r 30) along a tilted axis; along z it builds. The pier: union 127 of 307, a stair tread (a block) whose end touches the inside of the helical stringer (a sweep along a helix) near its middle, its corners going into the stringer.

*Cause, measured.* Tilted, the positions are given to a thousandth, so the cone's axis is 3.4e-6 rad off the drill's and its base centre 3.9e-4 mm off it: the base circle is up to 3.9e-4 mm (390 tolerances) off the wall, not on it, and the curve of cone and wall crosses the base circle at two points at about `2 e h / R²` = 2.6e-5 rad. Along z the inputs are exact and the curve is the base circle. Nothing axis-aligned decided; the inputs differ. One crossing lay 1.9e-4 mm from the cone's seam vertex, so on the cone's side face the boundary piece from the crossing to the seam was 1.9e-4 long, and `untie` reads the order of rays that leave together within a quarter of the shortest of them: there the curve and the base circle are 1e-9 apart, under the tolerance, and it refused. On the pier the stringer's curve on the tread's top and bottom faces leaves the end edge along it where the end touches the stringer, and the pieces at that vertex end 0.015 and 0.0025 m further (other vertices of the imprint), where they are still within a few tolerances of the edge. In both the tie is between a piece of the face's boundary and a curve imprinted on the face, and the distance to tell them apart by geometry is longer than the pieces.

*Method* (`regions::order_run`). An imprinted arc lies in its face: it is clipped to the face and arcs do not cross between vertices (the invariant `untie` already rests on). The face lies to the left of its boundary seen from outside: counter-clockwise from the boundary use that leaves the vertex, clockwise from the one that arrives. So in a run of rays that leave a vertex together, with exactly one boundary ray, the imprinted rays go right after that ray when its use leaves the vertex and right before it when its use arrives; only the imprinted rays among themselves are measured (`untie`, as before, when there are two or more). This is topology, the same for any surfaces and placements, at any angle however shallow and any distance to the next vertex. New conditions, each with why it is general: (1) the rule applies only to a run with exactly one boundary ray: with two (a corner of no angle) the face may lie between them or around them, which topology does not say, so the run is measured as before; (2) the boundary ray's arc must be used once by the face's boundary (`Sides::once`): a seam or a slit is used twice, with the face on both sides, and says nothing of the side; (3) the cusp branch (every ray along the normal) is unchanged. No tolerance or constant was added. Where the rule does not apply and the pieces are too short, the refusal stands as before (two imprinted curves, or two boundary pieces, that stay together beyond the shortest piece); the general method for that is to read the order beyond the vertex of the shortest piece, following the arcs that continue it to where the curves are a few tolerances apart, which is owed.

*Held to.* `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_tangent_order.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, square to the world and turned three ways (about (1, 1, 1) by 0.3, (0.2, −1, 0.5) by 1.1, x by π/2 − 0.01): (a) a cylinder (r 1, h 3) and a cone (r 1, h 2) on it 0.1 below the lid, its base pushed 13 tolerances off the axis and its seam 1e-5, 1e-4 and 1e-3 from the crossing on the curve's side (and 1e-4 on the other, built before): union, common part and both differences against the coaxial closed forms within the tolerance times the area (the crescents differ by `O(e²)`), the identity of sets, the union and common part meshed closed at a chord of `e` (the crescents are 13 and 26 tolerances across); refused before the fix from the first seam on the curve's side; (b) a block whose end touches the inside of a tube (r 1, wall 0.02) at 8e-3 and 1e-2 from its side: union, common part (refused as zero thickness: its two pieces meet along the line of contact, held as made without the refusal), both differences against `t (S(notch) + S(wide))`, `S(a) = a R − (a √(R² − a²) + R² asin(a / R)) / 2`, the identity of sets, three results meshed closed at 5e-3; refused before the fix. `crates/engine/tests/boolean_findings.rs` › `the_pier_stair_tread_on_its_stringer` (the stair's stringer and tread from the pier's own commands; the feature builds and passes the deep check, the identities of the three booleans in place and turned) and `the_rover_drill_tip_on_a_tilted_drill` (the rover's steps; the identities hold in place and turned); both refused before. The whole pier on the Strix (Heavy `pier`, 50d9c50): all 307 unions built and the document's check clean, 26 min 24 s wall, 7.1 GB peak. The corpus unchanged: 11,988 operations, 11,591 agreeing, 0 MISMATCH, 0 INVALID.

*Open.* (The rover's feature was refused next by the self-check's mesh, whose crescents ran out of rounds of halved chords; meshed since 2026-10-21, [`tessellator.md`](tessellator.md) › *Three faces the tessellator could not mesh*; the self-check then refused it as touching itself, a vertex 5.6e-9 mm from an edge that is not its own: a sharp corner, not a touch, and the feature builds since the touch rule reads corners, *The rover's drill tip, measured* below.) Two imprinted curves that stay together beyond the shortest piece are still refused.
### A tangency line on which another wall ends (2026-10-20, fuzz seed 20811107: accepted, and a question)

**Finding.** A block (a cylinder of radius 10000, 7500 high, with more cut from it) less a quarter of a barrel of radius 7500 about a line in the block's top face, turned a quarter turn from a start wall: the barrel's ceiling is tangent to the top face along the line, and the start wall (the revolution's start cap) ends on that line, inside the top face. The result is one shell whose top face has the start wall's top edge and both its ends lying inside it, unsplit; along the line three sheets meet: the top face through it, the ceiling tangent to it from below, the start wall square to it. The fuzzer's `document.check` called it a certain crossing, which was the measure's fault (`tessellator.md`, the walk over the touched face); the boolean accepts it, and after the measure was put right so does the check (the design is a fixture of `fuzz_wrong.rs`).

**Why it is accepted, and the question.** The developer's decision of 2026-10-18 keeps a wall that thins to nothing within one shell (a hole tangent to a side, cones sharing a ruling): `walls_apart` sees the ceiling bending into the top face's material and accepts the pair, and the start wall hanging below the top likewise. The pinned `cones_that_share_a_ruling` has the same structure at a point: the inner cone's base rim meets the shared ruling inside the outer cone's face, with the base plane ending there, so a rule that refused this groove would refuse that too. A general rule was prototyped and not committed: *a vertex or an edge of the shell lying strictly inside a face it is not part of, with two or more faces of the shell at it none of which is joined to that face there* (the joins of `within_a_shell`: a shared vertex or edge within reach, or a shared edge at a sharp angle within its margin), applied in `within_a_shell` for the boolean and in `parts_touch` for every body regeneration hands on; it told this groove from a thinning wall (which has one other face through the place) but refused the cones at their rim. Whether such a place (three sheets along a line or at a point, two of them ending there) is a wall that thins to nothing or the shell meeting itself is for the developer to settle (D-open-15); until then the letter of 2026-10-18 stands and the body is accepted.
### The fuzzer's timeouts of 2026-10-20 (2026-10-21)

The one-hour run left 11 seeds whose process ran past 60 s. Run one by one with the time of every command (`CRAFT3D_FUZZ_TRACE=1 craft3d-fuzz check`) and sampled (`gdb`, every busy thread), none was a loop that does not end: each was work that grew faster than it needed to, in four places, and on this machine the test profile runs these as fast as release. Three are in the boolean (the fourth, the search of a face operation, is in [`face-operations.md`](face-operations.md) › *Bounded work*; the pattern's own test of whether its instances meet in [`regeneration.md`](regeneration.md)).

**Zero thickness looked at every place many times, and at every face a place lies in the box of** (`zero_thickness`, `within_a_shell`). A union of two clusters of five shelled balls (seed 20757788, 286 + 286 faces, 60 shells) took 49.5 s, 47.4 of them here: 39,671 places, 2,282 of them distinct (a vertex of the section is also where an operand touches itself, the same touch comes from both operands), and the box of every face a piece of a ball is the whole ball, so every place lay in the boxes of all 1,100 faces, each projected onto and its outline asked whether the foot is inside; then the second half asked the same of every pair of them in one shell. *Method*, each a function of the inputs alone, every answer the one given before: the places are taken once each, in the order they came (`distinct`, by their bits); a face is a candidate at a place when the box of what it holds holds it too (`Body::held_face_bbox`: a piece of a ball or a torus boxed near where it is; grown by the tolerance more than the face's own box, since a foot on a face's boundary may lie that far outside it; the face's own box still sets how far `within_a_shell` looks about a place); and the first half's faces at each place, with their feet, are what the second half pairs (a pair one of whose faces is not at the place went on to the next whatever else was true of it, so the same pairs are tried in the same order). The union now takes 1.1 to 1.9 s.

**The numeric intersections were remembered 64 at a time** (`geom::intersect::meet`, the shelf). A union of 106 and 670 faces (seed 20830834, rings of a pattern all near one another) intersects 65,053 pairs of faces, 9,800 numerically; the identities of the fuzzer (union, difference and intersection of the same operands), the check that holds a boolean to every exact boolean of its operands and a regeneration done again ask the same questions, and each marched all of them again (16 s each). The shelf is now a map by the question, kept by what it weighs (131,072 questions, 4 million points of curves, 64 MB of questions; a couple of hundred MB when full): fewer than a boolean asks is no help, since the questions come in the same order and each would be let go of before it is asked again. The same boolean asked again takes 6 to 8 s.

**The nearest point of a piece of an ellipse took three sines a step** (`nearest_on_ellipse`, 730,000 calls and 48 million steps of its branch and bound in one union of seed 20757179): the distance and the speed at a point now come from one sine and cosine, kept with the stretch's ends; the same steps and the same numbers.

**The chart's speed near a point searched the whole tree of a surface** (`PatchTree::sigma_search`, the classification of points against freeform faces: 3,000 questions in one try of an offset of a chamfer on a torus, each through all 17,500 nodes of its tree, 48 s; seed 20795706). Each node now knows the least bound of the leaves under it (`Node::floor`; a parent's bound is at least the least of its children's, and a leaf's pieces keep at least its own), and a node whose floor is no lower than the least found so far is passed by: what it could give would not change the least, and every node after it is asked with the same least, so the answer is the same. The try takes 16 s; it counts a little less work, since the pieces of leaves passed by are not charged (`allowance::once`).

*What is left.* The cost of a boolean of bodies whose faces all lie near one another grows with the product of their face counts: every pair's boxes meet (65,053 of 71,020 pairs above), and each pair is intersected, its edges cut against the other surface and its curves clipped. Seed 20786729, a pattern of 5 of a pattern of 5 of three shelled tubes less a ball, unites bodies of 981 faces into one of 8,310 and still runs past two minutes (the union of 981 and 981 faces alone 100 s). A tighter test than boxes for whether two faces can meet is owed. So is a closed form for a circle against a ball: the full seed 20757788, a pattern of 6 of a pattern of 6 shelled balls (36 balls in one cluster), takes 114 s in one pattern, most of it the roots of circles against spheres found numerically (`hits::curve_hits`, for the classification and the edge cuts); the 15-minute run on the Strix still had it as its one timeout. (Both built since 2026-10-22: *Faces that lie near one another* below.)

*Held to.* `crates/geom/src/intersect/meet.rs` (the shelf keeps the questions of a big boolean and lets go of the oldest), `crates/kernel/tests/{boolean_contact,lumps,boolean_lumps,boolean_surfaces,chamfer,offset_freeform}.rs` unchanged, and `crates/engine/tests/fuzz_findings.rs` (a pattern of four of five shelled balls in the gate, in seconds; the ten seeds that end replayed, ignored, for release).

### Tolerant operands, the first slice (2026-10-21, checklist item 4)

The table, the check per entity, the working tolerance through the boolean and a perturbed-body oracle (Method › *Tolerant operands*; [`topology.md`](topology.md) › *Tolerant entities*). The oracle (`crates/kernel/tests/tolerant.rs`): a block, or a block less a cylinder (a boolean), against a cylinder, a sphere, a torus, a block with a NURBS roof or a block, each through a face of the first; each operand perturbed twice the way STEP files are (a face's surface moved, a vertex nudged, an edge's curve moved, a curve refitted as a coarse C1 cubic spline within the gap), by up to 3, 30 and 300 tolerances, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, two seeds each, every gap measured and recorded. 360 bodies (151 faces moved, 132 vertices nudged, 132 edges moved, 89 curves refitted) pass `check` with their tolerances; 263 fail without them and 267 with a tenth of them, among them all 207 whose gap moved a vertex or an edge's end (the places the check sees for sure; a face moved along itself leaves no gap). 522 booleans whose unperturbed operands give a result (18 more are refused unperturbed): **507 right, 15 refused, 0 invalid**. Right is valid with its own table and with a volume within `W (A_a + A_b + (L_a + L_b) D)` of the unperturbed boolean's, `W` the working tolerance, `A` the operands' areas, `L` their edge lengths and `D` the diagonal of their box (a slab of `W` over every face, a strip of `W` along every edge as far out as the bodies reach); the worst comes to 1.2 % of that bound. The 15 refusals are all "imprint lost" (a curve where two faces cross that does not divide them; the coarser working tolerance on the cut edges). Every right result has the reference's shells and faces, and is the same body bit for bit when computed twice. The test takes 4 s. (Numbers after the review below: 402 of the 507 results carry a table, worst 1.6 % of the derived bound.)

The check proves a loose edge on its faces (`certify::curve_on_surface`, at most `PROOF_PIECES` = 65 536 pieces) and keeps the five places for every exact edge: proving every edge cost 176 times the five places on the benchmark's models and fails for 210 exact coedges in 65 536 pieces ([`topology.md`](topology.md) › *A point without a distance is off its surface*), so a body without loose entities is checked exactly as before. Held: a bump between the five places on a loose edge is found (`EdgeNotProven`), and measured as the bump (`crates/topo/tests/tolerant.rs`).

Unchanged for exact bodies: the boolean corpus digest and the benchmark's counters (on the Strix, 2026-10-21: the corpus digest `895441d7261f1afe` as before, 11,591 outcomes agreeing, none wrong or invalid; the benchmark's 34 counters equal to its baseline). Open for slice 2 (done 2026-10-22 but for the fits and the measure, *Tolerant modelling, the second slice*): sewing loose faces into shells (`kernel::sew`) with its round-trip oracle; the other kernel operations (fillets, offsets, sweeps) still work at the document's tolerance on a loose body and give results without a table (refused by the check where they copy a loose entity); a fit that misses half the document's tolerance accepted as a loose edge; the measure's bound with a gap term; per-entity rules where measurement asks for them.

### Review of the first slice (2026-10-21)

Three findings of the independent review, fixed. (1) *A gap hidden as a tolerance*: with one operand loose, the other was checked only at the working tolerance, and every failing entity of the result was raised to it, so an exact block united with a cylinder whose face lay 5e-5 off its edges (nothing recorded) came out valid with that edge given 1e-4. Now each operand is checked at the document's tolerance with its own table first, only entities the boolean could have moved are given a tolerance, and each one given is reported (`an_unrecorded_gap_in_an_operand_is_refused_beside_a_loose_one`, which returned a body before). (2) *Regeneration ignored the document's cap*: `regen::checked` held bodies to the default cap; it now passes the document's, which is part of what the remembered check keys on (only when it is not the default, so every key of an exact document is what it was): `bodies_are_held_to_the_documents_cap`, both directions. (3) *Looseness spread*: every intersection edge and new vertex was stamped with the working tolerance, so 16 cuts on a block with one loose face made 52 of 124 edges loose; now they are measured and given a tolerance only where they need one: the same row keeps exactly the 4 edges and 4 vertices it started with (`looseness_does_not_spread_through_a_row_of_booleans`). The oracle's volume bound is now derived (`2 W (A_a + A_b)` for the shells of half-width `W` about both operands' boundaries, plus `W (L_a + L_b) D / 3` for the gaps the measure leaves out along edges) and the results are held to the reference's shell and face counts; the numbers: 507 right, 15 refused, 0 invalid, 0 with other counts, worst 1.6 % of the bound, 402 results with a table. `DEFAULT_LOOSEST` has one source (`craft3d_math`), `document.inspect` shows `loosest` only where the document sets one.

### Tolerant modelling, the second slice (2026-10-22, checklist item 4)
