Sewing loose faces ([`topology.md`](topology.md) › *Sewing*), STEP files read sewn ([`step-import.md`](step-import.md)), the operations on one loose body, and the mesh check on the entities' own tolerances ([`tessellator.md`](tessellator.md)).

**Before:** a fillet, chamfer, offset, move, draft or delete face, shell or thicken on a loose body ran at the document's tolerance; the face operations refused at the first loose edge ("the edge would no longer be a line or a circle on both its faces", 18 of 18 offsets, moves, drafts and thickens on sewn blocks), delete face returned bodies the checker refused, and what passed carried only what an inner boolean had recorded.

**Method** (`loose_op::run`, wrapped round each operation's public function; a body without loose entities goes straight to the operation, unchanged). The body given is checked at the document's tolerance with its table first (an unrecorded gap is refused, never hidden). The operation runs unchanged at the body's working tolerance. The result's table is rebuilt from nothing: an edge whose source (the operation's own map of where each entity came from, which names already use) was loose and whose curve is still that curve keeps its tolerance, a vertex likewise when it is still at its point; an entity with another curve or point, or no source, is new. Then the result is checked at the document's tolerance and each entity not within its own that the operation could have moved (new or loose; an exact edge at a new or loose vertex or on a face that is new, merged or on another surface than its source's; an exact vertex at the end of such an edge) is measured and given `1.125 ×` the measure, never more than the working tolerance; what still fails is refused (`InvalidBody`). No entity of the result is looser than the body given, and a body whose working tolerance is above the document's cap is refused (`*_capped` functions, which regeneration calls with the document's `loosest`), so an operation never grows the largest tolerance of a body. General: it reads only the maps every operation already returns and the geometry; it names no operation's configuration.

**Held to** (`crates/kernel/tests/sew.rs` › `operations_on_sewn_bodies_carry_their_tolerances_or_refuse`): a block and a block less a cylinder, exploded, every face moved by up to 3, 30 and 300 tolerances and sewn (loose at every edge), at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6; each operation's result valid with its table, none looser than its body, the volume within `2 W A + W L D / 3` of the same operation on the exact body: fillet 18 of 18 right, chamfer 15 (3 refused: an inner boolean's result invalid, at k = 300), offset, move, draft and shell 18 of 18, delete face 9 of 9, thicken 6 (12 refused by the boolean in the band, the coarser working tolerance); every right result carries a table; none invalid. Before: offset, move, draft, delete and thicken 0 right, fillet 12. Through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/step_import.rs` › `operations_on_a_loose_import_carry_its_tolerances`): a block whose file puts a corner 1e-4 mm off its edges, imported, then filleted, chamfered, shelled or offset: `document.check` ok, the result listed as loose.

**Open:** thicken and chamfer refusals in the band at 300 tolerances (the boolean at the coarser working tolerance); per-entity rules (the decision's later step) where those refusals ask for them. (The refusals: done in the third slice, below.)

### Tolerant modelling, the third slice (2026-10-22, checklist item 4)

T-junctions in sewing and the mass bound's gap term ([`topology.md`](topology.md) › *Tolerant entities, the third slice*), the deep check's mesh of loose bodies ([`tessellator.md`](tessellator.md)), and the boolean between a loose body and a tool made from it.

**Finding.** On the sewn bodies of the second slice, thicken was refused 12 times in 18 ("two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart", from k = 3 already, and "two faces between half and twice the tolerance apart") and chamfer 3 times at k = 300 (an inner boolean's result with edges used once). Both are a boolean between the loose body and a tool the operation made from it (thicken's moved copy, the blend's tool), run at the body's working tolerance `W`: an entity of the body lies up to `W` off where it belongs and the tool's entity made from it or its neighbour up to `W` the other way, so two that are one may be `2W` apart, while the boolean merges within `W/2` and refuses the band between `W/2` and `2W`.

**Method** (`loose_op::for_tool`). That boolean runs at four times the working tolerance (`TOOL_WIDENING`), for a body with loose entities only (an exact body's tolerance is passed through, bit for bit): thicken's subtraction of its two bodies and each step of a blend (fillet, chamfer). What it builds is measured afterwards and held to `W` all the same (`loose_op`'s settle step), so no entity comes out looser than the body was; one that needs more is refused. This is no per-entity rule: it is the working tolerance a pair of loose bodies needs, `2 (t_a + t_b)`, with both at `W`.

*Review (2026-10-22):* the wider band also refuses what lies between `2W` and `8W` apart, which at `W` was told apart; so a refusal at `4W` is tried again at `W` (`loose_op::boolean_for_tool`), whose refusal is then the one given. *Measured*: thickening each of the 8 faces of a sewn block with a step of 40 to 320 tolerances on its top (1.0 to 8.3 times `W`), three seeds: 54 booleans were refused at `4W`, and every one of them at `W` too; no case was found where the retry changes the outcome. It stays as the safeguard the reasoning asks for.

**Held to** (`sew.rs` › `operations_on_sewn_bodies_carry_their_tolerances_or_refuse`, now asserting no refusal of fillet, chamfer or thicken): chamfer 15 → **18 of 18**, thicken 6 → **18 of 18** right, valid with their tables, none looser than the body given, the volume within the bound; the other operations as before (fillet, offset, move, draft, shell 18 of 18, delete 9 of 9).

### A plane beside a rippled surface (2026-10-21, the Garabit valley's finding 5)

**Finding.** A pit (a block 30 × 20 × 40 m) subtracted from the showcase's terrain (a loft of 64 sections, one B-spline face 1.4 km across) was refused with its bottom at z = 735 m, "an intersection curve needs more points than the cap allows", and cut fine at 735.5, 745, 730 or 700. Under the pit the terrain is 10 to 25 m higher; 20 m beside it lies the lake, whose fitted surface ripples about 735.000 by a few millimetres (control points 734.999, 735.0, 735.001 …). Not a plane coincident with a flat part of the surface: the pair of faces was intersected in the box both faces share grown by 1.3 % of the *larger* face's box, 22 m round the pit's bottom, which reached into the lake, where the plane and the rippled surface meet in long, nearly tangent curves the marcher could not trace within its cap of points. Neither face holds any of them.

**Method** (`section.rs`). The room is there so that a curve is not cut off where a face is; it is taken from the larger face since a room from the overlap of the two boxes cut half a drop's curve too close to the face (2026-10-15). So it stays, and when the meeting asked in it fails as `NoConvergence` or `Unsupported` (curves the marcher cannot trace, or tangent over too large an area), the meeting is asked again in the box both faces share grown by 16 tolerances and 1.3 % of the *smaller* face's box, which still holds everything of both faces with room; a failure there too is refused with the first reason, as before. General: it names no configuration and changes no result that was made (only refusals take the second path), and what the second box leaves out is what neither face can be at. The same retry serves a large face next to anything the marcher cannot follow far from the smaller face.

**Held to** (`crates/engine/tests/garabit_findings.rs` › `a_pit_at_the_height_of_the_lake_beside_it_cuts_the_terrain`): the reproducer's terrain and pit at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3 (every length and the tolerance scaled), subtract and intersect both made, `vol(T ∖ P) + vol(T ∩ P) = vol(T)` to 1e-9. Refused before at scale 1 (the release CLI of 14:21). A synthetic terrain rippling beside a pit (24 to 48 sections, ripples of a thousandth) did not reproduce the refusal before the fix: the marcher traced its curves; the reproducer is the showcase's data. *Synthetic* (2026-10-22, the review's follow-up): the re-ask is its own function (`section::in_room`), held by `section::tests::a_meeting_that_fails_in_the_larger_faces_room_is_asked_in_the_smaller_ones` without a terrain: a meeting that fails wherever its box reaches a point 12 m beside a 30 × 20 m face of a 1 km face's pair (in the larger room, not in the smaller) is asked again once, in a box that leaves the point out and holds both faces where they overlap; a failure there too gives the first reason, another failure is not asked again, faces of one size are asked once; at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3. A terrain whose valley touches the pit bottom's plane along a line 12 m away (a ruled quadratic B-spline) did not reproduce the refusal either: the marcher handles that contact.

### A crack that thins to a point (2026-10-21, the Garabit valley's finding 4)

**Finding.** Pier 2's union chain was refused at the diagonal S55, "the result would have zero thickness … at F23:side(e50) [the leg's web] and F135:face(5) [the diagonal's end face]", though the leg and the diagonal unite alone. Reduced by delta debugging over the 58 bodies before it to three: the leg, the girder G19 and the diagonal (`crates/engine/tests/garabit_findings/pier2_min.steps.json`). Measured: the girder's bottom lies on the leg's web at 1.19°, crossing it along a line, so the crack between them (the girder lifted off the web on one side of the line) stays within twice the tolerance (1e-6 m) of closed for 96 µm from the line; the diagonal's end face cuts across the crack 28 µm from where the girder's end face does. The exact union is one valid shell: the crack's walls meet in an edge (the line), and where the diagonal cuts it they meet in one vertex (leg, girder and diagonal), from which the crack opens at 1.19°; the diagonal's face between the crack's walls has a corner of 1.5° there. Three checks took the narrow crack for two parts touching:
1. The boolean's look at two walls of one shell (`within_a_shell`) joins two faces at a place by their shared edge, with a margin of `near / sin(θ/2)` that grows as the faces' angle θ shrinks (`wedged`), but only where the place's foot is inside the edge; here the place (the middle of the edge girder–diagonal) is past the edge's end, the vertex.
2. The same look caps that margin at a tenth of the smaller face, and in the intersection of the girder and leg with the diagonal one face is 55 µm across.
3. The same look, at the girder's bottom corner, pairs the leg's web with the girder's end face, which share no edge there: the corner is near the web only because the girder's bottom is.
4. Regeneration's `topo::touches` (vertex within twice the tolerance of an edge it is not an end of) found the far end of the diagonal face's 1.5° corner within 1.46 µm of the corner's other side.

**Method.** (1) The distance from a place to a shared edge is to the edge as a set of points: its foot when that is inside the edge's range, otherwise the nearer end. (2) Beyond the cap a shared edge still joins the faces at the place when the gap between them there is at least half of what two planes leaving the edge at θ have at that distance (`gap ≥ d sin(θ/2)`, against the wedge's `2 d sin(θ/2)`): they do not come back towards each other; the cap keeps its job for faces that do. This reading asks the faces to open at the edge (`sin(θ/2)` above `ANGLE_GUARD`: faces tangent there make no wedge) and the place to be further from the edge than the reach of a shared edge (four tolerances: a place on the edge is joined there or not at all). (3) Two faces at a place that are not joined there are still not two walls touching when one of them is joined at the place to a third face that makes a wedge with the other, by the reading of (2) alone: the place is one point, near the other face by the wedge's doing. (4) `topo::touches` does not count a vertex near an edge it is not an end of when an edge joins the vertex to an end `w` of that edge and the vertex lies within `near / sin(φ/2)` of `w`, φ the angle between the two edges' directions out of `w`, the corner opens (`sin(φ/2)` above 1e-6) and the vertex is at least half as far from the edge as the corner puts it (`d sin(φ/2)`) (`at_a_corner`): a face's corner sharper than the tolerance resolves, the same margin as (1). Each is general: it reads a wedge where the geometry has one, whatever made it, and only results that were refused change (the joining is wider, never narrower). What is still refused: two walls within the tolerance that share nothing near the place, a shared edge further than its margin, faces that come back towards each other (the gap smaller than the wedge's), faces tangent where they meet, and two vertices within twice the tolerance of each other (the rule of 2026-10-18). The first gate run of this branch found the guards needed: without the opening, the block on a saddle's tangent plane (two gaps meeting in the point of tangency, `boolean_freeform.rs`) was accepted; without the distance, a grid of blocks sharing an edge (`boolean_random.rs` › `pairs_that_once_went_wrong`, seed 1000568) was read as a wedge of 90°; both refused again, as pinned.

**Held to** (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_crack.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6): the three bars as the pier places them (the leg as the plate of its web), united in order: one shell, valid, the boolean's own check, no touch, `vol(U ∪ C) = vol(U) + vol(C) − vol(U ∩ C)` (the intersection made too); a prism whose face has a corner of 1/600 rad, its far side 1.5 tolerances from the other, touches nothing. Both refused before the fix. `crates/engine/tests/garabit_findings.rs` › `a_crack_cut_short_by_a_diagonal_unites` (the three bars through the commands, `document.check` deep clean), and the whole reproducer (339 steps, 78 unions) builds, `document.check` clean. The diagonal first, then the girder, is refused (seen at 1e-3) as two points between half and twice the tolerance apart (finding 3's class: the girder's end edge and the crack's vertex 1.7 tolerances apart).

### Faces at the edge of tolerance, as the Garabit valley met them (2026-10-21, finding 3)

**Finding.** Two chord bars of the arch rib placed by `feature.transform` with translations rounded to 0.1 mm were refused, "two faces between half and twice the tolerance apart"; the whole rib (92 bars) unites with nine decimals. Measured: the bars' side faces are exactly parallel (each bar turns about an axis in the rib's plane), and the rounding leaves them 15.8 µm apart between AL_E8 and AL_E9 (apart: a step of 15.8 µm, accepted) and 0.763 µm apart between AL_E9 and AL_E10, between half and twice the tolerance of 1e-6 m. The block shifted by 1.5e-6 m is the same, met first where edges cross (two points 1.5 tolerances apart). The band rule itself, not a separate cause.

**Kept, with a clearer reason** (`regen/src/boolean.rs`): a refusal at the edge of tolerance now names the faces by their persistent names and says the document's tolerance, the band (half and twice it) and what to do: put them onto each other within half the tolerance (positions with more digits, or placed from the same numbers) or at least twice it apart. The kernel's reasons are unchanged (they are fixed texts the corpus counts by).

**The method this asks for, owed** (not built: the developer decided on 2026-10-22 to keep refusing until a real model needs it, [`../decisions.md`](../decisions.md)): with tolerant modelling's first slice the boolean can work at a coarser tolerance and record what it costs. Faces within the band would be taken as coincident by running the boolean of two exact operands at a working tolerance `W` with the measured gap at most `W / 2` (`W = 4 × tol` covers the whole band), at most the document's cap (`loosest`, 1000 tolerances by default), and the result's entities measured and given what they need (`loose::carry`: only entities the boolean could have moved, each reported in `raised`), so the 0.76 µm step is closed and recorded as edge and vertex tolerances of a few µm. Not built: the decision of 2026-10-18 keeps a body without loose entities, every body 3DCraft builds itself, exactly as it was, and says an operation refuses rather than grow tolerances unseen; a boolean of two exact bodies that makes loose ones on its own (even reported) is a change of that, the developer's to make. It would stay bit for bit for the corpus (only refusals would take it).

### The rover's drill tip, measured (2026-10-22)

**Finding.** The Perseverance's drill tip (a cone of radius 30 whose base circle lies 3.9e-4 mm off a tilted drill's wall of radius 30) was right and meshed since 2026-10-21, and its feature was refused by the self-check as touching itself: a vertex 5.6e-9 mm from an edge it does not belong to.

**Measured.** The union has 6 faces and 6 vertices. The vertex is the cone's seam vertex on the base circle, 1.94e-4 mm along the circle from where the curve of cone and wall crosses it; the edges it is near are the two sides of the crescent on the wall (the curve of cone and wall, and the base plane's ellipse on the wall), which leave the crossing at 2.9e-5 and 1.3e-5 rad from the piece of the base circle that ends at the seam vertex. 1.94e-4 times those angles is 5.6e-9 and 2.5e-9, the distances found to two digits: the seam vertex is near the crescent's sides only because they leave a common vertex at an angle sharper than the tolerance resolves over that length. That is a corner, not the shell coming back to itself; nothing within tolerance was left unmerged, and the boolean had nothing to sew. The rule that refused it was the check's: `craft3d_topo::touches` read every vertex within twice the tolerance of an edge it is not an end of as a touch.

**Already fixed, generally.** Since the Garabit crack (2026-10-21, *A crack that thins to a point* above, `touch.rs › at_a_corner`) the rule passes a vertex joined by an edge to an end of the edge it is near when it lies within `near / sin(φ/2)` of that end and as far from the edge as the corner puts it. Here sin(φ/2) is 6.5e-6 and 1.4e-5, above the guard of 1e-6, and the distances are at least half of `d sin φ`, so both pairs are corners. The feature builds and passes the deep check (`engine/tests/boolean_findings.rs › the_rover_drill_tip_on_a_tilted_drill`, now required to build; the helper's allowance for the old refusal is gone). No new rule.

*Held to.* `kernel/tests/boolean_tangent_order.rs › a_cone_whose_base_circle_lies_off_a_cylinder_wall` now also holds the union and the common part of the reduced drill tip (seam 1e-5 to 1e-3 from the crossing, three scales, square and turned three ways) to the touch rule regeneration applies: no touch. With the corner rule switched off the first case fails (a vertex 4.8e-13 from an edge at scale 1e-3).

### Faces that lie near one another (2026-10-22, the nightly run's two timeouts)

**Finding.** The nightly fuzz run of 2026-10-21 left two timeouts, both owed since 2026-10-21 (*The fuzzer's timeouts of 2026-10-20*, *What is left*): seed 20786729, a pattern of five copies of a pattern of five shelled tubes (cups of radius 10 turned a fifth of a turn about an upright axis) less a ball, and seed 20757788 again (36 shelled balls in one pattern).

**Measured** (the test profile on this machine, one session; stage times printed from the boolean, stack samples with `eu-stack`). 20786729's last pattern took 102 s, almost all of it four unions. In its union of 894 and 894 faces 224,436 pairs of faces had boxes that meet; 51,510 were passed by their hulls, and the other 167,811 were intersected, their edges cut against the other surface and their curves clipped (40 s of the boolean's 55 s), for 3,735 pairs that kept any piece of curve: the tubes' walls are pieces of cylinders turned off the axes, whose boxes are loose, and the surfaces meet everywhere in that cluster while the faces mostly do not. 20757788's last pattern took 29 s, 17 s of it the classification of regions: rays against balls, whose roots the numeric isolation found (`hits::numeric`), with a few hundred evaluations per line and ball.

**Method 1, covers** (`boolean/cover.rs`). A face's cover is a tree over its surface's parameters: each node is the box of a patch, the box of its four corners grown by `(hu² max|S_uu| + hv² max|S_vv|) / 8`, the error bound of bilinear interpolation along any direction (so every point of the patch is in it; the bounds of the second derivatives are closed forms per analytic kind), cut to the face's box grown by the tolerance (nothing of the face lies outside that box, nor does its boundary). A node is left out only when its patch lies wholly outside the face: no piece of the boundary comes within its box (each piece's distance proven, a line or a circle in closed form, any other curve by halving with its speed bound, 4,096 stretches at most and "may come near" beyond), so the boundary does not cross the patch, which hangs together and is all on one side of it, and its middle is outside the face (`Outline::side`). A node is halved along the direction in which it is longer in space, down to a 64th of the face box's diagonal or 16 levels. The first nodes (a quarter of the diagonal; a sixteenth once a face has been asked about 64 times) are made at once and kept in a tree of their boxes; below them nodes are made as questions need them. Every node is a function of the face alone. How far a question goes down depends on what was asked before (a face asked 64 times starts from finer first nodes, a pair gives up after 4,096 pairs of nodes, a tree makes at most 8,192 nodes below its first ones, which bounds a cover's memory to about a megabyte for the boolean that made it), but only towards "may meet" or "may hold", the answer without a cover: results are the same bit for bit in any order and on any thread. Freeform faces have no cover (their box stands for them, as before). Covers are used in three places, each only to skip what would have come out the same: a pair of faces whose covers are further apart than `APART` = 16 tolerances is not intersected (beyond every distance a rule of the section reads: two tolerances for faces over or touching each other, the outline's margin of four, with room to spare; a pair whose cover question takes more than 4,096 pairs of nodes is intersected); a point further than four tolerances from a face's cover is outside it, so the ray casting and the clipping of a stretch's middle do not ask the outline (`Side::Out` is what the outline says of a point further than the tolerance from the face); and an edge further than 16 tolerances from the other face's cover is not cut against the other surface (the stretches between the cuts that are left cross no boundary of the other face and hold a point outside it, so they are outside it whole and are dropped as before). New conditions, each with why it is general: the bilinear bound is a theorem for any C² parametric patch; the cut to the face's box holds because the face and its boundary lie in it; "no boundary within, middle outside" is the connectedness of a patch; the margins are tolerances beyond those the section reads. No decision reads a cover except to skip.

**Method 2, closed forms against balls and cones** (`hits::turning_points`, `levels`, `monotone`). Along a line or a circle the squared distance to a ball's centre is a quadratic, or `h + a cos t + b sin t`, which turns at its vertex, or at `atan2(b, a)` and the angle opposite; along a line the distance from a cone (`cos α ρ(t) − sin α z(t)`, less a constant) turns where `cos² α (A t + B)² = sin² α z'² (A t² + 2 B t + C)` and has no derivative where the line meets the axis. Between those points the signed distance is monotone, so each stretch is clear of the surface on one side, near it, then clear on the other side, at most: the runs come from where the distance is plus or minus the tolerance, in closed form for balls (the roots of `(R ± tol)²`, measured from the curve's point nearest the centre so that nothing cancels when the line's origin is far away) and by bisection for cones, which a monotone function needs no bound for; the cone's turning points likewise from the line's point nearest the axis. A root in closed form is used only where the distance there is the level to within twice the rounding below; anywhere else bisection finds it, so a closed form that loses digits costs time, never a crossing (review of 2026-10-22: from a line origin 1e5 radii away the root of "a tolerance inside" lay where the line was outside, and both crossings of a line dipping three tolerances into the ball were lost; `a_line_from_far_away_crosses_a_ball_or_a_cone_it_dips_into`, origins 1e3 to 1e7 radii away at three scales, failed before). A circle piece of more than 64 turns is left to the root isolation. A run is called clear only beyond the tolerance plus what rounding could be (64 units in the last place of the largest of the piece's points at its ends and turning points and the surface's centre and radius: the distance's evaluation rounds about 13 times for a ball and 20 for a cone, each relative to that size, `hits::ROUNDING`; the rule of *Tolerances* above, no decision on the sign of a number that could be rounding): without it the corpus case `near/cone-sphere/011`, a cone whose base circle is built to dip exactly one tolerance into a ball, read 1.4e-16 deeper than that, gave two crossings 2.4e-3 apart where the root isolation (which halves no stretch shorter than a quarter of the tolerance) sees one touch, and the piece of the base between them was too thin to classify, its three outcomes refused (the first corpus run of the change: 11,588 agreeing, under the floor). With it the outcomes of the `near` category for every pair with a ball or a cone (13 pairs, 823 outcomes) are those of the root isolation, case by case. The runs are then read by the rules the numeric isolation reads its own by (`hits_of_runs`, split out of it unchanged): a near run between clear runs on opposite sides is a crossing, on one side a touch, at an end of the piece a crossing only when it crosses on the way. This is the same answer from exact runs rather than halved stretches; it is not bit for bit the old one (roots in closed form, not bisected), so it is held to the numeric isolation in the tests and to the corpus. Ellipses against balls and lines against tori have quartic turning points (2026-10-22, *Quartic turning points* below); circles against cones and tori stay numeric.

**After** (the same session and machine): 20786729's last pattern 102 s → 42 to 46 s (pairs intersected in its 894 × 894 union 167,811 → 18,495; the boolean's section 30 → 8.5 s, the classification 10 → 5 s); its whole check by the fuzzer (`craft3d-fuzz check`, every oracle and the regeneration again) 128 s in the test profile. 20757788's (36 balls) last pattern 29 s → 10 to 12 s (the classification 17 → 4 s), its fuzzer check 36 s. The random pairs (`boolean_random`) give the same digests with covers as without (`c2c88a846b3003e0`, and `a52f6ad308b68880` with the closed forms; `2cefb5eeeb6c4b95` before them), in about the same CPU time (570 to 670 s against 620 s for the second, under a varying load).

*Held to.* The boolean corpus with covers and without the closed forms gives the digest it gave before, `895441d7261f1afe` (11,591 agreeing): covers change no result of it. `cover.rs` tests: every point of every face (interior points and points along every edge) of blocks, cylinders, cones, balls, tori and the trimmed faces of united tubes and hollow balls is in its cover, at three scales and turned; a point on a half cylinder's cut-away side is outside the cover; unions, differences and intersections of tilted tubes and of hollow balls are the same bit for bit with covers and without, with a third or fewer of the pairs intersected (49 of 179, 184 of 576; all of them before), at three scales and turned; blocks, a cylinder and a ball a gap from a block of −3 to 40 tolerances (across `APART`) give the same results or refusals with covers and without. `hits.rs` tests: lines and circles against balls and lines against cones of both nappes placed at random about a turned frame at three scales find what the numeric isolation finds (count, kind, place within the tolerance), more than a thousand hits; lines and circles passing a ball 3 tolerances outside miss it, within 0.6 tolerance either way touch it once, 3 inside cross it twice, a piece ending on it meets it there. `engine/tests/fuzz_findings.rs`: both designs replayed (ignored in the gate, a minute in the test profile), every feature built and the document's check clean.

*Open* (then; the next section took up all three). Ellipses against balls and lines against tori in closed form (quartics). The pairs that still meet in curves without keeping any (18,495 against 3,735 in the union above) would need covers finer than a 64th near both faces; tried at a 256th, slower overall. Freeform faces have no cover.

### Two sheets (2026-10-22, checklist item 5)

Decided by the developer on 2026-10-22 (was D-open-17): a sheet cut by a sheet acts as a cut by a solid does, and a union sews along shared boundary edges. Built in `boolean/two_sheets.rs`, with the section, the imprint, the regions and the sewing unchanged; `mod.rs` gains a mode (`Mode::Sheets`) and remembers, per arc, the stretches it was made from (the faces of the tool the arc lies on); `verify.rs` holds the result to rule 2 (a cut within the target's box, a union within both) and a union to rule 3 (faces far from the other sheet kept as they were).

*Subtract and intersect.* The target's faces are divided by every curve the tool meets them in (`face_regions`, with no curve held to dividing). A curve that divides nothing is refused: as *the tool ends inside the target* when one of its ends is reached by no other curve of the face (the tool's boundary passes through the face there), else as *a part of the target on both sides* (a ring cut once). Every arc the tool meets the target along is a meeting: the region beside it is in front of the tool when `n × t` (the target's normal crossed with the direction of the region's loop, pointing into the region within the target's surface) points along the tool's normal, behind when against it; at a fold of the tool both faces' normals are read and must agree; square to it (within a sine of 1e-6) the tool is tangent and refused. Regions joined by an arc the tool does not meet are flooded to one side; a region no meeting reaches is refused (*a part the tool does not cross*), as is a tool that divides no face (*does not pass through*: it misses or only meets the target's boundary) and a tool on the target's surface (any coincident pair: nothing tells front from behind there; owed if a model needs a tool lying partly on the target). The kept regions keep the target's surface and side; no face of the tool is kept, so the result's faces are named as copies of the target's (`F9:copy(0)/F5:startCap(…)`), its new edges as intersections, as in any boolean.

*Union.* Every stretch of the section inside a face of either sheet (they cross, one ends on the other, or on one surface one's boundary lies over the other) is refused (*meet inside a face*; *overlap* when the pair is coincident); coincident faces with no such stretch are tested by a point inside the target's face (`on_coincident`), so the same sheet twice is refused as overlapping. The rest are whole faces of both: their edge pieces between the same vertices along the same curve are one arc already, so the sewing joins them; an arc used twice the same way by the two sheets is refused (*face opposite ways*: no orientation holds both; the decision of 2026-10-18 keeps a sheet's normal as made, so the user turns one), three uses as a branch. Faces on one surface that share an edge merge as coincident faces of two solids do: two halves of a plate are the plate, one face of four edges.

*Held to* (`crates/kernel/tests/two_sheets.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, as built and turned about an off-axis direction): a plate of 4 × 3 cut by a wall at x = ½ facing +x and −x (4.5 and 7.5 in front, every vertex of each part on its side of the wall), by a tube of radius 1 (12 − π in front with two boundary loops, the disc π behind) and by a tool folded along an edge through the plate (made by a union, the corner 2.25 in front); a tube cut by a plane square to its axis (3π in front) and by one through its axis (halves of 4π); the B-spline trough `z = y²` (a ruled loft) cut by a wall (2.5 and 1.5 lengths of the parabola) and by a tube along it of radius ½ (inside it the arc between the roots of `y² + y⁴ = ¼`; outside, two strips, one part each); each both ways, as sheets of the target's faces only, facing as the target's did, meshes consistently oriented, with `area(front) + area(behind) = area(target)` within 1e-9 plus the integrator's bounds and the tolerance times the length of the cut (the curve on a freeform face is fitted). Unions: two halves of the plate (12, one face, four edges, one boundary loop), an L of a half and a shorter piece sharing part of an edge (10), a fold at a right angle (one of the wall's two sides sewn, 12, two faces, one loop; the other refused as facing opposite ways), two halves of the trough sewn along their spline (the whole trough, one loop), and two sheets apart (two shells). Refusals with their reasons and the faces named: a wall ending inside the plate, a sheet on the plate's surface, a wall that misses and one at its edge, a ring cut once, a target of two parts of which the tool crosses one, a union of sheets that cross, of a wall standing on the plate, of overlapping sheets and of the same sheet twice, of halves facing apart. The same cut and union twice are the same bodies, bit for bit. Through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/sheets.rs` › `a_sheet_cuts_a_sheet_and_two_halves_are_sewn`): fills cut by a fill (100 and 300 of 400, named after the plate's face), two halves sewn (400, one face), the refusals as failed features, saved and reopened, checked deep. The two sheets of `sheets.rs` › `what_a_sheet_cannot_do_is_refused` (on one surface, overlapping) now refuse as lying on the surface (cut) and overlapping (union) instead of *two sheets*: an expectation changed under the decision (logged).

*Review fixes (2026-10-22).* (1) A union that closes the sheet (six squares sewn one at a time into a cube) failed at the last step as an invalid body (`SheetClosed`). Now `two_sheets::closed_union`: when every shell of the result is closed and each encloses a volume above the integrator's bound it is a solid's shell and the body becomes a solid, faces, edges and names as sewn; a closed shell facing inward (volume not above the bound) is refused (*faces inward*), as is a union that closes some shells and leaves others open. (2) A tool standing on the target (a wall whose bottom edge lies across the plate) was cut as if it passed through. Where the tool meets the target with its own boundary alone (one face of the tool, every stretch along that face's boundary) across the target (inside a face, or along an edge two of its faces share) it is refused as *standing on the target*; along the target's boundary (a sheet hanging from the plate's edge) it only touches it there and is no meeting, so a wall beside it cuts the plate alone (it read as both sides before). (3) A curve that divides nothing is refused as *ends inside* when one of its ends lies on the tool's boundary and not on the target's (by the edges, within the tolerance), else as *both sides*; before, the count of curves at its end decided, so a folded tool whose second face stops inside the plate (two dangling curves meeting at the fold) read as both sides. A tool that meets the target only where the whole target is on one side of it (through the fold of an L, beside it) cut nothing or kept everything; now some arc must have the target on both sides of it, else *does not pass through*.

Held to (added to `two_sheets.rs`, three scales, turned): the cube from six squares facing out (a solid of volume 1 and area 6, a closed mesh, a block cut from it: 0.875), facing in (refused at the sixth union) and with a sheet apart (refused); the standing wall across and partly across, the hanging sheet beside a crossing wall (4.5 in front), the folded tool stopping inside (*ends inside*), the plane through an L's fold with the L on one side (*does not pass through*); a 4 × 4 plate with a hole of radius 1 cut across the hole (halves of (16 − π)/2, one boundary loop each), an L cut through its fold by x = z (floor 6 in front, wall 6 behind) and crossed by x + z = ½ (9 in front, two strips apart). Through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/sheets.rs` › `six_sheets_sewn_into_a_cube_are_a_solid`): six fills on datum planes sewn into a solid cube (8, 24, valid, an eighth cut away by a block), the inward cube's last union failed with the reason, checked deep.

Not changed: two solids, a sheet with a solid (nothing of their path is touched beyond the mode test); the corpus has only solids.

Still refused (owed): a tool lying partly on the target's surface; a union of sheets that cross (the point-set reading, not taken) or of sheets facing opposite ways along the shared edge (there is no command to turn a sheet over yet); a part of the target the tool does not cross.

### Sewing two sheets (2026-10-31, the class-A tank)

`feature.boolean {"op": "sew"}` (`boolean::sew_sheets`, `boolean/knit.rs`; format 59). A union of two sheets intersects every pair of their faces first, so that it can refuse sheets that cross; sheets that meet tangentially along an edge they share (a side held in curvature to a crown: the surfaces touch along the whole edge) cannot be intersected there and are refused ("tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area"). Sewing does not intersect: both sheets' faces go into one body (`a`'s entities first with their ids, `b`'s after them) and `craft3d_topo::sew` joins the free edges that run between the same places within the tolerance, used against each other (one orientation across the edge), proving every joined edge on both faces' surfaces over its whole curve, with the tolerance as both the sewing distance and the cap (nothing looser is recorded). The result's faces are the operands' faces in order (`FaceSource` one to one), an edge both had is `a`'s, so regeneration names them as any boolean's (`F9:copy(0)/…`, `F9:between[…]`). Refused: an operand that is not a sheet, an operand with loose entities (owed: sewing at the operands' largest tolerance), sheets that share no edge (also sheets that face opposite ways across it), a closed shell facing inward and a result the checker does not pass; a sewing that closes every shell into an outward volume is a solid (`two_sheets::closed_union`). Whether the faces also pass through each other away from the edges is not looked at by the sewing; regeneration holds its result to `selfcheck::crossing_near` as every boolean's. The deep check (`document.check {deep: true}`, `boolean::verify`) does not hold a sewn body to its operands as it holds a boolean's (volume bounds, seeded points, faces kept): sewing cuts nothing, so its result is held only by the sewing's own proofs (every joined edge measured on both faces), the checker and `crossing_near`; `boolean_check` in `engine/src/commands.rs` skips it. A T-junction (one long edge against shorter ones along it) is split by `topo::sew` and each piece keeps the long edge's source (2026-10-31). Held to (`crates/kernel/tests/sew_sheets.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6): a plate and the B-spline trough `z = y²` tangent to it along their edge (area `3 + 3 L(1)`, seven edges, one boundary loop; facing apart refused), six squares sewn one at a time into the unit cube (volume 1, area 6; facing in refused), a solid, sheets apart and sheets three tolerances apart refused, the same result twice bit for bit; through the commands (`crates/engine/tests/sew_sheets.rs`): the tank's crown and sides, where a union fails as tangent and the sewing's seams are G2, saved and reopened.

### Quartic turning points, covers of freeform faces, covers against the exact surface (2026-10-22, bool-speed-3)

**Quartic turning points** (`hits::quartic_turns`, `by_quartic`, `boolean/poly.rs`). Along an ellipse the squared distance to a ball's centre turns where `(b² − a²) sin t cos t − a p sin t + b q cos t` is zero, a quartic in `u = tan(t/2)` (taken on `|t| ≤ π/2` and on the other half turn, so that `|u| ≤ 1`); along a line the distance from a torus, `sqrt(D) − r`, turns where `D' = 0` (and has no derivative where the line meets the axis), whose roots are roots of `(k s + n)² (A s² + 2 B s + C) − R² (A s + B)²`, measured from the line's point nearest the axis or, where that lies beyond the piece (a line nearly along the axis), from the piece's end nearest to it. No root formula: `Poly::changes` finds where the quartic may change sign from the roots of its derivatives down (between two roots of the derivative it is monotone and changes sign at most once, found by Newton's steps kept in the bracket), and gives as well every place it looks at where the value is within its rounding of zero (each coefficient carries the size of what it was made of; 256 units in the last place of that, about six times the roundings counted). Extra places only divide the curve where nothing turns. The factor that is no turning point (`(k s + n) ρ + R (A s + B)`) only adds places; where both factors vanish (the point nearest the axis, and `z = 0` as the line turns towards the axis) a place is given. Then the same discipline as the closed forms of balls and cones: every stretch between the places is held to the distance itself (monotone at its quarters, to twice the rounding) or the pair goes to the root isolation; the places where the distance passes `±(tol + rounding)` and zero are found by false position (Illinois) and held to the distance like a closed-form root, bisection where they are not near enough; a piece whose stretches are proven clear by their middles and the speed bound (beyond two tolerances and 64 ulp of the sizes; eight halvings at most) is answered at once. A run near the surface that is no plain crossing and in which the distance changes sign (a dip of half to one tolerance through the surface) is a touch whose place is either zero, whichever a search happens on; such pieces go to the root isolation, so that they are placed as before (*Corpus regression and review*, below). *Held to:* the root isolation for 2,000+ random pairs (ellipses and balls, lines and two tori, three scales, turned; same count, kind and place within the tolerance; without the turning points the path is wrong or sent back in 100+ of them); grazing at ±0.6 and 0 tolerances touches once (and 2,400 random grazing lines and ellipses at ±3 tolerances, some ending at the place they graze, answer as the root isolation does), 3 outside misses, 3 inside crosses twice (an ellipse over a ball's top, a line over a tube's top, a line along the axis past the tube's inner equator); a line from 1e3 to 1e7 radii away dipping 3 tolerances into the tube, and an ellipse of that size dipping into a ball, cross twice, by this path (not sent back). *Speed* (release, 4,000 random pieces each, root isolation → this path): ellipses missing a ball 0.95 → 0.88 ms, meeting it 12.2 → 6.9 ms; lines missing a torus 2.5 → 3.9 ms, meeting it 44.6 → 17.0 ms; lines grazing a tube's top within 3 tolerances 154 → 8 ms (missing) and 563 → 73 ms (touching or crossing). Not bit for bit (roots by false position): the corpus digest changes; its per-pair table must not.

**Covers of freeform faces** (`cover.rs`). A freeform face is covered from the Bézier pieces of its surface, one first node per knot span: a piece lies in the convex hull of its control points (weights positive), so their box, grown by `1e-12` of the largest coordinate times the spread of its weights (heaviest over lightest) for the rounding of knot insertion and halving, holds it (some `1e-14` times the spread is what the de Casteljau steps can round; the box only grows by it); a node's halves are its piece split by de Casteljau's algorithm, cut out of the span's piece when the node is halved (a node keeps the index of its span, not a piece; the cover holds the pieces once). How long a node is comes from the longest row of its control polygon (a plane meets a Bézier curve no more often than its polygon, so by Crofton's formula the curve is no longer). A surface of more than 4,096 spans or a weight that is not positive keeps its box; a front holds at most 4,096 nodes. Everything else is the analytic cover's: nodes cut to the face's box, left out only when no piece of the boundary comes within and the middle is outside, used only to skip. *Held to:* every point of a domed block's roof (Bézier and NURBS), of the roof with a round hole through it and of the roof with a ball sunk in it, inside and along the edges, is in its cover at three scales, turned; a point of the roof's surface over the hole's middle is not (shrinking the box to the piece's corner points fails at once); unions, differences and intersections of the roof with a block under it, a ball sunk in it and a second roof crossing it are the same bit for bit with covers and without, and the block under the roof is no longer intersected (1 pair → 0).

**Covers against the exact surface.** Where two covers are asked whether they meet, a node further than the margin from the other face's surface (its signed distance at the box's middle more than half the box's diagonal, the margin and 64 units in the last place of the sizes) is that far from the other face and passed over, for analytic surfaces; two leaves that come within the margin of each other are each looked at in 4 × 4 parts, worked out and let go (the first 8 pairs of leaves of a question; each part 4 evaluations); an edge whose box is further than `APART` from the other surface is not cut (as first written it asked the other face's nodes against their own surface and never fired: review of 2026-10-22). All of it only proves more pairs further apart than `APART`, which is what passing a pair over rests on. *Held to:* boxes called clear of a plane, cylinder, cone, ball or torus are clear at their corners and at random points (a bound of 0.4 instead of 0.5 of the diagonal fails), and hold no point of a 192 × 192 grid on cones widening either way and of radius 0 (boxes about the apex), tori of major 1 and minor 0.99 (boxes on the axis), balls and cylinders (0.3 fails); the cover tests bit for bit with fewer pairs (tilted tubes 49 → 45, hollow balls 184 → 174, of 179 and 576; the pairs that keep a curve: 40 and 139). A union of two clusters of twenty tubes (198 × 168 faces): pairs intersected 1,286 → 1,229 (exact surface) → 1,168 (and parts), CPU 4.3–4.7 → 3.6–4.1 s in the test profile. The pairs of seed 20786729 that meet in curves and keep none (18,495 against 3,735) are not measured here: its replay is a release run (owed to the Strix).

**Found on the way** (`kernel/tests/boolean_tube_clusters.rs`; fixed the same day, see *A spline edge that meets a surface at a shallow angle*): four patterns of five tubes, the patterns 3 and 1.7 apart, united one after the other: the third union is refused, "an imprinted face could not be divided into regions; this is a bug" (`regions::trace` meets a use of an arc twice while following loops, so the order of the arcs around some vertex of the face is not a turn). The same at 3843969 and with covers off.

**Corpus regression and review** (2026-10-22). The Strix corpus at 76a3a99 agreed on 11,588 outcomes, under the floor of 11,591: `near/torus-dome/018` (a torus whose tube dips one tolerance through a dome's flat side along a circle), refused in all three operations as two faces going through each other by between half and twice the tolerance. The dome's edges in that face are lines that dip 0.99998 tolerance into the tube: one touch each, placed by `deepest` at either zero of the dip, and the quartic path's runs (exact level crossings) put the search on the other zero than the root isolation's halved stretches did; the vertex made there then read the faces as going through each other. Fixed by leaving such touches (a near run, no plain crossing, the distance changing sign in it) to the root isolation. Held to: every outcome of every corpus case with a torus or a ball (2,654 and 2,648 outcomes) is the outcome at 3843969, case by case; the grazing test above fails without the rule (a touch 0.012 rad from where the root isolation puts it). Review fixes besides: the edge skip above, the clear pre-pass beyond the rounding of the sizes, the ellipse's coefficient sizes from the centres' distances from the origin, the pieces' rounding from their weights.

### A spline edge that meets a surface at a shallow angle (2026-10-22, the tube clusters)

Found by bool-speed-3 (`kernel/tests/boolean_tube_clusters.rs`): four patterns of five thin tubes (radius 10, wall 0.3125) united one after the other; the third union was refused, "an imprinted face could not be divided into regions; this is a bug". Measured: the face was the bore of a tube of pattern 1, with a hole whose edge is a spline (where a tube of pattern 0 crossed it, fitted within the tolerance of the curve of the two cylinders). The tube of pattern 2 parallel to it has its axis 0.46 off the bore's, so its outer wall meets the bore at about 2.5° along two straight lines, which cross that spline. The spline met the outer wall 1.2e-6 and 8.1e-6 from those lines (a spline a fraction of the tolerance off its surfaces meets a surface at angle `a` up to that fraction over `sin a` from where the three surfaces meet); `section::clip` drops a cut further than the tolerance from its curve, so each line ran on through the hole from one side of it to the other, and the loops of the bore could not be followed (a use reached twice at the vertex where the line entered the hole). The same placement of three tubes reproduces it, and of 48 placements of a tube, a tube across its end and a tube beside the first a third of a unit off, 30 were refused before: as this bug, as a lost imprint, or as a curve partly on a boundary.

The fix (`boolean/corner.rs`, called from `section::edge_cuts`): a hit of a spline edge on a surface is taken to the point where the surfaces of the edge's two faces and that surface meet (Newton's method on the three tangent planes, from the hit, at most 32 steps), when that point is more than a quarter of the tolerance from the hit and within the tolerance of the edge; otherwise the hit stands. Every face pair that asks where that edge meets that surface gets the same point, so the vertex is one, on the meeting curve of each pair, within the tolerance of the edge. Exact edges (lines, circles, ellipses) are not touched: they lie on their surfaces, and their hit is that point already. Hardened after review (same day):
- *How far.* The point may be as far from the hit as the angle explains: four times the hit's distance from the edge's own surfaces over the sine of the angle at which the edge crosses the surface, at least 256 tolerances, at most 65536; a hit that would have to slide further is refused as too shallow an angle ("an edge on a spline meets a surface at an angle so shallow …", never an invalid body). A hit where the edge only touches the surface stands as found: there the three surfaces are tangent and meet in no point of their own, and Newton creeps along the band where all three are near to wherever its stopping rule halts (48.0 or 48.7 tolerances from the hit at a rolling fillet's end on a cone, `fillet_contact.rs`, which the first hardening broke: two points closer than the tolerance can tell apart).
- *On the surfaces.* Newton stops when the guess is within a millionth of the tolerance of the three tangent planes (what is left is rounding, which a step only magnifies where two surfaces are close to tangent: at 5.7e-4 the steps never fell below a millionth of the tolerance) or a step is that small, and the point is then measured within a quarter of the tolerance of each surface (a nearest point clamped at a patch border, or a local one, makes the tangent planes agree where the surfaces do not). The normals are made unit, so the bound on their determinant (1e-10) is free of scale.
- *In order.* A moved hit must stay on the edge strictly between the halfway points to the edge's hits on that surface on either side, so two hits never become one place or swap.
- *A crossing taken for a touch.* Two cylinders on parallel axes, the outer wall of one 1e-6 off the bore of the other (offset 0.312501 against a wall of 0.3125), cross along two lines 0.043 apart, but geometry gives one line along which they touch; imprinted as a touch, it ended at a point where the bore's hole touched its rim, and the regions were lost ("this is a bug"). A curve geometry calls a touch is now held to the rule for lenses too (`section::clip`): where the surfaces go through each other beside it by more than half the tolerance, the boolean is refused as faces at the edge of tolerance.

**Open.** No test reaches the refusal of a slide beyond 65536 tolerances: the tubes closest to touching inside are refused by the lens rule first. The tube beside a tube is held at ten offsets from 0.3124999 to 0.3126 at two places of the crossing tube: all unite and pass the full check but 0.312501 and 0.312502, refused at the edge of tolerance.

Held to: the four patterns and the 48 placements unite, each step under the full check and the boolean's own check (`verify`, volumes and seeded points), against an independent reference volume: horizontal slices of tubes whose axes lie in one plane are unions of rectangles, whose area is measured exactly (Green's theorem over the pieces of edges no other rectangle covers), integrated over the height by adaptive Simpson's rule in angles that take the square roots out of the widths; the reference holds a tube, two apart and two overlapping on one axis to their closed forms to 1e-9, and the kernel's volumes agree with it to 1e-8 (4682.08999 against 4682.08995 for the four patterns). Also 600 seeded points near the walls against exact membership, a watertight mesh, three scales and two runs bit for bit. Whether the corpus digest changes: to be run (a spline edge's hit that moved by more than a quarter of the tolerance changes the bits of its vertex, and a touch whose surfaces cross by more than half the tolerance is now refused).

### Patch trees of thin strips, and a budget for the trees of one operation (2026-10-23, the spur gear's memory)

**Finding.** `craft3d-cli check-range spur-gear.3dcraftjig --samples 16 --deep` (the Jig of an involute spur gear drawn from formulas, `sketch.curve`, patterned twenty to a hundred times) took 21.5 GB on the developer's laptop and was killed by the kernel's OOM killer, twice, taking the machine down. Reduced: one parameter, the hub's diameter. At the default (12 mm, inside the root circle) the gear regenerates in 0.1 s and 60 MB; from about 20 mm, where the hub's bottom covers the face the teeth end in, the union of the hub reads every flank, tip and root face of the teeth, and from 28 mm (wider than the tips) it took 2.3 GB and 18 s at twenty teeth (`hubD` 20 / 24 / 28 mm: 375 MB / 1.4 GB / 2.3 GB). Measured with a print in `PatchTree::shelved`: the union built 160 trees, 80 of 30 Bézier spans (40,950 nodes, 21.6 MB each) and 80 of 35 (11,935 nodes, 6.3 MB), 2.23 GB, all held at once: a boolean holds the tree of every freeform face it looks at (each `Outline` keeps its tree) until it ends, the shelf's 768 MB caps only what is kept afterwards, and each tree is capped (`MAX_LEAVES`, 32,768 leaves, about 22 MB) but their number is not. A hundred teeth make five times the faces.

**Cause.** A flank is a cubic strip along a fitted formula curve (`regen::curve_fit`: 32 spans to start with, more where the fit is refined), extruded the gear's width. A span is a hundredth of a millimetre to a tenth along the curve and the face's width (6 to 30 mm) across. `subdivide` quartered each piece until its box was a 32nd of the largest span's: the width needed five halvings, and so did the length, which needed none, so each span became 1024 leaves.

**Fix.** A piece is halved only across the direction it is long in when it is short the other way (`long_ways`: the longest row of its control net each way against half the leaf size; a node keeps two children then, `Kids`). A box holds the net and any two points of the net are joined by a row each way, so a piece whose box is above the leaf size is long at least one way: square pieces are quartered as before, and every leaf ends as small in space as before. Only in trees whose spans times `4^depth` exceed 4,096 (`HALVED_FROM`; five spans or more at the full depth, see below: at 16,384 the gear's 35-span faces, quartered four times, kept 6.3 MB trees and 300 MB on the shelf): halving every thin piece of every tree moved what smaller trees answer, and a fillet's boolean at 1e-3 (`fillet_contact.rs` › `a_straight_edge_rounded_by_its_tool`) was refused at the edge of tolerance; smaller trees are quartered as before and answer the same to the bit. The flank's trees: 30 spans, 960 leaves, 1,890 nodes, 0.9 MB. The gear with the hub over its teeth (twenty teeth, hub 30 mm): peak 57 MB and 0.4 s in release, from 2.3 GB and 18 s; eighty teeth, hub 110 mm, 30 mm wide, with `document.check --deep`: 509 MB and 7 s, where the build before was killed at a 3 GB cap; the volume assertion holds. The Jig's `check-range --samples 16 --deep` (81 points, the Jig as it was that evening, measured while every tree still halved): all pass, peak 656 MB, 5 min.

**A budget for one operation's trees** (`freeform::holding`, `HELD_BYTES`): `boolean_with`, `interference`, `verify`, `contains_points`, `classify_points` and `parts_touch` hold the trees they ask for (`PatchTree::shared`) to 2 GiB together (512 MiB on WebAssembly), each tree counted once by its nodes and leaves (`base_bytes`, a function of its surface alone, whether built then or found on the shelf, so the refusal is the same on every run). Past it the operation is refused ("the freeform faces it meets need more memory for their indexes than one operation may take") and no further tree is built. The refusal is the operation's, whatever its work made of it: some callers take a face they cannot outline as one that holds nothing (`contact.rs` › `faces_at`, `parts_touch`, `corner.rs`, the covers of `section.rs`), which would have turned it into fewer contacts or a wrong side, so `holding` marks the operation once a tree is refused and returns the refusal at the end even where its work returned `Ok` (`an_interference_past_the_budget_is_refused_not_answered_short`). What a tree remembers of the surface is remembered only where the surface decides it: an evened tree (`PatchTree::evened`, kept with the tree for every later operation) is not remembered as missing when the budget refused it, and one remembered counts against the budget of each operation that asks (`a_refused_evened_tree_is_not_remembered`); before, a refusal would have left later operations of the process with the uneven chart.

*What the budget bounds and what it does not.* It counts each tree's nodes and leaves, not what the tree remembers: its three memos (16,384 answers each) and its fine pieces (16,384 at most) are capped per tree, about 13 MB at their caps for a bicubic surface, and grow with what was asked (so they cannot be counted the same on every run). The trees of one operation can then take the budget and one tree, plus up to about 13 MB for every tree it holds where its questions fill those stores; the gear's runs stayed far below that (68 MB on the shelf for 80 trees). The shelf (768 MB) holds trees between operations on top. The budget is per thread: `document.interference` checks pairs of bodies on up to eight threads (`interference_cmds.rs` › `in_order`), each pair an operation of its own, so in the worst case eight pairs can each hold up to the budget. It is not divided by the number of threads, because a refusal would then depend on the machine's thread count; trees of the same surface are shared between the threads through the shelf. A browser (WebAssembly, one thread) refuses earlier than a native build: the same operation can be answered natively and refused in the browser between 512 MiB and 2 GiB of trees. The impeller of the showcase, the largest freeform body measured, holds about 450 MB: the browser's budget was first 384 MiB, which could have refused it there, and is 512 MiB so that it still works (the developer's decision, 2026-10-23).

*Expectations.* The boolean corpus on the commit that brought the halving and the budget (89b28013, merged on `work` as b93f33c1): every case succeeds, 11,591 results agreeing (the floor), and the digest of all results is 119e9ff6efa87ed3, the same as before. The random digests and the benchmark's counts were not measured apart; the gate's benchmark passed.

*When trees are halved.* `HALVED_FROM` compares the spans times `4^depth`, the most leaves quartering could make, not the leaves it makes (a piece stops when it is small): at the full depth that is five spans or more, so a surface flips from quartered to halved between four and five spans, whatever its shape.

Held to: `boolean/freeform.rs` › `a_thin_strip_along_a_fitted_curve_is_indexed_by_few_leaves` (30,720 leaves before, at most 1,920 now) and `the_trees_of_one_operation_are_held_to_a_budget` (refused at the sixth tree of a budget of five and a half, a tree asked for again counted once, nothing held after); `crates/engine/tests/gear_tree_memory.rs` (the Jig's build with a hub 30 mm across: both assertions pass, the shelf at most 128 MB; measured on the Strix, 80 trees built and 68 MB on the shelf, and on the code before, 160 trees built and the shelf full, 57 trees of 804 MB).

**Open.** A body of hundreds of genuinely two-way curved freeform faces (each tree at its cap of 22 MB) is refused past 2 GiB rather than handled; the trees could be built lazily, leaf by leaf, as questions need them.

### The sea kayak's booleans (2026-10-27, diagnosed, owed)

The hull is the intersection of generalised cylinders (extrusions of formula curves on datum planes turned about x), so every end plane `x = const` of one contains the extrusion direction of the others. `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` pins both as they are.

- *Imprint lost (repro 6, `Half1 = Cyl0 ∩ Cyl1`).* On Cyl0's end plane `x = Xb` (face 3, a quadrilateral) the section has two arcs: one from Cyl1's other cap, edge to edge, and arc 41, the line in which Cyl1's cap at +1500 along its extrusion cuts the plane, from the face's top edge to the point (y 1261.2, z 838.6) where that cap meets Cyl1's spline side, inside the face. From there the section must go on along the ruling of Cyl1's spline side that the plane cuts (the plane holds the extrusion direction, so it meets the side in straight rulings at the profile's crossings) down to the face's bottom edge; that piece is missing, arc 41 ends in the face, divides nothing and is dropped. The owed method: the section of a surface of extrusion with a plane that holds its direction in closed form (the rulings through the plane's crossings with the profile, an extrusion's plane section being a line), not by marching, where the field along the ruling is identically zero across it. *Built since 2026-10-27*: the piece was a crossing proven absent by the rounding of the segment bound (below, *The sea kayak's tips*), the only change that builds it.
- *Edge of tolerance (repro 6, `TipHalf1`).* The tips' cylinder and Cyl1 come within 0.5 to 2 tolerances of each other where all the traces meet the keel; refused with the edge-of-tolerance message, as designed. *Built since 2026-10-27*, by the same change. What repro 6 still refuses is the coaming's ring cut from the hull (`RingIn`: an intersection curve that cannot be fitted within the tolerance), owed.
- *A wrong intersect (repro 7, `TipHalfD`).* The deep check finds the point (−1864.42, −1469.54, −1233.82) outside the target, inside the tool and inside the result: the check, not the intersect, was wrong: resolved below, *The sea kayak's tips: a ray proven clear of a face it crosses*.

### Touching plates of the sea kayak (2026-10-27, diagnosed in r1-kayakgaps2, built in r1-kayakbool)

The sea-kayak Jig proves its plates share no volume by intersecting neighbours (repro 12: `Ov01`, `OvKeel`) and asks `document.interference` and the `overlap` assertion the same (repro 3, two plates on the hull's bottom and side panels joined by `touch` along their chine). Every one is refused; none is wrong. Measured on repro 3 (release, the Strix): the intersect and the interference take 119 and 123 s and end in "the intersection needs more evaluations than the cap allows" for the pair of the two touch rims (each a ruled spline of 605 and 608 spans, 5 m long and 4 mm wide, traced past the chine's ends by the corners' reach); the union stops at the same pair at once. All 40 million evaluations go to the seed search over the first rim's chart (`intersect::march::seeds`, fewer than 2,000 cells), in its searches for points where the surfaces are tangent. The two rims meet exactly along their common border, the plates' inner chine edge (each plate traces it as where the two toward skins and the station plane meet, so the two traces agree within a quarter of the tolerance), so the meeting curve runs along the border of both charts and the field is zero to the tolerance all along that border. `border_curves` takes a border as a meeting curve only when the whole of it lies on the field (64 samples), and these borders do over the chine but not over the reach past its ends, so the border is not recognised and the cell search meets a zero of the field all along it. Repro 12's intersects are refused at the edge of tolerance at the same contact (an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with). What was owed: a stretch of a chart's border that lies on the other surface taken as a meeting curve (its ends found where the border leaves the field), so that two bodies that touch along an edge they both have give an empty intersection or a touch, not a refusal; until then the overlap of such plates is "cannot be told", never a number.

**The method: a stretch of a border on the other surface is a meeting curve** (`intersect/march.rs` › `border_stretches`, `onto_stretch`, `lying_curves`; general, for any freeform chart against any field).

- *Stretches.* Each border of a freeform chart is sampled at 64 places; a place whose distance to the field `d` exceeds the tolerance skips the next `(d − tol) / (L Δt)` places (the field is a distance, 1-Lipschitz, and the border moves no faster than the chart's bound `L` on its speed: a proof for analytic and Bézier charts, an estimate for NURBS charts, whose bounds are sampled), so a border far from the other surface costs a handful of evaluations. A whole border within the tolerance is one curve over the curve's whole domain, sampled at the same places as before. Where the surfaces are tangent along it (a fillet on the face it rounds: no quarter point passes the crossing test below) it is exactly what it was before, with no band and never divided; where they cross along it, it has the band and the junctions of a stretch. That is a change for whole borders that cross: the kayak needs it: with no band beside whole borders, repro 3 is refused "an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with (at the edge of tolerance)" (measured twice on the Strix; the plates' faces, traced each for itself and continued past the chine's ends, agree only to the tolerance), and `a_whole_border_that_agrees_only_within_the_tolerance_meets_along_it_once` holds it. Otherwise every run of at least three consecutive places within *half* the tolerance is a stretch: its ends are found by 48 halvings between the last place on and the first off, it is held to half the tolerance at 63 places between them, and its curve is the border's own iso-curve (the row of the net, exact on the chart) over the stretch's range. A stretch must also be a crossing: at its quarter points the field is more than twice the tolerance away 64 tolerances across into the chart, and the least of the three sines measured so (distance over the way across, 1/32 or more) is the stretch's own, which sizes its band. (Where the chart is itself within the tolerance of the field all around, as near a cusp's point against a plane through its axis, the border is no meeting of its own.) Tangent stretches (sine below 1/32) are left to the cell search as before.
- *The cell search leaves the stretches out.* Along a stretch the field is zero to the tolerance, so the search of the border's edges for a change of sign split them down to the floor all along (on the synthetic strips below, 21 million evaluations for one pair; on the kayak's rims, all 40 million of the cap). Border edges are searched only outside the stretches, each part from the last node inside one, so that a branch that leaves the border where a stretch ends is seen.
- *The band beside a stretch.* Next to a stretch `f = 0` runs within what the stretch is off the field over the sine of the angle between the surfaces, and where two borders agree only to the tolerance (the kayak's two traces of one chine) it wanders across the border, in and out of the chart, all along. Within `2 tol / sin θ` of a stretch, with `sin θ` the stretch's own (measured across it, 1/32 at least; not the sine at the point asked about, which near a tangent feature would make the band widest where it should not be), and where the field is within the tolerance halfway across from the point to the border (so that `f` stays at zero between them: a loop, an arch or a dip of its own a few tolerances off the stretch has the field clear of zero between and is kept), a seed starts nothing, and a march that comes there ends, on the border, at the stretch's parameter there clamped to its ends (the place found by halving the last step): beside the stretch it is the stretch. The stretch is held to half the tolerance so that such a branch's last span, to a point on the border, stays within the tolerance of both surfaces.
- *No band near a pole.* Beside a border that is one point, and where the chart's speed away from a border has fallen below a thousandth of its bound, there is no band: near a pole every meridian comes as close to the border as to the others, and a band there ended every march to the pole (a cusp's meridians in a plane through its axis, `boolean_poles.rs` › `a_cusp_quartered_along_its_axis_is_what_its_profile_says`, "imprint lost" until this rule).
- *Junctions.* A marched branch that ends on a stretch (the surfaces go on crossing past what they share, or another branch meets the border there) divides it at that place, so the curves meet only at their ends, as the imprint needs. Whole borders along which the surfaces are tangent are never divided (as before).

*Held to:* `crates/geom/tests/meet_border.rs`: two thin ruled strips (degree 3 × 1, 2.4 long and 0.01 wide, twisted) that share the middle of their border exactly where the end control points' basis functions vanish, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, each order: parting past the stretch (one curve, nothing past the few tolerances over which the borders still agree), crossing on past it (the curve goes on to where the second strip's ruling meets the first, located by bisection), borders that agree only to 0.12 of the tolerance either way, and a plane through the middle of a strip's border (parting and crossing); every curve within its deviation of both surfaces, the stretch covered by exactly one curve, no two curves together but at their shared ends, and none shorter than four tolerances (no two-point piece where a branch meets a stretch's end); also a slope through a strip's border bumped out of it by about 40 tolerances over a stretch of the border, whose arch between the two stretches is kept (the review's case for a band sized at the point: a branch a few tens of tolerances off a stretch dropped or clamped), and a plane crossing a strip's border at an angle of 5e-6, whose curve goes on into the strip below, the two strips' curves following the plane's line all along. Before: 70 to 113 s and 21 million evaluations per pair, and the plane refused. `crates/engine/tests/touch_along_a_border.rs`: two humps extruded from a Bézier and its mirror image, back to back, one taller, so the taller wall's border lies on the other's surface only up to the lower height; at three scales, each taller in turn, the intersection is empty, the difference is the target to 1e-7, the overlap assertion passes; moved into each other by half a unit the intersection is the lens between the curves times the lower height (`∫ (d − 2x) y′ dt` by Gauss, itself checked against a sum of strips). (These pass on the marcher before as well: the extruded walls end where their faces do, and the box of the pair keeps the cells within the shared height; they are the volume oracle, not the failing case.) `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` › `plates_that_touch_along_their_chine_share_no_volume` (repro 3): every step passes, the interference is a touch, the intersection empty.

*Measured* (test profile, the Strix): repro 3 every step in 15 to 18 s (the intersect and the interference were 119 and 123 s each, refused); repro 12, all 735 steps in 256 s, `Ov01` and `OvKeel` empty (no overlap), `Ov3D` not reached because the plate `Top4S` fails to trace a rim within the tolerance ("4.712e-5 off", a plate finding, not the boolean's). Repro 12 is a timed check, not a test (too long for the suite).

*Open.* A partial stretch along which the surfaces are tangent (two walls that continue each other only over part of a border) is not taken; the boolean corpus gives the same results bit for bit (digest `119e9ff6efa87ed3`, 11591 agreeing, as on main).


### The sea kayak's tips: a ray proven clear of a face it crosses (2026-10-27)

The sea-kayak model's `TipHalfD` (the tips' stock, an intersection of extruded formula curves, cut by the deck's cylinder: each operand a ruled spline face of degree 3 × 1 beside flat ends and sides, 5.3 m long and 3 m thick) was built, and `document.check` deep called it wrong: the point (−1864.42, −1469.54, −1233.82) outside the target, inside the tool and inside the result. Reduced to its two operands (`TipHalf3`, `CylD`), the result was right: a mesh's winding number agrees with it at every seeded point, as with the three booleans before it. The ray casting was wrong, and wrong about the tool alone (a body of six faces): the ray from that point crosses the deck's spline face 2432 mm on, and the root isolation (`hits::numeric`) proved the whole ray clear of the face at once by `PatchTree::segment_lower_bound`, which read the distance from the ray to a flat leaf it passes through as 1.5e-5 mm instead of zero. `segment_box_distance` evaluated each piece's squared distance as the expanded quadratic `qa t² + qb t + qc`; where a coordinate's offset `α + β t` is zero (the ray at the side of a box) that is `α²` less `α²` again, and with `α` the 2600 mm from the ray's start to the box the rounding is `√(ε · 2600²) ≈ 4e-5` mm, above the tolerance of 1e-6 it was compared with. A leaf of a planar piece has a box of no thickness, so the piece of the ray inside it is a point, and only that rounding stood between the ray and the face. Five per cent of the points seeded in the box of the deck's cylinder were classified wrongly; a boolean that had classified a region by such a ray would have been wrong, not refused.

The fix (general: no case, no tolerance): the squared distance on each piece is the sum of the squared offsets, each `α + β t` evaluated as it stands, at the piece's ends and at the vertex `−Σαβ / Σβ²`; the rounding is then that of the coordinates (5e-13 of the segment's length at most, measured). Held to: `boolean/freeform.rs` › `a_segment_through_a_flat_box_far_from_its_start_meets_it` (flat and nearly flat boxes 1000 units across, segments up to 5200 long through them, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6: at most 1e-11 of the scale; up to 4e-5 before), and `crates/engine/tests/boolean_findings.rs` › `the_sea_kayak_tips_against_the_deck` (the four features and the deep check; the found point outside the tool; at 1e-3, 1 and 10 times the size (the steps in micrometres, millimetres and centimetres), intersection and difference, their volumes summing to the target's, the full verify with 2000 seeded points each, and 400 points against the ray casting and against the winding numbers of the operands' and results' meshes; at a million times the size the hull would be 5000 km against 1e-6, beyond what any geometry holds). The point bound (`box_distance`) has no such expansion.

### Faces that lie on one another over a region, and surfaces side by side (2026-10-28, the sea kayak's `Ov3D`)

The sea-kayak Jig's last overlap check (repro 12, `Ov3D`: the sheer plate `Top4Sh` intersected with the deck `Deck4Sh` over it) was refused, "the surfaces are tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area to intersect (face 1 of the first body, face 4 of the second body)". Measured on the Strix: face 1 is the sheer plate's inner skin (a degree 5 × 5 offset fit, 1036 × 10 control points) and face 4 the deck's `over` rim, which lies on the sheer plate's *outer* skin (the hull's side, degree 3 × 1): two surfaces that never meet, a plate's thickness apart over five metres. The pair the task named as coincident (the sheer plate's `under` rim on the deck's inner skin) was already taken for one surface by `lies_on`. Two changes, each general; the first is what `Ov3D` needed, the second is what a pair whose surfaces part beyond the faces needs.

**Surfaces side by side** (`intersect/march.rs` › `Marcher::bounded`, `clear_by_bound`; `meet.rs` › `numeric`). The cell search leaves a cell out where the field's value at its centre exceeds the cell's reach (the distance moves no faster than the chart). Beside a surface `d` away, with the gradient of the field along the chart near zero, no cell is simple either, so cells shrink until their reach is below `d`: a 5 m × 3 m chart against a gap of 6 mm needs more than the 1,000,000 cells allowed. Where the cells run out, the intersection is marched again from the start with a second rule: a cell is also left out when `|f(c)| > 1.01 (|f_u| h_u + |f_v| h_v + ½ (M_uu h_u² + 2 M_uv h_u h_v + M_vv h_v²)) + tol` (Taylor's theorem about the centre `c`, with the half-widths `h` and the bounds `M` on the second derivatives of the field along the chart that the simple-cell test already uses: the field's bend from `Field::bend_at`, the chart's from its bounds). Only where the field is the signed distance throughout: the centre's `|f|` equal to its distance, and against a freeform surface the feet of the centre and the four corners not on its border (past a border the field is the distance to the border, with a bend of its own).
