The rule is a proof where the walked chart is analytic or a Bézier patch and the field analytic (their bounds are bounds), and an estimate where the chart is a NURBS surface (its bounds are sampled) or the field freeform (its bend is estimated from the projector's grid), as the simple-cell test is. Asked only where the first rule ran out of cells, so every intersection traced before is traced bit for bit as before. Held to: `crates/geom/tests/meet_side_by_side.rs` (a bicubic skin against itself lifted by 2000 tolerances: nothing; against itself lifted and tilted by a thousandth, so that they cross along an iso-curve: the curve on both surfaces and on the iso-curve within the tolerance over the sine, as long as the iso-curve to 1e-4; at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6); both refused without the second rule, measured on the Strix; each asserts through `intersect::bounded_marches` that the first march ran out of cells and the second answered), and a finer skin side by side whose upper surface dips through the lower one in a small closed loop where one control point is pulled down by three times the gap: the loop is kept, one closed curve, every point where the two heights agree.

**Faces that lie on one another where one is over the other** (`boolean/coincide.rs`; called from `section.rs` when `meet_surfaces` fails for a pair with a freeform face). `meet_surfaces` takes two surfaces for one when a 9 × 9 grid of one lies on the other everywhere in the box it is asked in (samples whose foot is on a freeform border left out). Two freeform faces can lie on one another over the whole of where they overlap while their surfaces, continued past the faces, part: a plate's rim traced past its ends, one skin fitted twice over different ranges, a patch that agrees with another only over its first spans. In the box the surfaces are then neither one nor two that cross, and the marcher refuses. What the boolean needs is that the *faces* are one wherever one is over the other: the region they share is then bounded by edges of the faces, which the faces beside them imprint as for coincident planes, and it is classified as coincident with the same or the opposite normal by the existing rules (`on_coincident_at` reads a point of the region against the other face, not the other surface). `coincide` samples each freeform face of the pair over the parameters its loops span (65 places per boundary piece projected, grown by a fiftieth), eight places per knot span each way and at least 32 pieces each way, 2^18 samples at most:

- a sample is *over* the other face when it is inside its own face and its foot on the other surface is inside the other face (the other face's cover proves most feet outside at no cost);
- every sample over the other face must be within a quarter of the tolerance of it, and the largest of them plus an eighth of the largest second difference of the distance along a row or a column must be within half the tolerance (the most a function with that second difference rises above the chord between samples; the same half tolerance within which the gap rule takes faces over each other for one surface): sampled, and between the samples an estimate of the kind the NURBS chart bounds are;
- at least 16 samples must be over the other face (an area, not an edge both share), and the surfaces' normals at every one of them (not only the first few) must all agree or all be opposite.

Then the pair is coincident (`SurfaceMeeting::Coincident`) and everything after it is as for any coincident pair (the gap rule, the imprint by the neighbouring faces, the classification, the merge, which does not join faces on two different freeform surfaces). When samples over the other face are within the tolerance and others, also over it, are apart, the faces share a region whose boundary is no edge of either (the surfaces part inside both faces): refused, "two faces lie on one another over part of where one is over the other and apart over the rest" (`coincide::PARTLY`). Anything else (too few samples over the other face, a face too large to sample, a side that cannot be told) is no evidence and the pair keeps the refusal it had. Only after a failure, so nothing traced before changes.

*Held to:* `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_coincide.rs`, hexahedra over rectangles of the parameters of one bicubic B-spline skin whose x and y are linear in u and v (so the walls are planes), each face on the skin given the skin itself, the skin cut down to the face and continued over the whole domain by its end spans (equal over the face, apart past the next knots), the same transposed or with u reversed; at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, every result valid and passing the full `verify`: two plates stacked on the lifted skin, touching over the rectangle both cover (intersection empty both ways, union one solid of the summed volume, each difference the target, all to 1e-8 of the closed form `h L W |R|`); two plates side by side on the skin overlapping by a known rectangle (intersection, union and both differences to the closed form); a block under the skin and a second block on a higher floor whose roof is the skin parted and reversed, sharing part of the roof (volumes from the skin's integral by Gauss–Legendre per span, exact for the cubics); the same skin in two other parametrisations, whole (passed before: a regression guard); and a roof equal to the skin over one span and bent away past it, refused with `PARTLY`; and the review's negative case, a flat rational patch whose middle control point is lifted by ten tolerances with a weight of 10⁴ (pulled into a spike, a dent ten tolerances high) on a plane and on a coarse flat patch: refused, or the union keeps the dent as a cavity, never taken for faces on one another. Without `coincide` four of the five are refused ("tangent or nearly coincident", "surfaces that touch along a curve"), measured on the Strix. `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` › `the_sheer_plate_and_the_deck_over_it_share_no_volume` (repro 12 trimmed to the 263 steps `Ov3D` needs): `Ov3D` is "the result is empty", the interference of `Top4Sh` and `Deck4Sh` a touch with no overlap, their union the sum of their volumes and the difference the sheer plate, to 1e-6. `Ov3D` passes with `Marcher::bounded` alone (measured with `coincide` switched off); `coincide` is not on its path.

*Open.* Coincidence is sampled, not proven between samples (as with `lies_on` and the border stretches); a proof would bound the difference of the two surfaces' second fundamental forms over each cell. A pair of faces that share a region bounded by where the surfaces part inside both faces stays refused (it needs that curve traced as the edge of a region of contact).

### The sea kayak's plate overlaps and inner hull (2026-10-28, repros 16 to 20)

The Jig proves its plates share no volume with the `overlap` assertion, one pair of neighbours at a time (repro 16: the garboards either side of the keel; 17, 18: planks above one another and the sheer plate; 19: the deck plates either side of the centreline), and builds the inner hull as the half hull cut by its mirror image (repro 20, `InHull`). With the full steps of the Jig, 16 to 18 pass on main (the touching plates of repro 3 and 12: *Touching plates of the sea kayak*); 19 and 20 were refused. `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` › `neighbouring_plates_share_no_volume`, `the_inner_hull_is_the_half_hull_cut_by_its_mirror_image`.

**A face's box from the part of each edge's curve it is** (repro 19: "the box has no size, or the tolerance is finer than rounding allows at its size"). The deck plate's end face on the stern plane is 17 mm across, but one of its edges is a stretch of a circle 860 m in radius (the deck's crown, nearly straight there), and a face's box (`Hull::bbox`) holds every circle and ellipse an edge is a piece of whole, and every control point of a spline edge's curve. Its box, and so the pair's room, was 900 m wide, at which size `Marcher::new` cannot hold a tolerance of 1e-6 mm (64 ulp of the box's size against a fiftieth of the tolerance) and refuses. The method: `Hull::near_bbox`, a box that holds each edge by the part of its curve it is (its ends; a piece of a conic by the polygon of its pieces of at most a quarter turn and the corners where their end tangents meet, which holds it exactly; the part of a spline by the control points of its Bézier pieces cut to the edge's range by de Casteljau's algorithm, `NurbsCurve::hull_points`, the same polynomial over a shorter interval and so within their hull, weights positive), and the surface as before. It is a bound for any face, as the box is. Where a pair's surfaces cannot be met in the room of the boxes (after `in_room`'s closer room), they are met in the room of the near boxes when that is smaller (`section::meet_in`), and where the near boxes do not come within twice the tolerance of each other the faces do not meet. Only on that refusal, so that every pair met before is met the same (the corpus bit for bit).

**A branch divided where it leaves the other surface** (repro 20: "an intersection curve could not be fitted within the tolerance"). Two of the hull's ruled spline faces meet along a curve that leaves one face's surface over its edge at a shallow angle (1.5 degrees) and comes back. The field of a freeform surface goes on past its edge (as its tangent continuation), and a march looks at the ends of its steps: one step passed over the stretch past the edge. The refinement then put points there, 0.03 mm past the edge, that no span through them could bring within half the tolerance of the bounded surface, and the fit was refused. The method (`intersect/march.rs` › `refine_pieces`, `edge_between`): where a point the refinement puts between two of a branch's points is past the edge of the field's surface by more than half the tolerance (its distance to the bounded surface beyond its distance to the surface continued), the branch is divided there: the piece before ends, and the piece after starts, at the last point from each side still on the surface (halving in the chart's parameters, each point brought onto `f = 0`), as a march that met the edge ends there, and each piece is refined on. A closed branch so divided is open, its first piece continuing its last. A piece traced already from a seed of its own is not added twice. Only where refining refused before (no span through such a point is within the tolerance), so nothing fitted before changes.

*Held to:* `crates/geom/tests/meet_past_edge.rs` (a flat patch and a vertical wall whose lower border dips below the patch but over five narrow bumps, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6: one curve per stretch between the bumps, each ending where the border crosses the patch, every point on both bounded surfaces; refused on main, Check run 40669662008); `crates/geom/tests/hull_points.rs` (sampled points of parts of a spline with a control point 1e6 away and of a rational circle inside the hull points, the far point left out, the whole domain giving the control points themselves); `boolean/outline.rs` › `the_near_box_holds_each_edge_by_the_part_of_its_curve_it_is` (a sliver under a stretch of a circle 1e5 across and one under the first span of such a spline: near boxes about their own size, boxes a thousand times larger); the kayak tests above (repro 19 passes, the inner hull built, what is left of the half hull outside its mirror image and the inner hull summing to the half hull, the deep check clean).

*Open.* The march's steps still look only at their ends: a stretch past the edge shorter than the refinement's samples is not seen (as before; the section's clip by the faces' outlines cuts it away).


### The kayak Jig at its own tolerance: the seam of a closed curve (2026-10-29, repros 21 to 24)

The sea-kayak Jig makes its documents at a tolerance of 0.01 mm (its `new.json`), and every repro filed before ran at the default 1e-6 mm: so repros 12 and 16 to 19 passed while the Jig, at the same steps, was refused. At 0.01 mm the overlap of the drilled sheer plate and the deck (`Top4Sh`, `Deck4Sh`, at the defaults: repro 21), of the plank under the sheer plate with the Fine and the Full ends (`Plank2Sh`, `Top4Sh`: 22, 23) and of the garboards of a single-chine hull (`Plank0Sh`, `Plank0Ph`: 24) were refused, "two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart". Measured on the Strix (a print at each place that refuses so): all four at one place, in `clip`, two cuts of one closed meeting curve 1.0 to 2.0 tolerances apart: the curve's seam (where a closed spline that is not periodic starts and ends) and a place where an edge meets a surface, an edge of the other plate crossing the hole's wall (22 to 24) or the vertex of the hole's rim, an edge lying on the other plate's skin (21). The curve is a hole's wall meeting the plate beside it along the hole's rim; its trace started next to the rim's vertex, found there only to the tolerance.

**The method** (`section::ends_at_places`). An end of an open curve next to a place has been that place since the first freeform booleans; the seam of a closed spline was left out, as no stretch may run across it. But the seam is no place of its own, only where the trace happened to start: it stays a cut, at its own parameter, and next to a place (within the guard, twice the tolerance) that the curve reaches from the seam without leaving it (the curve halfway between them within the guard of the seam, from its start or back from its end) it takes that place's point and edges, the nearest such place. The place and the seam are then one cut. A place near the seam in space that the curve reaches only round the loop is not taken. General: it reads no kind of surface, no size and no configuration, and uses the guard the vertices already use.

*Held to:* `boolean/section.rs` › `the_seam_of_a_closed_curve_next_to_a_place_is_that_place` (a closed cubic round a circle, a place 0.8 and 1.5 tolerances from its seam on either side taken, one 2.5 away not, one near in space halfway round not, an open curve's end as before; at three scales); `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` › `the_drilled_sheer_plate_and_deck_touch_at_the_jigs_tolerance`, `the_sheer_plate_and_the_plank_under_it_touch_with_fine_and_full_ends`, `the_garboards_of_a_single_chine_hull_touch_at_the_jigs_tolerance` (the overlap assertion passes and the interference is a touch; all three refused on main, Check run 40895304853). `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_coincide.rs` › `holes_through_a_plate_under_another_on_one_skin` (twelve holes drilled through the lower of two plates stacked on one skin, at tolerances up to 5 % of the plates' thickness: the intersection empty, the union the sum) is a guard that passes on main too: the marcher there starts its loops away from the rims' vertices, so it does not reach the case; the kayak's tests are the failing ones.

*Open.* Whether the seam lands next to a place depends on where the marcher seeds a closed branch; no synthetic solid was found that makes it do so (the unit test holds the rule itself).

**A chart's two directions at one pace** (2026-10-29, repro 25). A point of a freeform face is told in or out by the winding number of the face's loops about it in a chart of the surface, its own parameters (`chart.rs` › `turn`): each boundary piece is halved until each stretch stays within a disc that does not hold the point, the disc's size from the curve's speed times the chart's, and the chart's speed is one over the least singular value of the surface's Jacobian, the pace of its slower direction. On a strip far longer one way than the other (the kayak's plate rims, 4 mm by 5 m) a boundary along the long side moves the chart a thousand times slower than that bound says, and a winding took some 6000 steps a piece, 0.15 s for each point told. Now the chart's tree (`PatchTree::evened`) also draws u at the pace of v where the two paces (the net's mean polygon length over the width of the knots) are more than 16 apart (`NurbsSurface::paces_matched`): only u's knots are stretched, the point set and the net are the same, and a winding is the same number in any chart of the same point set. General: it reads no kind of shape, only the parametrisation's own paces; a winding that ran out of steps (`MAX_TURN_STEPS`) or out of the operation's work on such a strip can now finish. *Held to:* `geom` › `a_long_strip_is_drawn_with_both_directions_at_one_pace`; the kayak's repro 25 (thirteen minutes, now 2.4 s with its regeneration).

### A plane through a node of the marcher's grid (2026-10-31, the planetary gearbox)

*Finding.* The planetary-gearbox assembly of the Jigs measures the carrier's turn by the centroid of planet 1's section at its mid-plane (`sectionCentroid`, `kernel::below`: the planet intersected with a block whose top face is the plane z = 5 mm). Over a whole turn of the sun, one of 65 moments (t = 0.78125) was refused, "imprint lost" on the block's top face: 18 of the section's 108 pieces were missing, one for each tooth gap's root. The root is a short cubic spline (1 mm across, 35 spans) extruded 10 mm along z; at that pose the planet's bottom lay at z = -7.9e-16 mm (its pose, turned and moved, rounded it there), not at 0.

*Cause.* `geom::meet_surfaces` of the root's wall and the plane gave nothing. The marcher walks the wall's chart (u along the root, v up the wall) in cells; at the root's width the chart is one column, so every cell's sides across v are borders of the chart, whose edges are searched by `border_seeds`. The distance to the plane is linear in v and the plane passes exactly through the grid's nodes at v = 1/2. A border edge is left out as clear of zero when the distance at its centre exceeds its reach (its length times the chart's speed along it); for the edge below the node that distance *is* the reach, a tight bound, and the centre's point, rounded a hair further from the plane (4.374999999999999 instead of 4.375), made the edge clear. The edge above counted the node's zero as positive (the test of a change of sign is `f >= 0`), as was the far end, and showed no change. No seed, no curve: the flanks beside it, three columns wide, had inner edges whose reach includes the other direction's term and were found. A decision taken on the sign of rounding, which the Method forbids.

*Method.* A cell of `seeds` and an edge of `border_seeds` are clear of zero only when the distance at the centre exceeds the reach by more than `on`, the marcher's resolution of the field (`max(1e-4 tol, 64 ε |p|)`, how close to `f = 0` a marched point is brought): `Marcher::clear_of_zero`. A zero on a node is then within reach of both edges that share it, and the edge whose far end has the other sign shows the change. `field_border_seeds` already kept such a margin (`1.001` of the reach plus the cut). The root isolation of a curve against a surface (`intersect/curves.rs` › `numeric`) left an interval out by the same strict test and now reads it the same way (`clear_of_surface`: more than a ten thousandth of the tolerance or 64 ulps of the coordinates and the reach, whichever is more), found by the review of this change. General: no kind of surface, no size, no configuration; it only stops reading a rounding as a proof. What it costs is cells and intervals within that margin of their reach looked at instead of left out, and those now give the seeds and hits they should have.

*Held to.* The rule itself, whatever rounding a geometry meets: `march.rs` › `a_cell_whose_zero_is_on_a_node_is_not_clear_of_zero` and `curves.rs` › `an_interval_with_a_crossing_on_its_end_is_not_clear` (a centre at the reach, or a few ulps beyond it, is not clear; one a tolerance beyond is; at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6). The planet's case: `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_grid_node.rs` › `a_plane_through_a_node_of_the_grid_cuts_a_narrow_spline_wall`: a tooth gap's root (a cubic spline 1 wide, dipping 0.12, its control points at their Greville abscissae, closed by three lines) extruded from a hair below z = 0 to 10, cut at z = 5, for eight hairs of rounding's size at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6: the prism's volume, `measure.below`'s volume and section against closed forms from the profile (the area w² − w Σ cᵢ (t_{i+4} − t_i)/4, x being linear in the parameter), and with the block below the plane A ∩ H + A − H = A, each against its closed form, and A ∪ H = H plus the part above, every result valid. Before the fix 5 of 195 cases of a sweep of 65 hairs were refused "imprint lost", at all three scales (8e-16 and the planet's own 7.9e-16 at 1e-3 and 1, 9e-16 at 1e6); the test keeps those and five others. `crates/geom/tests/meet_grid_node.rs` sweeps a wall linear in v against a plane through a node of the grid, and a straight cubic against a plane through a node of its sixteen first intervals, for 64 hairs at three scales; both are guards only: a sweep of 9000 such placements (walls and straight cubics, random heights, offsets and boxes) lost nothing before the fix either, since whether a centre rounds past its reach is a matter of the last bit, which is why the rule is pinned by the unit tests above. The planet itself (exported at t = 0.78125 to STEP and read back) cut at z = 5: refused before, 2497.555 mm³ below and a section of 499.511 mm² after.

### A crossing closed in on (2026-10-31, r1-proofperf)

Found by profiling the thickness of the spline ring of `jig_sample.rs` (a revolved spline, a rational face; [`verification.md`](verification.md) › *The proven thickness*, Cost): of a measure's 7.4 s, 5.2 s were lines against that face, about 1.4 ms each, in `hits::numeric`. Per line 46 signed distances (13 µs each, a Newton search in the patch tree), 47 bounds below a point, and 8 bounds below a whole stretch (about 50 µs each; 18,010 of 18,931 failed). The cause: halving towards a crossing, each stretch beside it took a distance and was first offered to the bound below the whole stretch, which works through its allowance of pieces before it fails.

**What changed** (`hits::numeric`):

- *A window about the middle, kept with the halves*, against a surface with an index (freeform). With `d` the index's bound below the distance at a stretch's middle (asked up to 0.9 of the distance there), the curve cannot come within the tolerance while it stays within `(d − tol) / speed` of the middle: the reasoning that clears a whole stretch, applied to part of it. The stretch is halved as before; the window (narrowed by a relative 1e-9 and by four roundings of the parameter) goes with the halves and their halves, and a piece that lies within a window of a stretch it was halved from is clear on that middle's side without a look. Beside a crossing, where each piece took a distance and often a failing bound below the whole stretch, the pieces now cost nothing. The halving is the one it always was, so the runs near the surface are the ones it always made. A first version cut the window out of the stretch and divided only what lay beside it: fewer looks still, but the runs' ends moved, and near-tangent passages at the edge of tolerance went from answers to refusals (corpus `near/torus-dome/018`, three outcomes, while windows were cut for analytic surfaces too; `fillet.rs` › `a_rib_that_touches_the_round_below_it` at 1e6 and `fillet_contact.rs` › `a_straight_edge_rounded_by_its_tool`, Checks 41227843892 and 41231095994). Not for the analytic kinds, whose distance is cheap.
- *The bound below a whole stretch first only where a window would leave most of it* (a window under half of half the stretch): beside the surface for its length, what that bound is for, and not beside a crossing. Always on a piece too short to halve, which would otherwise be called near for its length alone (review of 2026-10-31: a stretch the bound would have cleared whole, with its ends between the tolerance and an eighth more, would otherwise come apart into near pieces of the finest length). What it skipped above is so cleared piece by piece; a piece whose ends the bounds at a point (a quarter of the tolerance fine) cannot tell from the tolerance stays near, where the whole stretch might have been cleared at once, and its hit is dropped (the distance there is above the tolerance), so the hits are the same.
- *The side of a clear run next to a near one, looked at* (review of 2026-10-31; `hits_of_runs`): a piece cleared in a window carries the sign of the middle it was cleared about, and the signed distance to a patch that ends can turn over beside its rim while staying clear of it. Where a run's piece next to a near run was cleared in a window unevaluated, the distance is looked at in that piece's middle (where halving would have looked), and its sign decides crossing against touch; every other run keeps the sign of the middle that cleared it, as before. Read at the near run's very end instead (the first version of this), a fillet's tool turned from a body into a refusal (`fillet_contact.rs` › `a_straight_edge_rounded_by_its_tool`): within a few tolerances of a patch's rim the sign is noise.

Not kept: a crossing's root by false position instead of halving (fewer looks, roots a few roundings elsewhere): `fillet.rs` › `a_rib_that_touches_the_round_below_it` failed at 1e6 with it (Check 41235641073) and passes without. With the halving unchanged, a run differs from before only where a piece inside a window would have been halved down to the finest length and called near for that alone (a run near the surface that is not; its hit is dropped either way, `|f| > tol`). The boolean corpus gives the same digest as before, `61ed3e127b07d809`, and the same 11,594 agreeing outcomes (Check 41244303993): bit for bit on all of it.

Not kept: stopping the patch tree's Newton walk at a step that moves neither parameter (7 % more on the ring) could end a walk an ulp from where it ends now.

*Held to:* `hits.rs` › `closed_forms_find_what_the_root_isolation_finds` (1200 random lines and circles against balls and cones at three scales), `a_line_through_a_ball_is_settled_in_fewer_evaluations` (new: both crossings to 1e-9 of the scale with an index's bound given; 198 distances and 51 bounds before, 157–159 and 60 now), the kernel and engine suites and the corpus.

### Contact without its area (2026-11-01, `contact.rs` › `contact`)

`contact(a, b, tolerance)` answers clear, touch or overlap as `interference` does, without the area faces on one surface share, which `interference` measures as half of (boundary of a + boundary of b − boundary of their union): a union and three bodies integrated. A touch whose area only that union could measure, and which the union refuses, is a touch here rather than an error. Test: `contact_alone_agrees_with_interference`.

### An edge that ran on past its vertex (2026-11-02, `section.rs` › `clip`, `nearest_to_vertex`)

*Finding.* The union of a cylinder of radius 5 along x (x from 17.5 to 27.5, axis through (y, z) = (30, 7.5)) and one of radius 12.5 along y (y from 30 to 35, axis through (x, z) = (17.5, 15)) was refused by the self-check after the boolean: "the result cannot be meshed to see whether it passes through itself", face 3 (the larger cylinder's wall) "the boundary crosses itself in parameter space" with both moved by (1000, −700, 300), face 0 (the smaller's wall) "the surface turns away from the chords of edge 1" at the origin. The body the boolean made was valid and its volume right (2893.4579, the closed form 2893.457918).

*Cause, measured.* The axis of the small cylinder lies in the large one's base plane, and its wall touches the large one's top plane along the line z = 7.5. Four faces meet at (27.5, 35, 7.5): both walls, the small one's end disc and the large one's top. The curve in which the walls meet has two cuts there: where the large cylinder's top circle meets the small wall, which it runs onto tangentially (the wall is tangent to the circle's plane), so the hit is placed along the circle only to 8.7e-8 (0.09 tolerances; at (27.500000052, 35, 7.50000007)); and where the small cylinder's end circle meets the large wall square, exactly. The two are one cut, and the merged cut kept the parameter of the first along the curve, the tangential one. The vertex is the exact point (a vertex of the small cylinder's end circle). So the edge on that curve began 8.7e-8 before its vertex, past the end plane x = 27.5, outside both faces it bounds, and came back. On the large wall the curve leaves the vertex tangent to the top rim (y = 35 − 10 Δθ²), so within 1e-8 rad of it the two are one line to the last digit: the overshoot laid the edge's first chords over the rim's, and on the small wall across its end circle.

*Method.* A stretch of a meeting curve ends where the curve comes nearest its vertex. When the vertex is not the point of the cut (cuts were merged, or a vertex of an operand or of an earlier section is there), the end is moved to the nearest point of the curve to the vertex (`outline::nearest`) within its room: from halfway to the cut before to halfway to the cut after (or the curve's own end; round a closed curve, a period on), so that it passes no neighbouring cut and the two stretches that share a cut move their ends alike. It moves only to a point nearer the vertex than the cut's parameter gives, on the same passage of the curve (the curve's point halfway between stays within the guard of the vertex, as `ends_at_places` tells a place at an end: the room is a stretch of parameters, not of space, and a traced curve that loops back near the vertex must not give the end its other passage), and only when the start stays before the end; otherwise the stretch is as before. A closed curve with one cut (the whole loop from the cut back to it) is not moved. The classification of the stretch (its middle, quarters and passage) is taken before, from the cuts, as before; only the range the edge is given changes. Where the vertex is the cut's own point (compared exactly), nothing changes, bit for bit.

*Held to* `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_axis_in_base.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, at the origin and moved by (1000, −700, 300), and with both cylinders as placed and turned about their own axes twice (then no vertex of either operand is where the four faces meet, and the vertex is made from the merged cut's own point, 1.7e-8 off the end plane; that body meshes and is clean too): valid and held to the boolean's own check in full, the volume within 1e-6 of the closed form (Va + Vb − Vi, Vi = 62.5 π plus a one-dimensional integral taken by Simpson's rule; found 1.7e-9 off), checked against a grid of 25.6 million points (0.014 % off), watertight meshes at three chords with their volume, and the mesh self-check clean; and every edge's curve nearer its vertex at its end than a little way in (the cause itself). Both failed before the change (the first at the mesh, the second at 0.09 tolerances against 1e-4 a little way in). `section.rs` › `an_end_is_moved_along_its_own_passage_only`: a closed spline whose end comes back to the vertex, nearer to it than the cut's passage, at three scales; it fails without the passage rule.

*Measured* (Strix; main beaac44e against the branch with the passage rule, Checks 41515442101 and 41515413467, each result of the corpus printed on a line of its own with `BOOLEAN_CORPUS_CASES`). The corpus gives the same table (11,594 agreeing, 228 refused, none wrong or invalid); its digest moves from `61ed3e127b07d809` to `7927eb7a078af68e`. 54 of 9,859 results differ, all three operations of 18 pairs of the category *flush* (block-cone 015; block-cylinder 002; block-dome 003, 007, 014; block-spline 002, 006, 018; cone-dome 007, 018, 023; cylinder-spline 014; dome-dome 001, 003, 017; dome-spline 002, 014, 018), and they differ only where their edges run: the shape without edge ranges (vertices, loops, faces, shells, sources), the counts and the volume to twelve digits are the same. `mesh_hashes` (441 meshes) is the same; of `mesh_digests` the engine block's three STL exports change (the same sizes, so the same triangles, their points moved), every other digest is the same. The work counts of the benchmark models are unchanged (Check 41509301597).

*Not this cause.* Four fillets and chamfers refused by the same self-check, "its loops do not bound a region: they enclose no area": a round or chamfer that uses up a face exactly (two R3 rounds on a 6 wide end face, two 2.5 chamfers on a 5 tall face, two R10 rounds on a 20 wide end, an R35 round as long as both inside faces of an L). The blend left the used-up face in the body as a face of no area; the mesh was right to refuse it. Built since 2026-11-13: the face goes and the faces on its two sides meet along the line ([`blends.md`](blends.md) › *Faces a blend uses up exactly*).

### The section's numeric meetings met ahead (2026-11-04, `section.rs` › `warm_meetings`)

*Finding*: the fuel tank's shell (`feature.shell` of 1.2 mm, no open face) spent 24 s in its subtraction of the hollow, 18 s of it meeting three freeform faces with their own offsets one after the other (about 6 s each, numerically, to find that they do not meet). *Fix* (reworked after review, 2026-11-05): helper threads from the process's pool (`craft3d_math::pool::lease`, none on a thread that works alone) meet the section's freeform pairs a few pairs ahead of it (`Ahead`, four a helper), so the section finds each on the shelf of numeric intersections, where a hit is the answer marching gives, refusals included, and charges the work the march took. The helpers test the pairs with the section's own tests (`to_meet`, the one place of them) on covers of their own: a cover's answers depend on the questions it was asked before (its finer front after `OFTEN`, its cap of `NODES`), and the section's covers are asked exactly what they are asked without helpers, so a cover at its cap answers the same on one core, in the browser and under a bound (held by `section.rs` › `pairs_met_ahead_change_nothing_the_section_gives_or_asks`: results and the covers' histories equal with helpers and without). They use only outlines already made: the section makes those of the pairs within the lead on its own thread, where the trees an operation holds are counted. A pair the section reaches while a helper meets it waits for that helper; past a refusal at most the lead was met for nothing. Not under a bound on the work (`allowance::scope`), where what an operation charges depends on what it asked before; not in the browser (one thread); not for operands without a freeform face. Each helper holds one thread of the pool and gives it back once no freeform pair is left ahead of it; a helper that panics is joined and its pair met by the section (`a_helper_that_panics_leaves_the_section_its_result`); after a refusal the section waits for a helper in the middle of a meeting, so the refusal can come that much later: since 2026-11-16 the helpers are called off once the section ends (`allowance::stopping_when`: a pair stops at its next question for a bound, as at its own bound, and keeps nothing), so it waits only for a march under way (a march cannot be stopped part way and asks for no bound). *Known edge case*: the section makes the outlines of the pairs within the lead a few pairs early (it stops at the first outline that fails), and the lead grows with the helpers it got; near the budget of trees an operation holds (`freeform::HELD_BYTES`) an outline made early can make a different face the one the refusal `TOO_MUCH_HELD` names, depending on how many helpers there were. Only a boolean refused anyway is concerned. The subtraction now takes the longest pair, 6 s; the shell 31 s instead of 45 s. Still serial and the larger share now: the hollow's offset (17 s), of which `tweak::buried` classifies its points one after the other (10 s) and the freeform faces are offset one after the other (5 s), both under the offset's bound on the work: sharing them out needs a replay of what each thread charged, `once` included (the way `boundary_integrals` replays its looks at the budget).

### A line along a ruling of a narrow cone (2026-11-14, `hits.rs` › `ruling`)

A sibling of the plate's smooth corners ([`plates.md`](plates.md) › *Corners*, *Limits*). `ruling` decides a line along a ruling through the apex by the apex's distance from the line plus the angle between the line and the cone (from the axis) times the reach to the apex, against a quarter of the tolerance. The angle from the axis was the `acos` of their dot, off near 0 by up to ε over the half-angle (1.5e-8 at most), and the reach to the apex is the radius over the half-angle's tangent, so for a half-angle below about 3e-5 rad the `acos`'s error alone times the reach was more than a quarter of the tolerance: the ruling was not recognised and the line was left to the root isolation, which has no bound on a cone's bend. The angle is read from its sine and cosine now (`Vec3::angle_to`). Held by `hits.rs` › `a_line_along_a_ruling_of_a_narrow_cone_is_on_it` (half-angles 1e-7 to 1e-4 either way, a tilted frame, three scales; the `acos`'s reading would have missed some). The boolean corpus is the same bit for bit (Heavy run 43550137755: digest `7927eb7a078af68e`, 11594 agreeing, as the nightly run of that morning on the same boolean code): no corpus cone is that narrow.

### A crossing left out: the nearest point of a face whose tangents differ millions of times (2026-11-14, `freeform.rs` › `descend`, `hits.rs` › `hits_of_runs`)

**Found** by the seeded hunt of plates (`hunt_sheet_plates.rs`, case 123): `classify_points` placed a point 6600 tolerances outside the planar rim of a plate inside it. The plate (a rounded rectangle 54.667 × 20.261 with corners of radius 10.126, 6.286 thick, at scale 1e6 in a turned frame 2.6e8 from the origin) is right; in that frame its short side's rim is not the plane it is elsewhere but the ruled spline the plate traced (`plate::ruled` keeps the plane only where the spline's control net lies within a quarter of the tolerance of one), `u` the length along the side and `v` from 0 to 1 across the 6.286e6 of the plate.

**Cause.** The first ray from the point crosses that rim at `t` = 8433.8. The root isolation found the crossing (a near run between a clear run on either side) and then dropped it, because the distance it read at the crossing was 190 and not 0: `PatchTree::descend`, the damped Newton iteration behind every nearest point of a freeform surface, had ended 190 short of the foot along `u`. Its damping was in units of the longer of the two tangents (`|∂v|` = 6.286e6 against `|∂u|` = 1), so even the least damping (1e-12) held the step along `u` to `1 / (1 + 1e-12 · 6.286e6²)` = a fortieth of Newton's, and its forty steps halve the distance to the foot at best. The smallest case is a flat strip 1e5 by 3e6 in a tilted frame at the origin, `u` its length and `v` from 0 to 1 across it: a point *on* the strip was reported 67.2 from it. Nothing in it is about the frame or the distance from the origin; those only decided that the plate's rim was a spline. It takes tangents a few million times apart: from a ratio of about 2e6 the error passes a tolerance, below 1e5 the first steps settle.

**How wide** (`crates/kernel/tests/classify_far.rs`: blocks, blocks with ruled sides, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori, plates with a rounded outline and a hole, unions and cuts of those, each with its field in closed form; points 2.5 to a million tolerances to either side of faces, edges and vertices; scales 1e-3, 1 and 1e6; the world's frame and turned frames up to 5e8 model units from the origin). Before the repair, of 636 000 points beyond the reach of the boundary 12 094 were placed on the wrong side, all at scale 1e6 and all on solids with ruled faces (blocks with ruled sides 12 066, plates made by `plate` 28), in every frame, the world's included, and at every distance (3158 within 10 tolerances, 1769 to a hundred, 3488 to ten thousand, 3679 beyond); none at 1e-3 or 1, and none on an analytic solid at any scale or distance from the origin. That is this generator's, not a rule of size: its ruled sides' `u` runs over a length of at most fifty times the scale and their `v` from 0 to 1, so only at 1e6 do their tangents differ by millions. What decides is that ratio, at any size: a pin 11 mm across whose wall is a B-spline on knots stretched by millions, as a file from another modeller can bring, was built wrong at the default tolerance (*Review* below). The other hunts did not see it because none has such a face: `hunt_common` stops at scale 1e3, where a ruled face's tangents differ by thousands, and a plate's rims are planes, cylinders and cones but for the rare straight side kept as a spline (one case of some 21 000 plates).

**The booleans stand on it, and were wrong.** `curve_hits` is where an edge of one body is found to cross a face of the other (`section.rs`) as well as where a ray is (`classify::inside`), and the chart of a freeform face reads a point's place from the parameters of its nearest point. Measured (the same file: three booleans of each of eight kinds of pairs, held to the closed form of the shared volume where there is one, to `V(A ∪ B) + V(A ∩ B) = V(A) + V(B)` and `V(A − B) + V(A ∩ B) = V(A)`, and to the same boolean of the same solids in the world): of 288 booleans at scale 1e6 in a turned frame 2.6e8 from the origin, 153 were right, 132 refused ("imprint lost", "an edge shared by faces that do not pair up", "produced an invalid body", twice by the boolean's own check) and **3 built wrong**: valid solids that are not the result, all of a block with ruled sides and a block that shares a corner of it (a union 12.5 % and a subtraction 15 % over their closed forms, 1.2418e23 for 1.1041e23 and 1.0526e23 for 9.149e22; a union 1.8 % under, 8.645e22 for 8.805e22: the first union is both blocks whole and the subtraction the first block untouched, as if the two did not meet, and the last union is the first block alone, the part of the second that stands out of it gone); the same three with the frame at the origin, and the same counts on `release/0.1`. At 1e-3 and 1 every boolean of these solids was right, their faces' tangents there within fifty times of each other; on faces of those sizes whose knots are stretched the booleans were wrong as well (*Review* below). In round numbers (`a_block_through_a_ruled_side_of_a_block`: a block 30 by 50 by 50 times 1e6 whose side at y = 50 is ruled, and a block 16 by 40 by 16 standing in it and out through that side) all three were refused. By command, the plate of the finding with a ball through its short side and a plate with a spline in its outline with a block through that rim (`crates/engine/tests/classify_far_commands.rs`) were right before the repair as after: the commands make such a face rarely, and an import brings one at any size (the pin of the *Review*, below).

**Repair.** (1) Where the first forty steps of `descend` end short (sliding the foot along its tangents, within the sides that hold it, would still bring it nearer by more than sixty-four last bits of the coordinates; the first steps end where no step brings the foot nearer by one last bit of the distance, so a foot they settled on is never taken for short) the iteration goes on, for at most forty steps more, with each parameter damped in units of its own tangent, which does not depend on how the parameters are scaled. A tangent that is rounding alone (at a pole, along the border that is one point) is no direction to go on in. Where the first steps settle nothing is added and the foot is the one it was, bit for bit. (2) `hits_of_runs` no longer leaves a hit out on the word of the search alone: a near run is a place no bound could clear, and where its distance reads as more than the tolerance it is held against the bound below the distance; where the bound shows a piece of the surface nearer than the search found, by more than the bound's own resolution, the answer is that the meeting is not known (`MISSED`), which refuses a boolean and makes the classifier abandon the ray for the next. So the classifier cannot count a ray that passed a crossing it could not read: the crossing is counted, or the ray is not used.

**After** (Check 43637462668, the same sweeps): of 651 716 points beyond the reach of the boundary none is placed on the wrong side and none is unknown (3224 solids: 70 more are built, their unions and cuts no longer refused); the 576 booleans at scale 1e6, at the origin and 2.6e8 from it, are all right and none is refused, the three that were built wrong among them (`the_booleans_that_were_built_wrong_are_their_closed_forms`). Counted and not this finding's: four points beside a cone's rim or apex are called on the boundary 2.4 to 3.9 tolerances from it (beside an edge the classifier's "on" reaches √5 tolerances: twice from the face's surface and once past its boundary; beside an apex further); 5e6 model sizes from the origin 480 of 858 booleans (those in which a freeform face meets another face) are refused, before and after, with "the tolerance is finer than rounding allows at its size" (the numeric meeting of two surfaces, `craft3d_geom::intersect::meet`), none wrong. 5e9 from the origin, where a coordinate's last bit is a tolerance, 59 of 168 solids are not built at all (the primitive fails the checker), and of 14 305 points one is still placed on the wrong side, 4564 tolerances inside a block with ruled sides (284 before): there the coordinates do not hold the solid.

**What moved.** Nothing that was right. The boolean corpus is the same bit for bit (Heavy 43633396591 on the base and 43637460111 on the repair, and again after `main` moved under the round, 43644845911 on `main` and 43644848487 on the repair merged with it: digest `7927eb7a078af68e` each time, the same row of totals), and on the surfaces of `freeform.rs`'s tests one search of 5280 changes, for the nearer: a point on a lune beside its corner pole, where the tangents are nearly parallel, read 9.2e-8 from the surface it lies on and now on it (`the_steps_taken_on_change_a_search_only_for_the_nearer`). That holds of those surfaces, not of every search. One descent from a leaf can only come nearer than its first steps did, but `search` descends from the leaves in the order of their boxes and stops at the first box no nearer than the best foot so far (and after `MAX_LEAVES`), so a foot that came nearer can leave a later leaf undescended from which the first steps would have ended nearer still: in the review's sweep (below) 47 of 450 360 searches ended farther than with the first steps alone, 22 by less than 0.03 tolerances and 25 by up to 9.5e3 tolerances where both answers were wrong (weighted sheets 1e6 to 1, *Owed* below).

**Cost** (the Strix, one thread, the wide sweep's 56 500 points per kind): a point of a block with ruled sides took 2.40 ms to classify before and 1.41 to 1.73 ms after, with sides that reach past their faces 6.15 ms and 3.66 to 4.55 ms: a search that ends short leaves more leaves to descend from. Solids without a freeform face are untouched (a block 2 to 3 µs, a plate 0.1 ms). `classify_far.rs` took 98 s of the gate's 8017 s of test-binary time (Gate 43644850826; about 50 s alone on one thread).

**Review** (2026-11-15: means of its own, with the base `main` adfdafb4, the repair and `release/0.1` 21348721 each built apart). Solids whose curved faces are the exact rational NURBS of a cylinder, a cone, a sphere, a torus or a block's sides, on even knots or stretched ones (the knots of `u` or of `v` times 3e7, of `u` times 1e9, one parameter at unit speed), each held to the closed form, to its analytic twin's own booleans and to the two identities; for the nearest point, points `q + offset` with `q` on the surface. **Booleans**, 972 (nine kinds of pairs, six parametrisations, scales 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, the world and a frame 260 sizes out): before the repair (the base and `release/0.1` line for line) 336 right, 406 refused, **85 wrong and 70 suspect** (a result against two of its measures, or against the only one it had), at every scale and in both frames; after it 960 right, 12 refused (*Owed* below), none wrong or suspect; on even knots 162 of 162 right before and after, with the same bits. **The classifier**, 81 837 points 2.6 to 3e4 tolerances off the boundary: 21 319 on the wrong side before (26 %, all on stretched faces; at scale 1 in the world 2042 of 8887), none after, none unknown. **The nearest point**, 450 360 searches (twelve kinds of surface, fourteen pairs of stretch and aspect, three scales, three frames): 211 219 wrong before, 7796 after, all of them of the kinds under *Owed*; on stretched parameters of an ordinary shape none of 225 162 after (104 803 before). `MISSED` never fired, so no right boolean is newly refused and no point left unknown; 126 ordinary bodies (domes, spline cones and extrusions, NURBS on even knots and analytic solids, at three scales and in three frames) gave the same bits on all three builds. The boolean corpus's only freeform faces are domes whose tangents differ by three times at most, so its equal digest shows ordinary freeform booleans unmoved and says nothing of the repair.

*The example a user meets* (`classify_far_commands.rs` › `a_block_through_an_imported_pin_whose_wall_is_on_stretched_knots`): a STEP file whose pin, r 11.3412 and h 32.2413 mm, has for its wall the exact rational B-spline of the cylinder on knots of `u` times 3e7, imported at the default tolerance (`file.import`), with a block through its side (`feature.block`, `feature.boolean`). Before the repair the subtraction gave a valid body of 13028.08 mm³ for 12060.03, the pin untouched, and the union (*"the result passes through itself"*) and the intersection (*"the result is empty"*) were refused (Check 43669015955 on `release/0.1`; the same on knots of `u` times 1e9 and of `v` times 1e-7); on even knots all three were right. After it all three are their closed forms on every stretch, the even file's bodies. Commands alone built no wrong body: the faces they make reach the ratio only past the size the kernel accepts for its tolerance.

**Open.** The chart of a freeform face reads a point's place from the parameters of its nearest point without holding the distance found against the tolerance: a search that ended short would misplace a point there unheard (the bound of (2) guards the crossings only). The plate keeps a straight rim as a ruled spline, continued 1400 times the side's length, where its control net rounds off the plane ([`plates.md`](plates.md)): a representation the frame decides.

**Owed** (measured by the review and after it, 2026-11-15; each pin states today's result and fails the day its case is repaired, to be held to the truth then and taken off). *The nearest point of long weighted sheets:* a sheet whose weights run from 1e-3 to 1e3 and that is 30 to 1 or longer in space is still missed, also for points that lie on it (the review: 7169 of 35 280 searches, the worst by 6e6 tolerances; 22 266 before the repair). Pinned on the plane drawn so (`freeform.rs` › `the_nearest_point_of_a_long_weighted_sheet_is_missed_owed`): 1000 to 1 on knots of `u` stretched a thousand times, 8 of 81 points on it missed by up to 9377 tolerances; a million to 1, all 81, by up to 6.1e5; square, none. What the booleans make of it, on blocks whose sides are such sheets: one 30 by 900 was refused before any boolean, because the checker read an edge of it as 1.65 from its face (the projection it asks, `Surface::project`, ended short on that face where the patch tree finds the edge on it); repaired on 2026-11-15 in the projection ([`geometry.md`](geometry.md) › *A foot that ends short*), it passes the checker and its union, intersection and subtraction with a block through three of its sides are built with the faces, edges and vertices of the plain blocks' booleans (`classify_far.rs` › `a_block_whose_long_side_is_a_weighted_sheet_is_valid_and_its_booleans_are_built`), right by the review's polygon oracle; owed: none of them can be meshed (`TooFine`, 0.6 to 10.5 million segments a side) or measured (as below), and the boolean of another seed of the same block (the review's seed 4) ran for 44 minutes in the chart of its freeform faces (`PatchTree::sigma_search`, made inside `allowance::uncounted`, so no bound sees it; a boolean sets none: *A boolean that runs for most of an hour* below), where `main` refuses the operand at the checker; those 30 by 30 and 30 by 90 (eight seeds, a block over a corner, the three operations) were built with the faces and vertices of the plain blocks' booleans (to 1e-5) or refused (*"a freeform face whose parametrisation stalls or folds"*). None was found wrong, but none could be measured either: the mass integration takes weights at most 1e4 apart within a span, and the tessellator would need millions of segments a side; an intersection with three such sides took up to five minutes. *Tangents a millionth of a radian apart:* a parallelogram whose sides lean a millionfold, on knots of its short side shrunk a millionfold, is missed in a turned frame (the review: 500 of 2520 searches, as many before; pinned, `the_nearest_point_of_a_sheet_whose_tangents_are_a_millionth_apart_is_missed_owed`: 37 of 81 points on it, by up to 28.5 tolerances). Sheets a million to 1 in far frames are missed by at most 2.1 tolerances (127 searches; their size is 3e13 tolerances), not pinned. *Cones on stretched knots whose results are not measured:* the 12 booleans the review counted as refused after the repair are cones whose side is on knots of `v` stretched 3e7 times (or at unit speed at scale 1e6), with a block over the tip or through the middle: the boolean builds them, but their volume is not taken (refused on the base too). Six such at scale 1, in the world and 260 sizes out, pass the checker with as many faces as the analytic cone's boolean, but the mass integration says a face *"is not bounded by its loops"* and the tessellator would need some 13 000 segments a side, so whether they are right is not known (pinned, `classify_far.rs` › `booleans_of_a_cone_on_stretched_knots_whose_volume_is_not_measured_owed`). *The cost of a face whose speed rises along it:* ruled sides whose speed along `u` rises from 2e-6 of their width to twice it cost 1.6 to 2.6 s a point on the build server, leave many points unknown (4 of 8) and a boolean with them was refused or not done in 150 s (the base not measured); from 2e-4 (the pin, `a_point_beside_a_ruled_face_whose_speed_rises_ten_thousandfold_costs_millions_owed`) a point three tolerances below such a bottom is placed right but costs 18.6 million units of work (`craft3d_geom::allowance`) where at even speed it costs a few ten thousand.

### A boolean that runs for most of an hour: the fine pieces of a weighted chart, and no bound on the work (2026-11-15, `freeform.rs` › `PatchTree::finer`, `craft3d_geom::allowance`; the store and a bound repaired 2026-11-16)

**Found** by the review of the nearest point on weighted sheets ([`geometry.md`](geometry.md) › *A foot that ends short*): the intersection of the long block of `classify_far.rs` (30 × 900 × 20, its sides facing +x, +y and +z the plane as bicubic NURBS with weights from 1e-3 to 1e3, the review's seed 4) with a block 27 × 765 × 16 through three of its sides ran 44 minutes (debug at opt-level 3), and past 90 s under `allowance::within(5e6)`. **The smallest case** is the square block of the same file, 30 × 30 × 20, seed 4, with a block 27 × 25.5 × 16 at (12, 10.5, 9): it passes the checker in 0.01 s, and its intersection runs past 120 s on `main` 0f1665c9, on `release/0.1` 21348721 and with the repair of the nearest point, and past 100 s under `within(5e6)` (debug at opt-level 3, the laptop, one run at a time). Seeds 1 to 5 (60 s each): the square block's 1 is built in 51 s, 2 and 5 are refused in 0.6 s (*"a freeform face whose parametrisation stalls or folds"*), 3 and 4 run past 60 s; the long block's the same, its 1 built in 54 s.

**Where the time goes** (stacks sampled with `eu-stack`, every sample the same: `section::to_meet` → `Solid::cover_in` → `Cover::new` → `cover::divide` → `Outline::side` → `Chart::winding` → `Chart::turn` → `PatchTree::sigma_near` → `sigma_search` → `finer_in` → `quartered`, inside `allowance::uncounted`, → `fine_piece_with` → `piece_bounds` → `RationalBezierPatch::weighted_normal_net`; counted by instrumenting the searches, the instrumentation taken out again). Each step of a winding asks for the least singular value of the chart near it (`sigma_near`). A search splits the pieces of the leaves within reach where a piece's bound is more than `SIGMA_SLACK` below what its corners show, up to `FINE_LOOKS` (1024) looks a leaf, and keeps the splits in the tree's store of fine pieces (`FINE_PIECES`, 16 384): one store per tree, shared by every operation and every thread that asks the tree. On these faces one search adds some 30 000 pieces (5700 to 9500 quarterings, 0.5 to 0.8 s): the store lets go of what the next search needs, and of what the section's helper needs (`Ahead`, which meets the pairs ahead of the section and asks the same trees at the same time), and those pieces are made again, uncounted. Measured on the square block's seed 4, the helper's first 4000 searches: looks and counted units the same whatever the store's size (2 138 383 looks, 4 633 993 units), but 215 445 quarterings in 19.4 s with the store at 16 384 pieces against 34 488 in 3.3 s at 131 072. The intersection is built (6 faces) in 16.2, 9.2 and 8.4 s with stores of 65 536, 131 072 and 524 288 pieces, 2 633 278 units counted each time. At 131 072 pieces the square block's seeds 1, 3 and 4 are built in 8.6, 8.5 and 9.2 s and the long block's 1 and 3 in 10.5 and 17.7 s, with the same counts as at 16 384 where both end; the long block's seed 4 needs more than the cliff: at 524 288 pieces it ran past 180 s, 39 500 searches, 178 million looks and 363 million units counted, still going. Two prices are low as well: the normal net of a rational piece (`weighted_normal_net`, some 2160 products of Bernstein coefficients for a bicubic piece) is charged nothing, where its polynomial twin `normal_net` charges its products, so a quartering of a weighted piece counts about 11 units for some 88 µs (about 125 000 units a second, where the allowance's own measure is one to three million); and the trees of these faces (4782 to 5802 leaves) take 0.35 to 0.40 s for 39 000 to 45 000 units. Not the face operations: an offset of each weighted face of the square block is refused (*"a pole or a border or corner drawn into a point"*) in 15 to 21 s for 15 to 22 million units counted, about the allowance's own pace.

**How the work is bounded today** (read from the code). `allowance::within` is set by the face operations (`offset_face`: `FACE_OP_WORK`, 450 million units, more per span and edge moved), `plate` (`LADDER_TRY_WORK`, a million a try of its ladder, and a bound per face), `stepread` (each body read) and the tessellator's self-check (`SELF_CHECK_WORK`, 20 million). Regeneration sets none around a feature (it reads `allowance::scope` only to run a pattern's unions one by one under a bound), and the engine none around a command. A boolean sets none, nor do `interference`, `common_volume`, `contact`, `verify`, `parts_touch`, `contains_points` and `classify_points` (all entered through `freeform::holding`, which holds them to a budget of the trees' memory, `HELD_BYTES`, and of no work), blends, sweeps, lofts, delete face and pattern joins; a boolean inside a face operation runs under that operation's bound. A boolean has caps per question instead (a winding `MAX_TURN_STEPS`, 65 536 steps a piece of a curve; a sigma search 65 536 nodes and 1024 looks a leaf; a march `MAX_EVALS`, 40 million evaluations; a cover `NODES`, 8192, and `PAIRS`, 4096; an edge's crossings `CROSSING_PIECES`, 65 536) and none on how many questions. Under a bound the count would not have seen the cliff: a fine piece is charged once an operation (`allowance::once`), so one made again after the store let it go costs nothing more (under `within(5e6)` 0 to 5662 units were counted a search for 63 000 to 105 000 done uncounted, and the bound was never reached); outside a bound every look is charged, made or kept, which keeps the count independent of the store, and a remake costs nothing more there either. And outside a bound the count of a boolean depends on what other threads and earlier operations put in a tree's three memos (the nearest point, the sigma, the lower bound: a hit charges nothing), so a bound held to it would not decide the same on every run unless those memos keep and charge what their search cost.

Everything made inside `allowance::uncounted`, and what bounds it:

| Where | What is made | What bounds it | How it is charged |
|---|---|---|---|
| `geom` `Surface::project_grid` | 17 × 17 samples of a freeform surface, kept with it | 289 evaluations | never |
| `geom` `meet::remembered` | a numeric meeting asked again within one bounded operation and not on the shelf | the march's caps (`MAX_EVALS` 40 million, `MAX_CELLS` a million, `MAX_POINTS` 16 000) | at its first ask in the operation |
| `geom` `Projector::shared` | a grid of 25 × 25 points and normals | 625 evaluations | never |
| `geom` `Pieces::shared` | the marcher's tree of a surface's pieces | its spans and `PIECE_BUDGET` (8192) nodes | never |
| `kernel` `tweak::inside_out` | the volumes of every shell of two bodies | the mass integration's caps (`BUDGET` 2^27 a face, `MAX_PANELS` 65 536) | never, by design |
| `kernel` `PatchTree::shared` | the tree of a surface (kept on the shelf, 768 MB) | `MAX_SPANS` (262 144) spans, quartered to a depth of up to 5 within `MAX_LEAVES` (32 768) leaves, each settled within `LEAF_PIECES` (256) halvings | its build's units, once an operation (the rational normal net at nothing) |
| `kernel` `PatchTree::evened` | the tree of the surface drawn evenly | as a tree | as a tree |
| `kernel` `PatchTree::finer` | a leaf's first fine piece | one piece: up to five evaluations and a normal net | once a piece and operation under a bound, at every look outside one |
| `kernel` `PatchTree::finer_in` | the four quarters of a fine piece | four pieces; 1024 looks and 16 halvings a leaf, 65 536 nodes a search; nothing on how many searches, and a piece the store let go of is made again | as the first piece; a remake nothing more |
| `topo` `check` | a body checked again within one bounded operation (not kept: it had defects) | the checker's own caps | at its first ask in the operation |

Each is bounded per call; what is open is the number of calls, the remakes, the price of a rational piece, and the operations with no bound.

**Reach.** The square block passes the checker on `main` and on `release/0.1`, so this is a hang of the released kernel. Through the program it was not reached: written as STEP (weights 1e-3 to 1e3) and imported into 0.1.0 (`craft3d-cli run`, `file.import`), the block is refused before any boolean (*"the imported body cannot be meshed to see whether it passes through itself (patch 1 needs about 170807 segments per side for this tolerance, above the limit of 1024 …)"*, at the document's tolerance 1e-6, 1e-3 and 1e-2 alike). With the weights from 10^-1.5 to 10^1.5, 10^-2 to 10^2 and 10^-2.5 to 10^2.5 the square block's seeds are built, in 2.2 to 13.7 s. What a user does reach on 0.1.0 is minutes, not an hour: a block 30 × 30 × 20 whose bottom is a ruled spline without weights whose speed rises from 1e-5, 1e-6 or 1e-8 of its width to twice it (`classify_far.rs`'s `block_with_a_ruled_bottom`), imported from STEP, passes the check, and its intersection with a block through the bottom is refused after 121, 90 and 103 s (*"a point too close to a face boundary to tell inside from outside"*): counted work (each step of a winding asks for a nearest point; 88 million units in the first 25 s), which a bound would end. A user who meets such a boolean in the window sees its work in progress until it ends: the window's engine thread runs one task at a time and has no way to stop one.

**Repaired (2026-11-16): the store holds what the searches look into** (`freeform.rs` › `FINE_PIECES`, `fine_room`, `patch_of`). A surface keeps up to 524 288 fine pieces (32 768 in a browser), and no more than the room the process has for them: what the operations hold together may take `HELD_TOGETHER` (4 GiB; 512 MiB in a browser), and the fine pieces of every tree count in it with the trees the operations hold. At either cap the leaves asked about longest ago go, as before; nothing is refused. A quarter keeps no patch, the most of what a piece took: it is cut out of its leaf's again, by the same splits in the same order (across `u` at the middle, then across `v`), so the same patch bit for bit, and a piece takes some 270 bytes instead of 750 (the shelf counts a tree's pieces at the old size, so it keeps and lets go of the same trees where a store holds as many pieces as before). *A store per thread was tried and left:* the section and its helper then each made what the other had made, and a thread's store of 96 MB held less than the three weighted faces' stores need together; the square seeds took 13 to 30 s and the long ones 46 and 77 s (debug, laptop), against 9 to 14 and 11 to 28 s with one store a surface. *Why nothing that is built changes:* what a search answers and what it charges do not depend on what a store keeps (a kept split is the piece a search would make afresh, bit for bit, and is charged its cost when made, a function of the piece), and below the old cap (12 287 pieces and the room for one search) nothing is let go of either way. *Measured* (Check 43772349322 on f857a9c5, release on the Strix): the square block's seeds 1, 3 and 4 built in 6.2, 3.4 and 4.8 s and the long block's 1 and 3 in 9.7 and 8.0 s, each held to the polygon oracle and with no piece made twice; the same booleans with a store of the old size, each in a process of its own, 27.6, 59.8, 103.3, 27.3 and 76.0 s, making 62 000 to 1 140 000 pieces again; the corpus digest `7927eb7a078af68e`; the hunts' pinned outcomes (`hunt_offset`, `hunt_shell`), `classify_far`, `boolean_rational` and the kernel's unit tests of `freeform.rs` green; the benchmark's counts (Heavy 43772401761) the same as `main`'s code gave them, its 95 older counts those of Heavy 43755383334 and its five `fold_checks` the baseline's, its models' stores at most 36 MB together (CPU 2.1 to 9.9 s a model, within the two earlier runs' 1.3 to 10.7 s; the Strix ran a Check beside it); the body digests of the square and long seeds the same with stores of 16 384 to 524 288 pieces (laptop). Tests: `boolean_weighted_chart.rs` (the square block's five seeds, under a deadline that ends the process, the polygon oracle, no piece made twice; by hand in release the long block and the old store in processes of their own), `freeform.rs` › `a_store_that_holds_the_searches_along_a_boundary_makes_each_piece_once` (counted: a weighted sheet's boundary at the old cap 3066 pieces made again, now none, the same answers and units) and `a_quarter_s_patch_cut_out_of_its_leaf_is_the_one_it_was_made_from`.

**Repaired (2026-11-16): a bound on the work** (Method › *Bounded work*; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_bound.rs`, `boolean_ledger.rs`). *Why on the ledger:* the same intersection of the square block's seed 1 counted 4.6 million units with the pool's helpers and cold caches, 1.2 million warm, 10.2 million with one helper and 10.7 million alone, and asked 618 362 817 of the ledger every time; the block with a bottom slowed ten thousandfold 4.1 to 5.2 million counted and 5 170 079 on the ledger every time (Check 43776852734, and on the laptop). *The distribution* (a twin that printed the ledger of every operation, Check 43776852734, debug): over the 98 452 operations of the corpus, the hunts, the benchmark's models, the examples (`mesh_digests`, `guide_examples`) and the 65 documents of the side repositories, half ask less than 8 units, 90 % less than 6.6e4, 99 % less than 5.2e5, 99.9 % less than 1.7e7, and the dearest 54 349 180 (the fuel tank's shell, the hollow taken away; 13 to 15 s in debug with its caches cold); the dearest of the corpus 3.3e7, of the side documents 2.1e7, of the hunts 1.9e7, of the benchmark 9.7e6. The bound is ten billion: 184 times the dearest, and above what a face operation allows itself (450 million of the count). *Within a factor of a hundred of it* (1e8 or more): nothing of those sets; of the kernel's own tests, the booleans of the blocks with weighted sides (the square block's seeds 1, 3 and 4: 6.2e8, 9.8e8 and 1.5e9; the long block's 1 and 3: 1.1e9 and 2.9e9; `classify_far.rs`'s long block 1.1e9), each built and right, and the blocks with a bottom slowed a hundred thousandfold and a millionfold (6.2e8 and 6.7e8), refused before the bound as before (below). *What it means in time* (release on the Strix): on faces whose weights are far apart, where a fine piece is in the ledger at every look, some 20 to 30 s (2.9e9 in 8.3 s, 1.5e9 in 2.9 to 4.6 s); on other freeform work some 10 minutes to an hour (the slowed bottom 6.7e8 in 56 s; the corpus' dearest 3.3e7 in 12 to 18 s with the test threads side by side). *The long block's seed 4:* its counted work is the windings about the points of the covers of its long weighted faces (`section::clip` → `Solid::may_hold` → `Cover::holds` → `Outline::side` → `Chart::winding`, each step a sigma search that splits fine pieces anew; sampled), more than the store's cliff; at the bound it is refused, *"boolean does not support this: it needs more work than one operation is allowed: … (face 5 of the first body, face 0 of the second body)"* (under a bound of 2e8 in 6 s in debug, `boolean_bound.rs`; at the real bound in release after 28.8 s, Check 43780154452, having made 2.0 million fine pieces and 441 384 of them again: its faces need more than the store's 524 288 a surface). *The slowed bottom* (a ruled spline without weights whose speed rises from a hundred-thousandth or a millionth of its width to twice it, imported from STEP into 0.1.0 and refused there after 90 to 121 s): under the bound it does what it did, refused after 35 to 63 s in release, *"a point too close to a face boundary to tell inside from outside (at the edge of tolerance)"*, at 6.2e8 and 6.7e8 on the ledger. That text is not what happened: the refusal comes from the winding's cap of steps on one piece of a boundary curve (`MAX_TURN_STEPS`, 65 536, `chart.rs`; seen at that return, probed), which the step sizes of a chart that moves a million times faster at the slow end than elsewhere run through; nothing shows the point near the boundary. *Nothing else moved:* the 142 mesh digests the same as `main`'s (against Check 43755313374), the corpus digest `7927eb7a078af68e`, the benchmark's 100 counts the same, the hunts' pinned outcomes green; a bound of 1 to 1000 units refuses a freeform boolean and a classification, and lets two plain blocks through or refuses them, never panics.

**Repaired (2026-11-16, the review's): the ledger depended on what ran before** (`allowance::costs_of`; `boolean_ledger.rs` › `the_ledger_of_a_boolean_does_not_depend_on_the_operations_before_it`, `freeform.rs` › `an_answer_searched_again_where_the_ledger_does_not_grow_keeps_what_it_asked`). *Finding:* a question an operation asks again after the shared memo of a tree let go of it (where the operation's own memo, as the ledger counts it, would still hold it) is searched again where the ledger does not grow (`allowance::unasked`), and `costs_of` measured nothing of the ledger there: the answer was kept with 0, and so were the fine pieces made in that search, and a later operation that asked it first paid 0. The same question was 238 units on the ledger cold and 0 after such a search; the long weighted block's intersection 1 103 978 774 cold and 971 883 155 after its subtraction; the subtraction 546 165 204 cold, 543 256 296 after the intersection and 480 578 418 interleaved with it. Cold, with one, two, four or all helpers and two operations at once, the ledger was the same to the unit, and it only ever came out lower, so nothing that builds was refused; but near the bound, whether an operation was refused would have depended on what the session did before. *Repair:* `costs_of` measures through both regions that hold back the count or the ledger, and puts back what the region held back. There are two and no others: the count and the ledger grow only through `charge` and `replay`, and only `uncounted` (neither grows) and `unasked` (the ledger does not) hold those back. Every cache that keeps a cost measures it with `costs_of` (a tree, a fine piece, a memo's answer, a meeting's count through `cost_of`) or charges it outside both regions (the shelf of meetings: `measured`, and its count on every hit outside a bound on the count). Work on another thread is in neither until it is added, and the helpers' never is: what they find is kept with what it took, which the operation that asks pays. While it measures through a region the ledger's bound stands where it stood on entry (reached inside if it was reached, not otherwise), so a search done again only because a shared cache forgot is not cut short where the cold one was not. *Measured* (Check 43832841544: a twin that printed every operation's ledger, the steps' times and the operands): the long block's intersection and subtraction after each other and interleaved twice, the same to the unit as cold, in debug and in release; the 98 452 operations of the corpus, the hunts, the benchmark, the examples and the side documents the same in number and in their spread (half below 8, 99 % below 5.2e5, 99.9 % below 1.7e7), and of their heaviest hundred 85 the same and the others up by at most 0.3 % (the corpus's dearest 32 790 813 to 32 847 111; the fuel tank's shell, the dearest, 54 349 180 as before); of the sets the review added, the dearest 51 482 363 (plates on one skin, as the review measured), the closed twins' 22 049 042, `boolean.rs`'s 21 230 341 and the rolling fillet's 10 997 889. Nothing comes within a factor of a hundred of the bound that did not: at or above 1e8 are only the kernel tests' weighted blocks (5.5e8 to 2.9e9, each as before), the slowed bottoms (617 181 426 and 671 870 067, up by 13 415 and 2 141: re-searches within the one operation that were kept with nothing) and the bound's own test. The long block's seed 4 is refused at the real bound after 28.2 s in release, as before (28.8 s). *After a refusal:* the section's helpers are called off once it ends (`allowance::stopping_when`, above), so a refusal no longer waits for a helper's pair, which could take as long again under its own bound.

**Owed.** A reason of its own for the winding that runs out of its steps (*"the boundary is dear to follow here: …"*, where today it says a point is too close to the boundary): a refusal's text, which the coordinator decides. The operations that set no bound and do not enter through `freeform::holding`: blends, sweeps, lofts, delete face, pattern joins. A bound per feature in regeneration and per command in the engine (neither sets one). The window cannot stop a running task (passed to the developer). **The bound is on work counted, not on time.** Where an operation's time goes to work that no counter sees, the bound does not hold its time, and the same ledger takes very different times: some 350 million units a second on faces whose weights are far apart (a fine piece is in the ledger at every look), one to three million in the corpus's classifications of points, and a few hundred to a few thousand a second where nearly nothing is charged (the review's 180 to 6 000, under the load of thirty test binaries side by side). So ten billion is some 30 s on weighted faces, about 14 minutes on the slowed bottom and over an hour at the slowest rates seen; no single value ends every operation within two minutes without refusing bodies built in seconds (2.4e8 would refuse all six weighted blocks, 3e9 none, with some 25 minutes at worst). What no counter sees: everything on analytic faces (planes, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori are not counted at all, `craft3d_geom::allowance`): their meetings in closed form, splitting their edges and faces, telling the pieces in or out (`classify::select`), sewing the result; the checks of the operands and of the result (`checked`: a closed analytic body and its twin took 1.0 s for 91 152 units, 0.69 s of it checking the operands and 0.29 s checking the result, Check 43832841544); cutting a fine piece's patch out of its leaf again (`patch_of`, uncounted: what is charged at a look is what making the piece took); and waiting, for a helper's march under way or for a lock. Those grow with the number of faces, pairs, edges and points and are held by sizes and caps of their own, not by the ledger. A bound in time would need those charged too, by what they do (pairs, edges, loops, points), priced as they cost, and the looks into fine pieces priced apart from their making, which a ledger that does not depend on what other threads kept cannot tell apart.

### A face of 2¹⁸ spans: the limit of a freeform face's spans lifted (2026-11-17, `freeform.rs` › `Group`, `MAX_SPANS`; `Surface::key`; the marcher's `Pieces::new`)

**The limit.** A boolean took a freeform face of at most 32 768 Bézier spans (`MAX_FREEFORM_SPANS`, then `freeform::MAX_LEAVES`): a cubic grid of 184 × 184 points was cut, 185 × 185, 400 × 300 and 1024 × 256 refused ("a freeform face with more pieces than booleans can index"), while the grid itself admits 2¹⁸ points. `MAX_LEAVES` is a budget of leaves for one patch tree (memory: "about 22 MB" a tree against the 2 GiB an operation may hold), not an index type, a packed key or a table: the indices are `usize`, the quartering depth is lowered until the spans times `4^depth` fit (above 8 192 spans every span is a leaf), and past 32 768 spans the tree was refused. Nothing else was sized by it but two search caps (the nearest search descends at most that many leaves, the point bound's refinement splits at most that many pieces).

**What grows with the spans** (measured before any change, with the cap lifted in a measurement build: stage clocks in release on the build server, a synthetic terrain of hills and a ripple 100 mm wide at a tolerance of 1e-3 mm, cut by a block of its footprint and by one over its middle half each way; the tables in the dev log and `plan/surface-grid/boolean-spans/`). Every stage linear, none quadratic or n log n: a boolean asks about 300 bounds below a distance, 330 nearest points and 11 to 48 least singular values of a sheet whatever its size, but **the tree's spans were its roots, flat**: each question queued every span's box, 76 to 105 million keys at 512 × 512 points (4.8 to 6.2 s of a 12 s cut). Then the tree's build (6.6 µs a span), the meeting of the block's sides with the sheet (the marcher's pieces of the surface, 1.9 s a build, made by the section and its helpers side by side), the surface written out as the key of every shelf asked (0.12 s at 2¹⁸ points, some fifty times a boolean), the regeneration's check that the result does not pass through itself (7 µs a span), and copies of the surface's 150 MB of spans (the tree's build, the marcher's pieces, the covers). Classification, sewing, the topology check and the boolean's own check cost nothing to speak of.

**What was done.**
- *Groups of spans* (Method › *Speed*): a tree of 64 spans or more takes them in groups of four neighbours (two by two along u and v where the spans are a spline's grid, four in a row otherwise), level by level up to the whole surface, each with the box around its members'.
