The heap searches (`search`, `bound_by`) queue a group by a distance no larger than any of its spans' and ahead of a span at the same distance, and open it when it comes first: the spans come out in the order and at the moments they came before, so every answer, every split and every unit of work is the same, by construction (for a segment the group's key is the distance between the segment's box and the group's, less a margin of 1e-10 of the largest coordinate, far above what rounding takes from the distance to a span's box). `sigma_search` takes the spans the groups leave within reach in the order of before (those out of reach it passed over untouched). The least bound over the spans and the size of the surface are worked out once. The groups add to a tree's bytes (counted in its budget) 3.2 % for a bicubic, 5.8 % at degree 1 and at most 0.3 % for a quartered tree (the review's measure). A segment whose ends or whose surface's coordinates are beyond 1e150 (where the squares of the distances to boxes may overflow, a span's distance infinite or no number, below which no group's stays) is bounded without the groups, as before them (the review found the grouped bound +inf where the flat one was 0 past 1e299).
- *The cap*: `MAX_SPANS`, 2¹⁸ in the desktop program, as many as the largest grid has points (and so more than its spans: a compile-time assertion in `regen::grid` keeps it so), counted from the knots before a span is made (`span_count`); `MAX_LEAVES` stays the leaves' budget, so every tree of up to 32 768 spans is built as before. A face of more is refused naming the face, its spans and the limit ("a freeform face of more Bézier spans than the 262144 a boolean indexes: face 0 of the tool (262656 spans); build it of fewer spans").
- *A budget of bytes* (the review's round, 2026-11-17): the cap counts spans, and a tree's bytes grow with the degree (a node about 360 bytes and a patch of 32 (p + 1)(q + 1) a span: 2¹⁸ spans 229 MB for a bicubic, 2.2 GB at degree 15, 9.2 GB at degree 32, where the old cap's worst was 1.15 GB), and the budget of held trees counts a tree only once it is built. So a face of more than `MAX_LEAVES` spans is held to `MAX_INDEX_BYTES`, 1 GiB, for its spans and the tree over them, worked out from its knots and degrees before either is made (`index_bytes`: a node, a span and two patches a span and the groups, never less than they take and within a tenth, held by a test at degrees 1 and 3): a bicubic at the grid's cap takes about 380 MB, a face of degree 5 × 5 up to the cap, of degree 7 × 7 about 240 000 spans, of degree 15 × 15 about 64 000. Past it the face is refused before any of it is made ("… whose Bézier spans and their index would take more than the 1024 MiB one face may: face 0 of the tool (64516 spans of degree 15 × 15: n MiB); build it of fewer spans or of a lower degree"). Held by `kernel/tests/boolean_index_budget.rs` (a sheet of degree 15 just over the budget cut by a block: refused, the process's peak memory held to a tenth of what it would take; just under, ignored, cut in release) and `regen::boolean` › `a_face_whose_index_would_take_too_much_is_named_with_its_bytes`.
- *In a browser* (WebAssembly: at most the module's 4 GiB, and what the browser grants; a failed allocation ends the module, which outranks the feature) the cap stays the old one, 32 768 spans (`MAX_SPANS` there; the known-safe floor: at the desktop's cap the module would need about 1.1 to 1.3 GB, the review's estimate); the refusal says the desktop program takes up to 262 144, and a grid's sheet of more than the browser takes is warned of there (`grid.warnings`), as every sheet past 184 × 184 points was before.
- *The spans borrowed, not copied*: the tree, the marcher's pieces and the cover read the spans a spline keeps (`bezier_spans_shared`); a cover is refused (past 4 096 spans) before a span is copied.
- *A surface keeps its key* (`Surface::key`: its debug text and the text's FNV-1a hash, made once for a spline and shared by its clones, not part of its value): the projectors', the marcher's pieces', the intersections' and the trees' shelves and the trees' numbers take it from there, the same text and hash as before.
- *The marcher's pieces* of a surface with as many spans as their budget (8 192) skip working out splits that none could be made of (the same pieces, bit for bit; 1.9 s to 0.18 s a build at 512 × 512).

**After** (release, the build server; the cut through the commands, footprint and middle block, seconds): 100² 0.53 and 0.43 → 0.23 and 0.18; 184² 1.59 and 1.37 → 0.62 and 0.47; 256² 3.21 and 2.64 → 1.17 and 0.90; 400 × 300 5.53 and 4.85 → 2.07 and 1.56; 512² 12.06 and 12.48 → 4.44 and 3.22. The bounds below a distance at 512² 3.43 s → 0.003 s, the nearest points 1.35 s → 0.08 s. Peak memory of the footprint cut, each in a process of its own: 184² 218 → 161 MiB, 256² 411 → 291, 400 × 300 757 → 511, 512² 1 635 → 1 067 (339 of it the sheet before the cut). Left at 512²: the tree's build 1.5 s on one thread, the regeneration's check not through itself 1.9 s (footprint; 0.56 s middle), the marcher's pieces 0.2 s a build.

**Nothing else moved.** The boolean corpus's digest is `7927eb7a078af68e` with the groups and the cap (Heavy 44070274829) as on the base (Heavy 44065240065), 11 594 agreeing; `freeform.rs` › `the_groups_of_spans_change_no_answer_and_no_work` holds 1 440 questions of every kind (nearest point, bounds below a point's and a segment's distance with and without a threshold, least singular value at four reaches) on trees with groups against the same trees without, bit for bit in answer and work, on sheets of 900 and 1 849 spans (polynomial, folded, rational) and a strip of 300, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6 (it fails with the groups queued after a span at the same distance); `field.rs` › `a_surface_of_more_spans_than_the_budget_has_its_spans_for_pieces` and `surface.rs` › `a_surface_s_key_is_its_text_and_a_spline_keeps_it` hold the other two.

**Oracles** (`engine/tests/surface_grid_cut.rs`): the volume under the sheet over the block by exact quadrature per span (the divergence theorem on the sheet the feature interpolates, worked out from the same points) against the cut's measure, the part above and `V(A) = V(A ∩ S) + V(A − S)`, the union of the two parts the block again, vertical rays at the nodes on the sheet's points, the deep check and closed meshes; a terrain of 40 × 33 points at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6 with both blocks and a sheet that runs back under itself (three crossings of a vertical line) in the gate; 256², 400 × 300 and 512² in release, by hand. With the block of the footprint (the sheet's border on its sides, the ordinary case of a terrain) the volume agrees to the rounding (1.2e-15 at 256²); with a block across the sheet the side planes meet it in fitted curves within the tolerance of where they meet it, and the volume moves by what that band holds (4e-3 mm³ of 56 830 at 256² and 1e-3 mm, within the tolerance times the band's area and the block's height, which the test allows). In release (Check 44077298388): the footprint's volume within 1.2e-15 at 256², 6.2e-15 at 400 × 300 and 2.4e-14 at 512 × 512, the middle block's within 7.1e-8, 4.3e-8 and 1.1e-7; every ray at every 32nd node on the sheet's point; the deep check and the meshes clean at every size; the three booleans (intersect, subtract, the union of the two) with the regeneration compared against a full one after each, 2.5 to 2.8 s at 256², 5.5 to 6.3 s at 400 × 300, 14.7 to 20.4 s at 512². *Owed behind the boolean*: the deep check takes 27 to 39 s at 512² ([`surfacing.md`](surfacing.md) › *Limits*). (The 2.7 s a ray that test showed at 512² was the test's own harness, `common::checked::run`, comparing the document with a full regeneration after every command not in its list of reading ones; through `Session::execute` three rays took 0.05 s together: the review's measure.)

*The review* (`review-boolean-spans`, 2026-11-17; its tests on `check/review-boolean-spans`, not merged): 4 520 lines identical between `main` and the branch in release, in two rounds (3 698 feature bodies of 98 documents with refusals, measures and STLs; 239 bodies with a face of 64 to 32 761 spans; with 0, 2 and 7 threads of the pool); grouped trees the same as flat ones on 12 486 questions over surfaces of other kinds; the large cuts right by its own oracle up to 259 081 spans, also on lofts of 37 950 and 68 950 spans. Its findings, the budget of bytes, the browser's cap, the segment past 1e150 and the docs, are in the round above.

### Contact refused where a curve lies exactly on a face's border (found 2026-11-19, repaired 2026-11-20; `section.rs` › `clip`, `contact.rs` › `evidence`; the gear reducer Jig)

*Found* by the measurement of what 0.2.0 answers differently from 0.1.3 on the published documents: over the 898 points of the Jigs' ranges, one point of the gear reducer (a corner of its range) has two clearances, a wheel's bore on its shaft's seat, that 0.1.3 answered as *touch* and that are now refused: *"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with (at the edge of tolerance)"* for the one pair and *"a curve that is partly on and partly off a face boundary (at the edge of tolerance)"* for the other. *touch* is the right answer: the bore sits on the seat by design.

*Cause, bisected to the solver's exact held values* (`e7188a094`, 2026-11-13; its parent answers *touch*). The keyways' sketches hold their arc centres at x = 0 and their sides at half the key's width; 0.1.3 solved them to −2.2e-16 and −3.9999999999999996, and they are now exactly 0 and −4. With the slot's centre exactly on the axis, the keyed shafts come out with more edges and vertices (the intermediate shaft's 594 and 400 became 595 and 401, the output shaft's 36 and 28 became 38 and 30), each otherwise the same body to 2e-5 mm³, valid, and the design exactly. The contact check then imprints the bore on a shaft where a curve lies on such an edge to the tolerance, and cannot place it (inferred from the counts, the places of the new vertices and the refusals' words, not traced).

*Not a change of the kernel.* The refusal travels with the bodies, not with the build: the shafts as 0.1.3 builds them are answered *touch* by both versions' `contact`, and the shafts as they are built now are refused by both, in the same words (each body written to STEP and read into a new document on each version). Exact held values are right (the same values give the same body); they made a design reach a gap that was there.

**Owed** (2026-11-19): `contact`, and the imprint it shares with the booleans, where an imprinted curve coincides exactly with a face's border; and where the shafts' extra edges come from (not traced). A reproduction of two bodies and one `measure.clearance` exists in the workshop (the bodies are a published Jig's, so they are not in this repository); the fix starts by making a synthetic pair that fails the same way.

**Repair (2026-11-20).**

*Traced: a curve that leaves a border, not one that lies on it.* The rim of a keyway's rounded end is the curve in which the end's cylinder (radius 4 mm on the output shaft, 3 on the intermediate one) meets the seat's. The wheel's bore lies on the seat, and its own keyway is as wide as the shaft's: the side of the wheel's keyway, a straight edge of the bore's face, is tangent to that rim where the rounded end begins. So the curve in which the bore's surface meets the end's touches the border of the bore's face without crossing it, and runs on outside the face within the tolerance of the border for a while: `r (1 − cos φ)` from it after an angle φ round the end, 0.01 mm (the Jig's tolerance) after 4.05°, 0.28 mm along. `section::clip` cuts a meeting curve where an edge of either face meets the other's surface and reads each stretch between two cuts at its middle and its quarter points: outside a face at the middle, it is left out; within the tolerance of the border at all three, it is taken to lie along the border. The keyed shafts' new vertex (below) is such a cut, 7.12° round the end of the output shaft's keyway and 6.11° round the intermediate shaft's: the stretch from the place of tangency to it is 0.50 and 0.32 mm long and ends 3.1 and 1.7 tolerances off the border. The first is within the tolerance of the border at its middle and at its first quarter and clear of it at the third: refused, *"a curve that is partly on and partly off a face boundary"*. The second is within the tolerance at all three, taken as along the border, and is then no piece of an edge of the bore's face (its far end is no vertex of that face): refused in the imprint (`boolean::run`), *"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with"*. Without the vertex the stretch is the half rim, outside at its middle and left out: the shafts as 0.1.3 built them. The offset grows as the square of the way along, so the gap is a band: a stretch that leaves the border tangentially and ends between 1 and 16/9 tolerances off it is refused the second way, between 16/9 and 4 the first way; below 1 its end is on the border's edge (one vertex, one arc), above 4 its middle is outside. At any tolerance and size, for any vertex there: the tests below reach both refusals with a block and a cylinder.

*The shafts' extra vertices* are where the marcher's trace of each end's rim began, a finding of its own: *The seam of a closed meeting curve left as a vertex* below.

*Method.* Between its cuts a stretch crosses no edge of the face (a crossing is a cut), so it is inside the face, outside it or along its boundary as a whole: a sample within the tolerance of the boundary says nothing by itself, one clear of it says which. *Slivers read at their boundary* above drew that conclusion for a sample inside; the same holds outside, and for the ends:

1. *A stretch with a sample clear outside the face and none inside is outside it*, and left out, as one that is outside at its middle always was. (Before: refused, unless the surfaces only touch along the curve.)
2. *A stretch within the tolerance of the boundary at its three samples lies along the boundary only when it ends on it.* An end at a vertex clear of the boundary which is no vertex of an edge of the face is a sample like the three: outside there, the stretch is outside and left out; inside there, it is inside and imprinted across the face (a boss on a plate whose footprint is tangent to the plate's edge, with the vertex of its rim next to that place). The end is read where the vertex it will be already is (`Vertices::find_with`: `find_or_add_with`'s search without the adding), and a vertex of an edge of the face is on the face's boundary wherever the merging of vertices has put it. (A branch of its own for surfaces that only touch along the curve, which left such a stretch out without a hold, was taken out after the review: *The five parts, settled* below.)

Both are held to the band, at the seven places the rule for inside reads: each is clear of the boundary on that side, or within half the tolerance of the boundary (the distance at which two points are one, and what the marcher holds a traced curve to of the curve it stands for: a stretch that truly leaves the boundary on one side never fails this), or on that side by the sign of its offset from the nearest edge (`Outline::inside_by_sign`). A place on the other side by more than half the tolerance (a crossing whose cut was lost in the band) keeps the refusal it had, in its old words.

*Only refusals are read differently.* A stretch along a face's boundary is a piece of an edge of the face between two vertices that are ends of the face's edges or that `clip` splits an edge at, and it splits an edge only at a vertex within the tolerance of it; rule 2 reads an end only where the vertex is neither. Rule 1 takes the place of a refusal. So every result that was built is built as it was. *Measured:* the boolean corpus gives the same report line for line and the same digest, `7927eb7a078af68e`, on the commit before and with the repair (11 988 outcomes, 228 refused, none wrong or invalid; Checks 44745739216 and 44745711585): none of its refusals is of this kind, which is why the gap stood until a design met it. A gate of the whole change (Gate 44747459848) is green: no test's expectation moved. The benchmark's 110 counters are the same, one by one, on the commit before and with the change (Checks 44768155562 and 44764358871): 99 equal the baseline file, and 11 stand within its margins by the same amounts on both, so none of them is this change's (the turbofan's and the engine block's `boolean_edge_candidates`; the engine block's `blend_sections` and `boolean_face_pairs`; `mass_evaluations` of the turbofan, the engine block, the impeller and the rotor; `triangles` of the engine block, the rotor and the plate of holes).

*General.* The rules name no surface, curve, configuration or size; they follow from what a cut is. The numbers are the tolerance, its half (the merging distance) and the seven places of the rule for inside.

*Contact in a point where no stretch is left* (`contact.rs` › `evidence`; found by the grid below, the same day). A cylinder beside a plate, its rim touching the plate's top front edge in one point, was answered **clear** at a distance of 0 wherever it was not refused: no vertex of either lies there, the circle in which the wall meets the top's plane leaves no stretch within both faces, and no surfaces touch in a point. The section keeps such places (`Sections::edge_meets`, which the boolean has read for parts that touch since 2026-10-18, *Where an edge meets the other body's surface* above); the interference query read vertices, touches of surfaces and stretches, not these. It reads them now, after the other places: a place that lies on a face of both bodies is a contact in a point, the only one or one among others. (At first only where nothing else was found. No answer needed that condition, and with it a pair that touches on a face and, elsewhere, in such a point had the point left out: taken out after the review, *The five parts, settled* below.) Without this the pairs rule 1 and 2 no longer refuse would have been answered clear too: a refusal turned into a wrong answer.

*Held to* (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_border_tangent.rs`, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, each at a tolerance of a millionth of the unit and at the Jig's, a quarter of a percent of the radius):

- *A plate and a cylinder at its top face's edge*: the cylinder's footprint tangent to the edge from inside (a boss) and from outside, and 40 tolerances further in and further out; resting on the level of the top, lifted 5 tolerances and sunk 50; the rim's vertex 0.4, 1.4, 3 and 8 tolerances off the edge and a quarter turn away; the place of tangency at the middle of the edge and at its end, a corner of the face, with the vertex on either side of the corner; in the world's axes and turned by half a radian (the box of the top face leaves the stretch out in the world's axes when its end is more than twice the tolerance outside the plate); the plate's edges as lines and as cubic splines (a freeform border). 2 160 places a scale; the booleans where the bodies meet, and as a feature runs them where the footprint is tangent (5 760 boolean calls a scale, thinned from 12 960 after the review). Each is held to its closed form (the area a disc and a half plane share): `contact` clear, touch or overlap; `interference` clear, a touch in a point or along a line with its kind (a point; an edge along a line, on a lens too thin to be told an area, and where the rim's vertex is within the tolerance of the edge on the plate's side, the piece of the rim up to it one with the edge), a touch on that area, or an overlap of that area times the depth; and the union, the difference and the intersection valid, held to the boolean's own check in full and to their volumes as the query runs them (parts that touch allowed), and as a feature runs them built, empty or refused as zero thickness, never for the contact.
- *A keyed shaft and a hub*, made by the kernel's own operations (a cylinder less an extruded slot whose rounded end is two arcs that meet 0.4, 1.4, 3 or 8 tolerances off the side of the hub's keyway, or one arc; a hub extruded from its outline, its bore on the seat and its keyway as wide as the shaft's): the rim is a spline the marcher traces, the border it leaves is a line and the border it lies along a fitted spline. Either way round, `contact` a touch, `interference` a touch on the bore's area (`R (2π − 2 asin(w / R)) l`), the three booleans valid, checked and at their volumes (the union the sum, the difference the first body, nothing in common).
- `crates/engine/tests/contact_border.rs`: the same shaft and hub through the commands a Jig uses (`sketch.path`, `feature.extrude`, `feature.boolean`), in millimetres at 0.01 mm, with a seat of 25 and a key of 8 (the Jig's output shaft) and a seat of 18 and a key of 6, the vertex at the Jig's 1.7 and 3.1 tolerances among seven offsets: `measure.clearance` a touch at 0, `document.interference` a touch on the bore's area.

*Before and after* (Checks on the build server). On the kernel as it was, with these tests (Check 44746653978): of the grid's 6 480 places 120 are refused (96 as a curve that does not fit its edges, 24 as partly on and partly off; half of them with the tangency at the middle of the edge, half at the corner with the vertex on the plate's side) and 60 are answered clear where the bodies touch in a point (at the middle of the edge; at a corner the plate's own vertex is there, and they were told to touch); of the 60 pairs of shaft and hub (five offsets, either way round, three scales, two tolerances) 24 are refused (12 each way); every other place and pair is right, its booleans included. With the repair (Check 44746650592, with clippy; the unit tests of the modules changed and the engine's test in Check 44745652785): every place and pair. The engine test fails on the commit before the repair at the first offset in the band (Check 44742809997: *"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with"*) and passes with it (Checks 44743494838 and 44745652785).

*Still refused, or still owed.*

- A stretch whose band holds a place that is neither clear on its side, nor within half the tolerance of the boundary, nor readable by sign (`inside_by_sign` reads none where two pieces of the boundary are as near, or the nearest point is a piece's end: beside a corner of the face). The tangency at a corner of the plate above is not such a case, with the vertex on either side; none was found, and none is held by a test.
- A stretch with a sample inside the face and another outside (a crossing without a cut), and one whose band holds a place on the other side by more than half the tolerance: refused as before.
- A union whose result keeps a vertex within twice the tolerance of an edge it is not on (the boss with its rim's vertex 1.4 or 3 tolerances from the plate's edge) is refused as zero thickness, by the rule every body is held to; the tests run the booleans as the interference query does.
- *The Jig's own bodies* were not at hand when the repair was made (the tests reach the same two refusals at the same tolerance and proportions with bodies made here); they are confirmed on a build since: *The Jig's own point* below.
- The RC wing's eight points are another gap: *A spar's face beside a rib's skin* below.
- `mass_properties` does not measure three of the lenses the grid's intersections make at 1e-3 and 1e6 (each valid and held to the boolean's own check; measured at 1): [`topology.md`](topology.md) › *A lens a few dozen tolerances thick that is not measured at every scale*. The test lists them per scale.

*The review* (`review-contact-border`, 2026-11-20; its tests on `check/review-contact-border`).

*Each reading brought back in turn* (a switch per reading on a throwaway branch, the repair's own tests under each: Check 44758676140). They fail, in the old words, with rule 1 off (8 places of the grid a scale and 6 pairs of shaft and hub a tolerance: *partly on and partly off*), with rule 2 off (32 and 6: *does not fit the edges*), with either of its branches off (outside: the grid and the pairs; inside: the grid), with the contact's reading off (24 places a scale told clear) and with the sign taken out of the seven places. They pass with the seven places taken away altogether (every stretch counted as held), with the half tolerance taken out of them, with an end at a vertex of the face counted as clear, with an end read at its cut and not at the vertex it will be, with the branch for surfaces that only touch off, and with the edge meets read always. So nothing held the guard of both rules, and five parts of the repair change no answer of its tests. Every place of the grid that fails on the kernel as it was, or with a reading off, has the footprint tangent and the cylinder resting (240 of the 2 160 a scale): the places 40 tolerances off, lifted and sunk are neighbours that never told the two kernels apart.

*The seven places, held* (`section.rs` › tests › `a_stretch_past_half_the_tolerance_inside_is_not_left_out`). A unit test of `clip` with a crossing's cuts left out, a cut lost in the band: a circle 0.9 tolerances into a plate's top face, between two cuts outside it, keeps the reading it had (refused as partly on and partly off, or taken as along the boundary); 0.3 tolerances short of the face it is left out, by either rule. It fails with the seven places taken away, with either rule off and without the sign (Check 44762020209). Still held by nothing: the half tolerance (at scale 1 it has 39 more booleans of the review's pairs built or found empty, all right; no pair needs it), the exception for a vertex of the face, `Vertices::find_with`, the branch for surfaces that only touch, and *only where nothing else was found*: no answer of any pair measured differs without them. Settled the same day: *The five parts, settled* below.

*The band and borders that curve* (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_border_review.rs`; 1 344 places of the band and 108 of the curved borders a scale, 33 s for the file in a Check). The plate and cylinder of the repair's tests a fraction of a tolerance, or six, short of the edge or over it, lifted or sunk by a fraction of one; and a cylinder tangent to a border that curves (an arc of a circle, a parabola drawn as a spline) with a radius well under the border's radius of curvature, within a hundredth of it, equal to it and over it. Held to closed forms, a refusal allowed: bodies that share a point are never clear, bodies more than three tolerances apart never meet, bodies without a common volume never overlap, `contact` and `interference` agree, a boolean built is valid, passes the boolean's check in full and has its volume; the tangencies are answered. On the kernel as it was, 40 places of the band a scale are told clear where the bodies share a point, and the tangencies are refused (Check 44762020209); with the repair every place holds (Check 44764358871, with clippy and the benchmark).

*Measured through commands*, the released 0.2.1 against a build of the repair (f93760c7): pairs made in one document, then `document.interference`, the three booleans as a feature runs them, `document.measure` and `document.check {deep}`. The plate and cylinder in the band (13 offsets across from six tolerances short of the edge to six over it, 7 heights, 6 of them for the booleans, the rim's vertex at four places, a boss and beside the plate, at the middle of the edge and at its corner from both sides; 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, two tolerances but for the coarse one at 1e6, which `file.new` does not take, in the world's axes and turned: 21 840 pairs, 56 160 booleans); the plate with a bump (5 100 pairs, 15 300 booleans); a cylinder standing rim to rim on another (1 925 pairs); a ball at a cylinder's rim (220); a cylinder lying over a plate's edge, along it and across it at a few tolerances over its length (12 600); the shaft and hub with keyways up to three tolerances apart in width (72 pairs, 216 booleans). Every difference between the two is one of three: *clear* become *touch* (792 pairs), a refusal answered (1 394 pairs; of the booleans 1 291 built, 1 219 empty, 1 201 refused as zero thickness and 24 refused because their result cannot be meshed), or a refusal in other words. No boolean that 0.2.1 builds or finds empty differs (18 723 built on both, their volumes the same to 1e-12 wherever the measure lists them), and no answer of the build is wrong by its closed form (of the 41 757 pairs 17 075 are refused on both). The 112 items measured before 0.2.0 (the 65 published side documents, the 25 showcase documents, the 22 scripts: `document.inspect`, `document.check`, `document.measure {bound}`, `assert.list`, `output.list`, the STEP's data) answer the same on both, line for line.

*What was clear and is touch* is more than the release note's cylinder beside a plate: a ball against a cylinder's rim, a cylinder standing rim to rim on another, a cylinder sunk beside a wall that is a spline surface (a touch along a line, told as a point), parts up to 0.9 tolerances apart at such a place, and parts into each other by under half a tolerance there (the ball: 0.8). The clearance itself was 0 and is 0.

*Results that cannot be meshed* (found by the curved borders). Where a rim stays within the tolerance of a border that curves, for a long way (a radius within a hundredth of the border's), a boolean the kernel builds with parts that touch allowed can pass `topo::check`, the boolean's own check in full and its volume, and not be meshed (`craft3d_tess::body_self_intersections`: a face whose loops do not bound a region, tapering to a needle between the rim and the border): 9 of the 324 booleans a scale of the review's curved pairs, 14 of 432 before those were thinned, where the kernel as it was built 7. A feature refuses each by a check of its own: as zero thickness, or *"the result cannot be meshed to see whether it passes through itself"* where the kernel used to refuse in words that named the cause (through commands 66 unions of the 15 300 booleans on the build, 42 of them on 0.2.1 too). Older than the repair, which reaches a few more of them. **Owed:** such a result refused by the boolean itself, in its words, or meshed.

*The Jig's own point* (measured by the coordinator on that build, 2026-11-20): both clearances of the gear reducer's listed point are *touch*, and its range passes at 104 of 104 points (0.2.1: 103); the RC wing fails at the same eight points as before. The ranges of 27 more published Jigs pass at every point on that build, 2 875 points (the coordinator, the same day; the last four were still running).

*The five parts, settled* (2026-11-20, after the review). Of each part that no answer needed the review asked one of two things: a pair of bodies, or a case of `clip`, whose answer needs it, as a test that fails without it; or the code without the part. Two were taken out and three are held.

- *The branch for surfaces that only touch along the curve*: taken out. It left a stretch out with no hold at all, where rule 2 holds every other stretch to the seven places; a meeting along which the surfaces only touch is now read by rule 2 like any other. No answer of the tests there are is another for it: the repair's tests pass with the branch in and with it out, and the review's file counts every answer by its kind in 140 lines that are the same, line for line, before and after (Checks 44764358871 and 44769787052). **Owed:** no test reaches a tangent meeting read by rule 2; the review's second round built a pair to reach it (a wheel standing beside a bump or over a dent of a plate's front, 48 places: with the ends 3 tolerances off all 24 are a touch along an edge with right booleans, with the ends 1.4 off all 24 are refused as two points closer than the tolerance can tell apart) and did not, so the path is right by reading only.
- *Edge meets only where nothing else was found*: taken out. No answer needed the condition, and one was the poorer for it: a pair that touches on a face and, elsewhere, in a point where an edge of one meets the other had that point left out of its places. `boolean_border_tangent.rs` › `a_touch_in_a_point_is_listed_beside_a_touch_on_a_face` (a frame of a plate, a wall and a roof, and a cylinder under the roof beside the plate: a touch on the lid's area, and the place at the plate's edge among the contact's places) fails with the condition back. The kind of a contact cannot change by it (a point never outranks what was found), and the review's 140 lines are the same.
- *The half tolerance* of the seven places: kept, and held by two more cases of the review's unit test of `clip` (`a_stretch_past_half_the_tolerance_inside_is_not_left_out`): a circle 0.3 tolerances into the plate's top face, between cuts outside it, is left out, by either rule. Within half the tolerance of the boundary is on it: the distance at which two points are one, and by which faces that go through each other still only touch (*Tolerances* above). Without it the sign of the middle's offset has the stretch refused, or taken as along the boundary (the review counted 39 booleans of its pairs at scale 1 that are answered, rightly, only with it).
- *`Vertices::find_with`* (an end read at the vertex it will be, not at its cut): kept, and held by `section.rs` › tests › `an_end_is_read_at_the_vertex_it_will_be`. A cut within the merging distance of a vertex is that vertex, and the cut can be clear of the boundary where the vertex is within the tolerance of it: cuts 1.3 tolerances outside the plate, vertices 0.45 tolerances nearer. The stretch then runs between two vertices on the face's boundary and is along it, as it was before the repair; read at its cuts it is left out, and an imprint that was made is lost.
- *The exception for a vertex of the face*: kept, and held by `an_end_at_a_vertex_of_the_face_is_on_its_boundary`. A face's own vertex is on its outline where it stands, as the review reasoned. But in a boolean a vertex stands where the first of the points merged into it stands, and a loose vertex (off its edges by as much as its own tolerance) that is one with a vertex of the other body beyond it (within half the working tolerance) is further than the tolerance from its face's boundary: a plate's corner 0.9 tolerances off its edges, one with a vertex 0.45 beyond it. A stretch that ends there is along the boundary, as it was; by the distance alone it is left out.

*Under each switch* (the parts behind switches on a throwaway branch, the tests under each: Check 44768383921). With nothing switched every test passes. The grid's faults are a scale's; the shaft and hub's are of the three scales at one tolerance.

| Switched | The grid | Shaft and hub | `clip`'s tests | A touch in two places |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rule 1 off | 8 places | 6 pairs | the seven places fail | passes |
| rule 2 off for an end outside | 16 places | 6 pairs | the seven places and the end at its vertex fail | passes |
| rule 2 off for an end inside | 16 places | none | pass | passes |
| both rules off | 40 places | 12 pairs | not run under it | passes |
| both rules and the contact's reading off: the kernel as it was | 60 places | 12 pairs | not run under it | fails |
| the contact's reading off | 24 places told clear | none | do not read it | fails |
| the seven places taken away | none | none | the seven places fail | passes |
| the sign taken out of them | 40 places | 12 pairs | the seven places and the end at its vertex fail | passes |
| the half tolerance taken out of them | none | none | the seven places fail | passes |
| an end read at its cut | none | none | the end at its vertex and a vertex of the face fail | passes |
| no exception for a vertex of the face | none | none | a vertex of the face fails | passes |
| edge meets only where nothing else was found (back in) | none | none | do not read it | fails |
| the branch for surfaces that only touch (back in) | none | none | pass | passes |

So each part that is kept has a test that fails without it, and neither part taken out is missed by any. A gate of the whole with the two parts out (Gate 44792303657) is green: no test's expectation moved, the benchmark within its baseline.

*The grid thinned* (the same Check, the grid in full and thinned under each switch). The booleans are run where the bodies meet (not lifted clear) and, as a feature runs them, where the footprint is tangent: 5 760 boolean calls a scale for 12 960 (the file took 225 s in a loaded gate and takes 146 s in one now; alone, 26 s). Under every switch the full grid and the thinned one have the same faults, to the line, at each of the three scales (64 at 8 places, 128 at 16, 320 at 40, 360 at 60, 48 at 24, or none), and no fault of either is at a place that is not tangent and resting: nothing is lost. The kind of a touch without an area is pinned as well (it had passed a line told as a point): a point; an edge along a line and on a lens too thin to be told an area; and an edge where the rim's vertex is within the tolerance of the edge on the plate's side, the piece of the rim up to it being one with the edge.

### Between one and two tolerances apart, a touch where a vertex is near and clear where none is: owed (2026-11-20, `boolean/mod.rs` › `on_the_other`, `contact.rs` › `faces_at`; the review of the border repair)

*Found* by the review of the repair above, through commands, on the released 0.2.1 and on a build of the repair alike. A cylinder beside a plate, its rim 1.2 tolerances short of the plate's top front edge (resting at the level of the top, 0.4 tolerances above or below it, and sunk): `document.interference` answers *touch*, in a point, with the vertex of the rim next to the plate (0.4 tolerances off the tangent at the place nearest the edge) and *clear* with the vertex 1.4 or 3 tolerances off it or a quarter turn away. The bodies are the same sets of points; only where a vertex stands differs. At 1.9 tolerances short of the edge every place of the vertex is answered clear, and at 0.8 every one a touch (since the repair; before it, clear or refused).

*Cause, read in the code, not traced.* A vertex of one body is held to a face of the other within twice the tolerance of its surface (`on_the_other`; and `faces_at`, through which every place of a contact is asked which faces it is on), where an edge meets a surface within the tolerance and surfaces touch within it. So between one and two tolerances the answer follows the vertices. It is the vertices' own band (one place within half the tolerance, two at twice it: *Tolerances* above) read as a contact.

**Owed:** one distance at which bodies touch, whatever is nearest there, a vertex, an edge or a face; then the band of the review's file (`boolean_border_review.rs`), which allows either answer there today, held to it. Older than the repair, which does not move it.

### The seam of a closed meeting curve left as a vertex: owed (2026-11-20, `section.rs` › `clip`; the gear reducer's keyed shafts)

*Where the keyed shafts' extra edges and vertices come from* (the question the finding above left open). Each extra vertex lies on the rim of a keyway's rounded end, where nothing else of the body meets: it divides the rim, one spline edge in the shafts 0.1.3 built, into two spline edges on one curve (the output shaft's at 7.12° and 16.9° round the ends of its two keyways, the intermediate shaft's at 6.11°). The rim is half of a closed curve, the loop in which the end's cylinder meets the seat's, which the marcher traces from where it seeds it. `clip` cuts a meeting curve where the curve itself starts and ends: the seam of a closed spline, which cannot be followed across it, and the joint between two pieces of one trace where the box of the two faces cut the loop (*The kayak Jig at its own tolerance: the seam of a closed curve* above made such a cut one with a place within the guard of it; any other stays a cut of its own). Where the trace began in the half of the loop that is kept, that cut becomes a vertex of the result. (Which of the two it is here, the seam or a joint, was not traced: no build was at hand; either is where the trace began.) Where the marcher seeds a loop is not a matter of the design: the shafts 0.1.3 built, their keyways' centres 2.2e-16 off the axis, have no vertex there, and the shafts built from the exact values have (read from the two builds' bodies; the seeding itself was not traced either). A shaft made here by the same commands (a cylinder less an extruded slot, in millimetres at 0.01 mm) has such a vertex with its slot's centre on the axis and 2.2e-16, 1e-13 and 1e-9 off it alike, 14.9° round one end: 15 edges and 11 vertices where the design has 14 and 10 (the released 0.2.1). (The review, 2026-11-20: that shaft has its 15 edges and 11 vertices on 0.1.3 as well as on 0.2.1 and on a build of the repair above, with the slot's centre on the axis, 2.2e-16 and 1e-9 off it: such a vertex is older than 0.2, and it is the Jig's shafts that did not have one before.)

*Is the edge right?* The body is valid and the design exactly: two edges on one curve where one would do. But the vertex is an artefact of the trace, not of the design: the same design gets one more or one fewer edge with the last bit of a coordinate, an edge a later feature names (a fillet of the rim) is then a piece of what it was, and a vertex next to a place where the curve touches another body's border is what met the gap above.

**Owed:** a stretch that runs across the seam of a closed meeting curve, or across the joint of two pieces of one trace, as one edge (the pieces joined into one curve there, where the trace is as smooth as anywhere else on it), so that where a trace began is a cut only where it is a place. Not built here: it changes the edges of results that are built today (every closed meeting curve whose seam is kept), an expectation change of the corpus digest and of the documents' counts that wants its own measurement.

### A spar's face beside a rib's skin: marching stalled (2026-11-20, the RC wing Jig; `geom` › `intersect/march.rs`; the stall repaired the same day, the fit's refusal on 2026-11-22)

*Found* with the gear reducer's point (above): eight of the 133 points of the RC wing Jig's range cannot measure *Spars clear of the ribs* since 0.2.0, where 0.1.3 passed them without proving the gap. All eight reproduce with the released 0.2.1 on a copy of the Jig (`param.set` to the listed values, then `measure.clearance` of each spar and the ribs): seven are refused *"marching an intersection curve stalled (the surfaces are nearly tangent along it, or one of them has a crease)"*, the eighth *"two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart (at the edge of tolerance)"*; each names the outer face of a spar (the bottom spar's bottom, the top spar's top) and a face of the ribs. The nearest places found are 0.027 to 0.05 mm apart (the spar's fit in its slot, 0.01 mm the tolerance).

*Another gap than the reducer's.* Neither refusal is one of the two above, and neither comes from `clip`'s reading of a stretch: the first is the marcher's (`craft3d_geom`), raised before any curve is clipped; the repair above does not reach it (the tests of that repair have no such pair).

*Reduced* (2026-11-20, with the released program, no build): the bottom spar and the ribs written to STEP at the first listed point and read into a new document at 0.01 mm give twelve bodies, the spar and eleven ribs; `document.interference` of them refuses the spar against nine of the eleven ribs, each *"marching an intersection curve stalled … (face 3 of the first body, face 4 of the second body)"*: two bodies of 6 and 14 faces and one command. (`measure.clearance` of the same pair answers *clear*: there the distance is proven first and the kernel is not asked; in the Jig the ribs are one body of eleven lumps, the proof stays at 0 and the kernel is asked.) The faces: the spar's bottom, a plane that rises 0.003 along the span, and the rib's lower skin behind the spar's notch, a strip 4 mm wide of the lofted wing's skin (the ribs are slices of the wing, which is swept back 30° and tapered at this point: a spline surface from the root's outline to the tip's). The spar lies in a notch cut through the skin with its bottom about level with it: 0.055 mm inside the skin's surface at the notch's front edge, 0.15 mm outside it at the rear edge, so the plane crosses the skin's surface inside the notch, where the skin was cut away, at about 0.85° (the skin's slope there, 0.0148), and the skin's two faces end 0.055 and 0.15 mm from the plane at the notch's edges.

*A pair made here* (by commands, on the released 0.2.1 at 0.01 mm; not yet a test): a wing blank lofted from an outline with a spline lower side to the same outline at 0.7 or 0.9 of its size 1000 mm further on, a notch cut along it, a rib the 4 mm slice of it, and a long block in the notch, its outer face 0.02 mm inside the skin's lowest line and rising 0.003 along the span. `document.interference` and the intersection of the two are refused by the marcher as well, in other words: *"an intersection curve could not be fitted within the tolerance"*, naming the block's outer face and the rib's skin. With the outer face 0, 0.01, 0.03 or 0.04 mm in, or a rise of 0, 0.001 or 0.006, or the blank at 0.5 or 0.68 of its size, or extruded and not lofted, the same pair is answered (nothing in common): as scattered as the Jig's eight points among its 133. The stall's own words were not reached by a pair made here.

**Owed:** the trace in the marcher, of the stall on the reduced pair (the bodies are a published Jig's and are not in this repository; the workshop keeps them, with the commands that made them) and of the fit on the pair made here; that pair as a test, and one that stalls the same way; the fix; and, apart from the kernel, a clearance that proves the gap of a body of several lumps as it does lump by lump, so that the kernel is not asked where 0.0475 mm is proven for each rib alone.


*Traced and repaired: a point held to the curve where the surfaces cross at a shallow angle* (2026-11-20, `craft3d_geom` › `intersect/march.rs` › `held`, `correct`, `run`; the branch `check/marcher-shallow`).

*The eight are two causes.* Seven are the marcher's stall, one question each: the skin of every rib is a face on the whole lofted skin, a ruled spline surface from root to tip, and a freeform face's box holds its surface's whole control net, so the box the pair is asked in is the whole spar's bottom, the same for every rib (the nine refusals of the reduced pair are one answer remembered; ribs 1 and 2 are not asked, their covers being apart). The eighth (the seventh listed point) is not the marcher's: it is the bottom spar's bottom against the flat side of one rib of eighteen, plane against plane, where the two corners of the notch's mouth on that side lie 1.06 and 2.04 tolerances from the bottom's plane (read from the two bodies written to STEP at that point). It is unchanged by this repair and by the border's (*Still refused*, below).

*What 0.1.3 did.* The same path with the same numbers in the kernel: 0.1.3 refuses the reduced pair's `document.interference` with the stall's words too, and the pairs made here by commands at the same settings as 0.2.1. It answered the Jig's eight points (*clear*, 0.027 to 0.05 mm, on the Jig's earlier build, which it can open) because its `measure.clearance` did not ask the kernel where the distance found was more than the tolerance; 0.2 asks where the gap is not proven.

*Where it stalls* is not at the rib and not at the 0.85°: it is at the wing's root. The spar's bottom all but holds a ruling of the skin (at the first listed point the ruling at `u` = 0.6918; the plane is 4e-5 mm from the skin's root border at `u` = 0.6945, and the signed distance has its saddle about a centimetre beyond that border, by its values along it). The plane crosses the skin in one curve that runs from the tip down one side of that ruling, turns round 1.1 mm inside the root border and runs back up the other side (13 mm apart at the third rib, where the earlier account measured them). At the turn the curve bends with a radius of 0.016 mm, 1.6 tolerances, and the surfaces cross at a sine of 3.3e-5. A marched point was brought to within `on` of the other surface (`max(1e-4 tol, 64 ε |p|)`, 1e-6 mm here): on both surfaces to that, but only within `on / sin θ` of the curve along the chart, 0.03 mm at this sine, twice the turn's radius. The march holds a step to its own length and to the turn of the tangent at its end; with its points anywhere across a band wider than the turn, no step, however short, landed where it was held to, and at the least step the march stalled. Whether it stalled depended on where the steps happened to fall: in a port of `run` on the two surfaces, 6 of 36 marches from seeds along the curve stalled at the turn and the others went round it, and of the Jig's 133 points eight are listed.

*The same weakness answered wrongly, quietly.* The closed-form pair of the tests (a trough against a plane, below) showed what a march that went round such a turn by luck gives: of 180 pairs, 80 stalled and 40 came back with a curve 1.0 to 2.2 tolerances off the parabola it should be, each point of it on both surfaces to 1e-4 of the tolerance. A curve near both surfaces is not the curve where they cross, where they are nearly tangent; the fit's check of a span's middle against "a point of the intersection" (`deviation`) used a point placed the same way.

*Method.* A point of a branch is held to the curve, not only to the field: `correct` goes on from the first place within `on` until `|f| ≤ held`, and `held` is `on` where a point within `on` of the field is within a sixteenth of the tolerance of the curve along the chart (`on ≤ tol sin θ / 16`: a sine of 1.6e-3, 0.09°, at the usual `on`), and `on sin θ` below that, which is within `on` of the curve, but no less than what rounding leaves of `f` at the size of the box (`64 ε |p|`, the floor `on` itself has; since 2026-11-21 at the size of the place and of the surfaces' own numbers: *A surface far larger than the box*, below). Above that sine nothing changes: the first place within `on` is returned as before, bit for bit. Where the nearer place is not reached (the surfaces tangent there, `f` without a slope to follow: a fillet's border on the face it rounds), the first place within `on` is returned, as before, so what probes a place (a stretch along a border, the box, the field's edge) answers as it did. A step of the march must end on the curve: one whose end was not brought to `held` is not a step (past the turn of a hairpin there is no curve to come onto, and a point 0.3 mm short of the turn was taken for one at a sine of 3e-6); the step is halved, as for any other miss, and the step's last resort (a step scaled anew where the pace of the parameters jumps) takes only a step that ended on the curve. *How far it reaches.* The rule names no kind of surface (the field is a plane, an analytic surface or a projection onto a freeform one alike), and above its sine it changes nothing: measured by the review, every place of the corpus below the rule's sine lies in 132 cases of the families *near* and *tangent*, and the other 3 050 cases meet none in 20 million evaluations. It was **not** general in size: the floor was that of the pair's box, `64 ε |box|`, which holds only where both surfaces are computed to that, and a surface whose centre or radius is thousands of boxes away is not (mended on 2026-11-21: *A surface far larger than the box*, below). And a curve is within the tolerance of the true crossing in the tests' range, not everywhere: at a sine of 1e-5 the closed-form pair comes back 1.13 tolerances off (the fit holds a span's middle to half a tolerance across, not every place of the span). The pairs of the review's wider grid that came back 1.004 to 1.012 tolerances off at 3.3e-5 (Check 44833214463) stood 1e10 tolerances from the origin, where `on` is the floor itself and the repair does nothing: they say what rounding leaves that far out, not how far the repair reaches.

*Held to.* `march.rs`: a point within `on` at a steep crossing is left where it is (at a sine of 0.5 and at 2e-3, just above the rule's), one at 1.2e-3 is brought from 400 `on` off the curve to within `on` of it, one that cannot be brought nearer is kept, none is asked nearer than rounding leaves (a million from the origin, where `on` is the floor), a place without a normal is held to `on`. `crates/geom/tests/meet_shallow.rs`: the crossing in closed form, a trough `z = a (y − y0) − x² a / (2 r)` (a Bézier patch of degree two by one, and the same as a spline of two spans, half of them twisted as a tapered loft is and two in three askew to the axes) against the plane `z = 0`, which cross in a parabola that turns with the radius `r` at the sine `a`, `y0` from the trough's end: sines 1e-4 and 3.3e-5, radii of 1.6, 0.4 and 0.1 tolerances, 30, 110 and 500 tolerances from the end, at tolerances from 1e-8 to 10 (the lengths are in tolerances: troughs from millimetres to thousands of kilometres); every point of the curve is held to the parabola along the surface and the parabola to the curve from side to side, one open curve. 180 pairs: before, 80 stalled and 40 were more than a tolerance off (Check 44808224846); after, none, at most 0.704 tolerances off (Check 44818014159). The same trough walked against a flat spline (the field a projection): 6 pairs, at most 0.610. The spar's bottom against the lofted skin of the engine's test in its own numbers (a cubic of seven control points lofted to 0.7 of its size, or extruded), every curve held to a change of the sign of the skin's distance to the plane within the tolerance along the skin, and every crossing of a hundred sections found by halving held to a curve, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3: 15 pairs, of which 9 stalled before and 3, the Jig's own proportions, came back more than a tolerance off the crossing (Check 44812544680). `crates/engine/tests/shallow_crossing.rs`: the pair by commands, in millimetres at 0.01 mm (a blank extruded or lofted to 0.7 from an outline with a spline lower side, a notch, a rib sliced from it, a spar in the notch whose bottom follows the skin's lowest line and rises off it at 1e-4, 3.3e-5 or 3e-6, through it 1.1 to 30 mm from the root): `measure.clearance` clear by the spar's fit of 0.03 mm (the distance, no boolean), `document.interference` clear, the intersection empty, and the union two shells with the faces, edges and vertices of both and their volume within the measure's own bound. Seven settings; five answered *unknown*, "marching an intersection curve stalled", before (the stall's own words by a pair made by commands: Check 44806472100). Each part against its tests: with a step taken whether or not it ends on the curve, the skin's pairs at 3e-6 stall again (Check 44816367069). The same rule in the step's last resort was first left out, no test needing it; the review measured what that cost, and it is back with a test that does: a cylinder's side and a ball of 1e5 sunk 3 tolerances, in the two boxes a boolean asks them in, take 2 632 and 2 428 evaluations to the stall with the rule and 2 632 and 210 938 without (Checks 44837856304 and 44837861033; that pair is answered since 2026-11-21, and the rule is held by `march.rs` › `a_march_that_comes_to_where_it_cannot_place_the_curve_says_so_at_once`, a far cylinder: with the last resort taking such a step it fails, Check 44895430627). The first place within `on` rather than a later one is held by `of_places_within_on_none_of_them_held_the_first_is_returned`; where an evaluation fails or the parameters are no numbers after a place within `on` was found, that place is returned (before the repair it had been returned already, so the answer there is the one it was; with none found yet there is none, as before). Nothing measured takes either exit and no test holds them: they are spelled the way that keeps the earlier answer. The fall-back itself (the first place within `on` where the nearer one is not reached) is held by those unit tests and by nothing else: with no place returned there, the twelve test files of the surface meetings and the corpus's digest are the same (Checks 44836498692 and 44836502375), and by the review's table the tests of this repair too. It is kept so that what probes a place of true tangency answers as it did on everything not measured here; taking it out would also make the step's own rule redundant, and belongs with the sizing of the floor.

*What moved.* The boolean corpus's report is the same line for line on the base (`main` 448d4cc2, Check 44814512378) and on the repair (Check 44818054529): 11594 agreeing, no outcome moved, but for its digest, `7927eb7a078af68e` to `40e78253b2aa3546`. Case by case (the corpus made to print every result, Checks 44815582702 and 44815577644): 114 of 9859 results are other bits, the three operations of 38 cases, all of the families *near* (faces a tolerance apart) and *tangent*, where curves pass crossings shallower than the rule's sine; none changed its vertices, edges, faces or the hash of its shape, and no volume moved by more than 1.6e-7 of itself. Every other result is the same bits. One expectation changed, by the repair's first trial Gate (44818008183, where it was the only test binary of the workspace that failed; the benchmark stayed within its baseline, its exact counts unchanged): `crates/engine/tests/hunt_checks_distance.rs` held a known wrong answer, a ball of radius 10 000 sunk 3 and 10 tolerances into a cylinder's and a cone's side answered *clear* by `boolean::contact`, and was written to fail when that changed. Both are answered *overlap* now and both of its assertions fail, as the closed form in the test has it (Check 44824223991): the ball crosses the side in a loop half a unit across at a sine of a few 1e-5, the same weakness; the test holds the right answer ([`verification.md`](verification.md) › *Answered clear though sharing volume*).

*Still refused.*

- **A step of the march onto the curve beside the one it follows** (the pair the finding above made and three of the settings above: *"an intersection curve could not be fitted within the tolerance"*): mended on 2026-11-22, below, with what was written here and what is owed now.
- **A curved surface along the trough: "the surfaces are tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area to intersect".** The closed-form trough against a cylinder that lies along it, and with the flat patch walked and the trough measured against, never reaches the march: the bound on how far the distance can bend takes the bend across the trough for a bend along it as well, so no cell along the trough is proven to hold one crossing at most until it is millionths of the trough long (the plane, which does not bend, is proven at once). The limit of *Surfaces side by side* above, met from another side; the ignored test `a_curved_surface_crosses_a_ruled_patch_in_a_hairpin_at_a_shallow_angle` (12 pairs, 3.4 s each).
- **The eighth point: "two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart".** Plane against plane (above), at a corner of the notch's mouth between one and two tolerances from the plane of the spar's bottom, 0.37 mm beyond the end of the spar's own face there. Two pairs made by commands (a long bar askew beside a block, and beside a prism whose bottom slopes up from the corner at 0.3 and at 0.015, the bar's bottom 0.1 to 3 tolerances above and below the corner, 5 tolerances beside it) were all answered by the released 0.2.1. Reduced and repaired on 2026-11-23, amended after two reviews (*A spar's flat bottom beside a corner of a notch*, below): not the rule's refusal, but the rule asked of two places outside the spar's face where the whole spar is 34 tolerances from them.
- **A surface far larger than the box** (found by the review of this repair, 2026-11-20): mended on 2026-11-21, below, with what is still refused there.
- **A sine below what rounding leaves.** Where `64 ε |p| / sin θ` is itself wider than the curve's turn (a turn a tenth of a tolerance across at a sine of a few 1e-6, a thousand units from the origin at a tolerance of 1e-6), a point cannot be placed better and the march stalls as before, with its words.
- As listed above and untouched: a clearance that proves the gap of a body of several lumps lump by lump.

*Confirmed on a build* (the coordinator, 2026-11-20, a build of 65e1cec5): the wing Jig's range 132 of 133 points, the seven listed points clear, the reduced pair's `document.interference` with no pair *unknown* (nine before), the eighth point as it was; the 112 published items answer as on 0.2.1.

*A surface far larger than the box: traced and mended* (2026-11-21, `craft3d_geom` › `intersect/field.rs` › `ball_distance`, `Field::scale`, `Chart::bounds_in`; `march.rs` › `floor_at`, `placed`, `closes_on`; `meet.rs` › `numeric`; the branch `check/marcher-floor`; [`verification.md`](verification.md) › *Answered clear though sharing volume*).

*What the review found.* Against an analytic surface whose centre or radius is thousands of boxes away (a ball of radius 2e4 to 2e5 by a part of millimetres, at a tolerance of 1e-6), the repair above told fewer right answers than the marcher before it, and one wrongly. Its sweep, a cone 4 by 12 and a cylinder of radius 4 against balls of radius 1e3 to 1e6 from 10 tolerances outside the side to 1e5 inside (242 pairs by commands; 182 share volume 3 tolerances deep or more): before the repair 108 right, 61 not told, 13 clear (wrong); the repair 110, 70, 2. Eight right answers had become *"marching an intersection curve stalled"*, and one had become wrong: the cone and a ball of 2e4 sunk 5 tolerances, answered *clear* with no step turned down.

*The wrong pair, traced* (Check 44846308538, the marcher made to tell its seeds, its steps and its branches). It is not the floor and not the touch rule: 23 cells look for a critical point and none finds a touch, no disc is made, and of the 95 steps turned down none is turned down for being off the curve. The cells give 900 seeds; the first, at the loop's lower tip, is brought to 1.6e-11 of the cone at a sine of 2.6e-5 and is marched: 186 points, *closed*; every other seed lies on that branch; one closed curve comes back, 3.5788 long. The loop is 0.894 long along the side: 1.789 round. **The march went round the loop twice**: it passed its own start without seeing it and closed on it the second time round. A closed curve twice round divides nothing, and the boolean reads the pair as having nothing in common. (The same ball sunk 5.5 tolerances comes back 1.8767 long, once round, and is an overlap.) The other wrong answer of the sweep is the same: the cylinder and a ball of 3e4 sunk 5 tolerances, seeded at the loop's tip, 185 points, closed, 4.3834 long round a loop of 2.191. A step closes on the start where the start lies ahead within the step, heading the same way, and no further beside the step's line than a fifth of the step and `on`. At a tip the loop turns within 47 and 73 tolerances, so the steps there are a few tolerances long; and a point lay anywhere across a band 2.5 and 5.5 tolerances wide about the curve (held to `64 ε |box|`, 6.7e-11 and 1.0e-10 in boxes grown by 900 and 1350 for the ball, at sines of 2.6e-5 and 1.8e-5): room enough for the start to lie beside the step's line by more than the test allowed. (That last step is read from the numbers, not traced; what is measured is the curve twice round, and that with the points placed no march of the sweep passes its start.) Over the sweep the repair marched 21 closed branches past their start (counted by Check 44849005851).

*The stalls.* In the smaller box a boolean asks after the larger fails, the floor is that of a box of a few units, 3e-13, and a ball of 1e5 tells its distance, `|p − c| − r`, in steps of 1.5e-11: a point is on the curve only where the distance rounds to exactly 0, and step after step is turned down. Where the ball was the surface walked (against a cone it was), its own points are rounded at its size as well.

*Method.* Five parts, each needed by a test (the table below).

1. *A far ball's distance without the loss* (`field.rs` › `ball_distance`). Where a ball's centre and radius together are more than 8 times the size of the places asked, its distance is `(|p − c|² − r²) / (|p − c| + r)`, the difference of the squares summed exactly: each `p − c` with what rounding took from it (Knuth's two-sum), each square likewise (Dekker's product), the large terms added first with their errors kept. A place near the surface is then told its distance to the last bits of the place itself, where the plain difference is rounded at the size of the radius (1e-11 at 1e5). Only a ball's: a far cylinder, cone, torus or plane still tells its distance at its own size.
2. *The floor is that of the place and of the surfaces' own numbers* (`march.rs` › `floor_at`): `64 ε (|p| + reach)`, `reach` being the larger of what the two surfaces' evaluations add and subtract beyond the place (`Field::scale`: a plane's origin, an axis point and its radius, an apex, the furthest control point; nothing for a ball measured without the loss). Of the place, not of the box: the box of a pair with a ball of 1e5 is grown by thousands, and at its size the floor was `on` itself. `on` is the box's alone, as it was (first sized by the surfaces too, and their numbers summed; the review of this round, below).
3. *Against a surface far larger than the box, a place that cannot be placed is not on the curve* (`placed`). Where the surfaces' numbers are more than twice the box's, and what the far surface's distance is told to leaves a point more than half a tolerance from the curve along the chart at the sine the surfaces cross at, a step that ends there is not a step, and a march that can go no further for it refuses in words of its own: *"an intersection curve cannot be placed within the tolerance where the surfaces cross this shallowly: one of them is so much larger than the part that the nearest a point can be brought to the curve is more than half the tolerance"*. This is the review's rule that left no answer wrong; with the first two parts it refuses nothing of the sweep, and it is what a far surface that is not a ball is refused by. (First asked of every place, by 64 units of rounding: the review of this round, below.)
4. *A march closes on its start within the room its points have* (`closes_on`): beside the step's line by a fifth of the step, `on`, and a tolerance: half a tolerance for each of the two points, which is as far from the curve as `placed` lets a point be. Where the start is seen today nothing changes; this is the traced cause itself.
5. *A cone is walked rather than a far ball, and bounded by its width over the part asked* (`meet.rs` › `numeric`, `field.rs` › `Chart::bounds_in`). A ball's own points are rounded at its size whatever is done to its distance, so where the other surface is a cone the cone is walked and the ball measured (a ball was walked against a cone, and against a spline surface with a pole at a corner of its parameters, which is left as it was). A cone's chart had the bounds of its wider end in the whole box, which in a box grown by thousands is hundreds wide: its speed and bend around the axis are now bounded by its radius over the cell asked, a bound still (the radius is linear along the cone). Without it the same answers cost 19 times the evaluations and 45 questions of the sweep ran into the cap.

*The sweep, as a test.* `crates/geom/tests/meet_far.rs`: the review's pairs, both sides of zero, each asked in the two boxes a boolean asks it in (484 questions). The truth is the placement: the ball's distance is taken exactly in the test, the loop is found along rays from its middle by halving, and a curve must lie within a tolerance of the crossing along the side (not only on both surfaces, which are within the tolerance of each other all over such a loop), pass every direction in which the loop is in the box, and pass it once. 480 right, 4 refused, none wrong (0.503 tolerances from the crossing at most; 134 million evaluations); with every part off, 237 right, 183 refused, 64 wrong (63 with a curve 2 to 12.6 tolerances from the crossing, one twice round). By commands (the 242 pairs, a throwaway test on the same code, Check 44895432880): overlaps 179 right, 3 not told, none wrong (the repair: 110, 70, 2); touches 20 of 20 (14); clear 30 of 30; 70.5 million evaluations where the repair took 196 million. `crates/engine/tests/hunt_checks_distance.rs` holds the pair that had turned wrong and the eight that had turned to stalls as overlaps (`a_ball_tens_of_thousands_of_times_the_body_sunk_into_a_side_overlaps`, no longer ignored; the pin of the wrong answer is gone).

*Each part against the tests* (Check 44895430627; the sweep's numbers are right, refused, wrong of 484, by the oracle before its band at the edge of tolerance, which counts one pair of that band wrong on every line):

| Without | Unit tests that fail | The sweep |
|---|---|---|
| nothing | none | 479, 4, 1 |
| the distance without the loss | the far ball's distance | 107, 377, 0 |
| the floor of the place and the surfaces (the box's instead) | the march that cannot place, the place | 313, 170, 1 |
| `placed` | the march that cannot place, the place | 479, 4, 1 |
| the room in the closing test | the closing test | 477, 4, 3: two loops marched twice round |
| the cone walked (the far ball instead) | none | 297, 187, 0 |
| the cone bounded over the part | none | 431, 50, 3; 2 582 million evaluations, 45 at the cap |
| the rule in the step's last resort (the first round's) | the march that cannot place | 479, 4, 1 |

(The table is of the round as it was first handed in, where `placed` was asked of every place and `on` was sized by the surfaces too; what the review changed, and its own table, are below.) `placed` is held by the unit tests and by the cylinder of a million below: with a ball's distance exact nothing of the sweep reaches it, and with the plain difference it is what turns 377 questions into refusals and none into a wrong answer. The tangency fall-back of `correct` (the first place within `on` where no nearer one is reached) stays as it was, held by its unit tests: nothing measured here takes it.

*The review of this round: ordinary parts far from the origin, and a part by a cylinder of a million* (2026-11-21, the same day; its tables are kept by the workshop). The round as first handed in turned right answers into refusals. (1) Two ordinary bodies a million from the origin were refused outright, *"the box has no size, or the tolerance is finer than rounding allows at its size"*: `on` was sized by the surfaces' numbers, and those were summed, so two surfaces of a million read as two million, past what the box's own rule allows at 1.4 million. (2) A ball sunk 100 tolerances into a cylinder's side 100 m from the origin, crossing it at a sine of 0.005, was refused as not placed: `placed` judged every place by 64 units of rounding at the place's size and the surfaces' together, 2.8e-9 there, where rounding leaves a hundredth of a tolerance along the curve. (3) A ball of 5 in the wall of a cylinder of radius 1e6, and a cylinder of 5 through it, were refused outright in the box's words, for the cylinder's own numbers; the box was fine. All three are answered on `main`, rightly (the subtract's volume to nine digits at every distance).

The rule taken (the coordinator's, for the developer to confirm): a pair that was answered rightly keeps its answer; a refusal is for where the curve cannot be held, and says what is true there. So: `reach` is the larger of the two surfaces' numbers, not their sum; `on` is the box's alone, as on `main`, so no pair is refused outright for a surface's size; and `placed` is asked only against a far surface (numbers more than twice the box's), and judges by what a point is in fact brought to: as near as it is held where the floor is within `on`, and where the floor is above `on` (the point is then asked to `on`, as it always was) `on` and what the far surface's distance is told to, 4 units in the last place of its numbers (an estimate: a few additions and a root). A part far from the origin is under the box's own rule alone: a point brought to `on` there is on the curve, as it always was. **What that leaves as it was, not mended:** a million from the origin `on` is a seventieth of the tolerance, and at a sine of 1e-3 a point brought no nearer than that is, by what is asked of it, up to 14 tolerances from the curve along the chart; in fact Newton leaves it at what rounding leaves, a hundredth of a tolerance, and the answers are right; but nothing proves it, and the round as first handed in refused there.

*Held to* (Checks 44904602736, 44904605401 and, for the unit tests under each line of the table, 44905730357). `crates/engine/tests/hunt_checks_distance.rs` › `ordinary_bodies_far_from_the_origin_are_answered_as_at_the_origin`: eight pairs (a cylinder through a block, a sphere in a block, a lofted body against a block, a cylinder across a cylinder, a sphere in a cylinder's side, and a sphere sunk 1e-4, 1e-3 and 1e-2 into a cylinder's side) at ten distances from 0 to 1.3 km along (0.6, 0.64, 0.48), at a tolerance of 1e-6: clearance and interference say overlap and the subtract has its volume at the origin to 1e-7 of itself, 80 rows of 80 (the review's far-from-the-origin tables, and more). `a_small_part_in_the_wall_of_a_cylinder_of_a_million_is_answered`: the three pairs of (3), overlap and a subtract of the cylinder's volume; a ball sunk 1e-5 to 0.01 into the same wall is an overlap from 1e-4 and not told at 1e-5 (the interference in the words above), never clear. `crates/geom/tests/meet_far.rs`: the same wall in closed form (its distance without the loss): the ball half in it and the cylinder through it come back as one closed curve, once round, within half a tolerance of the crossing, in both boxes a boolean asks; the ball sunk 0.01 and 0.001 (sines of 0.063 and 0.02) likewise; sunk 1e-4 it is met in the small box and refused in the large, and sunk 1e-5 and 3e-6 it is refused in both, in those words and no others. `march.rs`: against a cylinder of 3e4 a place is on the curve at a sine of 1e-3 and not at 1e-4, and a part a million out is not judged; two ordinary surfaces a million out are not a far surface.

| Without | Tests that fail |
|---|---|
| nothing | none (the sweep 480, 4, 0; 133 837 900 evaluations) |
| the larger of the surfaces' numbers (their sum instead) | `two_ordinary_surfaces_a_million_from_the_origin_are_not_a_far_surface` |
| `placed` only against a far surface (everywhere instead) | the unit test of what is judged, and that of the two ordinary surfaces; the bodies far from the origin |
| `on` the box's alone (the surfaces' rounding too) | the two unit tests of the far cylinder; the part in the wall, in geom and in the engine; the ball sunk a little into the wall |
| a place judged by what the distance is told to (by the floor instead) | the unit test of what is judged; the ball sunk a little into the wall |
| all four (the round as first handed in) | all of those |

The sweep is the same under every line (480, 4, 0), and so are the three tests of the shallow crossing: what the review changed is outside them.

*Not built: two loops close together.* A test of the closing rule's room with two crossings a few tolerances apart (two loops whose tips all but meet come back as two curves, each once round) is owed: between such loops the distance has a saddle within a few tolerances of zero, which the marcher reads as a crossing point with four arms where it is within the tolerance, so the pair needs its own closed form and a trace before it is a test. What is measured instead (the review's count, Check 44902225497): of the corpus's 754 closings none is made by the room alone, and of the sweep's 359, two (the loops a ball of 1e6 crosses the side in a third of a tolerance deep, which without the room are marched twice round).

*What moved* (the round as it stands, against `main` before it). The boolean corpus's report is the same line for line but for its digest, `40e78253b2aa3546` to `35e8fbc97343ca2d` (11 594 agreeing; Checks 44833214463 and 44904602736; the round as first handed in gave `c7e2e8a5a0afe8ca`). Case by case, 72 of 9 859 results are other bits: 18, the three operations of 6 cases of the family *near*, from the floor of the place (no vertex, edge or face count, no shape's hash and no volume moved); and 54, the three operations of 18 cases of cone against cone, from the cone's bounds (the cells fall otherwise and the curves are fitted through other points: 15 with a vertex moved in its last digits, no count moved, no volume by more than 4.6e-8 of itself). The benchmark's counts are the same but one: the engine block's `march_evaluations`, 915 531 to 915 527 (Checks 44846784316 and 44904605401).

*On the published items* (the coordinator's passes on one build of each round, against 0.2.1). On the round as merged (def850aa, round three): the RC wing Jig's range 132 of 133 and its seven points clear, as after the first repair; all 33 Jigs' ranges 3 537 of 3 538 points (the wing's one); **all 112 published items the same, every printed line and the STEP export's bytes**; the review's three far-from-origin scripts (66 rows: ordinary pairs to 1.3 km from the origin, parts in the wall of a cylinder of 1e6 and 2e6) the same as on the first repair, row for row. On the round as first handed in (6be14804) one item differed in last digits only, the radial engine assembly: two bodies' volume, area, centroid and inertia from the 15th digit on, two clearances in their 16th digit, the STEP export's bytes, and at a tie the nearest face a clearance's detail names (`F14:copy(0)/F2:side(e6)` became `F14:copy(0)/F3:chamfer(1,5)`), every verdict the same; it was put down to the cone's bound, cone against cone. On round three the assembly is the same as 0.2.1 again though the cone's bound is unchanged, so that account was wrong or incomplete: what that round took back (`on` no less than the surfaces' rounding, the floor's reach as a sum) moved it, or moved it together with the bound. Not traced. The corpus's 54 cone-against-cone results with other bits stand as measured on the merged code; no published item shows them.

*Owed, as a choice: the cone's bound only where the old one fails.* The bound over the part is never larger than the old one, but it moves a result wherever it settles a cell one size sooner: the leaves differ, and with them the seeds. It can be kept from moving any result the old bound reached, the way the bound of surfaces side by side is (`Marcher::bounded`: asked for only where the first march ran out of cells, so that what was traced before is traced the same way): march with the bound of the whole box first, and with the bound over the part only where that ran out of cells or of evaluations. Every cone against a cone would then be bit for bit what it was (the radial engine, the corpus's 54 results), and the price falls on the pairs that were refused before: a cone against a far ball in a box grown by thousands would first spend the million cells it runs out of, about as much again as the 0.5 to 2 million evaluations it takes now. Worth doing if results that stay bit for bit across releases are worth a second march on pairs that only this round answers; not measured, and not done in this round.
