*Still refused, or owed.*

- **What a far surface's distance is told to, as a budget of 4 ulp, is an estimate** (the reread of round three, 2026-11-21). By the arithmetic of each field's distance it covers the analytic kinds (a plane about 3 ulp of |p − origin|, a plain sphere 2.5, a cylinder and a torus 4 to 4.5, a cone about 5; typical values 1 to 2; a place is past one tolerance only at about twice the budget; measured at the edge, a ball sunk 1e-4 into the wall of a cylinder of 1e6: 0.0157 tolerances from the true circle against the rule's own 0.30). It is not shown for a far spline, whose evaluation can accumulate about 1.5 (n + m) ulp of its control net's size (9 for a bicubic), and no test has a spline whose net is far larger than the box. **Owed:** a far spline judged by 2 (n + m) + 2 ulp, or by the 64-ulp floor, until a test measures it; 8 ulp for the analytic kinds if the worst case is to hold half a tolerance. On `main` before this repair such a place was not judged at all.
- **A far surface that is not a ball, crossed at a shallow angle.** A cylinder, cone, torus, plane or spline surface whose own numbers are thousands of times the part's tells its distance at its own size; where that leaves a point more than half a tolerance from the curve at the sine it crosses the part at, the march refuses in the words above. Against a cylinder of 3e4 that is below a sine of 3e-4; against one of 1e6, below 4e-3 in the part's own box (a ball of 5 sunk 1e-5 into its wall) and below 9e-3 in the box grown by a share of the cylinder's (sunk 1e-4). Crossed more steeply it is answered. Owed: their distances without the loss, as the ball's.
- **A loop that ends on the cone's apex** (a ball of 1e5 sunk 200 tolerances, one of 2e5 sunk 100): *"surfaces that touch along a curve, or with more than second-order contact, without a closed form"*, as before. And by commands, a ball of 1e6 sunk 10 tolerances into the cylinder, whose loop runs out over the cylinder's rim: *"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with"*, the boolean's, not the marcher's.
- **A crossing read as a touch at a loop's tips.** The marcher takes a place for a touch where the gradient of the distance is below `TOUCH · tol · |H|` (4 tolerances times the curvature): a ball of 1e6 sunk 1.5 tolerances into the cone crosses it at a sine of 1.7e-6 at the loop's tips, and comes back as the loop and two touching points. At that depth a boolean refuses the pair as neither touching nor safely crossing, and the sweep's test does not hold points against a loop there. Deeper in it was not met on this code (with the cone bounded by its width in the whole box the same ball sunk 3 and 10 tolerances came back with a touching point beside its loop: where the cells fall decides), and larger balls were not asked. Owed: the touch rule held against a loop it lies on.
- **What a cone costs.** A cone against a far ball takes 0.5 to 2 million evaluations where a cylinder takes 2 to 12 thousand: the bound on the twist of a cone's chart (`|S_uv|`, the tangent of its half-angle) does not know that the ball's normal is all but square to it, so along the loop's middle line no cell is proven to hold one crossing until it is very small. Fewer than before (the sweep's cones took 195 million with the ball walked), and owed all the same.

*A step of the march onto the curve beside the one it follows: traced and mended* (2026-11-22, `craft3d_geom` › `intersect/march.rs` › `trace`, `run_on`, `refine_to_edge`, `nearest_the_start`; the branch `check/march-beside`).

*What was owed* (2026-11-20, as it stood under *Still refused* above). The pair the finding made (the spar's bottom 0.02 mm inside the skin's lowest line, rising 0.003, the blank tapered to 0.7 or 0.9) and three of the settings above (tapered, 1e-4, through the lowest line 0.3 to 5 mm from the root; they stalled before the corrector's repair) were refused, *"an intersection curve could not be fitted within the tolerance"*. The section's spline does not only dip to its lowest point: beside it the section rises to a shoulder and dips again, so where the plane is level with the shoulder it crosses the skin in stretches side by side along the span, a few millimetres apart, that turn into one another. The march's step is bounded by the turn of the tangent at its end and by a thirty-second of the box (32 mm here): a step lands on the stretch beside, which heads the same way, and is taken; the fit then asks for points between two points that are not neighbours on one curve. Three ways were named, with their cost: (1) *a step held at its middle too* (a point half-way, by the same prediction and correction, between the two in the branch's own sense and within the turn of both): the direct mend, about half as many evaluations again per step, and every marched curve's points move in their last bits, so the corpus's digest and every count that follows the bodies move at once; (2) *the span marched again where the fit finds no middle*: bit for bit wherever the fit succeeds today, and a wrong step that the fit happens to accept stays undetected; (3) *the step bounded by what is near* (the size of the cells that were neither empty nor simple along the way): cheap at run time, but it leans on the cells' bounds, which are estimates for a spline chart.

*The rule it was built under* (the coordinator's, as for the round before): what `main` answers rightly keeps its answer, bit for bit where it can; a refusal only where the curve truly cannot be followed; never a wrong curve accepted. So the second way, and its named weakness answered by a count.

*Traced* (Checks 44991041889 and 44993423984: the marcher made to tell which span the fit gives up on, and to follow the curve from that span's first point in sixteenths of it, by the step's own rule). All 21 pairs of the test (seven settings at three scales, the same at each scale) are refused by the fit, each at one span, and each a step of the backward run: 11.7 to 32.2 mm long (the longest step is 32.1), its two points 4.2 to 7.3 mm apart across the skin and 9 to 32 along it. At six of the seven settings the curve followed from the first point comes to the second, after 41 to 565 mm: one curve, on which the two points are 2.8 to 23 such steps apart, as the finding had it. At the seventh (the blank extruded, the bottom rising 1e-4 through the lowest line 1.1 mm from the root) it leaves the skin after 320 mm without coming to it: two curves there. The fit gives up early, 102 to 1 686 evaluations after it began on the branch (the trace of 2026-11-20, of the code as it then stood, saw it run to the cap of 32 000 points).

*A second place a step lands beside, found by the tests made for this* (Checks 44993423984 and 44997015829). A corrugated sheet under a plane level with its crests (`crates/geom/tests/meet_beside.rs`, below) is refused in the same words, all 24 pairs, and there the wrong step is a run's last. The plane crosses the sheet in curves that begin and end on the sheet's near edge; one of them turns back 0.6 to 2.4 inside that edge (the sheet 100 long), where the plane is level with a trough. A march down that curve, in steps of up to 5.6, comes within 2 to 4 of the edge; its next step leaves the chart's rectangle, and `exit` puts the run's end on the border where Newton along the border finds a zero from where the step pointed: at a foot of the crossing 2.4 to 7.6 across. In 6 of the 24 pairs that is the far foot of the same curve, which the curve reaches seven such steps on; in the other 18 the curve followed from the step's first point leaves the sheet without coming to it (by the sheet's profile, not traced: the foot the curve began at in 12, a foot of another curve in 6). `exit` holds that place to nothing but being on the border and on the other surface. The same sheet with a shoulder between two of its crests has the wing's kind again, inside the sheet: 24 pairs refused, each at a step 2.3 to 5.2 long onto a stretch 0.9 to 2.4 across, on the same curve 4 or 23 such steps on in 12 pairs and on the other curve in 12.

*A third, found by the trial Gate: a step over a stretch where the curve is past the border of the surface walked* (Gate 44999231432, Check 45003717367). The sea kayak's coaming, the cockpit's ring where it lies inside the hull raised by its rim, was refused in the same words since 2026-10-27, and `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` held that refusal as owed. The ring's wall and the raised hull's skin cross in a curve that runs along the wall's lower edge, 0.01 to 0.06 mm inside it at the two points of a step of 37 mm (the longest), on which the fit gave up; the wall is the surface walked. Taken up from the first of the two, the run ends on the wall's border one step on, and what lies beyond is traced from its own seed. Read from that and from the kayak's other side, not followed on this one: the curve passes the wall's edge between the two points and comes back. (On the other side the hull's skin is walked and the wall measured against: the curve followed there leaves the wall's edge 19 mm on, 0.003 mm past it at most; the fit accepted that branch, dividing it at the edge as `refine_pieces` does where a middle point is past the other surface's edge, which is read from the code, not traced.) The coaming is built now, and the Gate's one failure of 719 test binaries was the test that expected the refusal.

*Method* (the second way, as built). Five parts.

1. *The fit says which span it gave up on* (`refine_to_edge`, `Marcher::unfitted`): of the points it was given, the two between which no point it could add brought the curve within the tolerance, whatever it had put between and before them by then.
2. *Where that span is a step of a run, the run is taken up again from the point before it* (`trace`, `run_on`, `Again::past`): on from that point, in the run's own sense, in steps of at most a quarter of the span until it has gone twice the span's length (a step ends no further than twice what it was asked, so what the wrong step passed over lies within that of its start), then as any run. What the run had marched beyond that step is dropped. Where that was another curve, its own seeds find it: a seed is left out only for lying on a branch that was kept. Where it was this curve further on, the run comes to it again. A run that closed and no longer does gets its backward run; one that now closes loses it.
3. *A backward run is put right from the wrong step nearest the start* (`nearest_the_start`). The fit meets a forward run's spans in the order of the march, so the one it gives up on is the first wrong step; a backward run's it meets from the far end. Taken up from a later wrong step a run follows the curve that step was made on and is as wrong as it was up to there, so what lies between the span found and the start is fitted on its own until no nearer span is given up on.
4. *Bounds.* A branch is taken up at most eight times; a span shorter than four least steps is not taken up; a span that is no step of a run (one drawn on into a cusp) is not. In each case the refusal stands, in the fit's words. What a run taken up again itself refuses with (a stall, a cap) is returned as it is.
5. *A pair is asked as before first* (`meet.rs` › `meet_surfaces`, `meet_surfaces_once`, `Marcher::once`; `craft3d_kernel` › `section.rs` › `meet_once_then_again`). `meet_surfaces` marches a pair the old way, by a march that refuses where its fit gives up, and a second time, taking branches up, only where that refusal was the fit's; the two are two questions on the shelf, the first under the words it always had. A boolean asks a pair in the room of its larger face and, where that refuses, in a smaller box and in a nearer one: it now asks every box the old way first; where every box refuses it asks, as it always did next, whether the faces lie on one another (`coincide`), and only where that does not know, which was a refusal, asks the boxes again, in the same order, by the march taken up (the order since the review, below: as first built the march taken up was asked before `coincide`). Where that refuses as well, the old way's first refusal stands, word for word. Without this part a pair the fit refused in the first box and a later box answered would have been answered in the first box, by other curves, and a pair whose first branch is now followed would be refused for a later branch in other words (random design 102900, below): right both, and not what `main` answers.

*Where it departs from the way as it was written down.* It does more in one place: the fifth part, which the way as written did not need to say, because it took a boolean's later boxes for granted. The run is not marched "to the other end" of the span: at one of the wing's seven settings, in 30 of the two sheets' 48 pairs and on the kayak the curve from the span's first point does not come to its second (another curve, this one behind the first point, or this one beyond a stretch off the chart), and a march held to arriving there would have had to refuse. Nor is the sense of travel read from the gradient at each end: a run is taken up in its own sense, which it has. *Only where the fit gives up:* a branch the fit brings within the tolerance is marched once, by the same evaluations in the same order as before, and comes back the same points bit for bit (`a_branch_the_fit_accepts_is_marched_once_and_is_the_same_bit_for_bit`).

*The named weakness, measured: a step onto the curve beside that the fit accepts.* A probe on a throwaway commit followed every step of every branch the fit accepted at its first pass: from the step's first point, in the step's own sense and by the step's own rule, in sixteenths of the step and again in 256ths, for up to three times the step's length, and asked whether it comes to the step's second point.

| Where | Branches fitted | Steps followed | Come to their end, both ways | Not | Two points at one place | Spans not followed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the boolean corpus (Heavy 44993426080) | 4 225 | 138 494 | 138 465 | 0 | 29 | 428 |
| the benchmark's five models (Heavy 44993428365) | 774 | 22 572 | 22 572 | 0 | 0 | 520 |
| the tests of this finding, on `main`'s behaviour (Checks 44993423984 and 44997015829) | 501 | 39 099 | 39 099 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| 19 239 random designs, seeds 100 000 to 119 238 (Heavy 45003539021) | 60 932 | 2 366 803 | 2 364 795 | 36 one way, 36 the other, in 12 designs | 1 972 | 34 531 |

**None on the corpus, the benchmark's models and the tests; on the random designs 36 spans in 12 of 19 239, and those looked at are another thing than a step onto the curve beside.** The 36: seeds 101124, 102900 (18), 104515, 105640 (4), 106507 (4), 106537, 107873, 114122, 114620, 115290, 117170 and 118868; nine of the twelve are flagged both in sixteenths and in 256ths. One design was traced, 102900 (Check 45002233364; a cone less a torus, filleted and cut, then a boolean that is refused before and after): all its flagged spans are a run's first or last span at the border of the fillet's surface, along which the fillet is tangent to the face it rounds. The run's end is put on that border by `exit`, 20 to 4 000 tolerances along the border from where the curve followed in 256ths comes to it (eight spans); or the span begins at such a point, whose tangent points across the border, and the follow cannot leave it (six); or the curve followed leaves the chart across the border between the span's two points and comes back (two). The fit accepts them because beside that border the two surfaces are within a hundredth of a tolerance of each other over a band hundreds of tolerances wide: a span there is on both surfaces wherever along the border it ends. Not steps onto another curve; whether a curve so placed along a border of tangency is the curve a boolean wants is the question of *Curves that run along a border of tangency* (owed, below), and the other eleven designs were traced by the review (below): nine are no wrong curve, two are a branch that runs back over itself, on `main` as here. The corpus's 29, and the random designs' 1 972, are two points at one place (a point within 64 `on` of a border and the border's own point: in the corpus 1.3e-8 of a tolerance apart at most). The spans not followed have an end without a tangent of its own (a crossing point, an apex, a border reached where the surfaces are tangent), or lie in a crossing's zone or a tacnode's disc. What the count does not say: the follow is itself a march by the step's rule, so a curve nearer than a 256th of the step is not told from the one followed; and the corpus and the benchmark hold no pair of this kind at all (no fit is refused in either, so nothing in them is taken up again): the zero says that what is answered there today is not wrong this way, not that the weakness cannot bite. Where it would: a span whose cubic is within half a tolerance of both surfaces at its three places and whose middle lies on some stretch of the crossing, which between stretches a few tolerances apart is likely and between stretches millimetres apart is not (not far off, though: in the trace of 2026-11-20 a span across two of the wing's stretches 3 mm apart was 1 to 2.6 tolerances off the skin at its three places, where the fit asks half a tolerance).

*Held to.* `march.rs`, on the corrugated sheet as a marcher's pair and on an oval of a thin ring: a branch whose first march steps onto another curve is marched again and comes back as its seed's own curve, from the sheet's edge up to where the plane is level with its highest crest and back, with the plane as it is and turned over (the wrong step the forward run's from the one, the backward run's from the other); the fit names the span it was given, the two points of that step; six points up three curves, as a backward run would hold them after two wrong steps, are put right from the one nearest the start; a march taken up past a span keeps to a quarter of it over twice its length and to no more; taken up half a step short of its start a run closes there, the points it had before counting; and a branch the fit accepts is the same bit for bit, for the same evaluations, as its two runs fitted by hand. `crates/geom/tests/meet_shallow.rs` › `the_bottom_of_a_spar_meets_a_skin_it_crosses_in_curves_side_by_side`, no longer ignored: the 21 pairs, every curve on a change of sign of the skin's distance to the plane within the tolerance along the skin, every crossing of the sections on a curve. (Its oracle scans 199 sections now, 99 before: the three pairs of the blank tapered to 0.9 came back right by every other check and were held to 40 crossings, fewer than the fifty the test asks; an expectation made stronger, below.) `crates/engine/tests/shallow_crossing.rs` › `a_spar_whose_bottom_crosses_the_skin_in_curves_side_by_side_is_clear_of_the_rib`, no longer ignored: the five settings by commands, clear by the spar's fit, nothing in common, the union two shells. `crates/engine/tests/kayak_findings.rs` › `the_coaming_is_the_ring_between_the_deck_and_the_deck_raised_by_the_rim`, in the place of the test that held the coaming's refusal as owed: the body by two routes that do not pass through the boolean (below, *Expectations changed*). `crates/kernel/src/boolean/section.rs` › `the_march_taken_up_again_is_asked_only_where_the_first_refuses_in_every_box`: the order of asking, with boxes that answer and refuse as told. `crates/geom/tests/meet_beside.rs`, new: asked once, the sheet is refused in the fit's words and the sharp ridge comes back from `meet_surfaces` the same, number for number; and curves side by side on other surfaces, each at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, in the world's axes and askew to them, held to the crossing as it is known without the marcher (every point of a curve on a change of sign of the distance within the tolerance, every point of the crossing on a curve, one curve to each curve of the crossing: once round the inside of a closed one, or by one open strand all its length).

| Family (pairs) | On `main` | Now |
|---|---|---|
| the wing's skin under its spar (21, `meet_shallow.rs`) | 21 refused | 21 built |
| a corrugated sheet under a plane level with its crests, four curves side by side (24) | 24 refused | 24 built |
| the same sheet with a shoulder between two crests, two curves of four and six stretches side by side (24) | 24 refused | 24 built |
| a fat torus and a thin ring cut by a plane just inside the plane that touches the inner equator, two ovals tip to tip (36) | 36 built | the same |
| the same just outside it, one curve with a waist (36) | 36 built | the same |
| a rippled ridge, broad and sharp, cut just above its saddles, a row of loops tip to tip (36) | 36 built | the same |
| the same cut just below its saddles (36) and level with its crests (36) | 72 built | the same |

The torus and the ridge were made to step wrongly and do not: no step of theirs ends on another curve than it began on (the probe above, on `main`'s behaviour). Read from the sign of the distance, not traced: about a saddle of the distance between the surfaces the tips of the two curves face each other, but the side of the one that lies straight on from a side of the other is travelled the other way, so a step that lands there has turned back and is not taken. They stay as tests of what was answered.

*Each part against the tests* (Check 45008437483 on the last code, every part switched off in turn on a throwaway commit; Check 44997018342 before it, on the first nine).

| Without | Tests that fail |
|---|---|
| nothing | none |
| any branch taken up again | the unit test of the step beside; the wing's 21 pairs and its five settings by commands; both corrugated sheets; the pair asked once and again |
| the shorter steps (a run taken up with a march's own first step and growth) | the unit test of the shorter steps; the corrugated sheet |
| an end to the shorter steps (held to them to the run's end) | the unit test of the shorter steps |
| the span nearest the start (the first the fit gives up on instead) | its unit test |
| the span as it was given (the span the fit stopped at instead) | the unit tests of the step beside, of the span named and of the span nearest the start; the wing, by both; both corrugated sheets; the pair asked once and again |
| a backward run's step taken up | the unit test of the step beside; the wing, by both; the sheet with a shoulder |
| a forward run's step taken up | the unit test of the step beside; both corrugated sheets; the pair asked once and again |
| a second taking up of a branch | the wing's 21 pairs; both corrugated sheets |
| the points a run had before, in its closing | the unit test of the run that closes |
| the march asked once that refuses (it takes branches up all the same) | the pair asked once and again |
| every box asked the old way first (the section asks by the march taken up at once) | the kernel's test of the order of asking |

Under every line the bit-for-bit test passes, and so do the torus, the ridge and the wing's pairs that were answered before. The wing is mended without the shorter steps as well: taken up with a march's own first step, a quarter of the longest (8 mm), against wrong steps of 12 to 32; the sheet's wrong steps are 2.4 to 7.6 and need the quarter of the span. Where the shorter steps end changes no pair's answer, only how many points a branch has: it is held by its unit test alone, and so are the span nearest the start (the wing's pairs that are taken up twice are mended either way) and the closing. The sheet with a shoulder is taken up in both runs (39 times backward, 36 forward, over its 24 pairs) and fails without either. Switched off by the review: the shelf's mark of the second march as a question of its own is held (five tests fail without it: the wing's 21 pairs, both sheets, the pair asked once and again, the wing by commands); that `meet_surfaces` marches a second time only for the fit's refusal and not for another changes no result, only work, and no test fails without it.

*What it costs* (evaluations of the walked surface, counted). Nothing where the fit accepts every branch: the pair is marched once, as it was. A pair whose fit gives up is marched twice, the old way to its refusal and then in full with its branches taken up (the second march does the first's work again up to the refusal: the price of the fifth part, which keeps the first march a question of its own); a branch taken up is fitted once more from its first point. The wing's four tapered settings along the lowest line: 81 596 to 83 593 evaluations to the refusal, as before, and 86 688 to 89 897 more to the curves (one or two take-ups; 6 to 8 % above the refusal); the other three, 4 791, 4 187 and 1 589 to the refusal and 5 724, 7 267 and 5 611 more to the curves. The two marches were counted on the code before the fifth part, each alone; their sum on the last code is read from that, not counted again. A boolean asks its boxes a second time wherever every box refused the old way, whatever the refusal: for a refusal that is not the fit's that second asking is the first march found on the shelf, where it is kept under its own question, and no march is made again (but where the shelf keeps nothing, under a bound of work that was reached, the march is made again to the same refusal: owed, below).

*What moved* (the head 05421688 against the base it starts from, `main` with the two boolean repairs then being gated). Nothing that was answered. *The boolean corpus* (Heavy 45008318122): the digest of all results `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, 11 594 agreeing, the score sheet the base's line for line but for its line of time. *The benchmark's five models* (Check 45008316126): every count the base's, `baseline.txt` untouched; no fit gives up in them, so nothing is marched a second time. *Random designs* (Heavy 45008320312, 21 365 designs from seed 100 000, against the runs of the same day on the code before the union's repair that this branch starts from, 3a2af3d6): 21 361 the same in verdict, groups and every refusal's words; the four that differ (110965, 111609, 115646, 116424) are four of the six by which that repair differs from those runs, in a refusal's words (the other two lie beyond the seeds this run reached), and none is of this. On the code before the fifth part (Heavy 44998433781) a fifth differed, 102900: refused before for the fit, *"an intersection curve could not be fitted within the tolerance"*, it was refused as *"marching an intersection curve stalled"*, because three branches whose last step `exit` had put some 1 900 tolerances along a fillet's border from where the curve comes to it were now followed, and a later branch of the same pair stalls; with the fifth part it says what it said. The random designs' record holds verdicts and refusals, not the bodies' numbers: that a design's bodies are the same bits is held there by the method (a pair any box answered is answered by the same march) and measured on the corpus and the benchmark only. *What is answered that was refused:* the pairs of the table above, and the sea kayak's coaming.

*Expectations changed* (under the four conditions of [`testing.md`](../testing.md) › *Changing an expectation*). Two.

*The kayak's coaming, held as an owed refusal, is held as built.* `kayak_findings.rs` › `the_coaming_boolean_is_refused_as_owed_not_wrong` asserted that the ring's cut failed and nothing else did. The reason it changes is a fact: the refusal was an owed limit of the marcher, written down as owed, and the body the boolean should give is known without it. The new test is not weaker: the body must exist, pass the deep check, and be what it must be by two routes that do not pass through the boolean. *Lines up through the ring:* on a vertical line the coaming is what of the line is inside the ring's stock and inside the raised hull and not inside the hull, and where each of the three begins and ends on the line is told by `measure.ray` of that body alone (the stock and the raised hull in the document as it is before the two booleans); 360 lines, 72 round the ring at five places across it: 350 held, 10 not read (they meet an edge or graze), 210 of them through the ring, every one the same to the last digit. *Its volume in closed form:* on every line through the ring the coaming is one stretch, from the deck up by the rim's 30 mm (held by the lines), so its volume is the ring's plan times 30 mm whatever the deck's shape, `π (430 · 240 − 400 · 210) · 30` = 1 809 557.3685 mm³; measured 1 809 557.3681, within the 0.69 the measure allows itself (Check 45003859719).

*The skin's oracle, made stronger.* The oracle of `meet_shallow.rs` › `check_skin` scans 199 sections of the skin where it scanned 99. The reason is the measuring method's: the test asks for at least fifty crossings to hold the curves to, and the plane of the blank tapered to 0.9 (rising 0.003, ten times as fast as the skin's lowest line) crosses the skin over a part of the span that 99 sections meet in 40 places, found by halving along the sections, without the marcher. Not weaker: the fifty stand, and every pair of the file is now held to twice the crossings. Still pinned independently of the code: the crossings are the skin's own evaluation. The two tests un-ignored are no change of expectation: their assertions are the ones they had.

*The review* (2026-11-22; ten Checks, 45014568258 to 45018994938; its tools and tables in the workshop's `plan/march-beside/review/`). The repair does what it says on what was measured, and one thing was changed for it.

- *The order in the section, changed.* As first built, `section_with` asked the boxes by the march taken up before it asked whether the faces lie on one another (`coincide`), so a pair that `main` settled as faces on one surface, after a refusal in the fit's words in every box, could have been answered in curves: not the same body by construction. Not met in anything measured (of 155 pair questions refused in every box over 22 test files, 114, all of `boolean_coincide.rs`, are refused in other words than the fit's and came back the same; the other 41, the kayak's one and the wing's 40, are answered by the march taken up, and for each `coincide` does not know, so `main` refused them). The order is now the boxes the old way, then `coincide`, then the march taken up only where `coincide` does not know (Check 45017447874); `main`'s refusal of faces partly on one another stands with it. Its price: where `coincide` does not know, the boxes asked the old way are asked once more, from the shelf.
- *Bit for bit.* By reading, up to `meet_surfaces_once`: the first pass of `trace` is the same runs in the same order, and the old shelf key is unchanged. Measured: 966 pairs of the review's own, 792 built before and after and equal, 174 refused before and built, none built before and refused; through the commands, every answer the same over four families (698 rows and runs).
- *Whether the take-up accepts a wrong curve.* None found: sheets of drawn profiles (192 pairs, 84 refused before, all 192 the crossing as known without the marcher), sheets under a plane 3, 10 and 100 tolerances either side of a trough (180, 90 refused before, all right), each at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, upright and askew. A saddle, a figure of eight and two parabolas that touch (594 pairs) are answered as on `main`: no pair of them is refused by the fit, so the take-up was not tried at a true crossing of two curves.
- *Through the commands.* The wing's spar in its notch at 59 settings: 41 right before and after and the same; 18 that `main` answers `unknown` and refuses (the intersection and the union; `measure.clearance` gave the gap, 0.03, at all 18) are right: clear by 0.03, nothing in common, the union with the counts and the volume of both. The released 0.2.1 refuses the same 18, ten in the fit's words and eight as *"marching an intersection curve stalled"* (the coordinator's run of the review's tool, the same day). A corrugated sheet under a slab, 20 cases: 16 built before and after and the same, 4 refused before are built, each volume above the plane within 2.4e-6 mm³ of an integral made without the kernel.
- *The kayak's coaming*, by routes of the review's own: the deep check; the volume 1 809 557.3681 against 1 809 557.3685 in closed form; 19 slices by `measure.below`, each within 1.2e-3 mm³ of the ring's plan on that side times 30 mm; the mesh closed, 400 vertical lines each crossing it twice, 30.0000 mm apart.
- *The eleven designs the probe flagged and the builder had not traced.* Nine are no wrong curve: a first point on a border in a band of tangency that arrives when followed back from the second point (105640, 106537); spans that arrive in 4096ths, the follow's own limit (107873, 114122, 117170); two points 1.7 and 0.0 tolerances apart (114620, 115290); a step over a stretch outside the box asked (101124, 104515). **Two are a wrong curve, accepted on `main` as here: a branch that runs back over itself (106507, 118868).** Two round surfaces of one small radius (fillet tubes) cross in two closed curves that cross each other at two points, where the surfaces touch. In 106507 (a cylinder of radius 1.2208e-3 walked against a torus, tolerance 1e-6) a march's first step is the tube's diameter, 2 775 tolerances, and lands on the same curve beyond a crossing point; the sense of travel flips through such a point, so the far point's tangent agrees with the step and the step is taken; the run goes on from the far point back towards the crossing point, and the fit fills the step's span with points of the true curve, the stretch the run then marches again: a turn of cosine −0.9995 between spans of 167 and 55 tolerances, in four fitted branches. In 118868 (torus against torus, tolerance 1e-9) the fitted points go into the crossing point, 0.22 rad out along the other curve's arm and back, in two branches. Each is inside a fillet that is refused before and after (whether the folded curve is why was not established), and both designs' verdicts are ok: no wrong body known. Counted over 12 034 random designs (100 000 to 112 033), only 106507 has such a branch; none among 477 branches the take-up mended, the builder's tests, the review's families, the kayak and the wing. Not counted on the corpus and the benchmark.
- *What no test fails without* (mutants): a run that closed and, taken up, no longer does, getting no backward run; at most two take-ups a branch where eight are allowed (no branch in any test or family needed more than two); no floor on the span; the closing step not taken up. Shorter steps of half the span for a quarter, or held over the span's own length for twice it, fail the unit test of the shorter steps only.
- *What it costs where it mends*, in evaluations beyond the old refusal, more than the wing's 6 to 8 %: the builder's sheets under their crests 1.95 to 2.42 times the work to the refusal, the sheets with a shoulder 4.2 to 23.8 times (median 11.6), the review's drawn sheets 2.0 to 12.3 times. Bounded: eight take-ups a seed's branch, and every loop counts against the marcher's 40 000 000 evaluations.
- *Not checked by the review:* `main`'s own commit under the probe (the branch's march as first made stood for it); the fold count on the corpus and the benchmark; the commands at other scales or turned; fallbacks above the kernel after a refused boolean.

*Owed.*

- **A step onto the curve beside that the fit accepts** is not looked for, and nothing proves there is none (the count above). The direct mend is the first way: a step held at its middle too. It moves every marched curve's points in their last bits, so every published answer, the corpus's digest and the benchmark's counts move with it, for about half as many evaluations again per step: the developer's to decide, not built.
- **Curves that run along a border of tangency** (the random design 102900, above). Beside a fillet's border on the face it rounds, a span is within the tolerance of both surfaces wherever it ends along the border, and the place `exit` gives a run's end can be thousands of tolerances along the border from where the curve followed finely comes to it; the fit accepts such spans, on `main` as now. Found by the probe, not by a wrong body: the boolean of that design is refused either way. The eleven other designs the probe flags are traced (the review, above). Owed: whether such an end is held to the border's stretch that lies on the other surface (`border_stretches` reports those as curves of their own) or to the step.
- **A step through a point where two curves of a crossing cross each other, onto the same curve beyond it** (the review, above; random designs 106507 and 118868): mended on 2026-11-23, below, with what is owed now.
- **A refusal that goes away changes which route answers, in two callers** (read by the review, not met in anything measured): `loose_op::boolean_for_tool` tries a boolean at four times the working tolerance and, on any refusal, at the working tolerance (a body with loose entities only: fillet, chamfer, thicken); and `contact` answers a refused union as a touch. A pair of theirs that the fit refused and the march taken up answers is answered by the other route than before. Fallbacks above the kernel were not searched.
- **Where a run's last step is put on the chart's border** (`exit`): the zero that Newton along the border finds from the straight guess, held to nothing else (not to lying ahead, nor within the step). On the sheet it is the foot of another curve, and the fit gives up on the span, which is how it is mended now; held to the step as any other step's end is, it would not be made. Not built: it changes a march before any fit, so no curve fitted today is known to stay the same bits under it.
- **The boxes asked again only where a refusal was the fit's.** A boolean asks its boxes a second time after any refusal in all of them where the faces are not known to lie on one another; only the fit's refusal can be answered the second time, and the others are the first marches come back from the shelf, or marched again where the shelf kept nothing. Telling the section which refusal was the fit's (`geom` has the words in one place now, `march.rs` › `not_fitted`) would spare that; not built, to keep the last code as gated.
- **What is not taken up again.** A fit that gives up on a joint of two traces (`united`), on the span the box's cut made (`solve`) or at the last check of a fitted curve: none met by the tests or the corpus (the probe counted none), refused as before. A backward run that steps onto a closed curve does not end (a backward run does not close): it runs into the cap of points before any fit, and is refused in the cap's words, as before; not met.
- **Not held by a test:** a forward run that closed and, taken up again, no longer does (its backward run is then marched), and one that did not close and now does. Spelled the way that keeps a branch whole; nothing measured takes either.

*A step through a point where two curves of a crossing cross each other: traced and mended* (2026-11-23, `craft3d_geom` › `intersect/march.rs` › `trace`, `folds`, `folded_step`, `folded`; `meet.rs` › `meet_surfaces`; the branch `check/march-fold`; its records in the workshop's `plan/march-fold/`).

*What was owed* (the review, above). Where two curves of a crossing cross each other the surfaces touch, and the sense in which the march travels a curve flips at that point. A step through it can land on the same curve beyond it where the tangent, the sense flipped, agrees with the one the step left with: the turn is all a step is held to there, and it is taken. The run goes on from that point back towards the crossing point, over the stretch the fit fills the step's span with, and the fit accepts the branch, which is within the tolerance of both surfaces all its length: a curve that runs back over itself. Counted (the coordinator's probe of 2026-11-22, branch `check/fold-count`, never merged) with the review's definition below: in 0 fitted branches of the boolean corpus (4 216), of the kernel's and geom's tests (11 469), of the engine's tests with the benchmark's models (5 551), of the 112 published items (4 973) and of the 33 Jigs' ranges (86 880 or more), and in 2 designs of 31 326 random ones (106507, 118868), each inside a fillet that is refused; no body was built from a folded curve.

*Traced* (Check 45260043956, a probe on a throwaway commit that writes out every march in which the test below finds a fold, with the pair, its box and the marched points). **106507**: the fillet's tools along a straight edge and along an arc of radius 5, a cylinder of radius 1.2207e-3 (walked) and a torus of radii 4.998779296875 and 1.220703125e-3, the cylinder's axis along the torus's and through its middle circle, asked in a box 0.45 by 5.45 by 0.45 at a tolerance of 1e-6 (the longest step a 32nd of its diagonal, 171 533 tolerances). They cross in two closed curves that cross each other where the two touch, at the inner and outer equator of the tube, within 0.15 of a tolerance of the two ellipses `±r sin θ` of two crossing cylinders. The backward run from a seed 0.51 radians round the cylinder from one of those points (858 tolerances from it) took its first step, a quarter of the longest halved until a step was taken, 2 775 tolerances across the cylinder: through that point and on round the same curve for 2.5 radians, where the tangent, its sense flipped, was 0.11 radians from the seed's. The run went on from there back to the crossing point; the fold is at the point the step landed on, a turn of cosine −0.9995 between spans of 167 and 55 tolerances. **118868**: the fillet's tools along the top edges of two cylinders of radius 0.0025 side by side, two tori of radii 2.490234375e-3 and 9.765625e-6, axes along z, middle circles in one plane, centres 0.0025 apart, asked in a box 5.2e-3 by 2.7e-3 by 5.2e-3 at 1e-9. They cross in four closed curves, two at each place where the middle circles cross: the section of the tubes by the plane between the tori, and a curve that ρ₂ = 2R − ρ₁ makes (`y = d/2 + 2 R r cos v / d` in the first torus's terms); the two cross at the top and the bottom of the tubes. The backward run's first step, 32 782 tolerances, went from 0.81 radians round the tube below the top through the top to 2.14 radians beyond it, and the run came back; the fit's filling of that span went into the top, 0.24 radians out along the other curve's arm and back. Two folds (cosines −0.9969 at the landing and −0.9989 in the filling), both on that step; and in another branch, the forward run's first step, 21 689 tolerances, through the top the same way.

*The rule as built.* Four parts.

1. *A fold is read from what the fit accepted* (`folds`): a point with a tangent of its own (a crossing point or an apex has none, and a branch ends there) between two spans in a row each longer than four tolerances and 64 `on`, whose chords turn by more than 120 degrees (a cosine below −0.5): the review's definition, which the count above used. (64 `on` is never the longer: a march whose `on` is more than a fiftieth of the tolerance is refused before it starts, so it is at most 1.28 tolerances, and the four tolerances decide.) Read from the places of the points alone: no evaluation, and nothing the march reads is changed. A true corner of a branch (a run's last step put on a border at right angles to the step before it, random design 102900's corners at cosines of −0.0023 and −0.0009) turns by a right angle, not back.
2. *The step it lies on* (`folded_step`): a fold at a point the march came to is put on the step that came to it (the forward run's from the point before, the backward run's from the point after), one at a point the fit put between two marched points on the step between them; of several, the step nearest the start. Where no step can be named, the branch is refused, not taken up: a fold at the start; beside a point the fit put on the edge of the other surface; inside the span by which a closed branch closes on its start (its last point is the start again, with the start's parameters where the loop does not go round a periodic chart, and is read back as the start: the review, below); past the join of a closed branch divided at the edge of the other surface that goes round a periodic chart (its points there are moved by the period). So is one whose nearest named step is no step of the march (a span drawn into a cusp), though a step of the march may be named for another of its folds.
3. *That step is taken up again as a span the fit gave up on* (`trace`): from the point before it, in steps of at most a quarter of it over twice its length, as built on 2026-11-22 (above); what the run had marched beyond is dropped, and the crossing point is found by the march's own rule (a crossing point straight ahead ends a branch) once the steps are short enough to come to it. Both designs' folds are mended by one taking up each; at most eight a branch, as before.
4. *Asked as first made, the march refuses such a branch* (`Marcher::once`), in words of its own, *"an intersection curve could not be followed: the points marched along it run back over themselves"* (`folded`), and `meet_surfaces` marches the pair again, taking branches up, after that refusal as after the fit's. So the order a boolean asks in (every box the old way, `coincide`, then the march taken up) stands as it was, and a pair that a later box or the march taken up answers is answered there. Where `main` accepted the folded branch in a boolean's first box, the later boxes and, for a pair with a freeform face, `coincide` are asked now before the march taken up: a later box answers by its march as first made (106507's fillet, below), and a pair that `coincide` finds lying on one another over part of where one is over the other is refused there (`PARTLY`); not met.

*Every other curve the same bits.* By reading: the fold is looked for after `refine_pieces` accepted the branch, from the points alone; where there is none, `trace` returns what it returned before, by the same evaluations in the same order (`a_branch_the_fit_accepts_is_marched_once_and_is_the_same_bit_for_bit` passes as it was, its count of evaluations included). What it costs: per fitted point, two differences, two lengths, a dot product and a division; nothing where nothing folds. Where a branch folds, the march as first made stops at the first fold and the pair is marched again with that branch taken up, as for the fit's refusal. Measured: below.

*The designs, before and after.* **106507**, its pair in its box: as first made, the fold, refused; marched again, the crossing as known in closed form: every curve on it, none running back, together as long as it (below; the number of curves was not written out). The fillet itself asks the pair in the box of its tools' faces first, where it is refused now, and next in the nearer box the section asks (`meet_in`), where the march as first made meets no fold and answers (read from the probe: no second march of that pair was made); the fillet is refused as before, word for word, *"the fillet does not end cleanly"*. **118868**, its pair in its box: as first made refused; marched again, the two folded branches each taken up once and each from the top of the tubes to the bottom (47 144 and 33 108 tolerances long), the eight halves of the four curves, 0.08 to 0.67 of a tolerance from both surfaces by their fit, held to the crossing (below); the fillet is refused as before, word for word, *"the profile crosses the axis of revolution"*. Neither fillet builds, then, and neither was refused for the fold. **102900** (the corners at a border): no fold read, and its design as before.

*Held to.* `crates/geom/tests/meet_fold.rs`, new: each pair held to its crossing as known without the marcher (in closed form, an angle that runs one way along every curve between the points where its curves cross, and the places of the crossing at each angle): every point of every curve on the crossing within four tolerances, no curve running back over itself by more than four tolerances of length, the curves together as long as the crossing in the box, every place of the crossing in the box away from where its curves cross on a curve; and where the march as first made answers, its answer the same bit for bit as the march taken up again.

| Pair | Asked | As first made | Answered |
|---|---|---|---|
| design 106507's, in its box | 4 scales (1e-3 to 1e6) × upright and turned | upright a fold at every scale; turned, answered, the same | 8 of 8 the crossing |
| design 118868's, in its box | the same | upright a fold at every scale; turned, answered, the same | 8 of 8 |
| 106507's in 48 boxes of 400 to 2 940 tube radii about the crossing | the same | turned 8 of 48 a fold at every scale, upright none | 384 of 384 |
| 118868's in 28 boxes of 800 to 2 470 tube radii | the same | upright 8 of 28 a fold at every scale, turned 2 of 28 | 224 of 224 |
| two cylinders of one radius crossing at right angles (Steinmetz's two ellipses), 48 boxes; the marcher alone, the pair having a closed form | the same | turned 6 of 48 a fold at every scale, upright none | 384 of 384 |
| a ball and a cylinder of half its radius inside it, touching it (Viviani's figure of eight), 48 boxes | the same | answered, the same, in all | 384 of 384 |

No step through Viviani's crossing point was found that can be taken: over 2000 by 2000 pairs of places, one on each lobe (the second beyond the point, its sense flipped), the two tangents never agree within a step's turn of 0.2 radians. Steinmetz's ellipses have such pairs, about one in sixteen of the pairs of places either side of a crossing point (the chord 2 to 2.83 radii). `march.rs`: `a_branch_runs_back_over_itself_where_two_spans_turn_back_not_at_a_corner` (a turn back and one of cosine −0.6 are folds; a right angle, a corner of cosine −0.002 with spans of 9 tolerances, a turn back over three tolerances and one at a point without a tangent of its own are not; 102900's corner as the probe printed it is not); `a_fold_is_put_on_the_step_that_came_to_it_or_that_holds_it` (forward, backward, inside a step, at the start, none, and of two the nearer).

*Expectations changed.* None: the tests above are new, and no test's expectation was touched.

*What moved* (the code of 86b6c0e3, the same as 85dba587's, against `main` 1f196da4). Nothing that was answered without a fold. *The boolean corpus* (Heavy 45265136314): the digest of all results `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, 11 594 agreeing, no mismatch and none invalid, the score sheet the nightly's on `main` (Heavy 45087810006) line for line but for its time. *The benchmark's five models* (Check 45266471390): its 117 counted lines `main`'s (Check 45228520108), count for count. *Random designs* (Heavy 45266585022, an hour from seed 20 780 000 beside the trial Gate and two runs of other branches, 42 024 seeds; against the nightly on `main` over the same seeds, by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word): 42 020 the same, and four that the nightly built in 34.5 to 56.5 seconds ran out of the 60 allowed (20814167, 20814658, 20817770, 20820548). Run again by the probe's build, which writes out every fold it meets, on a quieter server (Check 45282339457): each meets no fold and gives the nightly's verdict, groups and refusals word for word, in 56.2, 41.7, 51.6 and 39.7 seconds (the test profile, one after the other): the load, not this. No seed of that run meets a fold, so none goes from a body to a refusal or to another body; the two designs that fold lie outside it and are run by name above. *The trial Gate* (45266579575 on 86b6c0e3): passed (721 test binaries, FreeCAD, the web module's size). The Jigs and the published items were not run again (the count above found no fold among them). No release note: nothing a user sees moved in anything measured.

*The review* (2026-11-24, the branch `check/review-march-fold`, `crates/geom/tests/meet_fold_review.rs`; Checks 45287391492, 45288306141 and 45289074031). *Can a curve that runs one way be read as a fold?* Between two points of the fit, the chords of a curve followed in one pass turn by no more than its tangent turns between them. The march holds the tangents at a step's two ends to 0.2 radians, not what lies between, so a fold on such a curve needs it to turn by more than 120 degrees within a step and back, or within the two spans about a point, each longer than four tolerances; where a curve turns tightly the march's steps are short (the turn), and two spans that long do not lie about the turn. Tried on curves that turn by more than 120 degrees within a few to a few hundred tolerances, asked of the marcher alone as first made and taken up again, at four scales, upright and turned, in cubes from a few times the turn to thousands of times it, where the first steps are many times the turn: the tip of a long thin ellipse (a plane nearly along a thin cylinder, the tip 8 to 500 tolerances in radius, of aspect 20 and 400; 1 920 pairs), a thin slanted loop where two cylinders skew at a small angle overlap a little (tips of a few to a few tens of tolerances, the surfaces nearly tangent along it; 448), Viviani's figure and Steinmetz's ellipses opened at their crossing points by a millionth to a hundredth of a radius (two loops that pass close, or one curve with two tight turns; 720 each), and the small circle where a plane cuts a cap off a ball (10 to 400 tolerances in radius, the surfaces at 0.02 to 1 radian; 704). No fold is read on any of them, and where the march as first made answers, the march taken up again gives the same bits. With crossing points, two cylinders of one radius whose axes cross at 30, 45, 60 and 90 degrees, in cubes of 1.5 to 1 090 radii about a crossing point (672): at 30 degrees, turned, in the cube of 400 radii, the march as first made folds at every scale, and taken up again it is the crossing, as every answer of that family is. *Read in the code:* where nothing folds, `trace` returns what it returned, by the same evaluations in the same order (the reading is `folds` and `folded_step`, both of `&self`, after `refine_pieces` and before its pieces are returned untouched); the loops are bounded by the points; nothing indexes, divides by zero or panics there. `folded_step` names no step for a fold inside the span by which a closed branch closes (item 2, above): the point after the fold is looked up by its parameters and found as the start, not as the last point; looked up among the points after the one before it, it would be the closing step, which `trace` takes up as any. Not met. The callers of the march as first made are `meet_surfaces`, which marches the pair again after the new refusal, and the section's `meet_surfaces_once` (`section_with`; its helpers ahead of it only fill the shelf), which asks the later boxes, then `coincide`, then `meet_surfaces` in every box (item 4); a refusal that comes out of all of them reaches the fallbacks above the section (`loose_op::boolean_for_tool`'s second tolerance, `contact`) as any refusal does. *Not of the fold:* the opened figures held to their crossing are not all the crossing (owed, below).

*Owed.*

- **The arms of a crossing point seeded beyond small curves.** In a box 5 870 tube radii a side, the two tori of 118868 lose both ovals in the plane between them, on `main` as now (the probe of Check 45264346266: four curves of eight, the march as first made and the one taken up again the same; turned, the box along the world's axes about a cube of 4 150 tube radii loses them too, in the test's run of Check 45265128730, not probed): the arms are seeded a hundredth of the longest step from a crossing point, 31 021 tolerances there, more than three tube radii, and a seed within half that of a crossing point is left to the arms; every place of those ovals is that near the top or the bottom of the tubes. In the fillet's own box (7.9e-3 across) the arms are 2 461 tolerances out and all eight halves are found. The test's boxes stop below that size. Not of this item; not traced further.
- **A step through a crossing point onto the other curve's arm.** Where the two curves cross at less than a step's turn, the far arm of the other curve is travelled away from the point, the way the step goes: a step that lands there is taken and the branch passes through the crossing point onto the other curve instead of ending there. That is no fold and is not looked for; read from the sign of the distance, not met as such (the stretch given twice below may be it; not traced).
- **A stretch given twice beside a crossing point the surfaces are a little apart at** (found by the review, 2026-11-24; `meet_fold_review.rs` › `near_crossings_read_as_crossing_points_give_no_stretch_twice`, which fails, ignored as owed). Viviani's figure and Steinmetz's ellipses opened by a ten-thousandth of a radius: the surfaces are a tenth of a tolerance apart where the curves crossed, the march reads a crossing point there, and in 54 and 128 of 720 boxes, at every scale, upright and turned, its answer gives a stretch of the crossing twice: two curves that are the same half of a loop from one of the two points to the other; a curve once round a loop from a point back to it, over the halves of the loops given besides; one curve twice round a loop (the curves together 1.25 to 1.5 times as long as the crossing). No fold is read, and the march as first made gives the same bits: the answer `main` gives. A ball and a cylinder meet in no closed form, so a boolean asks the march for them and gets the same; two cylinders whose radii are within the tolerance of each other meet in closed form. Opened by a millionth or a hundredth, every answer is the crossing. Not traced.
- **Folds the trace does not see**: a branch joined from two traces (`united`) or cut by the box is not looked at again. The count above found every fold at the trace (its counts at the trace and after the box are the same everywhere).

### A plane that passes a vertex by less than a tolerance: traced, and refused in the rule's words (found 2026-11-20, a hipped roof of 24 planes; repaired 2026-11-22; `section.rs` › `edge_cuts`, `clip`, `Sections::near`; `mod.rs` › `near_places`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_plane_by_vertex.rs`)

*Found* by a hipped roof cut with `feature.planeCut` ([`plane-cut.md`](plane-cut.md)): 24 planes, one through every side of an outline at the eaves, their normals rounded to six decimals. Refused at the last plane: *"imprint lost: a curve in which two faces cross does not divide them; a piece of it is missing"*. With the normals unrounded it builds. The review reduced it to two planes at one convex corner of the outline: the second passes 0.497 tolerances over the vertex the first makes on the corner's upright edge. A datum plane, a sketch, a rectangle, an extrusion and a subtraction per plane are refused the same, so it is the boolean's, not the plane cut's block.

*The case, with plain booleans* (the test file above, which is the reproduction). A prism over a four-cornered outline, 40 high, one corner at (50.4, 0). A block whose top is a plane through that corner at height 14, holding the outline's side on one side of the corner, is intersected with it: the upright edge of the corner now ends in a vertex V at (50.4, 0, 14), and the edge that leaves V along the other wall rises from it at 23 degrees. A second block, whose top holds the outline's other side (to the six decimals of its normal), passes `t` tolerances from V and is intersected with what the first left. Its top either holds V, or crosses the edge that leaves V at a place W; because that edge leaves at a shallow angle to the plane, W is 3.23 `t` tolerances from V. Tolerance 1e-6 on a part of 50.

*What each distance answered until 2026-11-22* (the test printed it: Check runs 44767663080 and 44769475435 on `check/cut-planes`, 44767711875 on `check/contact-border` f93760c7 with only the test added: the same answers, so the change there to how a meeting curve near a face's border is read moves none of them):

| The plane's distance from V, in tolerances | Answer | V to W, in tolerances |
|---|---|---|
| over V: 0, 0.3 | built; V is taken as lying in the plane (11 vertices) | W would be 0 and 0.97 from V: one place |
| 0.45, 0.49, 0.497, 0.51, 0.55 | refused: "imprint lost: a curve in which two faces cross does not divide them; a piece of it is missing (face 1 of the second body)" | 1.45 to 1.78 |
| 0.7, 0.9, 1.0, 1.5, 1.9, 2.1 | built, V and W two vertices (12), the body passes its check | 2.26, 2.91, 3.23, 4.84, 6.14, 6.78 |
| under V: 0.3 | built, one place (11 vertices) | |
| 0.45, 0.55 | refused: "imprint lost …" | |
| 0.7 | built (12 vertices) | the two nearest 2.08 apart |
| 1.0 | refused: "imprint lost …" | |
| 1.5 | refused: "two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart (at the edge of tolerance)", with its faces | |
| 1.9, 2.1 | built (12 vertices) | the two nearest 6.14 and 6.78 apart |

*Traced* (2026-11-22, Check 45020571979 on the code before the repair: a test that printed, for each pair of faces whose curve passes near V, where each edge meets the other surface, where its cut was put, and how far each is from the pair's curves; and the sweep at every twentieth of a tolerance from −2.2 to 2.2). Near V the first cut has three faces: T, the roof's first plane; P, the wall along which the edge E rises from V; Q, the wall along which the eaves run. Their edges at V are E (T and P), the eaves (T and Q) and the upright edge U (P and Q). Against the second plane S, E runs at a sine of 0.310, the eaves at 0.337, U at 0.788; S meets T at 20.2 degrees (a sine of 0.346), P at a sine of 0.788, Q at 0.857.

1. `edge_cuts` takes a place where an edge meets the other surface within twice the tolerance of an end of the edge that lies within the tolerance of that surface to be that end (*"a hit next to an end of the edge that lies on the surface is that end"*, since 2026-10-14, for an ellipse that runs onto a face nearly tangent to it). Over V by `t` up to 0.619 tolerances, W (on E, 3.23 `t` from V) is taken to V; under V by up to 0.673, the eaves' place (2.97 |t|); under V by up to 1, U's place (1.27 |t|). V is |t| from S.
2. `clip` puts a place on a curve of the pair only where it is within the tolerance of the curve; otherwise it is left out, since the surfaces may meet in another curve too. V is |t| divided by the sine from each pair's curve: 2.89 |t| from T ∩ S, 1.27 |t| from P ∩ S, 1.17 |t| from Q ∩ S. Past one tolerance (T: |t| above 0.346; P: above 0.788; Q: above 0.857) the curve got no cut at the place, though the place itself is on it (0.000 tolerances in the trace). Over V by 0.5: E's cut at V, 1.446 tolerances from T ∩ S; its place 1.615 from V and on the curve.
3. Without that cut the curve's stretch from the place on was never made: the span from where the curve was asked to its next cut starts clear of the face's box and is left out whole. Over V by 0.5 and under by 0.45 no stretch of T ∩ S was made at all; under by 1.0 none of P ∩ S, nor of Q ∩ S near V.
4. The arcs along which the planes cross the second block's top then did not close, the regions dropped them, and the imprint's check refused it at that face (*"face 1 of the second body"*), naming no place.

The guess written here before (the curve's end snapped to V while V is not on the plane, so the piece between does not close) was near, not it: no piece between V and W was missing, but the cut of the curve that runs through W more than a tolerance from V. The sweep agrees with these thresholds at every point (over: built up to 0.33, "imprint lost" from 0.35 to 0.60, built from 0.63; under: built up to 0.33, "imprint lost" from 0.35 to 0.66, built from 0.70 to 0.77, "imprint lost" from 0.80 to 1.00, "two points closer together" from 1.01 to 1.56, built from 1.59), with one point the thresholds do not tell: under by 0.69 the eaves' place is 2.05 from V, just past the guard, and the result is refused as zero thickness at a place between them (below).

*The repair.* A cut keeps the place it was taken from (`Cut::hit`, set where `edge_cuts` takes a place to an end of its edge: next to an end that lies on the surface, and where an approximated edge hugs the surface from its end to the place). Where `clip` leaves such a cut out of a curve (the end more than the tolerance from it) while the place is on the curve, the two more than the merging distance and at most the guard apart, the place inside or on both faces, and the curve one along which the surfaces cross, the pair of faces is noted (`Sections::near`); the cut is left out as before. Where the regions then find an imprint left open at a face of a noted pair (the refusal *"imprint lost"*), the boolean is refused as two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart, naming the pair's two faces, the first such pair in the order the section met them (`mod.rs` › `near_places`). Nothing else reads what is noted: the section, every body built and every other refusal are what they were. What is noted is tied to the lost imprint by its face alone, not by its curve or its place: at a face of a noted pair an imprint left open for another reason is refused in these words too, naming the noted pair (argued from the code by the review; no result of the corpus or of the random designs moved). At a face for which no pair is noted, *"imprint lost"* keeps its words.

Why noted and not refused where it is known: refused in `clip` at once (the first version of the repair, Check 45021513502), the state refused a union the test of another finding holds as built: `boolean_level_touch.rs` › `what_lies_beside_it`, a block moved 0.45 tolerances from the side of a block whose other half is cut back at a slant from its top edge (*A union whose level tops meet along a line where the bodies part* below), refused at 1e-3 in these words (face 6 of the first body, face 4 of the second) where that test wants the zero-thickness refusal at the line and, without that refusal, one shell. Not traced; by the distances, the slanted face meets the moved block's side in a line about 1.2 tolerances inside the face along its top edge, and the places where the face's ends cross that side are taken to the ends of the top edge, more than a tolerance from the line, which loses its cuts as here and is not needed: nothing is left open. So the state is the rule's refusal only where it costs the imprint, and the place where that shows is the imprint's check. Why there and not at the snap: the snap is right for a curve the end lies on (over V by 0.5, P ∩ S takes V, 0.635 tolerances from it), and only the curve tells whether the end is on it. Which distance decides at 0.3 was not chosen: there V is within the tolerance of every curve, and nothing is noted.

*What each distance answers now* (Check 45022440837; the owed test passes, and only a refusal "imprint lost" can change, into this one): every "imprint lost" of the table above (over by 0.45, 0.49, 0.497, 0.51 and 0.55; under by 0.45, 0.55 and 1.0) is *"two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart (at the edge of tolerance)"*, with a face of each body (over V and under by 0.5: face 6 of the first body, the roof's first plane, and face 1 of the second, its top; under by 0.95 face 0 of the first, the wall along the eaves, and the top: Check 45021513502's print of the same distances beside a spline surface, where the planes' answers are); every other answer is as it was, the same bodies. Through the commands the roof as found (its normals rounded to six decimals) is refused at its last plane with *"planes.23: plane 24: two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart (at the edge of tolerance) (F3:cut(1), F3:cut(24))"*, then the document's tolerance and what to do (move the plane by more than the tolerance, or give planes that are meant to meet in one point the same point).

*Held by.* `boolean_plane_by_vertex.rs`: the owed test, no longer ignored (every distance of the sweep builds a body that passes its check or is refused at the edge of tolerance with its faces); the distances well clear of V, as before; the state at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6 (tolerance 1e-6 times the scale), as found and turned in space, over and under V (over by 0.2 one place, by 0.5 too near, by 0.9 two places; under by 0.2 one place, by 0.5 too near, by 0.72 two places, by 0.95 too near through the upright edge's place, by 1.3 too near as 1.5 was before, by 1.9 two places), each answer exact (a body with its count of vertices, or the refusal naming one face of each body); beside a spline surface at 1 and 1e3, the second block's top a spline on its plane or the roof's face one, the planes' answers at eleven distances (with the roof's face a spline, all but 0.7 either side, owed: below). `section.rs` › `a_place_taken_to_an_end_off_a_curve_through_it_is_two_points_too_near` at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6: the refusal with the pair (A's face, B's) at 0.5 over and 0.5 and 0.95 under, and *"imprint lost"* with nothing noted (`END_ONLY`, the code before the repair); at 0, 0.3, 0.7 and 2.1 over and 0.3, 0.7 and 1.9 under, the same body bit for bit either way. `plane_cut.rs` › `a_roof_with_its_normals_rounded_is_refused_where_a_plane_passes_a_corner`. `boolean_level_touch.rs` › `what_lies_beside_it`, unchanged: where the lost cut leaves nothing open, the boolean is what it was. `a_spline_roof_face_and_a_plane_past_its_vertex_by_more_than_the_guard_is_not_too_near` (the review's): beside a spline roof face at 0.7 either side, at 1 and 1e3, the place 2.26 and 2.08 tolerances from V, past the guard, whatever is answered is not this refusal.

*Measured* (on 85a81df4, the code as handed in). The boolean corpus (Heavy 45022452313): digest `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, 11,594 agreeing, no mismatch, none invalid, as before. The benchmark's work counts: the same, line for line (117 lines; Check 45020571979 on the code before, 45022440837 after). The random designs, from seed 100000 for 45 minutes (Heavy 45020574353 on the code before, 45022454406 on the repair): of the 30,551 seeds both ran, 30,550 give the same verdict, groups, accepted groups and refusals word for word, none moves between a body and a refusal, and none says *"imprint lost"* where it did not or the reverse (seed 105692's plane cut, *"imprint lost"* at group 10, is as it was: not this state); the one other, 121858, ran out of its 60 s on the repair (59.4 s before; both runs four seeds at once on a shared runner), and run alone (Check 45027870292, with a Gate on the Strix beside it: 99.6 s) it gives what it gave before, ok, 8 groups, 6 accepted, 2 refused. None of these is a quiet timing and the base was not run beside it: whether the repair costs that seed time is not measured. The same nine seeds refused as an invalid body on both.

*The review* (2026-11-22; three Checks, 45030943266, 45030956276, 45030958827; the workshop's `plan/imprint-by-vertex/review/`). By reading, the claim holds: the place kept on a cut is read by the noting alone, what is noted by `near_places` alone, and `near_places` changes only an *"imprint lost"* at a face of a noted pair; that refusal is raised only where the two wrapped calls raise it, and no caller acts on which of the two refusals it gets. Two weak points. *The noted pair is tied to the lost imprint by its face only* (above, *The repair*): an imprint lost for another reason at such a face is refused as too near, naming the noted pair; the nearest real case is a spline roof face under V by 0.95, where the eaves' place, past the guard, is taken to V as at 0.7 and the upright edge's place is in the band, so the owed loss is folded into this refusal (the advice still works there: 1.9 under and on V build). No wrong body comes of it: after the plane is moved the other cause shows as *"imprint lost"*. A tightening for later: the place kept with the pair, and a dropped arc of that face required to end within the guard of it. *None of the four conditions of the noting was pinned by a test*: with the guard dropped, and with all four dropped, every test of the branch passed; the review's test (above, *Held by*) fails on both and pins the guard. The refusal names the plane to move and the face it meets, with the tolerance and what to do; it gives no coordinates (the kernel's refusal carries none), and its advice may have to be followed twice (from 0.497 over, a move down by just over a tolerance lands at 0.503 under, refused again).

*Not decided, and owed.*

- **Which distance decides** at 0.3 tolerances over or under V (the plane's from the vertex, or the crossing's along the edge, 0.97 tolerances from V): as it was, one place.
- **Under V by 0.69**: the eaves' place 2.05 tolerances from V, past the guard, so not taken to it; U's place 0.88, taken to V. Refused as zero thickness at a point between V and the eaves' place before the repair (the trace's sweep; not one of the test's distances), and so after it, which changes no answer but *"imprint lost"*. Not traced.
- **Beside a spline face, 0.7 either side** (the roof's face a spline surface, its edges traced): *"imprint lost"* (Check 45021513502), and so before the repair, which changes that refusal only where it notes a pair; the planes build there, two places 2.26 and 2.08 tolerances apart. Not traced; by the distances, the traced edge is within the tolerance of the plane from V to its place, past the guard, and is taken to V as an edge that leaves a surface tangentially would be (`hugs`), V then 2.02 tolerances from the curve through the place. Held by an ignored test (`a_spline_roof_face_and_a_plane_past_its_vertex_by_more_than_the_guard`). The review's mutant without the guard (Check 45030956276) refused 0.7 over V at scale 1 as too near: the place is kept with a cut 2.26 tolerances from it, which only `hugs` takes that far.

*For whoever gives planes:* numbers from a computation unrounded, and a plane through a side of an outline with a normal exactly square to that side from the side's own numbers ([`plane-cut.md`](plane-cut.md) › *Planes from measured data*; the guide says so). That avoids the state; the refusal now says which plane, which faces and what to do.

### A spar's flat bottom beside a corner of a notch: traced and repaired (found 2026-11-19, the RC wing Jig's eighth point; repaired 2026-11-23, reviewed and amended the same day and on 2026-11-24; `section.rs` › `clip`, `clear_of_body`, `flat_face_clear_of`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_corner_beside.rs`, `boolean_corner_beside_review.rs`, `boolean_corner_beside_review2.rs`)

*Found* with the wing's seven other points (*A spar's face beside a rib's skin*, above): at the point listed seventh in the Jig's `range_known` (1620 mm span, 18 ribs of 1.5 mm sheet, spar choice 3, swept 27°), `measure.clearance` of the bottom spar and the ribs is refused, *"two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart (at the edge of tolerance) (face 3 of the first body, face 12 of the second body)"*, the nearest places found 0.0499995698 mm apart (the top spar there is clear by 0.0499974). After the marching repairs it was the one point of the Jig's range, and of all 33 Jigs' 3 538, still refused.

*Reproduced and reduced* (2026-11-23, with `main`'s program at 1f196da4; the bodies and the scripts are kept by the workshop, `plan/wing-eighth-point/`). The bottom spar and the ribs written to STEP at that point and read into a new document at 0.01 mm: `document.interference` of the spar and the eighteen ribs is clear but for the seventh rib, refused in the same words naming the spar's bottom and the rib's flat side (face 3 and face 0). The rib cut down by planes to a sliver about the left corner of the notch's mouth is refused still. With the notch's top cut away it is clear: the meshes then prove the gap (0.34 mm) and the kernel is not asked. With the spar cut to the 6 mm of its span about the rib it is answered (cut to 12 mm, refused): the cover of a long face turned 27° off the axes is boxes coarse enough to reach the corner, and with a short face no edge near the corner is asked where it meets the spar's plane.

*The corner.* At the rib's flat side the spar's bottom face ends 0.374 mm (along x; 0.339 mm square to the spar's edge) short of the notch's side. The notch's corner there is 1.063 tolerances below the plane of the spar's bottom. The skin's edge that leaves the corner, a spline fitted within the tolerance of the skin's section, is nearly level (0.99 to 1.10 tolerances below the plane over its last 2.7 mm; it rises through the plane 5.7 mm from the corner) and ends 0.066 tolerances from the corner, 0.998 tolerances below the plane: within the tolerance, where the corner is not. The rib's other flat side the same: 1.084 and 1.016.

*Traced.* The plane of the spar's bottom meets the rib's flat side in a line, and `clip` cuts that line where an edge of either face meets the other's surface: the skin's edge touches the plane at its end (a touch within the tolerance, not taken to the corner, which is further than the tolerance from the plane), and the notch's side crosses it 1.063 tolerances over the corner. The two cuts are 0.997 tolerances apart, and two cuts between half and twice the tolerance apart that are not one meeting of an edge of each body were refused there, before the stretches between the cuts are judged; both lie 0.37 mm outside the spar's face, and no stretch of the line near them is in it. On the Jig's own pair, the spar moved up and down by fractions of a tolerance: the refusal holds exactly while the skin edge's end is within the tolerance of the plane and the corner is not (this side from 0.0000265 mm up to 0.00063 mm down, the rib's other side from 0.00016 to 0.00084 mm down, by the numbers above), refused at each of the eleven heights tried inside those windows and answered at each of the eight outside them. The developer's rule of 2026-10-22 refuses faces of exact bodies between half and twice the tolerance apart; here nothing of the one body is within twice the tolerance of the other (the corner is 34 tolerances from the spar's face, the bodies 0.05 mm apart at their nearest, under the notch's top). **The rule applied wrong**: two places that close were refused whether or not anything near them is within twice the tolerance of the spar.

*The pair made by commands*, without the Jig: a prism over a triangle with a corner at the origin, one edge up the y axis and one up and to the left at 45° (the notch's side and the skin), 1.5 thick; and a block 6 by 3 by 800 turned 25° about the y axis (a swept spar), its bottom the plane `y = d` tolerances, its bottom face beginning `beside` tolerances beyond the corner where it crosses the prism's face. The line where the bottom's plane meets that face crosses the upright edge at W and the slanting one at H, `d` tolerances apart; with `d` from 1.06 to 1.9 they are the two places, crossings both here, where the Jig's were a touch and a crossing. Every boolean of the two and their interference were refused as the Jig's pair was, at `beside` of 3, 37 and 300 tolerances, as found and turned in space, at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6: 72 pairs (Check 45241997155, the test on `main`'s code). The two pairs made before (a long bar askew beside a prism whose bottom slopes up from the corner at 0.3 and at 0.015) are answered because the slope decides: H is `d / slope` from W, at those slopes 3.3 and 67 times `d`, past twice the tolerance (measured with `main`'s program on the pair by commands at 0.01 mm, the face 0.37 mm beyond the corner: at slopes 0.3 and 0.015 answered at `d` 1.06 and 1.5; at 0.6 refused at 1.06, H then 1.77 tolerances from W, and answered at 1.5, 2.5).
