With the block's face a spline surface on its plane `main` answered already (not traced why); with the prism's face one it refused too.

*The repair, as first built* (77e2a37c). In `clip`, two cuts closer together than the tolerance can tell apart that are not one meeting were two cuts, not a refusal, where both are clear outside one of the two faces: outside it (`Outline::side`) and further than twice the tolerance (the guard) from its boundary (`Outline::within`). The branch is taken only where the boolean was refused before, so every boolean that was answered takes the same path, bit for bit. On it the tests of the time passed (Checks 45241997155 on `main`'s code and 45242869126), and the benchmark's counts, the corpus and the random designs were `main`'s (Heavy 45244256703 and 45244263466 against the nightly run 45087810006; Gate 45244894007).

*The review* (2026-11-23; the workshop's `plan/wing-eighth-point/review/`, Check 45262455095): not fit as it stood. The two places alone do not say that the bodies do not meet there. A corner whose edges lean away from the face (the triangle (0, 0), (−2.5, 5), (−7.5, 5), the block 5 high, its bottom 1.5 tolerances over the corner, its face beginning 1.55 tolerances beyond it): the plane crosses the two edges 1.50 tolerances apart, 2.08 and 3.44 tolerances from the face's edge, both clear of it; but the leaning edge, between its place and the corner, passes the block's edge 1.90 tolerances from it, the least distance of the two bodies. `main` refused every boolean of the pair (by the two places, as the rule of 2026-10-22 has it); the first repair built the union, two shells 1.90 tolerances apart, and called the interference clear. The review also measured where the face may begin (the nearer place 1.95 tolerances from the face's edge refused, 2.0 to 2.10 told apart, the bodies then that far apart, at `d` 1.06 and 1.5, with the block's side a straight or a bowed spline the same) and a loose body's fillet and chamfer that meet the state through `loose_op::boolean_for_tool` (*Owed*, below).

*The second repair* (the round after the review, 66b3e191 and dbb92c1d). Besides both places being clear outside the flat face X, what of the other body lay between them had to keep further than twice the tolerance from X: the two edges that carry the places in full, every edge of that body at an end of either within twice the tolerance of X's plane, and the meeting curve between the places, each proven by halving with a bound on its reach (`beside_the_face`, `clear_of_face`). The review's corner was refused again; the tests, the benchmark's counts, the corpus and the random designs (two of the slowest timed out beside other runs and gave `main`'s answers alone, Check 45280666309) were `main`'s, the trial Gate passed (Checks 45265103821 and 45266336752, Heavy 45266343250 and 45266350083, Gate 45267220841).

*The second review* (2026-11-24; the workshop's `plan/wing-eighth-point/review2/`, Check 45288463969): not fit as it stood; the set measured was too small for its claim. Two pairs `main` refuses at the two places were united, two shells 1.90 tolerances apart, check passing, interference clear. A beak: from the far end of the edge that carries one place, 3.5 tolerances over X's plane (so not taken), the rib's edge runs down past the block's edge 1.8987 tolerances from it. A rod with a flat along its underside (X), turned as the spar is, whose round side bulges a tolerance past the flat's edge: the rib's upright edge, which carries a place, keeps 2.9 tolerances from X and passes the round side 1.9 from it. Smaller findings: a spline's speed bound holds only within its knots (a range beyond them was taken as given); a boundary piece of X that is an ellipse or a spline was measured by a search good to a thousandth of the distance; the 2.01 case left out of the leaning test was not pinned.

*The repair as it stands* (round three; `section.rs` › `clear_of_body`, `flat_face_clear_of`). Two such cuts are two cuts, not a refusal, only where both places are clear outside one of the two faces and every face of that face's body is proven further than 18 tolerances from both places: 16 about them (`NEAR_PLACES`, the section's `APART`, at which it passes over two faces as not meeting) and twice the tolerance. Then nothing within 16 tolerances of either place, of either body, is within twice the tolerance of that body's faces (the distance to a set changes by no more than the distance moved; an edge of an exact body lies within the tolerance of its faces' surfaces, not on them, and a face proven by its plane or its surface is proven so without its edges, so of such an edge only more than one tolerance: the third review), so neither place, nor the corner between them, nor any edge or face near them is where the bodies meet, whatever their shape: the review's corner, the beak and the rod are within reach and refused, as on `main`. Each face of the first body is proven far from each place in one of four ways, any other way out refusing: its box does not come within 18 tolerances of the place along any axis; for a flat face, the place is further than that from its plane, or the place's foot on the plane is outside the face (`side`; the face's nearest point is then on its boundary) and every piece of its boundary is proven further than that (`cover::may_come_within`: a line or a circle in closed form, any other curve by halving with its speed bound, a spline's piece only within its knots); for a face that is not flat, its surface keeps clear of the cube of that half side about the place (`clear_of_surface`, an analytic surface only: its signed distance at the place, never more than the distance to the surface, exceeds the cube's half diagonal), or the face's cover holds no leaf within 18 tolerances of the place (`Cover::holds`). A face whose box is empty is not proven; any other box is taken as it is, as the section takes it everywhere (a box that is not finite is not refused: the operands' check refuses geometry that is not finite). The box and the cover take a spline edge to lie within the hull of its control points, as the section does everywhere, which a piece beyond its knots does not: the knot check of the third way does not reach them (the second review's spline speed bound, owed with it). The cover's answer depends on what it was asked before (at its cap on nodes it answers "may hold"), only towards a refusal; the section alone asks its own covers, in its order, so the answer is the same on every run and thread, but `a ∪ b` and `b ∪ a` can differ there. The branch is still taken only where the boolean was refused before. At the Jig's point the spar is 33.9 tolerances from both places (its bottom face's edge 0.339 mm square to it, its side's plane as far), so the spar's six flat faces are proven at once. What this round gives up: places from twice the tolerance to 18 tolerances from the first body, which the earlier rounds told apart where the corner and its edges were clear, are refused again as on `main` (the tests' 3 tolerances beyond the corner, the reviews' 2.05, the leaning corners).

*Why there.* Both cuts are right: the skin's edge ends within the tolerance of the plane, and the notch's side crosses it; neither may be taken to the corner, which is beyond the tolerance. Two such places where the curve is in both faces are the rule's to refuse (the block's face reaching over the corner, beginning right over it, or one tolerance beyond it, is refused as before), and so is anything near them within twice the tolerance of the first body: a corner that leans nearer the face, an edge from beyond the corner, a round side of the first body. What was wrong was refusing the two places where the whole first body is far from them, as the spar is from the Jig's corner. Why the whole first body and not the face: the second review's rod. Why a ball about the places and not chosen edges: the second review's beak; any edge or face of either body near the places is inside the ball, whatever its ends. Why 16 tolerances: the distance at which the section already holds two faces not to meet; nearer approaches beyond it are not this refusal's to find (below). Why not the second review's other way (the second body's edges near the places, chosen by where they go, proven against every face of the first body near them): an approach of the first body's edges to a curved face of the second (the Jig's skin is a spline) would have to be proven too, and the kernel bounds a curved face's distance only through its cover, whose leaves are no finer than a 64th of the face's box: not at the Jig's distances. The ball asks only of the first body, which at the Jig is six planes.

*Held by* `boolean_corner_beside.rs`: the pair at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6, as found and turned, `d` 1.06, 1.5 and 1.9, the face beginning 21, 37 and 300 tolerances beyond the corner (72 pairs): the union two shells holding the two volumes, each less the other itself (its faces and volume), the intersection empty, the interference clear, every result passing its check and the boolean's own (`verify`); the same beside a spline surface at 1 and 1e3, at 21 and 37, the block's bottom (which `main` answers already) or the prism's face a spline on its plane (16 pairs); the face over the corner by 5 tolerances, beginning right over it, 1, 3 and 17 tolerances beyond it (`d` 1.06 and 1.5; 80 pairs): every boolean refused at the edge of tolerance with a face of each body, as on `main`; a corner whose edges lean away from the face (0.5 and 1.5 per unit up), the bodies 1.95, 1.99, 2.01 or 2.05 tolerances apart (all refused, 2.01 at `d` 1.06 among them, pinned as the second review asked) and the face 21 or 37 tolerances beyond the corner (told apart), at four scales as found and turned (1.99 and 2.01 at 1, as found). `boolean_corner_beside_review.rs` (the first review's): W 1.95, 2.05 and 17.9 tolerances from the face's edge refused, 18.1 told apart, at four scales as found and turned; the first review's leaning corner refused; a loose body's fillet and chamfer next to the state as on `main` (with a vertex elsewhere loose built by the narrow try, taking what a straight edge's blend takes; with the edge's own far end loose refused, in the narrow try's words). `boolean_corner_beside_review2.rs` (the second review's, unchanged): the beak and the rod not united, at 1 and 1e3 as found and turned. `boolean_corner_beside_review3.rs` (the third review's): the beak moved far beyond the notch, built where `main` refuses (a wrong body, failing by design: *Owed*); the second review's rod with its round side 37 tolerances from the places told apart, a face of the first body that is not flat proven far by its surface (`clear_of_surface`), at 1 and 1e3 as found and turned. On `main`'s code the told-apart pairs of the first file are refused but those with the block's bottom a spline (Check 45241997155).

*Measured* (round three, on 5325a00d, against `main` 1f196da4). Check 45290928742: fmt, clippy, the section's unit tests, twenty kernel test files (the three corner files, fifteen neighbouring boolean files, fillet and chamfer) and eight of the engine's on interference, clearance and contact pass; the benchmark's work counts are `main`'s, line for line (117 lines); the corner files' 328 lines as designed (the second review's beak and rod refused at the two places, as on `main`, at 1 and 1e3, as found and turned; their pairs without two places too near built, as on `main`). The boolean corpus (Heavy 45292929410): its report the same line for line as `main`'s nightly run 45087810006, digest `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, 11 594 agreeing, no mismatch, none invalid. The random designs, seed by seed against that nightly run (Heavy 45292933886 from seed 20 780 000 for an hour: 48 414 seeds): every one gives the same verdict, groups, accepted groups and refusals word for word; none moves between a body and a refusal, and the same eight are refused as an invalid body on both. The trial Gate on 5ffc7543 (this code with the texts; Gate 45292938159): passed (`ci`, 723 test binaries, none failed; `freecad`; the web module's size).

*Measured on the second repair* (on 66b3e191 and dbb92c1d): see *The second repair* above; the details are kept by the workshop (`plan/wing-eighth-point/round2/`).

*Owed.*

- **The Jig, on the repair as it stands.** The coordinator measured the Jig on a build of the first repair (1e129943): all eight listed points of the RC wing clear where `main` has seven, the 33 Jigs' ranges 3 538 of 3 538 points, the 112 published items the same as on 0.2.1. Not measured again on this round's code, which refuses more than the first, and only where some face of the spar is within 18 tolerances of the places (at the Jig's point the spar is 33.9 tolerances from them).
- **Callers that try again after any refusal.** `loose_op::boolean_for_tool` asks a tool's boolean of a loose body at four times the working tolerance and, on any refusal, again at the working tolerance; where the wide try was refused in this way and is now answered, the operation's body is the wide try's. The first review's case (a block's edge 800 long beside a notched rib, the state at four times the working tolerance, the block 5 of those tolerances from the places) is within reach and refused at the wide try again, as on `main`; a case with the first body further than 18 of the wide tolerances from the places would build at the wide try. None is measured.
- **What the proof does not reach.** Places within 18 tolerances of the first body are refused as on `main`, however clear the approaches near them (a false refusal, as before the repair). Approaches between the bodies further than 16 tolerances from the two places are not this refusal's: where no two cuts are this close `main` does not see such an approach either (below). But where two places are told apart, the boolean `main` stopped at them goes on, and meets such an approach elsewhere in the same pair as `main` meets it where it does not stop (the next point).
- **A wrong body `main` gave until 2026-11-24** (measured by the second review on 1f196da4 with `craft3d-cli` at 0.01 mm): the second review's beak without the notch (the rib's edge passing the block's edge 1.8987 tolerances from it, no two places too near) and its rod with the flat 2.5 tolerances over the corner (the rib's upright edge 1.9 tolerances from the round side): interference clear and the union built, two parts under twice the tolerance apart. An edge or a face that passes another body within the band without crossing it or touching it at a vertex is not found by the section, nor by the union's check of zero thickness. Owed, on `main`. This repair does not leave it as it was: in a pair that also holds two places told apart, a boolean `main` refused at them is now built with such an approach in it. The third review's pair (`boolean_corner_beside_review3.rs`, its first test failing by design; the workshop's `plan/wing-eighth-point/review3/`): the second review's beak and block moved 31.95 tolerances to the right together, the notch left where it was, so that its two places, 1.8 tolerances apart, are 33 tolerances from the block and the beak passes the block's edge 1.8987 tolerances from it, 34 tolerances further on. `main` refuses the union and both subtracts at the two places and calls the interference unknown (`craft3d-cli` at 1f196da4, at 1, as found); the repair builds them all, the union two shells under twice the tolerance apart, its check passing, the interference clear, at 1 and 1e3, as found and turned (Check 45305605973). Mending `main`'s blind spot mends this too: **mended 2026-11-24**, this repair merged together with the look for near approaches (*Near approaches inside an edge or a face*, below), which refuses that union as zero thickness in both orders, at 1 and 1e3, as found and turned, the subtracts built and the interference a touch (Check 45339516563, on a merge of the two), and the wing's own pair still told apart; the beak without the notch and the rod are refused by it too.

### A plane cut the boolean's own check refused as an invalid body (found 2026-11-20 by fuzz seeds 118385 and 115098, repaired 2026-11-21, reviewed and amended the same day, reread and amended 2026-11-22; `outline.rs` › `nearest_on_spline`, `nearest_by_pieces`, `left_of`, `left_at`, `touched_at`; `section.rs` › `edge_cuts`)

*Found* by the plane cut's step of the random designs ([`plane-cut.md`](plane-cut.md) › *As built: the machines*; Heavy 44825476736): two cuts refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"*, the boolean's own check of what it had made, so nothing invalid was handed on and each group counted as a refusal, not a finding. Seed 118385 (scale 1e3, tolerance 1e-3), group 1, at the cut's second plane: *"edge 1 is used 1 times …; edge 3 is used 1 times …; Euler-Poincaré fails: V - E + F - R = 3 - 4 + 2 - 0"*. Seed 115098 (scale 1), group 5, at the cut's first plane: *"edge 14 is used 1 times …; Euler-Poincaré fails: V - E + F - R = 10 - 15 + 7 - 1"*. Both are the boolean's, not the plane cut's: each is refused the same as a plain intersection with a block. They are two faults with nothing in common but the words, and neither is the plane that passes a vertex by less than a tolerance (above): that case has no spline edge and no loop touched at the middle of a piece, and its ignored test prints after this repair the table it printed before it, row for row (Check 44936308299: built, *"imprint lost"* and *"two points closer together"* at the same distances, the same vertex counts and nearest distances).

**Seed 118385: a place where a spline edge crosses a plane, not found again on its own edge.**

*The seed* (Check 44931053048 printed its commands): `feature.cone {radius 7500, height 7500, position [-10000, 0, 0], axis [1, 0, 0]}`, then `feature.planeCut` by four planes, of which the first two refuse it: `{point [-6250, 10.003936261680792, 0], normal [0, 1, 1], side above}` and the cone's base face moved in by 5000, `{plane "F1:face(1)", offset -5000, side above}`.

*Reduced*, at any scale (here 1, tolerance 1e-6), to a cone on the origin and two planes:

```json
[{"command": "feature.cone", "params": {"radius": 7.5, "height": 7.5, "name": "Cone"}},
 {"command": "feature.planeCut", "params": {"target": "Cone", "name": "Half", "planes": [{"point": [0.01, 0, 3.75], "normal": [1, 1, 0], "side": "above"}]}},
 {"command": "feature.planeCut", "params": {"target": "Half", "planes": [{"point": [0, 0, 5], "normal": [0, 0, 1]}]}}]
```

The first plane passes the axis by `c / √2`, 0.007 for `c = 0.01`: it halves the cone, and the edge it leaves on the cone is a hyperbola whose tip is that far from the apex, a rational spline of 16 spans whose parameter crawls about the tip (a quarter of its parameters, 0.37 to 0.63, lie within 0.13 of the tip on a curve 21.2 long; its one speed bound is 224.9 a unit of parameter). The second plane, level at height `h`, crosses that edge twice.

*What main did, measured* (`craft3d-cli` of `main` on the script, and the section traced on the build server, Check 44932333437): refused as an invalid body at every `h` tried from half the height up to 7.3, for `c` of 0.01, 0.003, 0.001 and 0.0001 (levels 4 to 7.3 for each; for 0.01 and 0.001 also every 0.025 from 3.5 to 4: 3.725 builds, 3.75 does not), and at the seed's own level at scale 1e3, also with the block made by hand and intersected; from 7.4 up refused as invalid or as *"the result is not the boolean of its operands"*. Below half the height it built for 0.01, 0.003 and 0.001; for 0.0001 only the lowest levels did, 3 and 3.5 being refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary"* (*Owed*). With `c` of 0.03 and more it built at every height tried. At `h = 5` the crossings of the hyperbola with the plane were found, at parameters 0.0716 and 0.9284 (`hits::curve_hits`); asked where on the edge the first lies (`outline::locate`), the nearest point of the edge was answered as the tip, parameter 0.5, **3.5305 away from a point that is on the curve**. `edge_cuts` then left that place out (a hit that is not on its edge is passed over, which is right for a line or a circle, whose closed form gives the hits of the whole curve). With one cut where there are two, the circle the plane cuts from the cone had no stretch between two cuts (its one stretch, right round, has its middle outside the face) and the line it cuts from the flat face ran from the one cut out of the faces' boxes: both were dropped, nothing was imprinted, the cone's face was kept whole and the flat face left out by one point each, and the two faces left went to the check.

*Cause.* `nearest_on_spline` is a branch and bound on stretches of the parameter: a stretch is no nearer than its nearer end less half its length, the length taken from the curve's one speed bound; depth first, the upper half of the parameters before the lower. It gave up after 20,000 stretches and answered with the nearest sample it had. For a point on the lower arm, the first sample is the tip (the middle of the parameters), and the upper half is searched first: about the tip the curve is 3.53 away over a quarter of the parameters while the bound says it could travel 56, so the stretches there are proven no nearer only at a thousandth of 3.53 each, and the 20,000 are spent before the lower half, where the point is, is looked at. The edge at exactly half the height is where a point of the arm is as near to the arm's end as to the tip: below it the search starts from the end, nearer than the tip, and the stretches about the tip are left at once. So the fault is in neither the intersection curves nor the classification: a helper every boolean with a spline edge asks answered wrongly, silently, and the section believed it.

*Repaired, at the cause.* (1) A search that runs out of stretches is done again by the curve's Bézier pieces (`nearest_by_pieces`): one piece per knot span, the first and last cut to the range by de Casteljau's algorithm, each bounded below by the distance to the box of its control points (the piece lies in their hull, its weights being positive), the nearest box first, a piece sampled at its middle and halved until its box is no wider than a thousandth of the best distance; when the nearest box left is no nearer than the best sample, that sample is the nearest to that resolution, and Newton's method finishes as before. Its bounds are each piece's own, so what it costs is set by the curve near the point, not by how the parameter runs. A search that does not run out is not touched: it answers as it always did, bit for bit. Past 200,000 pieces, or without positive weights, the answer is `None`, never a guess. (2) `edge_cuts` refuses, in the words of a bug with the two faces, a place of a *spline* edge that is not found on that edge again: a spline's hits are looked for on the edge alone, so one that is not on it is a cut lost, and what was a silent loss is a refusal the fuzzer reports as a finding.

*After the repair, measured* (Check 44934151587, the kernel alone: `c` by level by side, at scales 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, the same at each): for `c` of 0.02, 0.01 and 0.003 every level from 1 up to the tip builds on both sides and passes `topo::check`; for 0.001 up to 7.3, for 0.0003 up to 5, for 0.0001 only at 1; `c` of 0.00003 (the tip 21 tolerances from the apex) is refused at the first plane. No invalid body and no *"not the boolean of its operands"* is left among the 468 level cuts tried (156 a scale: six distances, thirteen levels, two sides). What is still refused is refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary to tell inside from outside (at the edge of tolerance)"*, with the face: see *Owed*.

**Seed 115098: a loop given to the wrong region where another loop touches it.**

*The seed*: a cylinder along x with its end rims filleted, taken out of a block whose side it is tangent to, the block then cut across the hole: `feature.cylinder {axis [1, 0, 0], height 20, position [-12.5, -5, 10], radius 2.5}`, `feature.fillet` of both rims by 0.625, `feature.block {position [-12.5, -7.5, 3.75367379315737], size [2.5, 20, 17.5]}`, `feature.boolean {op subtract, target the block, tool the cylinder, keepTool}`, `feature.planeCut {planes [{point [-10.625, 12.5, 12.50367379315737], normal [-1, 0, 0]}]}`. The fillets play no part.

*Reduced*:

```json
[{"command": "feature.block", "params": {"size": [2, 10, 10], "name": "Block"}},
 {"command": "feature.cylinder", "params": {"axis": [1, 0, 0], "position": [-2, 2.5, 5], "radius": 2.5, "height": 6, "name": "Drill"}},
 {"command": "feature.boolean", "params": {"op": "subtract", "target": "Block", "tool": "Drill", "name": "Holed"}},
 {"command": "feature.planeCut", "params": {"target": "Holed", "planes": [{"point": [1, 0, 0], "normal": [-1, 0, 0]}]}}]
```

The hole's wall is tangent to the side `y = 0` along a line: a wall that thins to nothing, which the boolean accepts (*A shell that touches itself*, rule 2, above). The plane across the hole cuts a circle from its wall and a rectangle from the block, and the circle touches the rectangle's side in a point that is a vertex of neither: two curves on the cut face that meet between their vertices, which only an operand that touches itself can give.

*What main did, measured*: refused as an invalid body (10 vertices, 15 edges, 7 faces, one ring: the cut face a whole rectangle, the circle used by the hole's wall alone), on either side of the plane, also as an intersection with a block made by hand, at scale 1e3 as at 1. With the cylinder turned 37 degrees about its axis (`"angle": "37 deg"`) the same cut builds, the circle a hole in the cut face. The circle a plane cuts from a cylinder starts on the x axis of the cylinder's frame; at 0 degrees that is opposite the side, so **the middle of the circle is the point where it touches the rectangle**.

*Cause.* The regions of a face are its loops grouped: a point just left of each loop (`outline::left_of`) says whether the loop is an outline or a hole, and which outline a hole lies in. That point is stepped in from the middle of a piece of the loop, and is taken when nothing is nearer to it than that piece. From the middle of the circle, on the side away from the disc, every step lands beyond the rectangle's side, exactly as far from the side as from the circle (the side passes through the foot): nothing is *nearer*, so the point was taken, outside the rectangle. The hole's loop was then given to the region outside the rectangle, the rectangle was kept without its hole, and the circle was left with one face. So the fault is in the regions, in what the boolean already had: the imprint and the classification of each region were right. Measured by switching the repair's rule off again (Check 44935626080, mutant 5 below): the cut is refused as before and the point lies outside the plate.

*Repaired, in the regions* (not at the cause, which is two loops of one face that touch between their vertices: the face has no vertex there, *Owed*; and at first only for a touch that is exact in the numbers: *The review, and the round after it*, below; what a touch is told by has changed twice since, and the rule as it stands is in *The reread, and round three*). A candidate is left out when a piece other than its own (and other than the same arc run the other way, which is in the loops twice) is as near as its own piece *at the place where its own piece is nearest* (`touched_at`: to 1e-9 of the distance, the two nearest points within a thousandth of it): that piece lies between the two. The loop's pieces are then tried from their other two stations (0.2764 and 0.7236 of a piece, the ones `interior_points` uses). A piece as near elsewhere (the far side of a strip twice the step wide) is not between, and such a candidate is taken as before; where no touch is found, the answer comes from the middle or not at all, as before, so every other loop gets the point it got.

*After the repair, measured* (Checks 44934151587 and 44934154290): the cut builds on both sides with the circle starting anywhere (opposite the touch, at it, at the second station, at 37 and 90 degrees), 10 vertices, 15 edges, 7 faces, volume `100 − 6.25 π` and area `240 − 7.5 π` to 1e-9 at three scales. Through the commands the same, but for one place: with the circle *starting* at the touch (`"angle": "180 deg"`) the cut body has a vertex there, on an edge of the rectangle it is no end of, and regeneration's check of every body refuses it, *"the cut body touches itself at (1, 0, 5) … in a point or along a line that is no edge of both"*, where the same body with its vertex elsewhere is accepted (with the touch away from the middle of the side the same cut is refused as *"cannot be meshed"* instead: *Owed*). That is older than this repair (`main` builds that cut in the boolean and refuses it the same way) and is the developer's open question of *A tangency line on which another wall ends* (above), now with a case in which one body is accepted or refused by where a circle starts.

**Both seeds as found** build: 118385's four planes (three leave a face, the fourth changes nothing) and 115098's cut to a plate of `0.625 × 20 × 17.5` less its hole, each passing the deep check (`crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs`).

**Held to.** `boolean/outline.rs`: *the nearest point of a spline whose parameter crawls is the nearest* (every point of the hyperbola is found on it, points beside it as near as any of 20,001 samples, on the whole edge and on parts of it, for three distances of the plane; and the search by pieces is what answered), *the search by pieces finds the nearest sample of any part* (alone, on 400 parts of the hyperbola and of a circle that begin and end inside spans, the circle's centre among the points), *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it* (a plate with a hole touching its side, the touch at the circle's middle, start and second station and elsewhere; the plate's own point bit for bit where a piece is as near elsewhere). `boolean/section.rs`: *a place of a spline edge not found on it again is refused* (with the search by pieces switched off on the thread, the cut is refused in the bug's words with its two faces; with it, built). `crates/kernel/tests/halfspace.rs`: *half a cone cut beside its apex is cut level* (three distances, five levels, both sides, three scales, to the closed forms of volume and area, `∫ A dr = r³ acos(d / r) / 3 − 2 d r s / 3 + d³ ln(r + s) / 3`, and to the boolean's full check), *a level cut near the tip is built or refused in words* (the grid above: what builds today must build, what does not is refused in the words of *Owed*, never by the boolean's own check), *a hole tangent to a side is cut across* (five starts, both sides, three scales). `crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs`: the two seeds as found and both reductions through the commands.

*Each part of the repair is needed by a test* (Check 44935626080, on a throwaway commit with each part switched off in turn; with none off all six pass):

| Part switched off | Tests that then fail |
|---|---|
| the search by pieces (the answer as before) | `outline` › *the nearest point of a spline whose parameter crawls*; `section` › *a place of a spline edge not found on it again* (its cut is not built); `halfspace.rs` › *half a cone cut beside its apex* |
| the first and last piece cut to the range | `outline` › *the nearest point of a spline whose parameter crawls*, *the search by pieces finds the nearest sample* |
| a piece left only when its box is no nearer than the best sample (left at half that) | the same two |
| the refusal of a place not found on its spline edge | `section` › *a place of a spline edge not found on it again* |
| a candidate left out where another piece touches its own | `outline` › *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it*; `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side is cut across* |
| the same arc run the other way not counted as touching | all six |
| the other stations tried after a touch | `outline` › *a point left of a loop*; `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side* |
| "at the place where its own piece is nearest" (any piece as near counts) | `outline` › *a point left of a loop* (the plate's own point moves) |

**What moved.** Nothing that was built before. The boolean corpus on the repair (Heavy 44935631209 on 6f21da38) gives the digest of all results the base's code is recorded with, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`: 11,988 outcomes, 11,594 agreeing (5,720 match, 2,960 match~, 1,891 empty, 1,023 zero thickness by design), 228 unsupported, 166 unscored, no mismatch and none invalid, so no row changed. (The base's digest is the one written above for the round `main` was merged from, not a corpus run of this round on `main`'s head.) The benchmark's work counts are the same on the repair as on the base in all 115, read count by count from the two runs on one runner (Checks 44935628644 on 6f21da38 and 44936917282 on aee56914). They are not all what `baseline.txt` says: the base itself differs from that file in twelve counts, all within the test's margin (the turbofan's `boolean_edge_candidates` 31,689 against 33,269, the rotor's `triangles` 6,718 against 6,404, the engine block's `blend_sections` 30 against 28, and nine smaller), which is older than this round and was left as found: the file is not this round's to change. *Not measured:* how often a search runs out of stretches, or a loop is found touched, on the corpus or the benchmark's models; that nothing moved there says the repair changed no result on them, not that those paths were never taken.

*The random designs, seed by seed against the base* (thirty minutes each from seed 100,000: Heavy 44941617067 on aee56914, 28,375 seeds, and Heavy 44937308177 on d5d7a279, 19,890 seeds; the 19,890 both ran, 100,000 to 119,889, compared by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word). 19,884 are the same. Six differ, each from a refusal to a body, none the other way, every one passing the oracles on both sides: the two seeds of this finding; 100962, a boolean refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary"*; 102119, a pattern whose join was refused as *"imprint lost"*; 110389, a cut extrusion refused as *"a piece of a face is not closed off by its boundary"*; and 114662, a plane cut refused as *"imprint lost"* at its second plane, which now reaches its fourth (which leaves nothing, rightly refused). Which of the two repairs each of the four rests on was not traced. No design reaches the refusal of a place not found on its spline edge, and neither run has a crash, a bug or a wrong result (one timeout in both, seed 117369, known). *"boolean produced an invalid body"* is left at eight seeds, the same eight in both runs and none of them a plane cut (the cut of a fillet at 103578, 110541, 110965 and 116424, a pattern's join at 108069 and 115646, a boolean at 111609 and 114519): not looked into here, owed as this finding was.

**The review, and the round after it (2026-11-21).** The repair was reviewed the same day by measuring it (its tests are on the branch, two passing and three owed when it handed them in; Checks 44948558534, 44949983905, 44950638367 and 44951286420) and found fit to merge after named changes: nothing it measured was worse than `main` and no body was wrong, but the touch rule held only for a touch that is exact in the numbers, the search by pieces stopped at a floor that was not the curve's, parts of the repair were held by no test, and the texts claimed more than was built. A second round made those changes; what it left is under *Owed*.

*What the review measured.* Through the commands, the base's program against the repair's, every cut into both of its sides, held to closed forms where there is one and to the deep check: the halved cone cut again (six families of second planes, 2,160 sides over four scales), of which `main` built 1,796 and the repair 1,996, none lost (the 120 invalid bodies, the 56 refusals at the edge of tolerance and the 24 *"imprint lost"* of `main` all build); and tangencies (528 sides), 176 built on `main` and 302 on the repair, none lost, every built body to its closed form. On the build server: a piece's box is a true bound (10,155 pieces of 160 drawn rational splines, weights over up to eight decades, cut unevenly), and the order of the search is the same every run; `nearest_on_ellipse` never passes 16,383 of its 20,000 stretches over 26,576 asks; of the four other seeds that turned from a refusal into a body, 100962, 102119 and 114662 rest on the search by pieces and 110389 on the touch rule; on the benchmark's models no search runs out of stretches and no loop is found touched. Thirteen mutants of its own, of which seven were failed by no test.

*The touch rule held only for a touch exact in the numbers.* `touched_at` told a touch by two distances being the same to 1e-9 *of the step*, and the steps shrink from half the distance between the quarter points of the piece (the radius of a hole) to a fifth of a tolerance. A circle that passes the outline by one unit in the last place of a coordinate (which is what a touch a designer gives exactly comes to anywhere but on binary fractions in the axes) is as near at the first steps and measurably further at the last, where the candidate beyond the outline was taken as before the repair. Measured by the review: the plate of the unit test with its hole moved towards the side is answered outside the plate from 2.3e-16 on in the axes and from 1.2e-16 on out of them; in the kernel 2 of 42 cuts at decimal places and 15 of 18 cuts of bodies turned out of the axes were refused as an invalid body; through the commands one of 24 decimal placements (8 of 96 sides over four scales), three of twelve turns by `feature.transform`, and two holes that touch one another at scale 1e-3.

*Changed: a touch is told to a billionth of the width of the piece as well, at every step* (`touched_at`, `left_at`). The width is the distance between the piece's quarter points, what the steps start from; a billionth of it is a length that does not shrink with the step, so a piece that passes its own by rounding is as near, at the place where its own is nearest, at the last step as at the first: 5e-9 on a hole of radius 2.5, a two-hundredth of a tolerance of 1e-6. That is what "to rounding" means here: a part of the size of the piece, whatever the tolerance. (Whatever the tolerance was wrong, and since the reread of 2026-11-22 it is no wider than a quarter of it: *The reread, and round three*, below.)

*Tried first, and thrown away: a piece found touched left for good at that place* (the review's own trial, which had passed its Checks on the three kernel files). It was built, passed this round's hand-in Check, the corpus, the benchmark's counts and thirty minutes of random designs unchanged (Check 44954841374, Heavy 44954846304 and 44954843944 on 0148fed1), and failed the trial Gate (44955620640) in `boolean_border_tangent.rs` › *a cylinder at a plate's edge at one*: 56 faults, the eight questions of seven places (a boss sunk fifty tolerances into a plate, its footprint tangent to the plate's edge, the vertex of its rim 0.4 and 1.4 tolerances off that edge) each refused as *"a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it"* where the first repair built them. Traced (Check 44958876927): the loop is one full circle that begins and ends where it is tangent to the edge. A step of the circle's radius in from *any* place on a circle lands on its centre, which is as far from every tangent to the circle as from the circle itself, and the place on the circle nearest to its centre is no place in particular: here the circle's seam, 1.8e-3 from the place of tangency, which is within a thousandth of that step. So the first step from each of the three places was the same point, told as touched, the piece was left at all three, and no step was left to answer; the next step, half the radius, is a good point, which the first repair took. A touch told at a long step need not hold at a shorter one, so each candidate is asked afresh, and what the trial was after (the touch told at the last step too) is had by the measure above instead. One build shows the choice (Check 44958876927 and again 44960586048, the grid under each rule): told by the step alone, it passes; with a touched piece left for good, the 56 faults; told to the piece's width as well, it passes. (Only one of that grid's three scales caught it, the reread of 2026-11-22 found: at a thousandth and at a million the grid passed with a touched piece left for good, and no other test of the five files failed. Since then *a hole that touches the outline in two places and its disc are answered* holds it in the unit tests, and since round three so do the thin walls and the grid at a million: *The reread, and round three*, below.)

*Measured, the rule as built against the first repair's* (the two switched in one build, Checks 44960586048 and 44960588354): the plate's hole moved past its side or short of it by nothing to 1e-11, in three frames, 13 of 30 outside the plate before and none after; in the kernel, 60 cuts at three scales (36 at decimal places, 12 turned out of the axes, 12 past the side or short of it by a billionth and a millionth of the tolerance), 36 of 120 sides refused as an invalid body before (4 at decimal places, 20 of the 24 turned out of the axes, all 12 past the side) and every side built after, to its closed form; through the commands at four scales, 2 of 64 sides at decimal places (the block at 1.1 with a hole of radius 1.7, at scale 1), 16 of 48 of the reduction turned by `feature.transform` and 2 of 8 of two holes that touch (scale 1e-3) refused before, and every side built after, to closed forms and the deep check.

*Changed: the floor of the search by pieces is the curve's own* (`nearest_by_pieces`, `SPLINE_FLOOR`; the review's trial). A piece is halved until its box is no wider than a thousandth of the best distance, or than a floor, which stops the search about a point that lies on the curve. The floor was the caller's, 1e-9 of the curve's length *by its speed bound*, a number made for stretches of the parameter; where that bound is far above the curve's true speed (a rational spline whose weights lie decades apart: the bound grows with their ratio) it was wider than any tolerance, the search answered with the middle of a box that wide, and Newton's method did not always finish from there. Measured by the review on points of drawn rational splines, not found on their own curve to 1e-9: 83 of 600 with weights over eight decades (the worst 2.3 away) and 23 of 600 over six, none of 1,800 over four, two and none (and on `main`, before the search by pieces, 287, 277, 236, 158 and 13 of 600: the repair mended generic rational splines, not only the hyperbola). The floor is now 1e-9 of the diagonal of the box of the control points of the range searched, a length of the curve itself: none of the 600 over eight decades is missed and no search looks at more than 58 pieces; with no floor at all none is missed either, by searches of up to 11,203 pieces.

*Held to, since the round after the review.* `boolean/outline.rs`: *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it to rounding* (the 30 cases above), *after a touch the other places of a piece are tried in their order* (a hole touched at its circle's middle is answered from 0.2764 of it, by a quarter of its width; with its middle touched by a side that leans over to it and 0.2764 of it by the lower side, from 0.7236), *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it does not touch it* (the wall of a pin in its hole, four tolerances to four hundred smaller: the point is the one stepped in from the middle, as without the pin), *the nearest point of a spline with weights far apart is the nearest* (the 600 points, and no search of more than 120 pieces), *a range continued to where a weight is not positive is not searched by pieces* (the weight check, reachable only by a range that goes on past the curve's domain: a curve's own weights are positive and halving keeps them so), *a piece of any rational spline is no nearer than its box* and *a search that runs out of pieces says so* (the review's: the bound, and the cap of 200,000 pieces between four turns of a spline circle asked from its centre, 16,380 pieces a turn, and twenty). `crates/kernel/tests/halfspace.rs`: *a hole tangent to a side to rounding is cut across* (the 60 cuts). `crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs`: *a hole tangent to a side at decimal places is cut across*, *… turned out of the axes …*, *two holes that touch are cut across* (32, 24 and 4 cuts at four scales, both sides). And `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_border_tangent.rs` › *a cylinder at a plate's edge at one*, which was there before, holds that a touched piece is stepped in from again.

*Each part is needed by a test* (Checks 44960586048 and 44960588354, a throwaway commit with each part switched off or changed in turn, the tests of the five files under each; with none, all pass but the two owed ones):

| Part switched off or changed | Tests that then fail |
|---|---|
| a touch told by the step alone, without the part of the piece's width | `outline` › *… to rounding* (13 of 30), *after a touch the other places …*; `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side to rounding* (36 of 120 sides); `plane_cut_found.rs` › *at decimal places*, *turned out of the axes*, *two holes that touch* |
| a touched piece left for good at that place (the review's trial) | `boolean_border_tangent.rs` › *a cylinder at a plate's edge at one* (the 56 faults) |
| the floor from the speed bound again | `outline` › *the search by pieces finds the nearest sample of any part*, *a piece of any rational spline …*, *… with weights far apart* |
| no floor | `outline` › *… with weights far apart* (11,203 pieces) |
| after a touch only the place 0.2764, only 0.7236, or the two in the other order | `outline` › *after a touch the other places …* (each of the three) |
| "as near" to a thousandth of the step | `outline` › *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it …* |
| no check of the weights | `outline` › *a range continued to where a weight is not positive …* |

*Not held by a test, and why:* that "as near" is 1e-9 and not another number between about 1e-11 and 3e-6, which the tests bound it by (an exact touch must be told; a pin four tolerances smaller than its hole must not): it is the same slack the caller leaves the other way, nothing nearer than `reach × (1 − 1e-9)`, and the same room `Boxes::near` leaves its boxes, and is said so in the code; that the floor is 1e-9 of the curve's size and not another small part of it (a cost, bounded above by the 120 pieces and below by nothing but Newton's method finishing); and the cap of 200,000 pieces anywhere between 65,520 and 327,600.

*Counted, what the touch rule adds* (the benchmark's models with counters on the throwaway commit, Check 44960588354; the benchmark's own counters see none of this): `left_of` is asked 146,129 times and `touched_at` as often, once per answer, of the one candidate that would otherwise be taken, and no touch is found; those asks make 32,173 of the 3,971,592 asks for a nearest point (0.8 %) and one query of the boxes each, beside 703,343 by `Boxes::nearer`. Of 20,745 searches for the nearest point of a spline none runs out of stretches, so the search by pieces is not reached there. (An earlier run of these counters on the same models, Check 44953814054, gave the same 146,129 and 32,173 and totals of 3,971,618, 703,385 and 20,790: the totals move by a few tens between runs, which the benchmark's own counters do not; not looked into.)

*What moved at the round after the review.* Nothing that was built before, on what this round measured. (The reread of 2026-11-22 then measured what it had not, and found one thing that was built before and no longer was: a tube with a wall far thinner than it is wide, which `main` bores and this round refused as too thin. Below.) On its head (70761352): the boolean corpus (Heavy 44961677949) gives the same digest of all results, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, and the same score sheet line for line but for the time it took: 11,988 outcomes, 11,594 agreeing, 228 unsupported, 166 unscored, no mismatch and none invalid. The benchmark's 115 work counts are the base's, count by count (Check 44961111453 against the base's Check 44936917282 on aee56914, on two different runners: the counts do not depend on the runner), with the same twelve off `baseline.txt` as before, which is still not this round's to change. Its hand-in Check is 44961111453 and its trial Gate 44961116667, both passed (the Gate that failed, 44955620640, was the first head's, above). The random designs (thirty minutes from seed 100,000, Heavy 44961675479: 27,558 seeds, compared as before by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word): against the first repair no seed differs, neither among the 19,890 of its run nor among the 7,668 past it, for which its head was run over those seeds (Heavy 44965949078 on 0744b430). Against the base's run, the 27,558 both ran: 27,548 the same and ten differ, none from a body to a refusal, and all ten are the first repair's: the six above, and four past where its run ended. Those four: 122388, a boolean refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary"*, is built; 126241, a boolean refused the same way, is refused as *"boolean leaves nothing: the result is empty"* (that it is empty was not checked by hand); 120111, a fillet whose cut was refused as *"a piece of a face is not closed off by its boundary"*, is refused by the fillet itself, *"the fillet does not end cleanly"*; and 122908, an offset face refused as too far before and after, with the last digit of the distance that fits changed (−0.00147208 to −0.00147209 mm). No run of this round has a crash, a bug or a wrong result (one timeout, seed 117369, known). *"boolean produced an invalid body"* is left at fifteen seeds, the same fifteen in the base's run: the eight above and seven past the first repair's run, one of them a plane cut (*Owed*).

**The reread, and round three (2026-11-22).** The round after the review was reread the next day by a second reviewer, who measured it (its tests are on the branch, one held and three owed when it handed them in; Checks 44968548848, 44968551078, 44971122228 and 44973308866), and found it fit for `main` after named changes. No body was wrong, and no side was lost in the first review's families, side by side on the programs of `main`, of the first repair and of the round after the review: the 2,160 sides of the halved cone, the 528 of the tangencies, and the touch families at decimal places and out of the axes. What stood in the way: two holes of one radius that touch were still refused; a wall far thinner than its circle is wide had become a touch, and one tube that `main` bores was refused, the one thing a round of this finding made worse than `main`; parts of the rule were held by no test; and the texts again said more than was built. A third round made those changes; what it left is under *Owed*.

*A step that lands on the centre of the touching circle was taken.* A point is stepped out from the middle of a circle by its radius, then by half of it, and so on. Where a second circle of the same radius touches the first at that middle, the first step is the second circle's centre; where the second is half as wide, the second step is. A centre is as near to its circle everywhere, so the point of that circle nearest to it is no place in particular, and `touched_at` asked for that point to be at the place the step began from: no touch was told, the centre was taken, and the hole's loop was given to the other circle's disc, as before the repair. Measured by the reread through the commands: of 60 cuts of two holes of one radius (1.5, 1 and 1.7, five pairs of starts, four scales) 36 were refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"* on `main` and 23 after the first repair and after the round that followed, all twelve with both middles at the touch in each; in the kernel 75 of 168 cuts (seven pairs of radii, the four pairs of starts, three scales, in the axes and out of them), every one with radii the same or one twice the other. The first repair's test and the first review's family have radii 1.5 and 1, where no step of either circle lands on the other's centre.

*"To rounding" was a billionth of the piece's width, whatever the tolerance.* On a circle of radius 5e5 at a tolerance of 1e-6 that is a thousand tolerances, and a second circle up to that far inside the first (the section of a tube's wall) passed its foot everywhere and was as near to every candidate to that measure: both loops of the ring were refused as *"a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it"*. Measured by the reread through the commands, on twenty tubes bored from a bar at the default tolerance (radii 500 to 5e5, walls of 20 to 10,000 tolerances): `main` bores 17 and refuses three as *"a piece of a face is not closed off by its boundary"* (radius 5e5 with walls of 20 and 100 tolerances, radius 5e4 with 20: the first step from a circle that wide across a wall that thin is nearer to the other circle by less than the billionth of the step that counts as nearer, and was taken); the first repair bores all twenty; the round after the review bored 16 and refused four as too thin, those three and the wall of 500 tolerances on radius 5e5, which `main` bores. The other way about, a small piece far from the origin, the touch is still not told (*Owed*).

*Changed: a touch is told by the other piece passing the place of the step* (`touched_at`). Two things are asked of a piece other than the candidate's own, each a distance of its own. It is *as near* to the candidate as the candidate's own piece: no further than that distance and rounding (and no nearer than that distance less a billionth of it, which the caller sees to: so a piece between the two is always as near, and a piece behind the candidate's own is further by its distance from it). And it *passes the foot*, the place where the candidate's own piece is nearest: it is no further from the foot than a thousandth of the step, or than rounding. The second was asked of the other piece's point nearest to the candidate; it is asked of the piece (the reread's proposal, which it had measured as a switch and as code on the five files). A centre is then no different from any other candidate: the circle about it passes the foot.

*Changed: rounding is no wider than the tolerance makes it* (`left_at`; the reread's proposal). Rounding is a billionth of the piece's width and no more than a sixteenth of the margin, the least distance from a boundary at which a point is looked for at all: a quarter of the tolerance where the regions of a face are told apart. Two pieces further apart than that have room for a point between them and are not in one place. Why a sixteenth and not another part: under three thirty-seconds of the margin, twice the nearest that a point is looked for, any wall with room for a point keeps it; and a quarter of a tolerance is still four thousand units in the last place of a coordinate on the circle of radius 5e5, so what a designer gives as a touch is told there as before. A piece narrower than 2.5e8 tolerances is not touched by the bound.

*Changed: "as near" is to rounding alone.* It had a billionth of the step to spare beside rounding, and the reread found that part dead: rounding was a billionth of a width that is twice the longest step. With rounding held to the tolerance it is no longer dead, and what it does is wrong: a piece *behind* a wide one by less than a billionth of the step (a pin a hundred tolerances smaller than a hole of radius 5e5) is told as touching it at the first steps. The reread's proposal kept it, and its own probe shows what that does: of 1,200 loops that touch nothing, drawn at that size, 68 were answered from another step than with no touch rule at all (with the part gone: none, below). It went. A piece between a candidate and its own piece is never further from the candidate than that own piece is, so nothing that lies between needs it.

*The proposal, judged as a rule.* Whether a sixteenth of the margin is the right tie to the tolerance is said above; the margin is the call's (four tolerances in the regions), and the bound moves with it. Whether "passes the foot" tells a touch for a piece that is not between the two: not for the far side of a strip, which is as near and two steps from the foot; not for a piece that crosses the candidate's own at the foot, which enters the ball about the candidate, is nearer, and has the candidate left out before the question is asked; it does for a piece that touches from behind, which no distance tells from one in front (*Owed*, as before); and it does for a piece that is as near at another place and also passes within a thousandth of the step of the foot. A straight piece does that when it is tangent to the ball about the candidate within two and a half degrees of the foot (a seventeenth of a degree while the nearest point was asked): the centre of a disc whose circle a side is tangent to, when the place of the circle that is found nearest to its own centre happens to lie that close to the tangency. That costs the one candidate, since each is asked afresh, and the next step answers; the grid of `boolean_border_tangent.rs` is where the round after the review traced such a centre, and it passes at its three scales. What the second distance costs: it is asked only of a piece that is as near, 563 times on the benchmark's models (`touched_at` asks for 32,736 nearest points where the round before asked for 32,173, of 3,972,181 asks in all, and tells no touch there, as before); a loop that is touched at its middle pays for it at every candidate that is left out (6,029 asks for one answer where the round before made 5,025).

*Held to, since round three.* `boolean/outline.rs`: *a point left of a loop is not the centre of a circle that touches it* (two holes that touch, radii the same and one twice the other, three frames); *a wall far thinner than its circle is wide is not a touch* (rings on radii 5 to 5e5 with walls of 2,000 tolerances down to half of one, both loops, two frames); *a point left of a wide loop is not beyond a loop that touches it to rounding* (the plate a million times the size at the same tolerance, its hole past the side or short of it by a unit in the last place and by a hundredth of the tolerance); *… to rounding* now also past the side by seven tenths of what is told; *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it does not touch it* now also a pin 20, 100 and 500 tolerances smaller than a hole of radius 5e5; and the reread's *a hole that touches the outline in two places and its disc are answered* (a corner and a slot, eleven starts, three frames), which holds in the unit tests that a touched piece is stepped in from again. `crates/kernel/tests/halfspace.rs`: *two holes of one radius that touch are cut across* (six pairs of radii, the four pairs of starts, in the axes and out of them, three scales: 144 cuts, both sides, to the closed form), and *a hole tangent to a side to rounding* now also past the side and short of it by a thousandth of the tolerance, which stood in the owed test though it was built. `crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs`: *two holes that touch are cut across* (radii 1.5 and 1, the same radius three times over, and 2 and 1; both middles at the touch; four scales: 20 cuts, both sides, to the closed form and the deep check); *two holes that touch at the start of one circle are refused in words* (the same radii with the middle of one circle and the start of the other at the touch: 40 cuts, each side refused by regeneration's check as touching itself, the developer's question above, and none by the boolean's own check); *a tube far thinner than it is wide is bored and cut across* (the twenty tubes).

*Each part is needed by a test* (Checks 44976301700 and 44976304340, a throwaway commit with each part switched off or changed in turn; under the rule as built every test passes but the two owed ones):

| Part switched off or changed | Tests that then fail |
|---|---|
| no touch rule (as before the repair) | `outline` › *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it*, *… to rounding*, *a point left of a wide loop …*, *after a touch the other places …*, *a hole that touches the outline in two places …*, *… the centre of a circle that touches it*, *a wall far thinner than its circle is wide …*; `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side is cut across*, *… to rounding*, *two holes of one radius …*; `plane_cut_found.rs` › the seven that were run under the switches (all but the first seed's two) |
| rounding not held to the tolerance (a billionth of the width, whatever it is) | `outline` › *a wall far thinner …* (24 of 32 loops refused as a sliver), *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it …* (the pin behind the wide hole); `plane_cut_found.rs` › *a tube far thinner than it is wide …* (four of twenty refused as too thin) |
| the bound on rounding a quarter of the margin | `outline` › *a wall far thinner …* (the wall of half a tolerance, both loops) |
| the bound on rounding a 1024th of the margin | `outline` › *a point left of a wide loop …* (past the side by a hundredth of the tolerance) |
| rounding 5e-10 of the width | `outline` › *… to rounding* (past the side by 7e-10) |
| "as near" with a billionth of the step beside rounding (as it was) | `outline` › *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it …* (the pin behind the wide hole) |
| no "as near": any piece that passes the foot | `outline` › *a piece that runs beside a loop behind it …* |
| no "passes the foot": any piece that is as near | `outline` › *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it* (the plate's own point moves), *a wall far thinner …* |
| "passes the foot" asked of the other piece's point nearest to the candidate (as it was) | `outline` › *… the centre of a circle that touches it*; `halfspace.rs` › *two holes of one radius …*; `plane_cut_found.rs` › *two holes that touch are cut across*, *two holes that touch at the start of one circle …* |
| "passes the foot" by the thousandth of the step alone, without rounding | `outline` › *… to rounding*, *a point left of a wide loop …*; `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side to rounding …* |
| "passes the foot" by rounding alone, without the step's part | `outline` › *a wall far thinner …* (the first steps across the wall are taken, as on `main`) |
| "passes the foot" within twice the step | `outline` › *a point left of a loop is not beyond a loop that touches it* (the plate's own point moves) |
| "passes the foot" within 5e-10 of the step | `outline` › *a wall far thinner …* |
| a touched piece left for good at that place (the first review's trial) | `outline` › *a hole that touches the outline in two places …* (the disc in the slot), *a wall far thinner …* (20 of 32); `boolean_border_tangent.rs` › *a cylinder at a plate's edge at one* and *… at a million*; `plane_cut_found.rs` › *a tube far thinner than it is wide …* (three of twenty) |
| the same arc run the other way counted as touching | every test of `outline` and `halfspace.rs` above, and `boolean_border_tangent.rs` › *… at one* (the engine's were not run under it) |
| after a touch only the place 0.2764, only 0.7236, or the two in the other order | `outline` › *after a touch the other places …* (each of the three) |

| rounding 5e-6 of the width | none |
| rounding the bound whatever the width (a rule by the tolerance alone) | none |

*Not held by a test, and why* (each is said so where the number stands in the code): that rounding is a billionth of the width and not more (a part five thousand times as large fails no test: rounding is held to the tolerance besides, so a larger part only gives more pieces that bound; with less than 7e-10 *… to rounding* fails); that the bound is a sixteenth of the margin and not another part between a four-hundredth and an eighth (past the side by a hundredth of a tolerance on the wide circle must be told, a wall of half a tolerance must not); that a piece passes the foot within a thousandth of the step and not another part between a billionth and 1.6 (the first steps across a wall a billionth of its radius thin must be told, the plate's own point must not: a thousandth leaves a curved sheet its room, where what lies between passes the foot within a billionth of the step over the cosine of the angle at which the straight way to the candidate leaves the sheet). And a rule by the tolerance alone, the bound for rounding whatever the width, fails no test either: what it changes is a touch within the tolerance but beyond rounding (in the owed unit test it answers the hole past the side by 1e-9, 1e-8 and 1e-7 in the plate, 9 of 12 where the rule answers none), which is owed as a design and not had by widening this (*Owed*).

*The reread's kernel cases under the rule* (the same Check): the reread's own probe of the kernel, run again on the rule as built and on the rule of the round before. Its two-holes probe (seven pairs of radii, four pairs of starts, three scales, in the axes and out of them): 168 of 168 cuts built on both sides, where the round before built 93 and no touch rule 42. Its drawn loops that touch nothing (a plate, a hole a drawn gap from its side, a pin a drawn gap inside the hole; 1,200 loops at each of six sizes), answered against no touch rule at all, bit for bit: every one the same at every size, the plate a million times the size at a tolerance of 1e-6 among them, where the round before answered 106 from another step and the reread's proposal, with the step's part of "as near" kept, 68. Its rings and pins (a ring, a pin in its hole and a strip, 20 to 10,000 tolerances wide on radii up to 5e5): all 160 answered, where the round before refused 24. Its touching cases (a hole touching a side at seven places of its circle, a hole of two arcs, a hole in a corner and in a slot, two holes, a spline circle and a spline arch, each past the touch or short of it by nothing to 1e-11 of the size, in frames at the origin and far from it, at six sizes: 27,336 loops): 27,261 right, 35 wrong and 40 refused, the reread's counts for its proposal, group for group. The 40 are the hole touched at all three places a point is stepped in from (*Owed*, on which side). Of the 35, 26 are at the largest size with the tolerance left at 1e-6, the loop past the other by 1e-11 of the size, which is ten tolerances there: no touch, and right at the round before only because its rounding was a thousand tolerances at that size. The other nine are one frame ten thousand million from the origin (*Owed*, a small piece far from the origin): the round before had nine wrong too, in another such frame.

*The two families through the commands*, on the programs of `main`, of the first repair and of the round after the review (the reread's runs, and for radii 2 and 1 this round's), and at round three as tests on the build server (Checks 44976304340 and 44981095875) and through the commands on the merge's program (the final reread: every cell of the last column the same):

| Through the commands, at four scales | `main` | the first repair | the round after the review | round three |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Two holes, radii 1.5 and 1.5, 1 and 1, 1.7 and 1.7, 2 and 1, both middles at the touch (16 cuts) | 16 invalid bodies | 16 invalid bodies | 16 invalid bodies | 16 built |
| The same, the middle of one circle and the start of the other at the touch (32 cuts) | 32 invalid bodies | 15 invalid bodies, 17 refused as touching itself | 15 invalid bodies, 17 refused as touching itself | 32 refused as touching itself |
| The same, both starts at the touch, or neither a start nor a middle (32 cuts) | 32 built | 32 built | 32 built | 32 built |
| Radii 1.5 and 1, the same five pairs of starts (20 cuts) | 8 built, 12 invalid bodies | 11 built, 3 invalid bodies, 6 touching itself | 12 built, 8 touching itself | 12 built, 8 refused as touching itself |
| Twenty tubes at a tolerance of 1e-6, radii 500 to 5e5, walls of 20 to 10,000 tolerances | 17 bored, 3 refused (*"not closed off by its boundary"*) | 20 bored | 16 bored, 4 refused as too thin | 20 bored and cut |

*What moved at round three.* Nothing that `main` built, on what was measured; and the tube the round before had lost is bored again. One thing the round before built and `main` did not is refused again, as on `main`: a hole wider than 2.5e8 tolerances that passes a side by more than a quarter of the tolerance and no more than a billionth of its width, which the round before told as a touch by its rounding (the final reread, below; *Owed*, a touch within the tolerance). On its head (3a2af3d6): the boolean corpus (Heavy 44981007902) gives the same digest of all results, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, and the same score sheet as the round before line for line but for the time it took: 11,988 outcomes, 11,594 agreeing, 228 unsupported, 166 unscored, no mismatch and none invalid. The benchmark's 115 work counts are the base's, count by count (Check 44981003285 against the base's Check 44936917282 on aee56914), with the same twelve off `baseline.txt` as before. Its hand-in Check is 44981003285 and its trial Gate 44981005816, both passed. The random designs, compared as before by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word (Heavy 44981010497, thirty minutes from seed 100,000, which reached 19,490 seeds beside the Gate, and Heavy 44984467346, fifteen minutes from 119,490: 36,030 seeds, 100,000 to 136,029): against the round after the review no seed differs among the 27,558 both ran; against the base's run, the 28,375 both ran, 28,365 are the same and ten differ, none from a body to a refusal, the ten of the round before. The 7,655 seeds past the base's run that the second of these runs reached (128,375 to 136,029) had no run to be held to, so `main`'s head was run over them (Heavy 44986646619 on 88162736): 7,651 are the same and four differ, each from a refusal to a body that passes the oracles (129589, a plane cut, and 130232, a boolean, refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary"*; 131192, a boolean refused as *"a piece of a face is not closed off by its boundary"*; 132589, a pattern whose join was refused as *"an edge shared by faces that do not pair up around it"*); which of the two repairs each rests on was not traced. One timeout in every run that reached it (seed 117369, known) and a second past the base's run (133461), which `main`'s head has too. No run of round three's head has a bug or a wrong result, and none refuses a face as too thin to find a point inside it. (`main`'s head, in its ten minutes, went on to seed 139,216, past where round three's run ended, and has one wrong result there: seed 138822, a cut that is built and whose measure below a plane is refused, *"its edges take more panels than a section may"*. That is the plane cut's oracle on `main`, found by the way; it was not looked into and not run at round three.) *Measured on the merge's program* (892bfee8, round three with the union repair below; the final reread of 2026-11-22, `plan/cut-invalid/final/` in the workshop, beside the programs of `main`, of the round after the review and of the union repair): every family of the first review and of the reread through the commands (the halved cone 1,996 of 2,160 sides, the tangencies 320 of 528, the small holes far from the origin 51 of 56, each as at the round before; the tubes 20 of 20 and the two holes that touch as in the table above): no side `main` or the round before built is refused but nine, and no body built on two of the programs differs in volume, area or counts. The nine are a family the final reread added: the hole of radius 2.5 S at S = 100, 1,000 and 10,000 with the tolerance left at 1e-6, past the side or short of it by nothing to 0.9 of the tolerance, in the axes and turned (90 cuts): `main` builds 4, the round before 78, the merge's program 69; past the side by 0.5 of the tolerance at S = 100, and by 0.3 and 0.5 at the two larger, the cut is an invalid body again as on `main` (the cap of round three, not its "as near": *Owed*, the first item). The two repairs do not meet: the union repair's program and the merge's answer every cut family the same, and the union review's 196 cases, 52 interference pairs and 39 fillet cases are the same on both. The Jigs and the published items on the same program (the coordinator's pass): all 112 published items as on 0.2.1, the RC wing's eight points seven clear and one refused, the 33 Jigs' ranges 3,537 of 3,538 points, Jig for Jig as before. *Not measured:* timings.

**Owed.**

- **A touch within the tolerance but beyond rounding.** The boolean that makes a hole takes a wall within the tolerance of a side for a touch; the point left of a loop tells a touch to a billionth of the piece's width, and since round three to no more than a quarter of the tolerance. Between the two, measured at the round after the review: in the unit test's plate (a hole of radius 0.5, tolerance 1e-6) a hole past the side by 1e-9, 1e-8, 1e-7 and 4e-7 is answered outside the plate in every frame (12 of 12; the first is the very edge of what is told), and one short of it by 1e-9 and 1e-7 was refused as a sliver in the review's run; through the commands, at three scales (Check 44960341198), a hole of radius 2.5 past the side by a thousandth of the tolerance is built, by a hundredth and a tenth refused as *"a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it"*, and by 0.4 refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"*, both sides at every scale; short of the side it is built at a thousandth and at 0.4 and refused as too thin at a hundredth and a tenth. (At round three the same, measured in the kernel at three scales, past the side and short of it: Check 44976301700. The thousandth is rounding on this hole and is held since: `halfspace.rs` › *a hole tangent to a side to rounding is cut across*.) The review tried a quarter of the margin (one tolerance) added to both distances of the touch: the hole's loop was then in the plate at every distance, 24 of 36 sides built and 12 were refused as too thin, and the disc's own loop was refused as a sliver at three distances. That is half a rule: it tells the touch, and does not say what two loops that touch within the tolerance are to be (one loop with a vertex there, as a boolean gives the rims of a hole tangent to a side, or a sliver refused in words). Owed, as a design; held by the ignored tests `outline` › *… within the tolerance* and `halfspace.rs` › *a hole within the tolerance of a side is cut across or refused in words*. Round three's bound takes back what the round after the review answered only because its rounding grew with the width: through the commands (the final reread: the hole of radius 2.5 S at S = 100, 1,000 and 10,000, tolerance 1e-6, 90 cuts), the hole past the side by 0.3 or 0.5 of the tolerance is built at the round before and an invalid body on `main` and on the merge's program (9 cuts); past it by 0.2 or less, or short of it, it is built.
- **On which side of a piece the one that touches it lies.** A touch is told by distances, which are the same whether the other piece lies between the candidate and its own (the side of a plate, seen from a hole's loop stepping out across it) or beyond its own (the same side, seen from the disc's loop stepping in): the second candidate is left out too, though nothing is wrong with it, and the loop is answered from another place. Where every place is touched so, on every piece a loop has, the loop is refused as a sliver, which it need not be. Two cases of it are known. The round after the review made one: with rounding a billionth of the width whatever the tolerance, the other circle of a ring far thinner than it is wide was a touch from every place, and the ring was refused (*The reread, and round three*, above: mended by holding rounding to the tolerance, not by telling the side). The reread made the other for it: a hole with three smaller holes touching its circle at the middle and at both other places a point is stepped in from is refused as a sliver, at four scales, in the axes and out of them, exactly there and past the touch or short of it by rounding (40 of 40, at the round after the review and at round three; with one or two of the three, answered). No design has met it.
- **The body with the tangent hole cut at a slant**, so that an ellipse touches the outline: none of 96 sides built on `main` or after the first repair (the review: *"cannot be meshed"* 36 and then 42, *"boolean produced an invalid body"* 48 and then 42, *"would leave parts that touch"* 12), and none of 36 at the round after the review (27 *"cannot be meshed"*, 9 *"would leave parts that touch"*, none invalid any more; Check 44960341198).
- **The seam of the hole's cylinder on the tangent line.** With the touch at the middle of the side the cut body is refused by regeneration's check as touching itself (the developer's question above); with it elsewhere along the side (the hole's centre at 2.764 of 10) as *"the cut body cannot be meshed to see whether it passes through itself (face 6 cannot be tessellated: its mesh is not closed: it has a border where the face has no edge (a seam whose two sides do not meet))"*, in four commands, on `main`, after the first repair and at the round after the review, at three scales. The same words, on `main` and after the first repair, for seed 115098's body cut through the circle where its fillet meets its cylinder (x = −11.875): not one of the two cuts, not looked into.
- **Two holes that touch one another**, cut across, with the seam of exactly one of them on the touch (a vertex of one circle on the other's edge): refused by regeneration's check as touching itself, the same question (12 of 30 sides at the round after the review, at three scales); with both seams there, both middles there or neither, built (18). At round three the same, now also for two holes of one radius and of radii 2 and 1, which until then were refused there by the boolean's own check as an invalid body: all 80 sides of 40 cuts at four scales (`crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs` › *two holes that touch at the start of one circle are refused in words* holds what happens today).
- **A hole tangent inside a round bar**, cut across: an invalid body on `main` (24 of 48 sides, 8 *"cannot be meshed"*), built by the boolean since the first repair and refused by regeneration's check, *"cannot be meshed … its loops do not bound a region: the boundary crosses itself in parameter space"* (30 of 48 after the first repair, 2 still invalid; all 24 of the four seam pairs tried at the round after the review, none invalid).
- **A cone-shaped hole that shares a ruling with a side**, cut square to its axis: 16 of 40 sides refused on `main` and after the first repair alike (8 *"cannot be meshed"*, 8 invalid bodies); at the round after the review, of the three seams tried, the one at 90 degrees is refused as *"cannot be meshed"* at every scale and the other two build.
