- **A plane through the place where a spline edge has its control points bunched** (a profile 20 wide closed by a cubic B-spline with three control points within a thousandth of one place, extruded; the review, at scale 1, stopped after 52 cuts): the plane square to y through the bunch is refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"* on `main` and after the first repair; the plane square to x a hair beside the bunch, *"imprint lost"* on `main` and a body that cannot be measured after. Not tried at the round after the review.
- **Fifteen random designs whose boolean is still refused by its own check as an invalid body** (a loop that runs over itself or over another, or an edge used once), the same in the base's run and at the round after the review: the eight above, and seven in the seeds past the first repair's run, which the base's run has too. Six in a fillet's cut (103578, 110541, 110965, 116424, 121649, 124545), four in a pattern's join (108069, 115646, 123174, 123635), four in a plain boolean (111609, 114519, 121704, 122490), and **one more plane cut**: seed 124506, group 4, the second of its cut's planes, *"edge 0 is used 1 times"*, the words of seed 118385 before its repair. Each seed is its reproducer (`craft3d-fuzz seed N`), none reduced; whether 124506 is a third fault or the reach of one of the two was not looked into (a third, repaired 2026-11-22: *A torus cut beside its axis and then square to it*, below). At round three the same fifteen (Heavy 44981010497 and 44984467346), and three more among the seeds no earlier run had reached, 128,375 to 136,029, which `main`'s head refuses the same way (Heavy 44986646619): 128676 in a fillet's cut, 130205 in a pattern's join, 135611 in a plain boolean.
- **A cut of a body with a spline edge whose parameter crawls**, refused as *"a point too close to a face boundary to tell inside from outside (at the edge of tolerance)"* though nothing there is at the edge of tolerance: the winding sum about a point (`chart::turn`) halves the stretches of a spline by the same one speed bound, and runs out of its 65,536 steps as the search for the nearest point did. Before the repair those cuts were invalid bodies; now they are refusals in words that are not true to the state. How near is a matter of the curve, not of the tolerance, so "within a few hundred tolerances of the axis", as this finding first had it, was the wrong measure: it sets in close under the hyperbola's tip when the first plane passes the cone's axis by about a ten-thousandth of the cone's size and reaches most levels at a hundred-thousandth (the sweep above), and the review found the same 6 of 42 sides refused with the model a tenth, 10, 100 and 1,000 times the size at one tolerance of 1e-6. And it is not the cone's alone: with the three control points of the extruded profile above bunched within 1e-4 of one place, each of the four planes the review tried is refused so, the one far from the bunch too, on `main` as after the repair. Owed: the winding sum by the pieces' own bounds, as the nearest point has them (the same pieces would serve), and then the grid of *a level cut near the tip* built throughout.
- **The nearest point of an ellipse** (`nearest_on_ellipse`) gives up after 20,000 stretches the same way. Its bound is the ellipse's true speed between quarter turns; the review found none of 26,576 asks past 16,383 stretches. It was not touched. Owed: the same "not found, not guessed" there, or a proof that it cannot run out.
- **A small piece far from the origin.** Rounding is measured by the width of the piece, and a unit in the last place of a coordinate by its distance from the origin: where the second is the larger, a touch that is exact in the designer's numbers is not told. Found by the reread through the commands (a hole tangent to a side, radii 2.5 down to 0.0025, at the origin and up to ten million from it, in the axes and turned out of them, at a tolerance of 1e-6: of 56 cuts `main` builds none, the first repair 39 and the round after the review 51; the one still refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"* is the hole of radius 0.0025 ten million away and turned out of the axes, where a unit in the last place is some four hundred times the rule's rounding), and in the kernel, where nine of its 3,528 holes touching a side are answered beyond the side at the round after the review and nine at round three, each time all in one frame out of the axes and far away (1e13 from the origin at a size of 1e6, and 1e10 at a size of 1e3). Through the commands on the merge's program (the final reread) the same 51 of 56 small holes far from the origin are built as at the round before, and the same one is an invalid body. Owed: rounding that knows how far from the origin it is.
- **Two loops that touch between their vertices** are told apart by the point's step alone, and only to rounding. A cut through a body that touches itself leaves them without a vertex at the touch (the rectangle's side is not split there); whether such a face should get one, as a boolean gives the rims of a hole tangent to a side, goes with the developer's question above, and would be the repair at the cause.

### A union whose level tops meet along a line where the bodies part (found 2026-11-21 by fuzz seeds 110965, 116424, 111609 and 115646, repaired 2026-11-22; `merge.rs` › `merge_faces`, `copies_used`, `on_both_sides`; `sew.rs` › `WorkEdge::arc`)

*Found* by the random designs (the runs of the finding above, Heavy 44941617067 and 44961675479): four of the fifteen seeds that finding left refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"*, each a loop that runs over itself. Seed 111609 (scale 1e-3) in a plain union, 115646 (scale 1e3) in the join of a pattern's two instances, 110965 (scale 1) and 116424 (scale 1e3) in the union of a fillet's second or third tool with the body. They were reduced to one fault before this round; the four are the same in every respect measured.

*Reduced*, at any scale (here 1, tolerance 1e-6), all planar:

```json
[{"command": "feature.block", "params": {"name": "A", "position": [0, 0, 0], "size": [5, 10, 5]}},
 {"command": "sketch.create", "params": {"name": "S", "plane": "zx", "offset": 5}},
 {"command": "sketch.polyline", "params": {"sketch": "S", "closed": true, "points": [[6, 5.4], [-1, 2.6], [-1, 7], [6, 7]]}},
 {"command": "feature.extrude", "params": {"sketch": "S", "distance": 6, "name": "W"}},
 {"command": "feature.boolean", "params": {"name": "X", "op": "subtract", "target": "A", "tool": "W"}},
 {"command": "feature.block", "params": {"name": "B", "position": [5, 0, 0], "size": [5, 10, 5]}},
 {"command": "feature.boolean", "params": {"name": "Y", "op": "union", "target": "X", "tool": "B"}}]
```

A's side x = 5 is cut back over y 5..10 by the plane x = 3 + 0.4 z, which passes through A's top edge; B stands beside A, its top level with A's. They share the side over y 0..5 and meet only along the top edge (5, y, 5) over y 5..10, a groove opening below it. The union was refused: *"loop 1 of face 1 is not a simple closed curve: it runs over itself along edges 11 and 12 (a stretch 5 long about (5, 7.5, 5))"*. Measured before this round from outside: the same at scales 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6, turned three ways, either way round, with the side cut back by any slant or by a cylinder tangent to x = 5 along the top edge; with B's top 0.05 higher, or B not sharing A's side, refused as zero thickness at that line instead; with B moved 0.05 into A or away from it, built.

*Traced* (Check 44968414659, on a throwaway commit with prints in the sewing and the merge, and the boolean's options switched; read where it says so). The section, the regions and the classification are right: the union is one shell, of volume 475, and the line is where its two parts touch. **Sewing sees the touch:** the arc along the line has four uses, A's top and A's slanted side, B's top and B's side, and gets a copy per fan, one for A's two faces and one for B's. **The merge makes the slit:** A's top and B's top are coincident faces of both (one plane, one side) and share the edge over y 0..5 where the blocks share their side, so the merge joins them into one face, and that face uses both copies of the line: it runs along it on both sides. `topo::check` then refuses the body, and the boolean returns that refusal before it gets to its zero-thickness check, which runs after the check (`boolean/mod.rs`, read). Measured: with the merge off, the body passes `topo::check`, `craft3d_topo::touches` finds its two vertices at (5, 10, 5), and the zero-thickness check refuses it there, *"two of its parts touch there along an edge or in a point"* (faces 1 of A and 1 of B, the two tops). With B's top 0.05 higher the tops are on two planes, nothing joins them, and the same check refuses the union at the same place: that is why the touch was caught there and not here. The same at scales 1e-3, 1e3 and 1e6, either way round, turned out of the axes, and with the side cut back by a cylinder of radius 8 about (-3, y, 5), tangent to x = 5 along the top edge.

*The right answer is the refusal as zero thickness.* By the decision of 2026-10-15 a result whose parts touch along an edge, a line or in a point is refused, and by that of 2026-10-18 ([`../decisions.md`](../decisions.md)) a wall that thins to nothing within one shell is accepted only where the boundary is still one 2-manifold; parts that touch each other, *"a non-manifold boundary: an edge or a point where the shell meets itself"*, stay refused. Here four faces meet along the line in two fans, the boundary meets itself there, and rule 2 of *A shell that touches itself* (above) reads the same: the walls that meet at the line, A's cut-back side and B's side, have the solid on their far sides and the groove between them closing up, the first of its two configurations (two parts touch), not the second (material between them thinning to nothing). With a cylinder tangent to the side the two walls are tangent at the line with opposite outward normals, which is the case that rule was written for, and the reading is the same. It is also the answer the same pair gets when the tops are not level, or when the blocks do not also share a side.

*Repaired, at the cause, in the merge.* Each edge sewing makes now knows the arc it was sewn from (`WorkEdge::arc`), so the copies of one arc are known as such. A group of faces to be merged never takes in two copies of one arc: before two groups are joined across an edge, the copies their faces use are compared (`on_both_sides`), and where both use a copy of the same arc and they are different copies the joining is left out, so the faces stay two; a group carries the copies of all its faces to its root (`copies_used`, kept per root as groups are joined). Two faces that use different copies of one arc lie on its two sides, so one face made of both would run along the arc twice; only such joinings are left out. Where no arc has copies, nothing is looked at. The body is then valid and touches itself along the line, and the zero-thickness check refuses it there in its own words, with a face of each part. Which joining is left out, where a group could lose either of two, is the first in the order of the edges, so the result is the same on every run. With the refusal off (the steps of a blend, the cut of an extrusion up to a face) the body is the valid one with the tops apart, which regeneration's check of every body then holds to its rules, as it does where the tops are not level.

*After the repair, measured* (Check 44968414659, the candidate behind a switch, and Check 44969509286 on the repair 329fb7ff): every case of the trace above is refused as zero thickness at (5, 10, 5), the end of the line, at every scale, either way round, turned, by the plane and by the cylinder, and made without the refusal is valid, one shell, of volume 475 (the plane) and `500 − 5 (40 − 2.5 √39 − 32 asin(5/8))` = 486.0835 (the cylinder), its tops two faces. B moved by 0.45 of the tolerance into A or away: refused the same (within half the tolerance B's side is A's), where it was an invalid body; by three tolerances away, two lumps, built as before. Nothing that built before changes: B moved 0.05 into A (473.734375), 0.05 away (two lumps, 475), B along the shared stretch only (350), B beside A uncut (one block of six faces). Through the commands, the reduction is refused as *"the result would have zero thickness at (5, 10, 5): two of its parts touch there along an edge or in a point, at F4:copy(0)/F1:face(1) and F5:face(1)"*, the two tops by their names.

**The four seeds as found.** 111609 is refused as zero thickness at (0.0025, 0.005, -0.0075), naming a face of each of its two quarter turns; 115646's pattern as *"instance 0 and instance 1 touch or overlap and cannot be joined: boolean: the result would have zero thickness at (-7500, 0, 1250) …"*. The two fillets, 110965 and 116424, are refused by the fillet itself, *"the fillet does not end cleanly: at one end of the edge it would stop in a face of its own …"* (110965's second edge, 116424's first): its tool's union now builds, and the fillet goes on to the refusal it gives these edges at a smaller radius, where the round does not reach the top edge and nothing touches. Word for word: the base's program gives the same at 0.8 of each seed's radius, and on the reductions at 0.4 and 0.8 (110965's also at 0.998), run locally; the repair gives the same at 0.8 (the test). Whether those words are true of the corner where a convex and a concave fillet meet is the blend's and not looked at here; at the seeds' radius the filleted body, if it were built, would also touch itself along the line. The deep check of each seed has that one problem, and none says invalid.

**Held to.** `boolean/merge.rs`: *a face is never on both sides of an edge* (the reduction's four faces by hand: the two tops, coincident faces of both sharing an edge, stay two where the line is two copies of one arc, and are one face, the shared edge gone, where it is two arcs) and *a group carries the copies of its faces to what it joins* (one top in two pieces, the piece beside the other top joined to it first: the other piece, which uses the other copy, stays apart). `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_level_touch.rs`, each through `solve_parts` (refused as zero thickness at a place on the line; made without the refusal, valid, one shell, of the volume in closed form, passing the boolean's own check in full, and touching itself at the place by the corpus's witness, which asks nothing of the boolean): *a block cut back to its top edge beside a block* and *a block rounded back to its top edge beside a block* (at scales 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6, either way round, the tops two faces), *turned out of the axes* (both, about an axis off every axis and moved off the origin, the place on the line turned with them), *a block reaching into the cut one over the side they share* (B an L that overlaps A over y 0..5 and stands beside it over y 5..10, so that A's top falls into two pieces, the one beside the line apart from B's), *what lies beside it* (B moved 0.05 into A or away, along the shared stretch only, beside A uncut: built, to their volumes and shells; B's top 0.05 higher and B moved 0.45 of the tolerance either way: refused at the line, at three scales). `crates/engine/tests/level_touch_found.rs`, through the commands: the reduction by the plane and by the cylinder, at four scales, either way round (the refusal's words, its place on the line, and the deep check's one problem, that refusal); the same turned out of the axes by `feature.transform` at two turns; B's top higher refused alike and B moved into A built to its volume; and the four seeds as found, each with its own words above, and for each the deep check's one problem, never an invalid body.

*Each part of the repair is needed by a test* (Check 44970324317, a throwaway commit with each part switched off in turn; with none off every test passes; the fillet seeds' test was then without its comparison at 0.8 of the radius, where nothing touches and no part of the repair is reached):

| Part switched off | Tests that then fail |
|---|---|
| faces that use two copies of one arc joined as before (`on_both_sides` never true) | all of `merge.rs`'s two and `boolean_level_touch.rs`'s five; of `level_touch_found.rs` all but *what lies beside it* (B's top higher was refused in these words before, B moved into A built), the four seeds as found included |
| a group's copies not carried to its root when groups are joined | `merge.rs` › *a group carries the copies of its faces*; `boolean_level_touch.rs` › *a block reaching into the cut one*; `level_touch_found.rs` › *seed 111609 as found* |

**What moved.** Through a boolean, a pattern or a fillet: nothing that was built before, and no body that was refused before. Where the zero-thickness refusal is switched off it does move, as the review measured (below): `document.interference` answers for such a pair, and three forms the base built with the slit are built with one face more. The boolean corpus on the repair (Heavy 44970224579 on 329fb7ff) gives the digest of all results the base was recorded with, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, and the base's score sheet line for line but for the time it took: 11,988 outcomes, 11,594 agreeing (10,571 outright, 1,023 zero thickness by design), no mismatch and none invalid. The benchmark's 115 work counts are the base's, count by count (hand-in Check 44973512393 against the base's Check 44961111453, two runners), with the same twelve off `baseline.txt` as before, which is not this round's to change. *The random designs, seed by seed against the base* (Heavy 44961675479 on 70761352, the code this round started from, 27,558 seeds from 100,000), by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word: thirty minutes from 100,000 on the repair (Heavy 44970226642 on 329fb7ff) reached 114,010, the build server being shared, and thirty more from 114,011 (Heavy 44973433113 on 160c433c, the same programs) reached 144,859. Of the 27,558 seeds both ran, 27,552 are the same. Six differ, each a refusal in other words and none from a refusal to a body or back: the four seeds of this finding, and two more of the fifteen, 123174 and 123635, each the join of a pattern's two instances, refused as an invalid body by a loop that ran over itself and now as zero thickness, at (5, 2.5, 0) and (5, 10, 5), the same fault. Past the base's range the second run found four things the comparison cannot place: a fillet's internal error (142482, *"the fillet face came out not tangent to a face it should run into smoothly"*), a `measure.below` refused after a plane cut of a torus (138822, *"its edges take more panels than a section may"*), both the same on the base's program run locally on their reproducers, and two seeds without a verdict within the fuzzer's minute (133461, 142058; the base's 117369 among the seeds both ran times out on both), not run again. The trial Gate on 160c433c (44971445800) passed: the full `cargo xtask ci`, FreeCAD and the web module's size.

**Owed.**

- **Nine of the fifteen random designs** the finding above left are still refused as an invalid body, word for word as on the base: in a fillet's cut 103578, 110541, 121649 and 124545, in a pattern's join 108069, in a plain boolean 114519, 121704 and 122490, and the plane cut 124506 (built since the finding below). Eight by an edge used once; 122490 by a loop that runs over another loop of its face (an outline and a hole of one face along one stretch), which the rule here does not reach, so not two of the sewing's copies in a merged face. Not this fault, and not looked at here.
- **The two fillets' words.** Seeds 110965 and 116424 are refused as *"the fillet does not end cleanly"*, as at a smaller radius: the review measured the same words on the base and on the repair at 0.999, 0.99, 0.9, 0.8 and 0.5 of the radius, and for chamfers of the same edges, so it is the blend's own verdict on this corner and not the touch passed on. Its advice, to fillet the edges it continues in as well, does not fit: both edges are filleted together already. What helps is leaving one out (110965's concave edge alone builds at every radius, its convex edge alone below the radius; at the radius the convex edge alone is the in-place blend's invalid body, and at 1.001 and 1.01 of it both edges together get an edge-of-tolerance refusal whose words are untrue, all three the same on the base). The blend's, not looked at here.
- **Which faces stay apart** where a group could lose either of two joinings is the first in the order of the edges: deterministic, and every choice leaves a valid body that the zero-thickness check refuses; with the refusal off (a blend's steps) the faces that stay apart could differ from the ones a designer would pick. No case of that is known.
- **Sheets** cannot reach the rule, as the review read the code (not measured): copies come only from an arc with more than two uses, a trim and a sheet's cut keep the target's pieces alone, and a union of sheets refuses an edge three faces would use and any meeting off the shared boundary.

**The review (2026-11-22; `plan/kernel-work-owed/invalid-body-seeds/review-union-slit/` in the workshop).** The rule is right and at the cause, and zero thickness is the answer the decisions give in each form (the closest call is seed 115646: its crescent alone is a wall that thins to nothing, but its knife edge meets the other instance's corner edge and leaves two sectors of material at the line). *Measured on the base and the repair as programs*, 196 cases of its own through `feature.boolean` and `feature.pattern` (the reduction, quarter tubes parting along a line, tube halves parting along a half circle or along a full-height line, ball halves, a touch in a point, two lines, a line at 31 degrees to the shared side, chains of three, a frame with the line in an inner loop, patterns and mirrors; scales 1e-3 to 1e6, turned, either way round): 121 from an invalid body to zero thickness, 24 zero thickness on both with other faces named, 51 the same, **none built on the base and not on the repair, none built differently**, and every refusal's place on the touch. *What moves with the refusal off.* (1) `document.interference` measures the area two bodies share by uniting them without the refusal: on the base that union was the invalid body and the pair came back *unknown*; on the repair it is built and the pair is a touch in a face with the area in closed form (24 of 52 pairs: 25 for the reduction, 10 for the tubes, 120 for the frame; six more differ in the area's fourteenth digit). The release note says so under *Results that changed*; held by `boolean_level_touch.rs` › `their_interference_is_a_touch_over_the_sides_they_share`. (2) Three forms whose slit `topo::check` passed on the base, so that the union without the refusal was built there, are built with one face more and the same volume: tube halves touching along a full-height line (the slit a full turn apart in the cylinder's chart, read as a seam; 12 vertices, 16 edges, 6 faces on the base, 14, 19, 7 on the repair), ball halves parting along half a great circle (6, 9, 4 against 6, 10, 5), and one pair of an existing test, `boolean_random` › `pairs_that_once_went_wrong`, seed 1000568, a subtract at three scales (19, 29, 11 against 20, 31, 12; the test pins no digest; why its flat face passed the check on the base was not traced). With the refusal on all three are zero thickness on both. Over 16 kernel and 4 engine test files, the corpus and the random pairs among them, seed 1000568 is the only place where the rule leaves out a joining; 432 other booleans met copies of an arc and were left as they were. *Mutants of the review* (Checks 44978672867, 44979807688): the rule off and the sewing numbering every copy as its own arc fail nearly everything; four that the round's tests let through are held by the review's tests (copies read from a face's outline only: `a_frame_round_the_cut_block` and the interference test; every copy asked instead of any: `tops_that_share_one_touch_and_part_at_another`; the faces asked instead of the groups' roots, and joined copies kept under the root from before the joining: `merge.rs` › `a_group_keeps_its_copies_at_its_root_and_is_asked_there`); one fails nothing and is the same rule unless one face uses two copies of one arc (a face keeps its first copy, not its last; none met). The mutants' switch is per thread and a pattern's joins run on other threads, so the engine's results under a mutant understate it, in the round's table too. Not checked by the review: an extrude up to a face or a blend's trim reaching this state, seeds 123174 and 123635 beyond the random designs' records.

### A torus cut beside its axis and then square to it (found 2026-11-21 by fuzz seed 124506, repaired 2026-11-22; `chart.rs` › `Chart::beyond_cut`; `outline.rs` › `left_of`, `left_at`; `regions.rs` › `group`)

*Found* by the random designs (Heavy 44941617067, the run the repair of *A plane cut the boolean's own check refused as an invalid body* was compared against, which lists it under its *Owed*): seed 124506, group 4, a ball and a torus intersected, which leaves the torus's face bounded by one closed spline, then cut by three planes in one feature; refused at the second plane, square to the torus's axis, as *"boolean produced an invalid body"* (*"edge 0 is used 1 times"*).

*Reduced* (by an investigator before this round, `plan/kernel-work-owed/invalid-body-seeds-2/` in the workshop, its group E), at any scale (here 1, tolerance 1e-6):

```json
[{"command": "feature.torus", "params": {"name": "T", "major": 7.5, "minor": 2.5}},
 {"command": "feature.planeCut", "params": {"name": "A", "target": "T", "planes": [{"normal": [0, -1, 0], "point": [0, -8, 0], "side": "below"}]}},
 {"command": "feature.planeCut", "params": {"name": "B", "target": "A", "planes": [{"normal": [0, 0, -1], "point": [0, 0, 0.5], "side": "below"}]}}]
```

The first plane keeps y > −8. It meets the tube between its inner and its outer side in one oval and leaves the torus's face bounded by one closed spline edge with one vertex, at (−5.996, −8, −0.105); that body passes the deep check. The second keeps z > 0.5. It meets the torus in two circles about its axis, of radius 5.05 round the tube's inner side (168.5 degrees round the tube from its outer equator) and 9.95 round its outer side (11.5 degrees), the outer one crossing the oval at (±5.915, −8, 0.5), and the flat face in a line. Refused: *"edge 1 is used 1 times …; edge 3 is used 1 times …; Euler-Poincaré fails: V - E + F - R = 3 - 4 + 3 - 1"*.

*What main did, measured.* By the investigator through the commands: an invalid body at every scale and position, with either plane moved by tolerances, for first planes 5.5 to 8.5 from the axis (built at 9 and 9.5); on the other sides of second planes with |z| ≤ 1 refused as *"imprint lost: a curve in which two faces cross does not divide them"* or *"an edge shared by faces that do not pair up around it (at the edge of tolerance)"*; built from |z| = 1.5; built with the planes in the other order, and with one block that holds both faces; 11 of 12 turns of the torus about its axis invalid. In the kernel (Check 44998859671, a throwaway commit with prints in the stages): of 112 cuts of the ring's side (first planes 5.5 to 9.5, second planes at 0, ±0.5, ±1, 1.5 and 2, both sides) 45 built, 18 were invalid bodies, 28 *"imprint lost"*, 18 *"do not pair up"*, and 3 zero thickness, rightly: there the second plane touches the oval's upper branch where it dips lowest (first plane 5.5 at 1.5, 6 at 2). On the grids of the tests below with the repair switched off (the mutants' Check 45003063557, mutant 1, which is `main`'s code path): 1,020 of the 4,104 cuts of the analytic torus refused (264 invalid bodies, 264 *"do not pair up"*, 492 *"imprint lost"*, every one on the ring's side, first planes 5.5 to 8.5, at every scale and in both frames), 372 of the 432 with the torus turned about its axis (the seed's planes, and the second at 1, built at one turn of the twelve, at every scale and in both frames; the planes at 6 and −0.5 at three), and 147 of the 1,152 on a freeform torus (6 invalid bodies, 51 *"do not pair up"*, 90 *"imprint lost"*).

*Traced* (Check 44998859671, and Check 45001893346 for the turn that built and the other words). The section and the imprint are right: the circles in closed form, the oval cut where it crosses the plane and found on it again, the line on the flat face, the arcs between the right vertices. So is the classification: each region is in or out as its point says. The regions of the torus's face are wrong. Its loops are four: above the plane the outer circle's long arc with the oval's arc over the top (call it L1) and the inner circle (L2); below it the outer arc with the oval's two lower arcs (L3) and the inner circle the other way (L4). They are grouped in a chart of the torus (`chart.rs`) that cuts it open along a circle about its axis no loop crosses, the middle of the widest stretch of angles round the tube that the loops leave: here from the inner circle's 168.5 degrees to the oval's lowest, 281.5, so at 225. The chart draws the torus as an annulus, the angle from the cut as the distance from its origin. The region below the plane holds the cut, so it is drawn in two pieces, one at each end of the annulus: L3 bounds the piece at the inner end, L4 the one at the outer end. A loop is an outline or a hole by whether it winds about a point just to its left (`outline::left_of`), stepped in from the middle of one of its pieces, first by half the piece's width, and taken where nothing of the loops is nearer to it than its own piece. The inner circle's width is its diameter, 10.1: its first step, 5.05, lands 232 degrees round the tube, past the cut, and nothing is nearer to it than the circle, so it was taken. It lies in the piece at the inner end, which L4 winds about: L4 was called an outline, L1 (a hole) was given to it as the innermost outline about L1's own point, and the regions came out as {L3}, {L4, L1} and {L2}, where they are {L3, L4} and {L2, L1}. By their points {L4, L1} is below the plane and was left out, L1 with it, and the band above was kept with L2 alone: the oval's upper arc and the outer arc used once each (edges 1 and 3), and one ring short. The other words are the same grouping. Keeping the side below, {L3} and {L4, L1} were kept, a face on the torus with the band's loop, which the sewing could not pair with the faces at its edges (*"do not pair up"*). With the second plane at the equator the cut is put on the upper side (129.2 degrees: the stretches either side of the inner circle are as wide, and the first is taken), the band above holds it, and L2's point, stepped from 180 degrees, lands at 116.6, past it: all four loops fell into one region, which runs along the inner circle both ways, the circle was dropped as dividing nothing, and the boolean refused it as *"imprint lost"*.

*Why it builds where it builds* (measured in the same two Checks, but for the last case): with the second plane at 1.5 the inner circle is at 143.1 degrees and the cut at 212.3, and its point lands at 208.7, short of the cut; with the first plane at 9 the oval reaches 53.1 degrees round the tube instead of 78.5, the stretch the loops leave ends at 306.9, the cut is at 237.7, and the point at 232 is short of it; with the planes in the other order the face the second plane cuts is the band above the level plane alone, whose chart is cut at 270 degrees, in what the first plane took away, so no region is drawn at both ends; at the turn that builds the middle of the inner circle, where the point is stepped from, faces the first plane, and the second step, by 2.525, lands at 213.7, short of the cut: the first is not taken, the oval's lowest point being nearer to where it lands than the circle is (2.1 against 2.6, computed from the geometry; the probe printed only the point taken). Read, not traced: with one block holding both faces, the oval's lower arcs are never imprinted (the block's side meets the torus only above the level), the loops leave a wider stretch of angles, and the cut is at 270 degrees, past the point.

*Cause.* `left_of` takes the first point that nothing of the loops is nearer to than its own piece, and on a surface its chart cuts open along a circle that circle is no loop: a point past it was taken. `group` then asks what one loop winds about at that point, and is answered about the other end of the chart. The fault is in the regions, with the chart: the section, the imprint and the classification were right. (`Outline::build` steps in by the same function, but asks what all the loops of a face wind about together, which is the same at every point of the face, at either end of the chart.)

*Repaired, at the cause.* `Chart::beyond_cut(from, to)`: for the charts that cut a surface open along a circle (a torus along a circle about its axis, `Ring`, or round its tube, `Tube`; a freeform surface closed both ways, `Layout::Torus`), whether two points' images differ in their distance from the chart's origin by a quarter or more. Then the short way round from one to the other passes the cut, or is a quarter turn or longer, which a step can be (from the tube's inner equator through the tube, past its core circle, a step lands on its outer side, half a turn round) and then which way round it went is not known. (The review's sweep of tori from a thin ring to a fat one, minor over major radius 0.02 to 0.99: a straight step along a tangent sweeps under half a turn about the axis and at most 267.9 degrees round the tube, so a step that passes the cut lands a quarter turn or more from its foot; the margin is thin for a thin ring, 268 degrees against 270. On a freeform torus the chart's share is not the angle, and a parametrisation uneven enough could miss; none was met.) Never for the charts that leave out a point or nothing: a region that holds a sphere's pole is drawn in one piece, the outside of its loops. True where it cannot be told (a freeform surface whose nearest point is not found). `left_of` takes a chart, and with one leaves out a point beyond the cut from its foot (the place of its piece it is nearest), as it leaves out one with something nearer: the next, shorter step is tried. `regions::group` gives it its chart; `Outline::build` gives none. A point that is not beyond the cut is taken as before, bit for bit; where one was beyond (past the cut, or a quarter turn round without passing it) the next, shorter step's point is taken in its place.

*After the repair, measured* (the hand-in Check 45001840952 on 4e57ec3b, and mutant 0 of the mutants' Check 45003063557): every cut of the grids is built, or leaves nothing where the closed form leaves nothing, each passing `topo::check` and the boolean's full check against the block it was cut with, and its volume and area within 0.020 and 0.029 of what the tolerance allows them (the tolerance times the area, and times a bound on the edges' length: the curves the planes cut are fitted within the tolerance of the surfaces). The analytic torus, 4,104 cuts: first planes 5.5 to 9.5 by halves, both sides; second planes at 19 heights from −2.4 to 2.4, both sides (none where the plane would be tangent to the oval at x = 0, its top or where it dips lowest, which leaves pieces that touch: 1.5, 2, √5.25, √6 and 2.5); at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6; in the world's frame and turned and moved out of the axes. The torus turned about its axis in twelve steps under planes that stay, 432 cuts (the seed's two planes, the second at 1, and the first at 6 with the second at −0.5; both sides; three scales, both frames). The same torus as one rational NURBS face closed both ways, with its seams in two places, 1,152 cuts (first planes 5.5, 6.5, 8 and 9.5; six heights; both sides of each; three scales, both frames). Through the commands: the seed as found builds, all three planes cutting, and passes the deep check; the reduction, the second plane at 1, and the first at 6 with the second at −0.5, both sides, at three scales, and the torus intersected with two blocks at three scales, each to the closed forms and the deep check.

*Held to.* `boolean/chart.rs`: *a point beyond the cut of a chart is told* (`Ring` and `Tube` charts on the seed's torus turned and moved out of the axes, the freeform torus of the module's tests both ways round, and never on the charts of a ball, a cylinder, a cone and a plane), *a point left of a loop on a torus is not beyond the cut* (the seed's inner circle with a `Ring` chart cut 56.5 degrees round the tube either side of it, a circle round the tube with a `Tube` chart cut 10 degrees either side, and a circle about the axis of the freeform torus with its chart cut half a radian either side: no point beyond the cut, the point bit for bit as without the chart where that was not beyond it, and some point beyond it without the chart). `crates/kernel/tests/halfspace_torus.rs`: *the pieces of a torus measure as known* (the quadrature against the whole torus, its halves by a plane through the axis and by the equator, and the four pieces of each pair of planes adding up to the whole, to 1e-12), *a torus cut beside its axis then square to it* (the 4,104), *a torus turned about its axis is cut the same* (the 432), *a freeform torus cut beside its axis then square to it* (the 1,152). The volume and area are written in the test without the kernel: the level section of a piece between the radii `R ± √(r² − z²)` is closed (`∫ ρ acos(d / ρ) dρ = ρ² acos(d / ρ) / 2 − d √(ρ² − d²) / 2`), and the outer integrals, over the height and round the tube, are adaptive Gauss–Legendre in stretches between the kinks where the tube's section reaches the plane y = −d; the same integrals written once more in Python, and the volume taken the other way round (over the distance from the axis first), agree to 2e-16, and `main`'s program, where it builds (the first plane at 8, the second at 1.5, both sides), measures within 1.7e-8 of them in volume and 1.5e-9 in area (the oval the first plane cuts is a fitted spline). `crates/engine/tests/plane_cut_found.rs`: *seed 124506 as found*, *a torus cut beside its axis then square to it*, *a torus intersected with two blocks is built*.

*Each part is needed by a test* (Checks 45001739447 and 45001968098, and 45003063557 for the kernel's tests as they stand; a throwaway commit with each part switched off or changed in turn; with none, every test passes):

| Part switched off or changed | Tests that then fail |
|---|---|
| no chart given where the regions are grouped (`main`'s path) | `halfspace_torus.rs` › *cut beside its axis* (1,020 of 4,104), *turned about its axis* (372 of 432), *a freeform torus* (147 of 1,152); `plane_cut_found.rs` › all three; the unit tests ask `beyond_cut` and `left_of` themselves and pass |
| a `Ring` chart never beyond its cut | `chart` › both; `halfspace_torus.rs` › *cut beside its axis*, *turned about its axis*; `plane_cut_found.rs` › all three |
| a freeform torus's chart never beyond its cut | `chart` › both; `halfspace_torus.rs` › *a freeform torus* |
| a `Tube` chart never beyond its cut | `chart` › both |
| beyond only where the short way passes the cut (no quarter turn) | `chart` › *a point beyond the cut of a chart is told* |
| where it cannot be told, not beyond | none |
| asked of the place the step began from instead of the foot | none |

*Not held by a test, and why:* no design is known in which a loop's point lands beyond a `Tube` chart's cut (held by the unit tests alone); a quarter turn round without passing the cut is known since the review (a fat torus of radii 3 and 2.4 with collars round its tube, two cases: the shorter step is taken and the body is the same bit for bit as without the quarter), harmless, and held by the unit test alone; two mutants of the review change no test and no case: the chart given to `Outline::build` too (the loops of a face bound it, so their winding about a point is the same at either end of the chart), and the cut asked after the touch instead of before it (it differs only for a point both beyond the cut and touched); where a freeform surface's nearest point is not found, which no test reaches, `true` leaves the point out and the next step is tried, the safe answer; and whether the cut is asked from the foot or from the place the step began: both are on the piece next to each other, and on the circles of the tests on one circle of the chart's family, at one distance from the cut.

**The review (2026-11-22; `plan/kernel-work-owed/invalid-body-seeds-2/review-torus/` in the workshop).** The rule is right and at the cause. *Measured on `main`'s program and the repair's*, 421 cases of its own through the commands (the seed's planes with the torus turned in twelve steps, planes at a slant from 1e-7 to 1.3 radians and through the middle, two planes parallel to the axis, four proportions from a thin ring to a fat torus at four scales and two frames, holes through the tube, blocks taken from and posts united with the torus, collars round the tube, revolves, sweeps and controls on a cylinder, a ball and a cone): 279 built on both and the same bit for bit (volume, area, counts), **137 refused on `main` and built by the repair, none built on `main` and refused by the repair**, 5 refused by both in the same words (two sweeps and two slants *"cannot be meshed"*, one slant that does not cut); every built body passes the deep check with its last plane's two sides adding up, and the 232 with a closed form meet a quadrature of the review's own. In the kernel 576 tori cut at a slant, analytic and freeform with three seams (`halfspace_torus.rs` › `a_freeform_torus_is_cut_at_a_slant_as_the_analytic_one_is`): none wrong; without the chart 159 refused. *Can every place of a loop be skipped* (a face `main` builds refused as a sliver)? With counters on over 137 cases: every call that skipped a point still found one; to skip them all a loop would have to lie within about 0.2 of a tolerance of the cut (*Owed*). A point is left out as beyond the cut in exactly the sampled cases `main` refuses (85) and in none of those it builds (52). *Other charts:* only the `Tube`, the `Ring` and the freeform torus have two ends; a revolve of a closed spline, a circle swept along a circle and a closed loft never make a face closed both ways (four, two and six pieces), so through the commands only an imported file can hold the freeform torus's chart, and no import was tried. *The six later steps* that are refused now that their design builds further (random designs 114490, 105675, 114695, 115080, 115247, 106833): each new body passes the deep check and the boolean's identities (to 1e-15 of the volume; 1e-10 for one), and each later refusal is true of that body or an honest limitation (an offset larger than the face's radius, a chamfer that is not built yet or cannot be fitted, a fillet too large, a plane cut of a torus face whose loops go round both ways); two of them are the same on both programs. Of the 21 designs that build further, five spot-checked hold; in 115732 and 106900 `main`'s refusal as parts that touch was untrue (a plane through the torus's centre at 18.4 degrees, where it would only touch at 23.6; two tori that cross). *Mutants of the review* (Checks 45009732738, 45009734780, 45013413271): the quarter as a sixth or a half fails the chart's unit test alone (460 more points left out, each call still answered); asked from the piece's start, or the skip made the end of the piece's steps, fail the round's grids and refuse 22 and 85 of 85 cases as a sliver.

*What moved.* Nothing that was built before, on what was measured, but steps of random designs that now meet another body, because a step before them builds. The boolean corpus (Heavy 45001909246 on 4e57ec3b, the code of the head) gives the digest of all results the base is recorded with, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, and the score sheet of round three above byte for byte: 11,988 outcomes, 11,594 agreeing (5,720 match, 2,960 match~, 1,891 empty, 1,023 zero thickness by design), 228 unsupported, 166 unscored, no mismatch and none invalid. The benchmark's 115 work counts are the base's, count by count (Check 45001840952 against round three's hand-in Check 44981003285 on 3a2af3d6 and the union repair's 44973512393, two runners), with the same twelve off `baseline.txt`. The trial Gate on f3c6724f (45003068208) passed: the full `cargo xtask ci`, FreeCAD and the web module's size.

*The random designs, seed by seed against the base* (Heavy 45001911967 on 4e57ec3b, thirty minutes from seed 100,000, which reached 119,556; against round three's runs above, Heavy 44981010497 and 44984467346 on 3a2af3d6), by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word: of the 19,557 seeds both ran, 19,527 are the same and 30 differ. Four are the union repair's, on `main` already, a refusal in other words (110965, 111609, 115646, 116424). The other 26 are this repair's: run again in one process with the repair switched off and on (Check 45006294213, the throwaway commit's mutants 1 and 0), each gives the base's record word for word off and this run's on, where the four of the union repair and six seeds that do not differ give the same both ways. The design of each of the 26 holds a torus (23 of them) or a revolved body (101814, 109368, 116092). In 21 a step that was refused is built: a boolean, a pattern's join, a plane cut or a revolve, refused as *"imprint lost"* (100791 g3, 104244 g8, 105461 g3, 106833 g3 and g6, 107277 g3, 111967 g9, 115247 g5, 118578 g3), as *"a point too close to a face boundary to tell inside from outside"* (104460 g5, 105675 g3, 107174 g7, 109368 g2, 114490 g3, 114695 g6, 115080 g6), as *"an edge shared by faces that do not pair up"* (100670 g5, 102930 g4, 106152 g7), as parts that touch (108788 g4, 115732 g4), and as a cut body that cannot be meshed because a loop winds around the axis (110271 g5): a face on a torus with its loops grouped wrongly, as in this finding. In six of them a later step now meets the body built there and is refused where the base's design, with that step refused, accepted it or refused it in other words: offsetting a face too far and a chamfer by a distance and an angle of a freeform edge, not built yet (114490 g5 and g8, so that seed has one accepted group fewer), a chamfer face that cannot be fitted (105675 g4, 114695 g10), a chamfer too large and an offset in other words (115080 g10 and g8), a plane cut of a face on a torus whose boundary crosses every circle round its axis and its tube (115247 g6), and a fillet too large and a face that cannot be deleted in other words (106833 g8 and g5). The other five: the two faces of a touch named in the other order (101814 g1, 111493 g8); a pattern's join refused as zero thickness, where its instances touch, instead of *"imprint lost"* (116092 g6, 116970 g5); and a boolean that was refused as zero thickness and is built, its body then refused by regeneration's check as touching itself at the same place (106900 g4). Every seed passes the oracles of every group as before; no crash, bug or wrong result, and the one timeout is 117369, known. Seeds refused as *"boolean produced an invalid body"* in this range: 103578, 108069, 110541 and 114519, where the base had those and the four of the union repair. 124506 lies past where the run ended; the seed as found is the engine's test.

*Owed.*

- **A loop within a few tolerances of its chart's cut.** Every step from it would be beyond the cut, and the loop is refused as *"a piece of a face too thin to find a point inside it"*, which it need not be. The cut is put in the middle of the widest stretch of angles the loops leave, so this takes loops that leave no stretch wider than a few tolerances; not met.
- **Not run:** the seed's own frame (the torus about −x) turned in twelve steps through the commands, the investigator's second planes tilted by 1e-7 to 0.1, and other tolerances at other scales (1e-3 at scale 1e3): the kernel's grids turn the torus about its axis and turn the whole out of the axes, at the tolerance of the scale. The 21 steps of random designs that build since this repair are held by the fuzzer's oracles alone (the deep check, regeneration, the identities where drawn), not reduced or held to closed forms.

### A tangent edge across a spline border (found 2026-11-21 by fuzz seeds 103578 and 110541, and 121649, 124545, 128676 and 121704 on 2026-11-22; repaired 2026-11-24; `corner.rs` › `touch`; `section.rs` › `edge_cuts`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_tangent_edge_border.rs`)

*Found* by the random designs, reduced by an investigator (`plan/kernel-work-owed/invalid-body-seeds` and `-2` in the workshop, their group B): a fillet's tool (the corner of a rim's section turned about the rim's axis) lies on the wall along its strip and touches the wall along its tangent circle, and the wall's other border is a spline (the curve in which the wall meets a ball, a cone or a torus) that the circle crosses. The boolean's own check refused what it built (*"boolean produced an invalid body: edge 0 is used 1 times …"*), and nearby, with nothing near a tolerance, it refused in edge-of-tolerance words (*"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with"*, *"an edge shared by faces that do not pair up"*). An ellipse border (the wall cut by a plane) built; where the tool's seam lay mattered. The fillets' side, that the blend passed the first tool's invalid body on as its error, was mended on 2026-11-23 ([`blends.md`](blends.md) › *A tool boolean's invalid body*); this is the boolean's.

*Traced* (Checks 45290850242, 45291954206 and 45293451158, a throwaway commit with prints in the stages; `plan/tangent-edge-border` in the workshop). Smallest form, `reduced-103578-boolean`: the cylinder of radius 5 about z from 10 to 15, less the ball about (0, 8, 8) whose surface meets the wall at y = 5 at z = 13.84, less the tool of a fillet of the top rim of radius 1.25, whose tangent circle is z = 13.75. The section is wrong; the regions and the classification are right for the arcs they are given (not kin to group E, whose regions were wrong).

- The wall and the tool's round face (a cylinder and a coaxial torus) meet in closed form in the circle, *tangent*: it is clipped in the pair loop like any meeting curve, its cuts being where an edge of either face meets the other's surface (`edge_cuts`). The wall's spline border meets the torus there in a **touch**: the torus is tangent to the wall along the circle, so the distance from the border to it grows with the square of the way along the border. `hits` places a touch where the spline comes nearest, and the spline, a fit about a tenth of a tolerance off the curve in which the ball meets the wall, comes nearest the torus 31 and 6.6 tolerances along the border from where the circle crosses it, 7.2 and 1.5 tolerances below the circle. Neither is on the circle, so the circle has no cut there; its quarter over the border's hump lies outside the wall, and a curve along which surfaces only touch is left out where it runs over a face's boundary without a cut: the whole circle went. The wall was not divided, its one region kept the strip the tool lies on, the tool's strip there was dropped as coincident: arcs used once.
- With the tool's seam where the circle's quarters lie on the wall, the circle was kept whole, across the hump, and its arc fitted none of the border's pieces: *"an imprinted curve that does not fit the edges it should coincide with"*.
- Where the cut was found (the border higher, 0.01 to 1 above the circle, the circle crossing it at 12 to 60 degrees), the ball's face meets the round face in a closed spline that has second-order contact with the border at each crossing (the round face is within (Δz)²/2.5 of the wall there), and the border's touch on the round face lies on that spline a few tolerances from the crossing: it became a vertex of its own that split the border (in the trace at 1e-3 above, `v8` at (0.0781, 4.99939, 13.75015)), and the stretch in which the ball meets the tool's strip, along the border from crossing to crossing, then fitted no piece of it: *"does not fit"*.
- An ellipse lies on the wall to rounding, so its touch is at the crossing, and the body builds; but where it crosses the circle at a degree or less (a border 1e-5 to 1e-3 above the circle), it stays within the tolerance of the torus over both crossings, one touch stands for two places, and it is refused in the same words.

*Cause.* A touch of a spline edge on a surface tangent to one of the edge's own faces' surfaces along a curve the edge crosses is placed where the spline comes nearest the surface, which its fit moves many tolerances along the edge and off the curve along which the surfaces touch. The exact curve the spline stands for reaches the surface at one point, where it crosses that curve, and that point is a vertex of the result: the circle leaves the wall there, and the curve in which the ball meets the round face ends there.

*Repaired, at the cause* (`corner::touch`, called from `edge_cuts` for a spline edge's touch). Where a spline edge only touches a surface, the touch is taken to where the slope of the distance from the surface along the curve the spline stands for changes sign: each place of the spline taken to the nearest point of both its faces' surfaces (Newton on their tangent planes, the least step, where they cross at more than a thousandth), the slope the surface's normal at the foot against that curve's direction, which crosses zero at a rate set by how the curve bends away from the surface; found by stepping out from the hit (from a quarter of the tolerance, doubling, up to 65,536 tolerances and the hits on either side) and halving. The place is taken only where the curve reaches the surface there (within a thousandth of the tolerance: a meeting, not a curve passing within the tolerance of a surface it misses), is within the tolerance of the edge between its neighbouring hits and a quarter of it of its faces' surfaces, and is more than a quarter of the tolerance from the hit; a sign change within a quarter of the tolerance of the hit leaves it where it is after three looks. The section takes the place only where it lies on the boundary of the face the edge touches (where the edge crosses that face's border: a vertex of the result, as group B's crossing is); inside that face the hit stands (*Narrowed*, below), except, since 2026-11-25, where one of the edge's faces is tangent to that face there and the result keeps both (*A pinch along a curve*, below). An exact edge's touch, and every other touch, stands as before.

*A second part tried and left out.* A curve along which surfaces touch, cut also where it meets the surface of the face beyond an edge it crosses (in closed form where there is one; the touch of the same edge in the same band superseded), built the ellipse 1e-3 above the circle and the spline 1e-5 above it. On the boolean corpus (Heavy 45297281764; a throwaway commit running each case with either part off, Check 45298186244) it refused four pairs of the near category (cylinder-cylinder 011, cylinder-torus 004, torus-dome 004, torus-spline 004, all three operations) as faces that go through each other at the edge of tolerance where they agreed with the reference, and with both parts the corpus took 83 s against 26 s. The first part alone changes no outcome of the corpus. So the second is not built (*Owed*).

*After the repair, measured.* The reductions as plain booleans, *"boolean produced an invalid body"* on `main` (128676 *"does not fit"*), are built and pass the deep check: through the commands with the repair's first form (Check 45294757009) all five, 103578 at 346.566292414 (the quadrature 346.566292458), 110541 at 467.961778963 (467.961781108), 121649 at 40.905628529 (40.905628363), 124545 at 50.445394059 and 128676; with the repair as built, 124545 and 121649 through the commands (Checks 45300555650 and 45302024144) and the configurations of all five in the kernel's tests below. There the seed's family (the border 0.01 to 0.75 above the circle, on `main` refused as *"does not fit"*, and the seed, 0.09 above, an invalid body at every seam) builds at every scale, turned and at five seams, within the tolerance times its area of the volume written without the kernel (within 1e-7 where measured at scale 1, where 2.8e-4 is allowed); the ellipse's edges are exact and untouched by the repair. Where the border rises 1e-5 to 5e-3 above the circle, spline or ellipse, the cut is built and right or refused in edge-of-tolerance words (*Owed*); `main` refused those cuts too, and at 2e-3 and 3e-3 above at the seam 0 (where the move builds them) as the boolean's invalid body (the review's probe, Check 45334254056 in the workshop: the spline border 0 to 3e-3 above the circle at eight seams and six placements, the move off and on in one process; no body that fails a check or its volume either way). The fillets: 103578 and 110541 are too large with what their faces leave, as since 2026-11-23 (`blend_invalid_tool.rs`); 124545 builds at 0.5 to 2.394 (each to the deep check; on `main` refused in the boolean's words from 1.25 up) and past its cap (2.45, 2.5, 3; at 1e-3, 1, 1e3 and 1e6; turned) is said too large, *"below 2.39436"* (*"below 2.39435"* from 2.45 and 3), where the cap leaves 2.39655: on the narrowed code (the review, Check 45334254056) the fillet builds up to 2.394358 (every 2e-6 from 2.3943, each to the deep check), its tool's cut is an invalid body from 2.394360 to 2.3963 and refused as an imprint that does not fit from 2.3964 to 2.397, and from 2.3964 on, just below the cap too, the fillet is said too large (*Owed*); 121649 is refused at every radius from 0.1 to 1 as *"the fillet reaches a face that is neither next to the edge nor at one of its ends … (the face … of the cone)"*, and 128676 at 5 as *"the fillet does not end cleanly"*, at every scale and turned, where both were the boolean's invalid body. Such a "too large" costs the search 113 to 143 booleans (1.8 million units) where the boolean's refusal cost one.

*Narrowed, after three random designs* (Checks 45313277227 and 45316009225 in the workshop: throwaway commits that run each design reduced, and the 99 that differed from the nightly run, with the move switched off and on in one process; `plan/tangent-edge-border/round2`). The first form moved every such touch; three steps that `main` builds were refused with it, and in each a plane touches a torus along a circle inside the plane's face, crossed there by a spline edge on the torus.

- 20791237 (scale 1: the torus about z through (2.5, 0, 5), major 7.5, minor 2.5, less a torus about x, less a ring about the line x = −2.5, y = 0 from z = 0 to 7.5, whose top touches the first torus along its top circle) and 20812686 (scale 1e-3: a block joined to a torus, a hole, then cut to y above 0.0049996 and z above 0.0075, where the second plane touches the torus along its top circle): the touch taken onto the circle made the circle a stretch of its own, which divides nothing (the torus lies wholly on the side taken away), and the torus's chart refused the face it bounded, *"a face on a torus whose boundary crosses every circle around its axis and every circle around its tube"*. `main`'s bodies are right: 20791237's volume 137.911411994 against 137.911424368 written without the kernel (`tools/csg_quad.py` in the workshop: each primitive's points along a line in closed form, the two outer integrals cut where the set changes form; 9e-8 apart, where the area times the tolerance allows 2.6e-4; the same body with the ring cut first is refused by the chart on `main` too), and 20812686's is the block's corner beyond both planes, a box 0.0125 by 0.0050004 by 0.0075, 4.687853023691212e-7, which the kernel's volume equals to the last digit.
- 20781906 (scale 1e-3, ten groups): the end x = 0 of a cutter (a ring about x) touches, along a circle C of radius 0.01, the cavity an edited torus (minor 0.005, centred at x = 0.005) leaves in a ring about z. Where C runs inside both rings (two arcs, 2.6e-3 and 4.8e-3 long), the body lies beyond x = 0 on both sides of C and the two sides meet only on C (`tools/pinch_81906.py`: the body as set operations on its primitives, probed 1e-4 to 1e-7 across C): it has zero thickness along those arcs. The first form's refusal, *zero thickness at (8.7e-19, 0.00397, −0.005)*, names a point of C (1.7e-18 from it) at the end of the longer arc, on the ring's floor: it was right, and `main`'s body (two shells, which pass the deep check) is wrong.

Taken only on the boundary of the touched face, all three build `main`'s bodies again, the same volume, area, faces and shells: 20791237's and 20812686's right, 20781906's wrong, as on `main` (*Owed*). Of the 99, run again with the narrowed move off and on: 26 the same both ways (24 as the nightly, the first form's difference gone, and 20782701 and 20802256, `main`'s own since), 73 differ: 18 accept a group more, none fewer, 55 as many in other words. Four that the first form built are refused again as on `main`: 20788885 (two coaxial tori less a plane's half, joined to the inner one, refused by the chart; the first form built it right, 3230725123826.5 against 3230725123249.4 in closed form, 577 apart where its area times the tolerance allows over 1e6), 20781060, 20788291 and 20797689. Four of the 18 are held to volumes written without the kernel or to the body before them (the rest by the fuzzer's oracles alone; a fifth reduction did not fit the round's runs):

- 20788512, group B's fillet (scale 1e-3: a cone's tip less a hole, the hole's rim on the cut filleted by 0.00125; on `main` the boolean's invalid body): 3.6355248557e-7 against 3.6355248422e-7 (`csg_quad.py`: the fillet the region between the rim and the rolling ball, run out at the cone; 1.4e-15 apart, where 4.2e-13 is allowed; the body before it, 3.684229767e-7, to 4e-9).
- 20780154 (scale 1: a cylinder cut by a slanted plane, the cap's circle and the line where the cap meets the plane filleted by 1.25 together; on `main` *"too large … below 0.874497 mm"*, though the circle's tangent line leaves the wall before the vertex at any radius): 146.554088443 against 146.554087918 (`tools/quad_80154.py`; each fillet the region between its edge and the ball, carried on along the edge, the two together; 5.2e-7 apart, where 1.7e-4 is allowed).
- 20797316 (scale 1000: a cylinder cut by a plane near its wall, the cap's line and the two lines where the plane meets the wall filleted by 2500; on `main` *"below 1876.59 mm"*): 3132848507268.7 against 3132848504497.6 (`tools/quad_97316.py`, the same reading; 2771 apart, where 1.25e6 is allowed). The cap's fillet, carried on along its line, runs out through the wall far past the line's ends (the plane meets the wall at 4.9 degrees): that is how the fillet's tools are made, not the boolean's choice.
- 20786172 (scale 1: an extrude's cut, a slot that only touches a torus and a ring) equals the body before it, 9830.152102412338, as it must: the slot lies wholly outside both (z at most 5 and y at most 2.5, the torus above z = 5 and the ring beyond y = 2.5).

Five more of the 18 are held to volumes written without the kernel by the review (Check 45334254056 in the workshop: each design cut down, group by group with the move off and on, to the groups its gain needs; the quadratures in `plan/tangent-edge-border/review/tools`, in coordinates of their own): the booleans 20813790 (scale 1000: an L-shaped prism cut to a torus, then to a ring; 289004854222.5 against 289004855059.0, 837 apart where the tolerance times the area allows 3.2e5) and 20820116 (scale 1e-3: a stepped turned body, a face moved and an edge chamfered, cut from a screw pattern of six cylinders and intersected with its mirror image, three shells; 4.8e-16 apart where 2.6e-12 is allowed), and the fillets 20803950, 20799425 and 20813166 (a rectangle turned half a turn or a quarter, two or three edges of a cap filleted, which `main` said too large; the union of the edges' tools, each the region between its edge and the ball carried along the edge, cut from the ring: 1.8e-17, 1.1e-8 and 1.9e-8 apart where 1.0e-12, 2.6e-4 and 5.8e-4 are allowed). The review redid 20788512's quadrature about the cone's axis, not the hole's: 1.35e-15 apart.

*Held to.* `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_tangent_edge_border.rs`: *a tangent edge across a spline border divides the wall* (the seed's family, the border 13.76 to 14.5 high, at four scales, turned, the tool's seam at five places: 70 cuts, each to `topo::check`, the boolean's full check and its volume written without the kernel, within the tolerance times its area); *the same tool across an exact border divides the wall as before* (32); *a border barely above the circle is built right or at the edge of tolerance* (32, 1e-5 to 5e-3 above, spline and ellipse); *seed 110541*, *seed 121649* (volumes written without the kernel), *seed 124545* (held to the same body made with the rim cut first), *seed 128676* (a cup in a hole through a ring, joined: the boolean's full check and the identity of sets, the cup's volume in closed form); *the volumes written here meet bodies built before*; and, owed and ignored, *seed 121704*. The review's `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_tangent_edge_border_review.rs`: *a closed spline border crossed twice by the tangent circle divides the wall* (a ball's hole inside the wall, four holes, four seams, four placements: 64 cuts, 48 of them refused on `main`, each held to both checks and a volume written without the kernel), *a touch standing for two crossings never makes a wrong body* (the border 0 to 3e-3 above the circle, three seams, five placements: built right or refused), *a straight tangent edge across a spline border on a plane* (a block less a ring, a fillet's tool along its edge: 45 cuts held to the other order). The volumes: the radial integral in closed form (the ball, the cone and the half-space meet a ray in a quadratic's interval), the angle in stretches between the zeros of continuous functions that mark where the radial set changes form (the ray tangent to the ball, a root at the wall), the height adaptively; the same written in Python (`tools/quad_kinks.py` in the workshop) agrees with `main`'s bodies where they built to 1.9e-7, and with the repaired ones to 2.4e-7 (where 2.8e-4 is allowed). Without those cuts the halves can agree by chance across a kink: an earlier form of this test, cut where the set's form changed among 512 places, missed two changes a thousandth of a radian apart and was 8e-3 off at one height, and one without cuts 2.9e-4. `crates/engine/tests/blend_invalid_tool.rs` as below (*What moved*).

*What moved.* The boolean corpus (Check 45300555650, the corpus command of the nightly run in a Check, on 993bbf65 with this repair alone, and Check 45316009225 on the repair as built, narrowed, with its move switched on): the digest the base is recorded with, `35e8fbc97343ca2d`, and the score sheet of the nightly Heavy 45087810006 line for line (11,988 outcomes; 11,594 agreeing; 228 unsupported; no mismatch, none invalid). With the second part too it was `1acd177a811221ec` and 11,582 (Heavy 45297281764). The benchmark's counted lines are the base's (the trial Gate's `benchmark` test, within its baseline). The trial Gate 45304211798 on 6032fcf4 passed every test binary but this file's seed 128676, whose identity read an empty intersection as a refusal (the cup lies in the hole; the union itself built and passed the full check at every scale), the FreeCAD step and the web module's size; the Gate on aa3738b1, with that read, passed (45308780561: the full `cargo xtask ci`, FreeCAD and the web module's size). The repair as built, narrowed, with these texts (cca8606e, its code 050abc63): the trial Gate 45327244028 passed (the full `cargo xtask ci`, 725 test binaries with the benchmark within its baseline, FreeCAD and the web module's size). The random designs with the repair's first form (Heavy 45301698963 on 6639d8d2, an hour from seed 20,780,000: 48,308 seeds, against the nightly below): 99 differ, among them 22 accepting a group more and **3 a group fewer**, traced and narrowed above. With the repair as built (Heavy 45318181298 on 050abc63, an hour from seed 20,780,000: 65,519 seeds; against the nightly Heavy 45087810006 on 1f196da4, by verdict, groups, accepted groups and every refusal word for word, over the 64,787 both ran): 64,682 the same, 105 differ, **none accepting a group fewer**. 75 are among the 99, each as the probe ran it with the move on: 73 this repair's (18 accept a group more, 55 as many in other words) and 2 `main`'s own since the nightly (20782701, whose `measure.below` answers since *A section small against its distance from the origin*; 20802256, a chamfer's words since the invalid tool's repair). 30 lie past the 99's range; run with the move off and on by the review (Check 45334254056, in one process as the 99 were), every one differs by the move (with it as in Heavy 45318181298; without it as in the nightly but for 20829548 and 20830471, whose fillets say the boolean's invalid body in `main`'s words since the nightly): 3 accept a group more (20830471, a fillet that was the boolean's invalid body; 20831492, a pattern's join refused as two points the tolerance cannot tell apart; 20843775, a fillet said too large), 27 as many in other words, among them 20837254, which accepts its group 2 (a fillet `main` said too large) and then, its design going on from another body, refuses three groups `main` accepted and accepts two `main` refused. The review also held the bodies after every group, with the move off and on, up to the first group where a design differs (Checks 45336350110 and 45337809167 in the workshop): of the 30, the 99 and the gains with every check (the fuzz run's own designs, 129 seeds), and of 10,108 designs generated without the identities or a deep check (seeds 20,780,000 to 20,789,999 and those), none goes from a body to a refusal; 23 of the 129 accept a group `main` refused there (the 21, and 20809398 and 20837254, which the comparison by groups counts as other words: after the gain their designs go on from another body), and two accept a fillet with another body within the tolerance of the first (20791645, volumes 5.3e-7 apart where the tolerance times the area allows 6.8e-5; 20812206, 7.3e-18 where 3.7e-13), as does 20781813 among the 10,108 (4.5e-8 where 6.8e-5). Of the 83 in other words, 56 are fillets (35 that were the boolean's refusal or its invalid body, now in the fillet's own words, 21 *"the fillet does not end cleanly"* and 12 too large, or 2 in the boolean's other words; 21 too large below another size, or now not ending cleanly), 3 chamfers, 11 pattern joins, booleans, a revolve and an extrude refused as zero thickness at another of the places where the parts touch, and 13 other booleans' and joins' refusals in other words (2 joins refused as two points the tolerance cannot tell apart now as the torus face the chart does not build). No crash, bug, wrong result or timeout that the base did not have: its bugs 20791978 and 20821184, its wrong result 20814570 and its timeouts 20786729, 20827917 and 20838232 are as they were. Expectations changed (`crates/engine/tests/blend_invalid_tool.rs`, the boolean now building those tools' cuts, each to the deep check here and in the kernel's tests): `seed_124545_builds_what_fits_and_is_too_large_past_its_cap` (was `seed_124545_is_refused_by_the_boolean_whose_faults_lie_below_what_fits`: 1.25 and 2.39 refused in the boolean's words; now 0.9 to 2.39435 build and 2.39437 is refused, and 2.45, 2.5 and 3 are too large with what fits said from 2.39435 to 2.39437, where they were the boolean's refusal; the chamfer's limit as before; after the review of 2026-11-24 a regression test of today's answer, held as tightly as the sweep found it, true of what builds and not of what the faces leave, which the owed test holds to); `seeds_121649_and_128676_are_refused_in_the_fillets_own_words` (was `…_by_the_boolean_in_the_fillets_words`; a regression test of wording, the cuts held in the kernel's tests). The two owed tests there stay owed: the short cone as before, and 124545 turned 30 degrees about Z at 2.5, said too large below 2.39436 (the cap's limit as said above).

*Owed.*

- **A border barely above the circle** (within a few thousandths, crossed at a degree or less, the spline or the ellipse within the tolerance of the round face over both crossings): one touch stands for two crossings, and the cut is refused in edge-of-tolerance words, as on `main`. The second part above builds some of them, and refuses four near pairs of the corpus; a form of it that leaves those as they are is owed. The fillet 124545 meets the kind near its cap's limit, where the tool's tangent circle on the cap meets the cap's border at its far point at a shallow angle: from 2.394360 (2.394358 builds) to 2.3963 of the cap's 2.396547 its tool's cut is still an invalid body (*"edge 4 is used 1 times"*), from 2.3964 to 2.397 refused as an imprint that does not fit (on `main`, which refused every radius from 1.25 up, as two points the tolerance cannot tell apart), and the fillet from 2.3964 on, past the cap and just below it, is said too large *"below 2.39436"* (or *"2.39435"*): true of what builds (the review swept the fillet every 2e-6 from 2.3943 to 2.39436 and every 1e-4 from there to 2.3967: nothing at or above 2.394360 builds) and not of what the faces leave.
- **Seed 121704** (a plain boolean): half a torus whose end rim is filleted, less a copy moved along its axis; both end caps lie in one plane, each bounded by its fillet's tangent edge (a spline), and the copy's crosses the original's. Both fillets are tangent to the plane there, so no surface meets either edge across it: the plane's meeting with the copy's fillet is marched as a crossing curve and cut where the original's tangent edge touches that fillet, at one crossing (−8.85, 6.25) and not the other (−15.61, 6.25); both stretches are left out, and the original's cap is not divided along the copy's tangent edge (Check 45294757009). The place is where the two curves cross on the plane. Ignored test in the new file.
- **121649's and 128676's fillets** are refused in the fillet's own words where they were the boolean's invalid body: the fillet reaches the cone's face (the cone's base circle passes through the rim's one vertex) at every radius tried from 0.1 to 1, and a fillet by the hole's own radius does not end cleanly. Whether a fillet that runs into the cone at the vertex should be built is the blend's question.
- **Random design 20781906, `main`'s own**: built, on `main` and here, with zero thickness along two arcs of the circle along which a cutter's flat end touches a torus's cavity (*Narrowed*, above). The first form found it by placing the touch inside the face exactly; the narrowed one leaves that touch where the hit is, as `main` does. Finding the pinch there without imprinting a circle that the chart then refuses (20791237, 20812686) is owed: a chart that builds a face of a torus bounded by a circle about its axis, or a section that tells a touch with the solid kept on both sides from one with it taken away on both. *Repaired 2026-11-25 by the second* (*A pinch along a curve*, below): the touch inside the face is taken onto the circle where the result keeps both faces there.
- **The torus's chart** refuses a face bounded by a circle about the torus's axis where a touch makes that circle a stretch: 20788885 on `main` and here (the first form built it right), 20791237 and 20812686 with the first form.
- An exact edge's touch stands where it is: near a crossing at a degree or less it is placed by rounding only to a few tolerances (the ellipse above).

### Near approaches inside an edge or a face (found 2026-11-24 by the near-miss investigation, repaired the same day, reviewed and amended the same day and on 2026-11-25; `near.rs`, `mod.rs` › `near_approaches`, `contact.rs` › `evidence`, `section.rs` › `Sections::met`, `beside`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_near_approach.rs`, `boolean_near_approach_review.rs`, `boolean_near_approach_review2.rs`, `boolean_near_dimple.rs`, `boolean_near_dimple_review.rs`)

*Found* by the investigation of the workshop's `plan/near-miss/` (on `main` abdcaed0e, measuring only). The developer's rules of 2026-10-15 (parts that touch are zero thickness), 2026-10-18 (a wedge or a sliver beside a vertex is accepted) and 2026-10-22 (faces or points of exact bodies between half and twice the tolerance apart are refused) were applied only at *places*: the points the section registers or makes (its two-points-too-near), the samples of `gap` on faces of one kind, and the places `zero_thickness` is given (the vertices both operands are at, the section's vertices, touch points, `edge_meets`, `on_the_other`, a point of every stretch). Nothing looked at the inside of an edge or of a face against the other body where no surfaces meet nearby. So `main` built, and `document.interference` called clear: two edges crossing 1.5 and 1.9 tolerances apart (the second review's beak, 1.8987), a circle over a plane and a straight edge over a rod's round side from 1.1 to 1.9 (the review's rod, 1.9), two rods side by side, a ball or a rod over a plane from 1.1 to 1.9, two rods across from 0.75, a vertex 1.9 tolerances from the middle of an edge (regeneration's post-check refused some of these as a feature, by chance: `touches` for the vertex beside an edge, `parts_touch` at an edge's middle). Counted by the investigation's probe (read-only, after every boolean that built): none in the 112 published items, the 33 Jigs' 3 538 points, the benchmark's models or the tests but slivers beside a vertex that the tests pin as built; 20 unions of the corpus's `near` built as two parts one tolerance apart; three random designs in 44 992.

*The look* (`near.rs`). After a boolean that refuses zero thickness has passed `zero_thickness` (and a sheet `touching_itself`): the pairs of the result's vertices, edges and faces that share no vertex and come less than twice the tolerance apart, one on a face of A and the other on a face of B. What makes it cheap is read before any geometry. The section met every pair of faces it did not prove further apart than twice the tolerance (`to_meet`: boxes, hulls, covers) and now keeps them (`Sections::met`); an entity lies on the faces around it, and a face of the result on the faces of the operands it is a piece of (`merged`), so two entities come that near only where a face of A around one and a face of B around the other were met. Pieces of one operand are not looked at (an approach that operand had already). Then the boxes, grown by twice the tolerance (a tree of one side's, asked with each of the other's; a face's whole box, and the box of what it holds only where a pair of faces is measured on a piece of a ball or a ring, which is dear to make), a shared vertex (joined there), and two vertices (the section's to refuse).
