By kind: a vertex to an edge or a face by its nearest point; an edge to an edge or a face by its distance at 24 places along it, each stretch between two of them bounded below by the edge's speed (`speed_bound`), only the stretches that may come within twice the tolerance searched by golden sections (three at most); two lines in closed form; two faces by the pairs of points square to both surfaces where their kinds have them in closed form, and by alternating projections from three seeds, with a descent where they crawl (since 2026-11-25, *The dimple and the caps*, below). A straight edge over a plane and two planes come nearest at a boundary, which is looked at as its edges and vertices. Before any of it a pair is passed over where an analytic surface is far from the other entity's box, or where the covers of the operands' faces prove a freeform face that far (`Cover::holds`, `reaches`, `meets`, at twice the tolerance). An approach counts only where two points of the two entities, each evaluated exactly, are less than twice the tolerance apart and inside both faces: a search that misses misses, it never finds an approach that is not there. At most 2^21 pairs are measured and 2^24 looked at in one look (65 536 and 2^20 until 2026-11-25); a look stopped there says so, and the boolean is refused and the interference unknown (since 2026-11-25, below).

*What is done with one* (`near_approaches`). Two parts (shells) that come that near touch: their nearest points are places for `zero_thickness`, which refuses them as zero thickness, and where it finds nothing at such a place the boolean refuses it in the same words, the two points being measured that near. Two walls of one shell are read at the place between the two points as `within_a_shell` reads two faces at a place (since the review, below): the walls are the faces around each entity but a face around both (the strip of a face that the approach runs across, which is joined to its neighbours by the entities' own edges whatever the walls beside it do); where a wall around the one and a wall around the other have a vertex in common within four tolerances of the place, an edge in common that passes that near, or an edge in common whose wedge reaches the place (`wedged`: within `2t / sin(θ/2)` of it, θ the walls' angle there, up to a tenth of the smaller wall, and beyond that where the gap is at least half what θ leaves there), they are a wedge, a wall that thins to a knife edge or a corner sharper than the tolerance resolves, accepted (2026-10-18), and left as they are. Otherwise, between half and twice the tolerance apart, they are refused as *"two points closer together than the tolerance can tell apart"*, naming a face of each: a web that thin, a strip of a face that narrow. `walls_apart` and the reading through a third face are not asked: they are for walls that touch, not for the band. The distance is the least the search found, a bound from above of the least there is (for two faces whose kinds are in closed form, the least between their insides, since 2026-11-25); where two faces were found by alternating projections it is polished by golden sections across the first face, since the projections crawl where faces touch tangentially and could read walls that touch, within half the tolerance, as the band. In `contact`'s evidence the same look between the two operands, where no place of the section is a contact, gives a touch, in a point between the two nearest points: interference says *touch* where it said *clear*.

*Cost* (Check 45322459175 on `check/near-approach-cost` 5743b1e7: the look as the repair runs it, its answers not acted on, timed beside the boolean on the same thread, release; the Strix also ran another builder's fuzz then; `plan/near-approach/logs/cost-45322459175.txt`). On the benchmark's models 0.093 s against 6.56 s of the booleans that look, 1.4 % (with zero thickness off, as in the steps of blends and in interference's own boolean, nothing is looked at): engine block 1.2 %, holes 2.9 %, impeller 0.8 %, rotor 0, turbofan 0.4 %; at most 8.4 ms in one boolean (the impeller's blades). The investigation's probe, which measured every pair of the result, cost 0.96 of the boolean on the same models. The benchmark's CPU per model is the same with the repair to within 5 % (Check 45324508556), and its counted work is unchanged. On the corpus (8 802 booleans that look) 24 % of their time, nearly half of it the boxes of what faces on rings and balls hold, which the repair makes only for a pair of faces measured on one. Measured again by the review (Check 45340056874 on a throwaway with a kernel feature that leaves the look out, as `main`; the corpus test's own wall time, the test profile, every thread of one check runner, three runs each, interleaved): with the look 28.82, 29.00, 29.13 s, without 28.91, 29.25, 29.27 s; no difference beyond the runs' own spread of 0.3 s. The booleans' CPU time alone was not measured there (the corpus prints it per case only when asked by the environment).

*What moved.* What `main` answered (the investigation's table, `main`'s program at t = 0.01) and what is answered now (the test file, the same at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6, as built and turned, every pair alike), for the union and the interference:

| Kind | Union in the band (0.75, 1.1, 1.5, 1.9 t) on `main` | Now | Interference on `main` → now |
|---|---|---|---|
| vertex to vertex | refused, too near (all four) | the same | refused (unknown) |
| vertex to the middle of an edge | too near, too near, zero thickness, **built** | too near, too near, zero thickness, zero thickness | refused, refused, touch, **clear** → touch |
| vertex to a face | zero thickness | the same | touch |
| edges crossing | too near, too near, **built**, **built** | too near, too near, zero thickness, zero thickness | **clear** at 1.5, 1.9 → touch |
| edges along each other | nearly coincident, nearly coincident, **built**, **built** (refused as a feature by the post-check) | nearly coincident, nearly coincident, zero thickness, zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| a circle over a plane | zero thickness, **built** ×3 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| an edge over a rod's round side (at its middle, and away from it) | zero thickness, **built** ×3 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| a ball over a plane | zero thickness, **built** ×3 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| rods across | **built** ×4 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| rods side by side | zero thickness, **built** ×3 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| a rod over a plane | zero thickness, **built** ×3 | zero thickness | **clear** → touch |
| planes over each other | nearly coincident | the same | refused (unknown) |

Measured on the amended look (Check 45348883919): the benchmark's counted lines are the same line for line, its CPU per model the same or lower (engine block 6.2 s, holes 3.1, impeller 7.7, rotor 1.3, turbofan 7.3; 6.3, 3.2, 7.7, 1.3, 7.6 in round one); the corpus test and the kernel tests of the finding, the border, the truss joint, the crack and contact pass; the corpus is as in round one line by line (Check 45350093878: 11 594 agreeing, the digest the same); a trial Gate on 545a21a2 passed (45350086682). *The second review* (`plan/near-approach/review2/REVIEW.md`, its tests in `boolean_near_approach_review2.rs`; Check 45362576161 on a throwaway with a kernel feature that leaves the look out, as `main`, and the look's own counts printed). The review's 144 booleans answer the same with the look and without it but one: the first review's boss beside the knife edge at 10 degrees, near the end of the edge and turned with a corner to it, and a hole through the wedge beside it, built both ways; a foot within twice the tolerance of the knife edge itself refused both ways (by the section or as zero thickness); a web 1.5 tolerances thick under a dimple of radius 20 at the plate's middle refused by the look (`main` built it). At 2 degrees as found and at 30 degrees `main` refuses the boss itself, for reasons not the look's (the section's two faces at the edge of tolerance, as found only; `zero_thickness`, whose reading through a third face asks the place to be more than four tolerances from the edge). Found: **the same web off the plate's middle is built**, as on `main`: the alternating projections between a ball that large and a plane that near close in on the nearest point by `1 − gap / radius` a step, so from a seed 8.5 degrees round the ball they stop 0.22 units off after forty steps, and the polish, tried only from a pair within twice the tolerance, is not tried (a wrong body by 2026-10-22, held by a test that fails today and is ignored as owed). The caps were not reached: fewer than 2 048 pairs looked at and 2 048 measured in any one look of the benchmark, the corpus and Garabit's findings (the most seen 1 588 looked at and 1 306 measured, in Garabit's findings), against 2^20 and 65 536. Two entities that share a vertex anywhere are still not measured, whatever the distance from that vertex to where they come near: the one exception read from the topology alone.

*The dimple and the caps* (2026-11-25; the workshop's `plan/near-dimple/`; `near.rs` › `least_between`, `square_pairs`, `descend`, `Looked`, `CUT_SHORT`; `contact.rs` › `evidence`; the tests of `near.rs`, `boolean_near_dimple.rs`, and `boolean_near_approach_review2.rs`'s dimple, which runs again). The second review's two findings.

- **The least distance between two faces.** Where two faces are nearest at a point inside both, neither on a boundary, the line through the two points is square to both surfaces: the distance is stationary there. For a plane or a ball against any analytic surface, and for two cylinders, those pairs are known in closed form (`square_pairs`): a plane and a ball, the foot of the centre and the ball's two points over it; a plane and a ring, the four points where the ring's normal is the plane's (`o ± R m ± r n`, `m` the part of the plane's normal square to the axis); a ball and any analytic surface, the points of that surface whose normal passes through the centre (the foot on a plane; along the line of centres on a ball; square to the axis through the centre on a cylinder; the feet on a cone's two generators in the half-plane through the centre, and its apex, where it has no normal; the tube's two points on the line from the tube's middle to the centre in the half-plane through the centre and in the opposite one, on a ring), each with the ball's two points on that line; two cylinders, the common perpendicular of the axes. Where the pairs form a line (a rod over a plane, two parallel rods, a cone's generator over a plane) a boundary of each face crosses it, measured there as an edge or a vertex; where they form a circle (a ring lying flat over a plane, a ball on a pipe's axis, two balls about one centre) the faces hold all of it or a boundary crosses it, and one pair stands for it. A direction that rounding could tilt out of its plane (the part of a normal square to an axis, the radius of a ball centred on an axis) is made square to the axis again before it is used, so every point lies on its surface. For these kinds the least distance between the two faces' insides is found exactly, wherever it lies, at the cost of a few points. For the other kinds (two of a cylinder, a cone and a torus but two cylinders, and a spline face against anything) the alternating projections from the three seeds are kept, and where they have not settled in their forty steps a descent goes on from where they stopped (`descend`): golden-section line searches, a quarter of the smaller box's diagonal at most, along the part of `q − p` in the first surface's tangent plane (the direction in which the distance falls fastest), bent by the last direction (Fletcher and Reeves), at most eight, each kept only where the distance falls; and a cone's apex with its nearest point. The projections step by about `gap · sin θ` (θ the angle between the two normals) and so crawl between faces nearly tangent and a little apart; a line search takes the step the distance asks for. The search as it was (the projections' nearest end, polished) is kept for every kind, and the least of everything is taken: no distance found is larger than before. For the kinds in closed form the distance is the least between the faces' insides; for the others it is a bound from above, measured but not proven: a spline paraboloid of radius 20 or 5 over a plane and a ball of radius 20 or 5 over a flat spline face, 1.5 tolerances apart at a point off the middle, found within a ten-thousandth of a tolerance of the least (which the construction gives, and a dense sampling of the spline surface confirms), either way round, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3, as found and turned. A proof for these kinds (a lower bound, from the covers refined) is owed.
- **The caps.** A look stopped at a cap says so (`Looked::finished`). The boolean then refuses, naming the cap (*"the look for parts or walls closer together than the tolerance can tell apart stopped at its cap (2097152 pairs measured, or 16777216 looked at) before it had looked at every pair…"*), where what the look found does not refuse the result already; interference is unknown where the look found nothing (refused in the same words, as every refusal of the boolean is unknown), and a touch where it found an approach before it stopped (two points of the two solids that near, and no volume in common). The entities left are counted as before; a test lowers the caps for its own thread to hold both answers. *The caps raised.* The second review measured the benchmark, the corpus and Garabit's findings, all far below the caps of 2026-11-24 (65 536 measured, 2^20 looked at). The engine's fuzz findings are not: with the refusal in, the trial Gate failed on `fuzz_findings` › `pattern_of_four_patterns_of_shelled_balls` (random design 20757788-four, twenty balls of radius 10 shelled 1.25 thick in two patterns), whose last pattern `main` builds and the branch refused at the cap (Gate 45591824725; no two of its twenty balls come within 1 700 tolerances of tangency, so nothing but the cap could refuse it), and the random designs refused one, 20786729 (shelled tubes in patterns less a ball, which `main` does not finish within the fuzzer's minute). Measured with the caps lifted on a throwaway (Check 45599454882, every look of more than 20 000 pairs looked at, 2 000 measured or 50 ms printed by kind): the twenty balls' three largest looks 140 055, 140 474 and 1 114 658 pairs looked at, 79 480, 79 255 and 330 218 measured, 0.4, 0.4 and 1.25 s in the test profile; over all 33 fuzz findings in release (the 25 the gate leaves out included) at most 5 079 035 looked at and 570 811 measured in one look, the longest look 15.8 s (433 k measured, about 36 µs each on average; by the probe's timing per kind most of it edges against edges, 103 020 pairs in 10.4 s at about 100 µs each, then edges against faces, 176 331 in 3.4 s, and faces against faces, 42 024 in 1.3 s), and none found an approach; all 33 pass. So the caps are now 2^21 measured and 2^24 looked at, above every look measured by more than three times; past them the boolean is refused as above. What `main` left unmeasured past its caps on these inputs is now measured: the gate's twenty balls took 3.8 s in the test profile with the caps lifted (the gate's binary of eight findings 18.9 s with the old caps, Gate 45591824725).

*Answers.* The second review's dimple of radius 20 off the plate's middle, built before: refused, 4 of 4 in the band, one shell at 3 tolerances (Check 45586908016). The dimple at eight places and three radii (6, 20 and 100; under the middle, off it, towards a corner): refused at 1.5 tolerances, 48 of 48, one shell and checked at 3, 48 of 48; over a plate whose bottom is a flat spline face (radius 20, three places), 18 and 18; a ring tilted over a plate, a ball beside a rod, beside a cone's side and beside a ring, and a ball of radius 9 in a cavity of radius 10 off its middle: at 1.5 tolerances the union zero thickness and the interference a touch, 30 of 30, at 3 the parts built, checked, and the interference clear, 30 of 30; each at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3, as found and turned (Check 45588677300, `logs/answers-45588677300.txt`). On `main`'s commit (Check 45591914825, a throwaway with the new test file): the review's dimple off the middle built in the band, 4 of 4, at the middle refused, 4 of 4, at 3 tolerances one shell, 8 of 8; the new file was not reached (cargo stops at the first test binary that fails).

*The corpus* (Checks 45588672400 on `main` and 45588677300 on the branch, each with every outcome printed by case on a throwaway, `logs/outcomes-*.txt`): 11 985 of the 11 988 outcomes are the same; three unions that were a `match` are refused as zero thickness, `near/block-sphere/017`, `near/sphere-torus/017` and `near/torus-torus/010`, whose parts lie 1.0000 tolerance apart by the cases' own numbers (a ball by a block's face and by a ring, two rings; `plan/near-dimple/neargap.py`), each refusal's point on two parts of the body made without it (`on_parts`); the investigation's probe, which searched as the look did, had not counted them among the 20. The digest (35e8fbc97343ca2d) and the sum of agreeing outcomes (11 594) are as before; `MATCHED_FLOOR` 10 534 to 10 531 and `ZERO_THICK_CEILING` 1 060 to 1 063 (an expectation change).

*The benchmark* (release, twice on each, the same Checks side by side): every counted line the same as on `main`, 23 for each of the five models, in both runs; the CPU per model the same within the runs' own spread (turbofan 7.6 and 7.7 s against 7.8 and 7.7 on `main`, engine block 6.5 and 6.7 against 6.6 and 6.6, impeller 7.7 and 7.8 against 7.8 and 7.8, rotor 1.3 against 1.3, holes 3.3 and 3.4 against 3.4 and 3.3). The corpus test, in the test profile, took 37.8 s on the branch and 38.0 s on `main` in the same two Checks (each printing its 11 988 outcomes; not measured quietly).

*Random designs* (Heavy fuzz 45591811353 on 76f82eee, the caps of 2026-11-24, from 20 780 000 for an hour: 44 331 seeds, no crash, the same two bugs and the same wrong cut as `main`; `plan/near-dimple/logs/fuzzcmp-*.txt`). Against `main`'s Heavy 45438163077 (22f0bb13, from 20 780 000: all 44 331 seeds in common) and the nightly 45516571671 (from 20 782 000: 42 331), three differ: 20786865, the same answers, the place a refusal names different in its last digit (the two balls 1.6863 tolerances apart, now measured by the closed form's pair); 20786729, which `main` does not finish within the fuzzer's minute, refused at the old cap (with the caps raised not run again: owed); 20802370, built on `main` in 38.5 s, past the minute here, in the hour's busiest stretch (three other Heavy runs on the Strix: seeds 20 794 000 to 20 806 000 took 1.9 to 3.6 times as long as on `main`, the others 1.06 to 1.2 times; `logs/fuzz-times-by-window.txt`), so not taken for a change of answer. The designs' time was not measured quietly.

*Gate.* A trial Gate on 76f82eee (45591824725) failed on `fuzz_findings`, the caps above; one on 54634ddd, the caps raised (45610423041): success in every step (`cargo xtask ci`, 776 test results ok and none failed, the benchmark within its baseline, `fuzz_findings` 13.1 s; FreeCAD; the web module's size).

*The review of the dimple and the caps* (2026-11-25; the workshop's `plan/near-dimple/review/`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_near_dimple_review.rs`, three tests failing, Check 45622243924). Read, and measured where it could be. (1) The closed forms give every pair square to both surfaces that is alone, and one of each circle, but one: a plane and a ring whose axis leans less than 1e-9 rad from the plane's normal, where `from_plane` takes the ring's own x for the part of the normal square to the axis (meant for a ring lying exactly flat), so its pairs miss the nearest by up to `2 R × 1e-9`. A ring of radius 3000 leaning 8e-10 rad, its lowest point 1.5 tolerances over a plate (default tolerance), is built as two parts and the interference is clear; the section takes such a ring as lying flat (`plane_torus` below the angular tolerance), and leaning 2e-9 rad refuses it as *"tangent or nearly coincident over too large an area"*, 3 tolerances over the plate too. The direction of that part is good to rounding long before 1e-9 (its error times `R |m|` is about `R ε`), so a threshold at the rounding of the projection, say 1e-12, would keep the flat ring and find the leaning one (proposed, not tried). The feet of a point on a cone and on a ring (`normals_through`) agree with the point's distance to the surface in closed form at 2000 random points, scales 1e-3 to 1e6, to 2e-15 of the scale (a port, `plan/near-dimple/review/`). Two cylinders whose axes are nearly parallel have the common perpendicular's feet from a formula that divides by `sin² θ`; the form `((o2 − o1) × d2) · (d1 × d2) / |d1 × d2|²` loses only `ε |o2 − o1| / sin θ` along the axis (small, proposed). (2) No distance acted on is smaller than the truth beyond rounding: every pair is two points on their surfaces, in closed form or projected and evaluated, inside both faces. (3) For the other kinds a descent is tried only from seeds whose projections did not settle; where all three settle on a pair square to both surfaces that is not the nearest (least across, greatest round), nothing goes on: a ring slid on a rod one tolerance off its axis, 1.5 tolerances from it at the nearest, is built as two parts and the interference is clear (a cylinder and a torus; every seed lands on the far side). (4) `craft3d_topo`'s whole box of a torus face reaches `R √(1 − z_i²) + r` along each axis, which loses `R sin α` once the axis leans less than about 1e-8 rad from that axis (`z_i` rounds to 1): a ring of radius 30 000 leaning 8e-9 rad sunk 140 tolerances into a block is left as two shells that overlap and the interference is clear, on `main` too; `R hypot(z_j, z_k) + r` keeps it (proposed). The look's pairing and its `far` tests read that box too. (5) The cost: in the two longest looks of the cap probe edges against edges took 10.4 and 10.2 s of 15.8 and 15.7 (about 100 µs a pair; up to 180 µs in another finding's looks), edges against faces 3.4 and 3.7 s (about 20 µs); at the caps a look of edges against edges would take some 3.5 minutes before the refusal, and could keep 2^21 approaches. Prunings that change no answer, since an approach counts only where two evaluated points are nearer than twice the tolerance: a lower bound of two edges' distance from both edges' speed bounds, piece against piece and halved where it does not settle, before any nearest point is sought; the box of what a face holds (a piece of a ball or a ring) against an edge's or a vertex's grown box before it is sampled, and in the pairing; the faces an entity lies on read once, not for every pair; a cap counted in nearest points sought rather than in pairs. (6) The corpus's three are one tolerance apart by their own numbers (the block and the ball by hand: the ball's top at z = 1.25, the block's bottom at 1.250001 over it), so by the rule of 2026-10-15, and the constants meet *Changing an expectation*; `neargap.py` left `near/block-sphere/017` out (its operands are the other way round) and divides the lengths of a case at another scale by the scaled tolerance.

*Round three* (2026-11-25; the workshop's `plan/near-look-3/`; `near.rs` › `from_plane`, `escape`, `edges_apart`, `Stretch`, `segment_gap`, `Look::away_from_piece`, `Info`, `Work`; `craft3d_topo` `transform.rs` › `face_extent`; `boolean_near_dimple_review.rs`, its three tests running again; `crates/topo/tests/transform.rs` › `a_torus_leaning_a_hair_off_a_world_axis_is_boxed_whole`; the tests of `near.rs` › `the_bound_of_two_edges_never_passes_over_a_pair_that_near`, `the_gap_of_two_segments_is_never_more_than_their_distance`). The review's F1, F2, F3 and F7.

- **A ring leaning a hair over a plate (F1).** `from_plane` takes a ring as lying flat only where the part of the plane's normal square to the ring's axis is within the rounding of its projection (1e-12, was 1e-9): the direction of a part `m` long is off by about `ε / |m|`, which moves the pairs' height over the plane by about `R ε` whatever `|m|` is, while a ring leaning `|m|` taken as flat puts its pairs a quarter turn from its lowest point, `R |m|` higher. The review's ring of radius 3000 leaning 8e-10 rad, its lowest point 1.5 tolerances over a plate: zero thickness, the interference a touch.
- **A saddle where the projections settle (F2).** Where the alternating projections settle, the pair is square to both surfaces, but it may be a saddle of the distance (a ring slid on a rod off its axis: least across, greatest round) or a greatest, where a descent has no slope to start from. From every such end (once for ends within the tolerance of each other), for the kinds with no closed form, `escape` reads the distance's curvature across the first surface from second differences an eighth of the search's reach either way along two directions and both diagonals; where it curves down by more than rounding (and a millionth of the tolerance), a golden line search along the direction it curves down most, the reach either way, and a descent from the pair it reaches. The review's ring slid on a rod: zero thickness and a touch, the approach found 1.5 tolerances apart at the rod's −y, where the least is. Where the distance curves up every way it costs eight more pairs of projections for each settled end; in the corpus, the benchmark and the engine's fuzz findings it never went on past the curvature and changed no answer (below).
- **The box of a torus face (F3, `main`'s own).** `craft3d_topo`'s box of a whole torus face reaches `R √(z_j² + z_k²) + r` along each world axis (the length of the axis's other two components, `hypot`), not `R √(1 − z_i²) + r`, which is `r` once the axis leans less than about 1e-8 rad from that world axis (`z_i²` rounds to 1) and lost `R sin α`. The review's ring of radius 30 000 leaning 8e-9 rad sunk 140 tolerances into a block is one body (one shell) and the interference an overlap. A topology test boxes whole tori leaning from none to a thousandth of a radian off each world axis, at three scales, against their furthest points. Elsewhere a torus box moves in its last bits at most: the corpus, the benchmark's counts and the engine's fuzz findings are as before, while `measure.below`'s answers for a turned torus, pinned to the bit, moved within their allowance (the Gate, below: an expectation change).
- **The look's cost (F7)**, every answer kept (measured below):
  - *Two edges bounded from below on both sides before any nearest point is sought* (`edges_apart`). Each edge is a stretch whose points lie within a reach of a segment: a line's chord; an arc of a circle or of an ellipse of at most half a turn, its chord and sagitta `2 R sin²(φ/4)` (R the larger semi-axis: an ellipse is a circle's arc stretched by no more); a piece of a spline, its chord and the farthest control point of the spans it crosses (the convex hull, weights positive), or its middle and its speed bound where that is less; any other, its middle and its speed bound. A spline edge whose range runs past its curve's domain is never bounded (`past_domain`): `NurbsCurve::eval` carries the end spans on past it, where the curve leaves the hull of its control points and the speed bound does not hold (found by the review of round three: a cubic piece over [0, 1] read over [−1, 1] was proven apart at 0.2 from a line 0.1 from it); nor is such an edge, or a piece of a ball whose boundary holds one, passed over by the pieces' boxes below (`craft3d_topo`'s box of the edge is its control net's, which does not hold it there either: `main`'s own, owed). Two segments' distance is bounded from below (`segment_gap`: the least of each end's distance to the other segment and of the lines' distance less what rounding could add), stretches not proven apart are halved, at most 32 pairs compared. Where every pair of stretches is proven further apart than twice the tolerance (and 64 rounding errors of the largest coordinate), no two points of the edges are nearer, and the search it spares could find no approach. A test holds the bound against dense sampling for lines, arcs of circles and ellipses and spline pieces at random, every pair of kinds, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6.
  - *A vertex or an edge against a piece of a ball or a ring* is passed over where the box of what the piece holds (`held_face_bbox`), grown by twice the tolerance (a point taken as inside a face may lie a tolerance outside its boundary), is that far from its box; asked only where the vertex or the edge is that far from the box of the piece's boundary, and only once eight such have been measured against the piece (`Look::away_from_piece`). The review proposed those boxes in the pairing, and the first form made them for every such face up front: they cost the corpus more than they saved (194 looks that measured nothing took 30 to 215 ms making them; the box of a face with a long boundary costs up to a few hundred milliseconds), so they are made where many pairs ask for them.
  - *What the filters read of an entity* (the faces of A and of B it lies on, its vertices) is read once per entity (`Info`), not for every pair looked at.
  - *The caps count work* (`Work`: a pair looked at, a point of an edge evaluated for the bound of two edges, a point sought or measured on a curve or a surface; `crate::work::near_work` holds the looks' total) and approaches held: 2^26 units and 65 536 approaches. The largest look measured did 16 105 070 units, the large looks 0.2 to 1.3 µs a unit on average; at the cap, at those averages, some 15 to 90 s of such work (an average over the workloads measured, not a bound: each unit's own search has caps of its own, which allow more), and 65 536 approaches hold some 7 MB. The approaches held count the joined ones, which the boolean leaves as they are, with the rest: a body with that many joined approaches is refused as cut short. Past either the boolean is refused as before, in the words *"…stopped at its cap (67108864 units of work, or 65536 approaches found)…"*, and interference is unknown where nothing was found.

*Measured* by two throwaway probes that run the look three times in each boolean, as the dimple branch's (without the escape, the bound of two edges and the pieces' boxes; the faces read once in all three), with the escape, and as built, time each and compare their approaches bit for bit (Check 45629057622 on the first form, Check 45633763772 on the built one; release for the benchmark and the engine's fuzz findings; the Strix busy with other runs; `plan/near-look-3/logs/`). As built and with the prunings off, the approaches are the same in every look of the corpus, the benchmark, the engine's 33 fuzz findings (their ignored ones too) and the near-approach tests. The escape changed two looks, the review's ring on a rod (its union and its interference). The look's time, before (as the dimple branch's) and after (as built), in the same process:

| Where | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| the longest look (a pattern of shelled tubes less a ball, 713 403 pairs looked at), release | 15.2 s, edges against edges 10.1 s | 7.5 s, 2.4 s (89 % of 103 014 pairs of edges proven apart before any nearest point) |
| the other long look of that finding | 15.1 s (9.9 s) | 7.6 s (2.4 s) |
| shelled balls, 3 336 771 pairs looked at, release | 1.84 s, 282 476 edges against faces measured | 0.76 s, 18 857 |
| the gate's twenty shelled balls (`fuzz_findings`, test profile, its 8 looks) | 1.76 s | 0.53 s |
| every look of the 33 fuzz findings, release | 60.1 s | 32.4 s |
| the corpus's looks, test profile | 14.0 s | 11.8 s |
| the benchmark's looks, release | 0.069 s | 0.072 s |

(The dimple branch's own probe, Check 45599454882: the two long looks 15.8 and 15.7 s.) What is left of the longest look is edges against faces (3.3 s of 7.5) and faces against faces (1.2 s), measured as before: owed. (The totals are those printed at the corpus's 8 800th look, the benchmark's 512th and the findings' 10 808th, the last printed.)

*The corpus* (both probes, every outcome printed by case on the throwaways, `logs/outcomes-probe*.txt`): all 11 988 outcomes as on the dimple branch (`plan/near-dimple/logs/outcomes-branch-45588677300.txt`), case by case; the digest 35e8fbc97343ca2d, 10 531 agreeing outright and 1 063 refused as zero thickness, 11 594 in all. The corpus test passes on the final code (Check 45636877741).

*The benchmark* (release, twice each, the dimple branch 55b1cf62 in Check 45636463009 and the final code in Check 45636877741, twelve minutes apart on a busy machine): every counted line the same (23 for each of the five models); the CPU per model the same within the runs' spread (turbofan 7.7 and 7.7 s against 7.6 and 7.6, engine block 6.6 and 6.5 against 6.5 and 6.6, impeller 7.9 and 7.8 against 7.8 and 7.9, rotor 1.3 against 1.3, holes 3.4 and 3.3 against 3.4 and 3.4).

*Random designs* (Heavy fuzz 45636535779 on 8b5384fb, the final code but for clippy's rewrite of two comparisons, which answer the same, from 20 780 000 for an hour beside another Heavy fuzz: 49 561 seeds, no crash, the same two bugs and the same wrong cut as `main`, three timeouts; `plan/near-look-3/logs/fuzzcmp-*.txt`, by the wing's eighth point's `fuzzcmp.py`). Against the nightly 45516571671 (`main` 22f0bb13, from 20 782 000: 47 561 seeds in common) two differ: 20786865, the place a refusal names different in its last digit (the two balls 1.6863 tolerances apart, measured by the closed form's pair: the dimple branch's), and 20802370, built on `main` in 38.5 s and past the fuzzer's minute here, as on the dimple branch, in the run's busiest stretch (seeds 20 796 000 to 20 804 000 took 2.95 and 1.77 times as long as on `main`, the others 1.01 to 1.23 times: `logs/fuzz-times-by-window.txt`; not run alone: owed). Against the dimple branch's own (Heavy 45591811353 on 76f82eee, the caps of 2026-11-24: 44 331 in common) one differs: 20786729, refused there at the old cap, past the minute here as on `main` (its patterns of shelled tubes less a ball are those of the engine's fuzz finding with the longest looks, whose design takes more than a minute without them). The seeds built in both took 0.90 times the dimple branch's time.

*Gate.* A trial Gate on e8eb21ee (45639379639) failed on `below_far` alone, of 737 test binaries (FreeCAD and the web module's size passed): the hashes of `measure.below`'s answers for a turned torus near the origin, pinned to the bit, moved with its box (the section reads the box of the body and of the body turned square to the plane: `below.rs` › `extent`), every one still measured within its allowance against the closed forms. *Changing an expectation*: 15 of `FAR_PINS` (the torus at 0 and 10 times its size from the origin, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e6) and 6 of `PINS` (the torus near its bottom at 1 and 1e6) take the new hashes; the reason is a fact (the reach `R hypot(z_j, z_k)` differs from `R √(1 − z_i²)` in the last bits for a torus turned in space, and the box is read), pinned to the bit again, held independently by the same rows' closed forms, said in the test's comments and in the records. A trial Gate on 71e1818a, with them (45650181286): success in every step (`cargo xtask ci`, 737 test binaries and none failed, the benchmark within its baseline, `below_far` 18.9 s, `fuzz_findings` 11.1 s; FreeCAD; the web module's size).

### A pinch along a curve (found 2026-11-24 by random design 20781906, repaired 2026-11-25; `corner.rs` › `kept_both`, `side_of`; `section.rs` › `Kept`, `edge_cuts`; `crates/kernel/tests/boolean_pinch_along_a_curve.rs`)

*Found* by the builder of group B (*A tangent edge across a spline border* › *Narrowed*, above): random design 20781906 (scale 1e-3, ten groups) is built on `main` with zero thickness along two arcs of the circle C along which a cutter's flat end x = 0 touches the cavity an edited torus leaves in a ring; the first form of group B's repair refused it there, the narrowed one built `main`'s body again (two shells, 19 faces, through the deep check).

*Reduced* (`crates/kernel/tests/boolean_pinch_along_a_curve.rs`, in units whose tolerance is 1e-6): a block x from −5 to 2.5, y from −7.5 to 7.5, z from −5 to 2.5, less the torus about the line along x through the design's (y, z) = (−0.6931, 3.8476), centred at x = 5, major radius 10 and minor 5, which reaches x = 0 exactly along C; less a block x from −10 to 0 whose face x = 0 holds C. Where C runs inside the block's section, two arcs 2.64 and 4.75 long, each from a wall to the floor, the body lies beyond x = 0 between the plane and the cavity on both sides of C, and the two sides meet only on C: the block falls into three parts there, the middle and two corners, which touch along the arcs (zero thickness, 2026-10-15). Held so without the kernel (`plan/pinch-along-a-curve/tools/pinch_reduced.py` in the workshop, and the test): across C at the middle of each arc, at x = e²/20 (half of what the cavity leaves there) and e from C, from 1e-1 to 1e-4, the points outward and inward of C are in the body, the point of C between them and every point before the plane are not; the cells of a grid beyond x = 0.05 fall into three parts.

*Traced* (Check 45440350762 in the workshop: a throwaway with the repair switched off and on in one process and prints in the section; `plan/pinch-along-a-curve/logs`). The cavity's face is bounded where C leaves it by four splines, where the torus meets the floor and the walls, and each crosses C there; the torus being tangent to the plane along C, they only touch the plane. Their hits stood where the splines come nearest the plane, 3.3 to 9.3 tolerances off C (the distance from the plane grows with the square of the way along a spline, so its valley is thousands of tolerances wide, and the fit puts its least anywhere in it), inside the plane's face, where group B's repair as narrowed does not move a touch. So C had no cut where it leaves the cavity's face, ran over that face's boundary without one, and was left out whole as a touch that divides nothing (`clip`). Neither face was divided: `main` built one shell of 11 faces, the plane's face and the cavity's touching along the arcs inside both and sharing the four vertices at the hits. Nothing looked along the arcs: `zero_thickness` was given those vertices, where the two faces are joined by them, and the middles of stretches, of which C left none; the look for near approaches passes over two entities that share a vertex anywhere (and would leave two walls of one shell within half the tolerance as they were); `parts_touch` looks between shells. The cause is the section's, not the near look's (`near.rs` is unchanged).

*Cause.* A curve along which two faces touch is an edge of the result where the result keeps both faces there: four faces meet along it, the material between the two on both sides of it (walls that thin to nothing at it from either side, as here) or beyond them on both (two parts resting on each other along it). A spline edge that crosses such a curve ends it, in a place that must be a vertex; with the touch left where the spline's hit is, the curve had no cut and was left out.

*Repaired at the cause* (`section::edge_cuts`, `corner::kept_both`). A spline edge's touch inside the face it touches is taken where `corner::touch` places it (where the curve the spline stands for reaches the surface) when one of the edge's two faces is tangent to that surface there (normals within a thousandth of a radian), keeps to one side of it about the place (walked from it in eight directions until the two are twice the tolerance apart, up to 65 536 tolerances, every walk that gets that far on the same side: `side_of`), and the boolean keeps both that face and the touched one there, by which side of the other operand each lies on and the table of `classify::solids` (`section::Kept`: A's faces where they are outside B for a union and a difference, inside for an intersection; B's outside A for a union, inside for the others). Elsewhere the touch stands where its hit is, as before: where the result keeps one of the two faces or neither, the curve divides nothing, and imprinting it only made faces the torus's chart refuses (20791237 and 20812686, the torus on the side taken away). Only a boolean of two solids reads it; with a sheet among the operands, and in contact's evidence (no operation), every such touch stands. Imprinted, the curve divides both faces, sewing gives each side its own edge along it, and the rules as they were decide: parts that touch are refused as zero thickness (2026-10-15), a wall that thins to nothing within one shell is built (2026-10-18). A void whose wall touches the outer wall only along such a curve (a block beyond x = 0 less the torus within the reduction's block, in `boolean_pinch_along_a_curve_review.rs`) stays a shell of its own, the curve imprinted as slits that end inside the outer face, and is refused as two shells that touch, now at a point of the curve, where `main` and 0.2.1 refused it at a hit 6.3 tolerances off it (decided by the developer, 2026-11-25, [`decisions.md`](../decisions.md)).

*Measured.*

- The reduction (the test file; and the probe, both ways, at 1e-3, 1 and 1e3, as built and turned): the cut is refused as zero thickness at a point of C (within 0.2 tolerances; on `main` built as one shell); made without the refusal it is three shells of the set's volume (143.656990 against 143.656989641 written without the kernel). The same with the block running on past the torus to x = 12 (the corners still end where the torus's outer wall sweeps past them: three parts, refused). With the cutter ending at y = ±6, the block beyond it before x = 0 joins the corners to the middle: one shell whose wall thins to nothing along the arcs, built as on `main`. The union, the intersection and the cutter less the block (the result keeps the cavity's face, the plane's, or neither), and the torus within the block less the cutter (it only touches), are built as on `main`, each within the tolerance times its area of the volume written without the kernel (16 143.656989641, 562.5, 15 437.5, 137.593010359). The torus within the block united with the cutter, two parts resting on each other along the arcs, is refused as zero thickness both ways, on `main` at a hit 6 to 13 tolerances off C, now at a point of it.
- The design: the ten reduced designs of group B's round two, with the move off and on (the probe): 20781906 refused, *"the result would have zero thickness at (0.00000000000000000087, 0.00397, −0.005)"*, the first form's words, on C to 1.7e-18 at the end of the longer arc, and the seed itself so at its group 11; the other nine as on `main`, 20791237 and 20812686 with `main`'s bodies.
- The corpus (Check 45440350762 with the move on, and Check 45587011400 on the repair as built): the digest `35e8fbc97343ca2d` and the score sheet of the nightly Heavy 45516571671 on `main` 22f0bb13 line for line (11 594 agreeing: 10 534 outright, 1 060 zero thickness).
- The benchmark (Check 45587011400 on the repair with its tests, release): its 115 counted lines identical, line for line, to `main`'s (Heavy 45589014998 on 22f0bb13).
- The random designs (Heavy 45586712887 on the repair, from 20 780 000, an hour: 47 204 seeds), seed by seed against `main`'s Heavy 45438163077 on 22f0bb13: 17 differ. 20781906 goes from a body to the refusal on C; twelve keep their groups in other words, ten of them a zero-thickness refusal named at another, rounder place and two that said "imprint lost" or "could not be divided into regions" and now say zero thickness; four went from ok to no verdict in 60 s on a Strix running three other runs, and alone at 900 s (Check 45600588460) each gives `main`'s groups (20814167, 20814658, 20814834, 20820548). No crash, no new bug.
- A trial Gate on 2357d7b6 (45593314617): success in every step (736 test binaries).

*Owed.*

- Two faces of one shell that touch along a curve inside both, where no section curve shows it (a body made by other means than a boolean, or a touch whose curve a spline crosses at a place `corner::touch` cannot find), are seen by nothing: the near look passes over faces that share a vertex and leaves walls within half the tolerance alone, and `within_a_shell` reads such walls as a wall that thins to nothing.
- A touch whose two surfaces part more slowly than twice the tolerance within 65 536 tolerances is not read by `side_of` and stands where its hit is, as before: a contact of higher order, and also a plain one whose surfaces curve apart gently (the review, 2026-11-25: across C they part as `κ s² / 2`, so a walk reaches twice the tolerance within the cap only where `1/κ`, the radius of the curvature they part by, is under 65 536² / 4, about 1.1e9 tolerances, and the walk nearest the way across, up to 22.5° off it, only under about 0.9e9; the torus's tube here is 5e6 tolerances).
- The torus's chart still refuses a face bounded by a circle about its axis where a touch makes that circle a stretch and the result keeps one face or neither (20788885, as on `main`).
